# SUPREME COURT OF THE UNITED STATES: STANDARDS AND TESTS

**Last completed October Term:** 1993
**Processed through:** June 30, 1994, after all eleven chunk-8 matters and all 95 OT1993 inventory Court events.
**Edition:** September 28, 2026

## Federal courts and judicial remedies

### Timely substantive reconsideration in a Government criminal appeal

**Governing question:** When a timely Government motion for reconsideration suspends appellate finality of a suppression ruling.

**Current rule:** A single timely motion seeking substantive alteration of a district court's suppression ruling renders the ruling nonfinal for appeal purposes until the court decides the motion, including when the motion renews a ground the Government earlier abandoned. The full appeal period begins on disposition of the motion. This finality rule does not determine whether the renewed ground remains waived on the merits.

**Authority by component:**

- Suspension of finality, restart of the full appeal period, and absence of an abandoned-ground exception: [United States v. Ibarra — Timely substantive reconsideration in a Government criminal appeal](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_Ibarra_merits_1991-10-15.md), October 15, 1991; unanimous participating per curiam opinion.

**Present operation effective:** *Ibarra*, October 15, 1991.

**Open question:** *Ibarra* leaves bad-faith and successive motions outside its decision.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Ibarra, 502 U.S. 1 (1991) — Appellate finality](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-v-ibarra-502-us-1-1991).

### Statutory channels for review of ongoing Federal Reserve Board proceedings

**Governing question:** Whether a district court may interrupt an ongoing Board notice-or-order proceeding under 12 U.S.C. §1818.

**Current rule:** Section 1818(i)(1) bars an injunction affecting issuance or enforcement of a Board notice or order except through the channels §1818 authorizes: challenges to temporary orders, appellate review of final orders, and Board enforcement proceedings. General bankruptcy jurisdiction and *Leedom v. Kyne* do not create another channel when the proceeding falls within the governmental police-and-regulatory exception and meaningful review of a final order remains available. Continuing nonfinal regulatory proceedings that neither seize estate property nor recover a prepetition claim are not stopped by §§362(a)(3) or (a)(6); §1334 does not override §1818's preclusion.

**Authority by component:**

- Exclusive review channels, bankruptcy-regulatory continuation, and the limit on *Kyne* intervention: [Board of Governors v. MCorp Financial — Jurisdiction to interrupt ongoing Board proceedings](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Board_of_Governors_v_MCorp_Financial_merits_1991-12-03.md), December 3, 1991; Stevens's unanimous participating opinion.

**Present operation effective:** *MCorp*, December 3, 1991.

**Open question:** Actual conflict with estate control at enforcement and validity of the underlying regulation remain outside this channel ruling.

**Holdings navigation:** [Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System v. MCorp Financial, Inc., 502 U.S. 32 (1991) — Bank regulatory review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#board-of-governors-of-the-federal-reserve-system-v-mcorp-financial-inc-502-us-32-1991).

### An appellate brief functioning as a notice of appeal

**Governing question:** Whether a document styled as a brief can satisfy the notice-of-appeal requirement.

**Current rule:** A document filed within the applicable appeal period may serve as the notice if its objective content identifies the appealing party, judgment or order appealed, and appellate court, as Rule 3(c) requires. Informality of title or form cannot alone require dismissal; every required item remains necessary. Under Rule 4's mistaken-filing provision, a notice mistakenly filed in the court of appeals is treated as filed in the district court upon the appellate court's receipt.

**Authority by component:**

- Objective content, timely functional compliance, and mistaken appellate filing: [Smith v. Barry — Law of the decision, Holding and operative rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Smith_v_Barry_merits_1992-01-14.md), January 14, 1992; O'Connor's opinion joined by eight Justices. Scalia's judgment concurrence is not counted as joining the full rationale.

**Present operation effective:** *Smith v. Barry*, January 14, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [Smith v. Barry, 502 U.S. 244 (1992) — Appellate jurisdiction and notices of appeal](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#smith-v-barry-502-us-244-1992).

### Modification of institutional consent decrees

**Governing question:** When Rule 60(b)(5) permits modification of a continuing institutional consent decree and how the change must be framed.

**Current rule:** The party seeking modification must prove a significant change in fact or law making continued enforcement inequitable. An unforeseeable catastrophe is unnecessary, but actual anticipation and acceptance of the relevant risk impose a heavy burden to justify relief. Dissatisfaction, a new administration, or the availability of a cheaper constitutional alternative alone does not suffice. Once the threshold is met, the modification must be suitably tailored to the proven change and preserve lawful protection of the decree's remaining purposes; the court may not simply rewrite the decree to the constitutional minimum. Practical administration and cost may inform the selection of lawful relief but cannot substitute for the threshold showing or authorize unconstitutional conditions. A clarification that the Constitution permits an alternative practice does not by itself eliminate a voluntarily accepted stricter obligation.

**Authority by component:**

- Significant change, inequity, movant's burden, and significance of accepted risk: [Rufo v. Inmates of Suffolk County Jail — Threshold for modifying an institutional consent decree](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Rufo_v_Inmates_of_Suffolk_County_Jail_merits_1992-01-15.md), January 15, 1992.
- Responsive modification, preservation of remaining purposes, and constitutional and financial limits: [Rufo — Fit and limits of the proposed modification](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Rufo_v_Inmates_of_Suffolk_County_Jail_merits_1992-01-15.md), January 15, 1992. Both components are controlled by Stone's opinion joined by White, Scalia, Kennedy, and Souter; O'Connor's remand vote does not join that rationale.

**Present operation effective:** *Rufo*, January 15, 1992.


**Sequence or decisionmaker:** The decree remains binding until lawfully modified; a request for modification does not itself suspend its obligations.

**Holdings navigation:** [Rufo v. Inmates of Suffolk County Jail; Rapone v. Inmates of Suffolk County Jail, 502 U.S. 367 (1992) — Prospective equitable relief](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#rufo-v-inmates-of-suffolk-county-jail-rapone-v-inmates-of-suffolk-county-jail-502-us-367-1992).

### Collateral Rule 11 sanctions despite absence of merits jurisdiction

**Governing question:** Whether an erroneous assertion of subject-matter jurisdiction defeats a Rule 11 sanction for completed litigation misconduct.

**Current rule:** A district court may impose and preserve a valid sanction under the then-effective Rule 11 for litigation misconduct before it even when its assertion of subject-matter jurisdiction over the action is later held erroneous. The sanction must address an independently authorized procedural violation collateral to the merits. It does not validate the jurisdictional error, restore the underlying merits judgment, or authorize another collateral order without that order's own lawful source and genuine independence.

**Authority by component:**

- Rule-based authority, collateral character, and Article III limit: [Willy v. Coastal Corp. — This collateral Rule 11 sanction survives the jurisdictional ruling](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Willy_v_Coastal_Corp_merits_1992-03-03.md), March 3, 1992; Stone's unanimous opinion, extending *Cooter & Gell* from voluntary dismissal to the presented jurisdictional defect.

**Present operation effective:** *Willy*, March 3, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Coercive discovery serving an unauthorized merits action does not acquire this completed sanction's independence. General inherent sanctioning power is not decided.

**Holdings navigation:** [Willy v. Coastal Corp., 503 U.S. 131 (1992) — Collateral procedural authority](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#willy-v-coastal-corp-503-us-131-1992).

### Judicial exhaustion and damages-only federal prison medical-care claims

**Governing question:** Whether exhaustion of an administrative process must precede an existing federal constitutional damages action when Congress has imposed no exhaustion command.

**Current rule:** An express congressional exhaustion requirement governs according to its terms. In the non-APA judicial-exhaustion setting addressed here, absent such a command the court exercises sound discretion consistent with the statutory scheme, weighing prompt judicial access against the actual interests in agency authority, expertise, correction of error, useful fact development, and judicial efficiency. Neither the general BOP delegation nor the contemporaneous §1997e imposes an exhaustion requirement on the existing *Carlson* federal medical-care damages channel. A federal prisoner seeking only damages for a completed medical-care wrong need not exhaust the general BOP procedure considered in *McCarthy*: it cannot award compensation; its initial 15-day filing period and successive 20- and 30-day appeal periods, even with valid-reason extensions, risk forfeiture; and investigation and written responses without a hearing or formal evidentiary findings provide insufficient adjudicative benefit to outweigh access to court. Speculative FTCA conversion or settlement does not supply an available monetary remedy in that process.

**Authority by component:**

- Statutory primacy, judicial discretion, and actual congressional scope: [McCarthy v. Madigan — Statutory design governs the exhaustion inquiry](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/McCarthy_v_Madigan_merits_1992-03-04.md), March 4, 1992.
- Damages-only BOP exception and its operative procedural premises: [McCarthy — This damages-only claim need not pass through the general BOP process](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/McCarthy_v_Madigan_merits_1992-03-04.md), March 4, 1992. Blackmun's opinion joined by Stone, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, and Souter controls both components; Scalia and Thomas join only the result on their no-compensation ground.

**Present operation effective:** *McCarthy*, March 4, 1992.

**Open question:** Prospective relief, a procedure able to award the requested compensation, and materially different administrative adjudication are not decided. The rule supplies no exception to a congressional command and no extension of *Bivens* to a new setting.

**Holdings navigation:** [McCarthy v. Madigan, 503 U.S. 140 (1992) — Administrative exhaustion](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#mccarthy-v-madigan-503-us-140-1992).

**Related current rules:** APA limits on exhaustion of superior-agency appeals states Darby's separate §704 restriction; Prior administrative exhaustion of an FTCA action states McNeil's express statutory prerequisite. Neither changes McCarthy's damages-only BOP holding.

### Interlocutory review of district-court bankruptcy appellate orders

**Governing question:** Whether 28 U.S.C. §158(d) excludes otherwise available interlocutory appellate review under §1292 of an order entered by a district court in its bankruptcy appellate capacity.

**Current rule:** Section 158(d) does not preclude §1292 review of district-court bankruptcy appellate orders. Review remains subject to the particular §1292 route's requirements; under §1292(b), these include a certified controlling legal question, substantial ground for difference of opinion, material advancement of termination, timely application and circuit permission. The construction creates no automatic appeal and does not extend district-court language to bankruptcy appellate panels.

**Authority by component:**
- Coexistence and preserved conditions: [Connecticut National Bank v. Germain](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Connecticut_National_Bank_v_Germain_merits_1992-03-09.md), March 9, 1992, “Section 158(d) does not exclude eligible interlocutory review”; Thomas for Stone, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter. The four judgment-only votes do not supply the text/coexistence rationale.

**Present operation effective:** Germain, March 9, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [Connecticut National Bank v. Germain, 503 U.S. 249 (1992) — Bankruptcy appellate jurisdiction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#connecticut-national-bank-v-germain-503-us-249-1992).

### Punitive pollution penalties under the Clean Water Act and RCRA

**Governing question:** Federal immunity from punitive fines for completed violations under the Clean Water Act and Resource Conservation and Recovery Act provisions effective April 21, 1992.

**Current rule:** The four statutory routes retain distinct limits:

- The Clean Water Act §505, 33 U.S.C. §1365, and RCRA §7002, 42 U.S.C. §6972, citizen-suit authorizations permit proceedings against the United States but do not unequivocally authorize punitive civil penalties incorporated from their separate enforcement provisions. Incorporation includes the penalty provisions' limits, whose generally defined liable persons exclude the United States.
- Clean Water Act §313, 33 U.S.C. §1323, does not unequivocally waive punitive fines for completed violations through its submission to requirements, process and sanctions. In that context, sanctions enforcing process support coercive compliance relief without clearly adding retrospective punishment.
- Section 313's qualified reference to civil penalties does not independently provide the missing punitive waiver. State-law penalties do not arise under federal law merely because EPA approved the state program, and the incorporated federal penalty definition still excludes the United States from liable persons.
- RCRA §6001, 42 U.S.C. §6961, submits facilities to substantive and procedural requirements, including permits, reporting, injunctive relief and sanctions enforcing that relief, without unequivocally authorizing punitive fines for past violations.

Enacted compliance duties and conceded prospective or coercive compliance remedies remain effective and distinct; no general immunity from every environmental remedy follows.

**Authority by component:**
- Both citizen-suit routes: [United States Department of Energy v. Ohio](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_Department_of_Energy_v_Ohio_merits_1992-04-21.md), April 21, 1992, holding 1, Souter's Part II-A for Stone, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas.
- Clean Water Act process/sanctions: the same record, holding 2, Part II-B.1, same six Justices.
- Civil-penalty clause and federal-law limitation: the same record, holding 3, Part II-B.2, same six Justices.
- RCRA facilities: the same record, holding 4, Part II-C, all nine Justices.

**Present operation effective:** Department of Energy v. Ohio, April 21, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Other express penalty waivers, later enactments and any particular coercive contempt order are outside the decision. Federal approval is not held irrelevant to every aspect of state permitting; the source distinction concerns the punitive waiver.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States Department of Energy v. Ohio, 503 U.S. 607 (1992) — Federal sovereign immunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-department-of-energy-v-ohio-503-us-607-1992).

### Factual frivolousness and review of fee-screening dismissals

**Governing question:** Dismissal as factually frivolous under then-effective 28 U.S.C. §1915(d), and appellate review of that screening determination.

**Current rule:** A court may dismiss a claim proceeding without prepayment when its factual allegations are clearly baseless—irrational or wholly incredible—even without judicially noticeable contrary facts. The initial assessment must favor the plaintiff; mere improbability, extraordinary circumstances or ordinary disbelief is insufficient, and screening cannot decide genuine factual disputes. Review is for abuse of discretion, with legal errors independently correctable. The reviewing court must address relevant circumstances, including pro se status, resolution of genuine factual disputes, legal errors, adequacy of reasons, prejudice and whether more specific pleading could cure the defect. Such dismissal is not a merits adjudication barring a paid complaint with the same allegations; its possible effect on later fee-screening decisions makes prejudice and curability material.

**Authority by component:**
- Irrational-or-wholly-incredible threshold and plaintiff-favorable limit: [Denton v. Hernandez](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Denton_v_Hernandez_merits_1992-05-04.md), May 4, 1992, “Factual frivolousness under section 1915(d).”
- Review, relevant circumstances and nonmerits character: the same record, “Appellate review and the consequences of dismissal.” O'Connor for Stone, White, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas controls both propositions; the dissenters' agreement with the factual standard is not a whole-opinion join.

**Present operation effective:** Denton, May 4, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Corroborating affidavits are not a pleading prerequisite. Rule 12(b)(6) merits pleading remains distinct from factual-frivolity screening.

**Open question:** No absolute notice-and-amendment entitlement or comprehensive preclusion rule for later fee applications is adopted.

**Holdings navigation:** [Denton v. Hernandez, 504 U.S. 25 (1992) — Fee-waiver screening and appellate review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#denton-v-hernandez-504-us-25-1992).

### Concrete personal interests and procedural citizen-suit standing

**Governing question:** Article III standing for a citizen-suit plaintiff alleging a deprivation of procedure, including ESA §11(g) enforcement.

**Current rule:** A plaintiff invoking federal jurisdiction must establish a concrete and particularized, actual or imminent injury; a causal connection fairly traceable to the defendant's challenged conduct rather than to independent third parties' own decisions; and a likelihood, not mere speculation, that the requested relief will redress the injury. Authorization for any person to sue does not supply standing for only the public's undifferentiated interest in lawful administration. A procedural right must protect the plaintiff's own threatened concrete interest; when that connection exists, the plaintiff need not prove that the required procedure will certainly alter the ultimate agency decision. Aesthetic, recreational or professional wildlife interests may qualify, but past visits and indefinite return intentions without concrete plans or another supported basis for imminent renewed exposure do not establish threatened interference with personal use. At summary judgment the plaintiff must support challenged standing elements with specific facts by affidavit or other evidence, with favorable reasonable inferences available; allegations cannot replace missing evidence.

**Authority by component:**
- Stage-specific evidentiary requirement and existing injury, traceability and redressability elements: [Lujan v. Defenders of Wildlife, “1. Article III elements must be supported at the litigation stage”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Lujan_v_Defenders_of_Wildlife_merits_1992-06-12.md), June 12, 1992; Scalia Part II, joined by White, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas (five).
- Prospective personal-use boundary: Lujan, “2. Indefinite return intentions and general species interests do not establish threatened personal injury here,” June 12, 1992; Scalia Part III-A, same five-Justice opinion; Stone separately supports the record-specific conclusion.
- Citizen-suit and procedural-right boundary: Lujan, “3. Citizen-suit authorization does not dispense with a threatened concrete interest,” June 12, 1992; Scalia Part IV, same five Justices.

**Present operation effective:** Lujan v. Defenders of Wildlife, June 12, 1992, for the identified citizen-suit and procedural-right clarification; ordinary Article III elements and evidentiary burdens are retained.

**Exceptions and limits:** No purchased-ticket or particular-document requirement is adopted. The redressability application to absent agencies, binding force of the challenged regulation upon those agencies, effect of particular funding changes and ESA §7(a)(2)'s foreign reach are not controlling holdings.

**Holdings navigation:** [Lujan v. Defenders of Wildlife, 504 U.S. 555 (1992) — Standing and personal injury](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#lujan-v-defenders-of-wildlife-504-us-555-1992).

### The domestic-relations exception to diversity jurisdiction

**Governing question:** Whether a dispute involving family relationships falls outside federal diversity jurisdiction under §1332.

**Current rule:** The statutory domestic-relations exception is limited to issuance of divorce, alimony and child-custody decrees. It does not exclude an otherwise proper diverse-parties tort action for damages over alleged child abuse that seeks no such decree. Article III imposes no categorical domestic-relations bar. Family relationship alone does not supply Younger or Burford abstention: their ordinary prerequisites remain, including an ongoing state proceeding for the interference asserted under Younger and the relevant unsettled-status or state-policy interference for Burford.

**Authority by component:**
- Statutory exception and constitutional boundary: [Ankenbrandt v. Richards, “Diversity jurisdiction and the limited domestic-relations exception”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Ankenbrandt_v_Richards_merits_1992-06-15.md), June 15, 1992; White, joined by O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter (five).
- Preserved abstention limits: Ankenbrandt, “Younger supplies no ground for dismissal” and “Burford supplies no ground for dismissal on this record,” June 15, 1992; same five Justices, applying rather than changing those doctrines.

**Present operation effective:** Ankenbrandt v. Richards, June 15, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [Ankenbrandt v. Richards, 504 U.S. 689 (1992) — Diversity jurisdiction and domestic-relations abstention](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#ankenbrandt-v-richards-504-us-689-1992).

### Commercial sovereign debt and direct effects under the FSIA

**Governing question:** The commercial-activity exception in 28 U.S.C. §§1603(d) and 1605(a)(2), especially its third clause for activity outside the United States causing a direct effect here.

**Current rule:** The nature of the conduct controls commercial character: conduct of the kind a private market participant can undertake is commercial despite a sovereign public purpose. Issuing and rescheduling ordinary negotiable debt does not cease to be commercial because it implements a monetary program or refinances existing obligations. An effect is direct when it follows as an immediate consequence of the activity; the statute adds no separate substantiality or foreseeability requirement, although wholly trivial effects remain outside its ordinary reach. Missing a New York payment contractually required after the holder validly exercises an offered payment option supplies a direct United States effect even when the holders are foreign and their ultimate economic losses occur abroad.

**Authority by component:**
- Nature rather than purpose: [Republic of Argentina v. Weltover, Inc., “1. Ordinary negotiable debt remains commercial despite a sovereign monetary purpose”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Republic_of_Argentina_v_Weltover_Inc_merits_1992-06-12.md), June 12, 1992; Scalia, joined by White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas (eight).
- Direct domestic effect: Weltover, “2. Missing the contractually required New York payment has a direct domestic effect,” June 12, 1992; same eight Justices.

**Present operation effective:** Republic of Argentina v. Weltover, Inc., June 12, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** All sovereign financial activity is not automatically commercial, and every dollar-denominated payment is not automatically a direct domestic effect. Foreign-sovereign constitutional personhood, an independent personal-jurisdiction claim, contractual liability, attachment and execution remain unresolved.

**Holdings navigation:** [Republic of Argentina v. Weltover, Inc., 504 U.S. 607 (1992) — Foreign sovereign immunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#republic-of-argentina-v-weltover-inc-504-us-607-1992).

### Red Cross charter jurisdiction and removal

**Governing question:** Federal original jurisdiction and removal for actions to which the American National Red Cross is a party under its federal charter.

**Current rule:** The charter's sue-and-be-sued authorization expressly naming State or Federal courts confers original federal jurisdiction over Red Cross actions. Specific mention of federal courts permits the jurisdictional construction that general corporate-capacity language does not; jurisdiction rests on that grant, not federal incorporation alone. Section 1441 therefore permits removal of a Red Cross action alleging state-law torts without a §1331 federal claim on the face of the complaint. Article III permits that particular corporate-party grant even though state law supplies the immediate claim.

**Authority by component:**
- Charter construction: [American National Red Cross v. S. G., “The express federal-court clause grants original jurisdiction”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/American_National_Red_Cross_v_S_G_merits_1992-06-19.md), June 19, 1992.
- Removal distinct from §1331: Red Cross, “The special grant supports removal without a §1331 complaint,” June 19, 1992.
- Constitutional permission: Red Cross, “Article III permits the special corporate-party grant,” June 19, 1992.
- Each proposition: Souter, joined by White, Blackmun, Stevens and Thomas (five).

**Present operation effective:** American National Red Cross v. S. G., June 19, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Section 1349's application to nonstock corporations, materially different charters, diversity, joinder and independently available abstention remain unresolved. Jurisdiction creates no federal substantive tort law, immunity or liability.

**Holdings navigation:** [American National Red Cross v. S. G., 505 U.S. 247 (1992) — Federal corporate charters and removal](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#american-national-red-cross-v-s-g-505-us-247-1992).

### Meaningful partial relief after summons compliance

**Governing question:** What instructions govern meaningful partial relief after summons compliance?

**Current rule:** Compliance with an IRS summons-enforcement order does not moot a timely appeal when a court could still afford meaningful partial relief by ordering return or destruction of retained copies if enforcement proves unlawful. Complete restoration of secrecy is unnecessary. Availability of review does not establish entitlement to return, destruction, privilege protection or future-use restrictions; the full scope of enforcement-condition authority remains unresolved.

**Authority by component:**

- Controlling holding: [Church of Scientology of California v. United States — Controlling holding](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/church-of-scientology-v-united-states-merits-1992-11-16.md), 1992-11-16. Stevens’s opinion for the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Thomas: nine votes for this rule and its application.

**Present operation effective:** 1992-11-16; Church of Scientology of California v. United States, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Church of Scientology of California v. United States, 506 U.S. 9 (1992)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#church-of-scientology-of-california-v-united-states-506-us-9-1992).

### Associations seeking statutory fee relief

**Governing question:** What instructions govern associations seeking statutory fee relief?

**Current rule:** An association is not categorically excluded from then-effective 28 U.S.C. §1915: the Dictionary Act's inclusive definition applies because ordinary agency and representation permit the affidavit, financial-inability and counsel provisions to operate. Context can displace that definition, but these features do not. An authorized affidavit must establish actual inability to pay costs or give security and asserted entitlement to redress. Courts may examine legally available resources and sham concealment without automatically aggregating every member's assets. Inability to hold an entity account or a balance-sheet deficit alone does not compel relief. Lawful representation and screening remain necessary; §1915 neither licenses lay entity advocacy nor guarantees appointed counsel. No universal organizational means test is established.

**Authority by component:**

- Holding 1 — associations may apply.: [Rowland v. California Men’s Colony, Unit II Men’s Advisory Council — Holding 1 — associations may apply.](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/rowland-v-california-mens-colony-merits-1993-01-12.md), 1993-01-12. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Kennedy: five votes for this construction and its operative limits. Kennedy’s separate account of permissible workability analysis adds no condition to the Court’s holding.
- Holding 2 — eligibility does not establish the showing.: [Rowland v. California Men’s Colony, Unit II Men’s Advisory Council — Holding 2 — eligibility does not establish the showing.](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/rowland-v-california-mens-colony-merits-1993-01-12.md), 1993-01-12. The same five-Justice Opinion of the Court supplies this remand rule and limitation. The dissenters would require denial because of entity status and do not join the Court’s application process.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-01-12; Rowland v. California Men’s Colony, Unit II Men’s Advisory Council, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Rowland v. California Men’s Colony, Unit II Men’s Advisory Council, 506 U.S. 194 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#rowland-v-california-mens-colony-unit-ii-mens-advisory-council-506-us-194-1993).

### Exclusive interstate jurisdiction and private boundary-related claims

**Governing question:** What instructions govern exclusive interstate jurisdiction and private boundary-related claims?

**Current rule:** Under §1251(a), a federal district court cannot grant relief on an actual controversy between two States, even if joined to private litigation or this Court previously declined an original action. Such a complaint must be dismissed for want of jurisdiction. An otherwise lawful private-title action may determine a boundary incidentally without binding the States on their sovereign boundary; a State's intervention seeking no relief against another State is not barred on that ground. Original-forum discretion does not transfer exclusive jurisdiction to another court.

**Authority by component:**

- An interstate complaint belongs to the exclusive original forum: [Mississippi v. Louisiana — An interstate complaint belongs to the exclusive original forum](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Mississippi_v_Louisiana_merits_1992-12-14.md), 1992-12-14. Stone-Zsela’s opinion, joined by all eight Associate Justices; unanimous controlling holding.
- Private title questions remain distinct from binding interstate relief: [Mississippi v. Louisiana — Private title questions remain distinct from binding interstate relief](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Mississippi_v_Louisiana_merits_1992-12-14.md), 1992-12-14. Stone-Zsela’s opinion, joined by all eight Associate Justices; unanimous controlling holding and remand limitation.

**Present operation effective:** 1992-12-14; Mississippi v. Louisiana, for the components identified above.

### Continuing forfeiture jurisdiction and lawful return of proceeds

**Governing question:** What instructions govern continuing forfeiture jurisdiction and lawful return of proceeds?

**Current rule:** Once valid attachment establishes in rem jurisdiction, removal of property or substituted proceeds does not by itself defeat appellate jurisdiction. Failure to obtain a stay or bond does not prevent appeal when lawful effectual relief remains possible; an entirely useless judgment is a distinct limit. If a claimant succeeds and obtains the requisite judgment, §2465 supplies return of substituted proceeds, with payment under 31 U.S.C. §1304 and 28 U.S.C. §2414 when not otherwise provided, certified by the Comptroller General, and satisfying the final-judgment and payable-judgment requirements. No appropriation-free judicial spending authority follows. Application of the October 28, 1992 §1355(c) amendment to earlier transfers or appeals remains unresolved.

**Authority by component:**

- Removal does not itself terminate perfected forfeiture jurisdiction: [Republic National Bank of Miami v. United States — Removal does not itself terminate perfected forfeiture jurisdiction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Republic_National_Bank_of_Miami_v_United_States_merits_1992-12-14.md), 1992-12-14. Parts I, II and IV of Blackmun’s opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter; eight Justices adopt this rule. Thomas supports the judgment on a different statutory ground.
- The return statute and judgment appropriation permit effective relief upon success: [Republic National Bank of Miami v. United States — The return statute and judgment appropriation permit effective relief upon success](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Republic_National_Bank_of_Miami_v_United_States_merits_1992-12-14.md), 1992-12-14. Stone-Zsela’s statutory-payment opinion, joined by White, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas, supplies six express joins and independently controls. Stevens expressly accepts the same sufficient statutory proposition in his own writing, making seven supporters without an invented formal join.

**Present operation effective:** 1992-12-14; Republic National Bank of Miami v. United States, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Republic National Bank of Miami v. United States, 506 U.S. 80 (1992)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#republic-national-bank-of-miami-v-united-states-506-us-80-1992).

### Forfeiture venue, process and protection during appeal

**Governing question:** What instructions govern forfeiture venue, process and protection during appeal?

**Current rule:** Under 28 U.S.C. §1355(b)(1), a forfeiture proceeding may be brought where an act or omission giving rise to forfeiture occurred or where §1395 or another statute specifically provides venue. For property abroad or detained or seized under foreign legal process or competent foreign authority, subsection (b)(2) also permits the District of Columbia. Under (d), a court with subsection (b) jurisdiction may issue process in another district to bring the property before it. Under (c), removal by the prevailing party of property subject to an appealed final civil-forfeiture order does not deprive the court of jurisdiction. On the appellant's motion, the district court or court of appeals shall issue any order necessary to preserve the right to full value, including a stay or appeal bond; a stay is not the exclusive required measure. The text enters law October 28, 1992; its application to pre-enactment events is not determined by its admission or by Republic National Bank.

**Authority by component:**

- Venue and extraterritorial process: [Pub. L. 102-550, §1521, adding 28 U.S.C. §1355(b), (d)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Forfeiture_jurisdiction_amendment_1992-10-28.md), effective October 28, 1992. Removal during appeal and protection on motion: the same enactment, §1355(c).

**Present operation effective:** 1992-10-28; Civil-forfeiture jurisdiction amendment, for the components identified above.

### Immediate appeal of conclusive Eleventh Amendment immunity denials

**Governing question:** What instructions govern immediate appeal of conclusive Eleventh Amendment immunity denials?

**Current rule:** A State or asserted arm of a State may immediately appeal under §1291 a conclusive denial of Eleventh Amendment immunity from federal suit when the issue is separate from the merits and the protection would be effectively lost by awaiting final judgment. A dispute over arm-of-State status does not itself require postponement until trial. Appealability establishes neither immunity nor entity status, waiver or abrogation; prospective officer-relief rules retain their separate limits.

**Authority by component:**

- Controlling holding: [Puerto Rico Aqueduct & Sewer Authority v. Metcalf & Eddy, Inc. — Controlling holding](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/puerto-rico-aqueduct-v-metcalf-eddy-merits-1993-01-12.md), 1993-01-12. White’s Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Blackmun, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas: seven votes for this rule and its controlling explanation. Stone-Zsela supplies an eighth vote for reversal and the narrower procedural rule in his judgment concurrence. No fractured-decision analysis is necessary.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-01-12; Puerto Rico Aqueduct & Sewer Authority v. Metcalf & Eddy, Inc., for the components identified above.

### Senate committee evidence-taking in an impeachment trial

**Governing question:** What instructions govern senate committee evidence-taking in an impeachment trial?

**Current rule:** The Senate's use of a committee to receive testimony is not judicially revisable on the objection to that evidentiary allocation when the full Senate receives the record, hears argument and retains deliberation and judgment. This is a nonjusticiability rule for that procedural claim, not constitutional merits approval. Judicial enforcement of the oath, presidential-trial presiding-officer and two-thirds requirements, and review of a purported proceeding abandoning adjudication altogether, remain open.

**Authority by component:**

- The presented committee-evidence challenge is not judicially revisable: [Nixon v. United States — The presented committee-evidence challenge is not judicially revisable](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Nixon_v_United_States_merits_1993-01-13.md), 1993-01-13. Stone-Zsela’s opinion of the Court, joined by Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas. Six of nine Justices adopt this exact ground and its limits; White and Blackmun’s merits ground is noncontrolling, and Souter does not formally join the Court’s opinion.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-01-13; Nixon v. United States, for the components identified above.

### Commercial activity supplying the basis of an FSIA action

**Governing question:** What instructions govern commercial activity supplying the basis of an FSIA action?

**Current rule:** Under the first clause of §1605(a)(2), commercial activity carried on in the United States with substantial United States contact must supply the action's basis: the conduct constituting elements entitling the plaintiff to relief. Prior domestic recruitment or contracting and a commercial retaliatory motive do not by themselves meet that requirement for foreign police detention-and-torture claims. Abuse through sovereign police authority is sovereign in nature despite that motive. This addresses the asserted commercial exception, not legality of abuse or other exceptions. The warning-claim disposition in Nelson supplies no controlling general rule for independent recruitment-warning omissions; the competing rationales are not logical subsets.

**Authority by component:**

- 1. The first commercial-activity clause requires the action to rest on that activity: [Saudi Arabia v. Nelson — 1. The first commercial-activity clause requires the action to rest on that activity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Saudi_Arabia_v_Nelson_merits_1993-03-23.md), 1993-03-23. Souter's opinion for the Court, excluding its warning paragraph, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas: six. White and Blackmun agree in the intentional-claim judgment on a separate domestic-nexus ground.
- 2. Alleged abuse of sovereign police power is not commercial conduct: [Saudi Arabia v. Nelson — 2. Alleged abuse of sovereign police power is not commercial conduct](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Saudi_Arabia_v_Nelson_merits_1993-03-23.md), 1993-03-23. Souter's Court opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas: six. Intentional-claim reversal receives eight judgment votes; White and Blackmun reach it without joining the sovereign-nature characterization.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-23; Saudi Arabia v. Nelson, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Saudi Arabia v. Nelson, 507 U.S. 349 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#saudi-arabia-v-nelson-507-us-349-1993).

### Appellate connection required for preappeal fugitive dismissal

**Governing question:** What instructions govern appellate connection required for preappeal fugitive dismissal?

**Current rule:** When flight and recapture both precede a timely appeal, fugitive status alone does not justify dismissal. The sanction requires a sufficient connection to the appellate process. Reasonable rules for recurring classes of disruption and dismissal for established interference, inability to conduct meaningful review or prejudice to a legally available retrial remain possible. Retrial prejudice cannot justify dismissing a sufficiency appeal whose success would prohibit retrial.

**Authority by component:**

- A sufficient appellate connection is required: [Ortega-Rodriguez v. United States — A sufficient appellate connection is required](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Ortega-Rodriguez_v_United_States_merits_1993-03-08.md), 1993-03-08. Stevens's opinion for the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter; six Justices adopt the rule, qualifications and remand.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-08; Ortega-Rodriguez v. United States, for the components identified above.

### Unreasonable-rate counterclaims, recoupment and primary jurisdiction

**Governing question:** What instructions govern unreasonable-rate counterclaims, recoupment and primary jurisdiction?

**Current rule:** A shipper may assert §11705(b)(3) unreasonable-rate damages as a counterclaim in an undercharge action on the same shipments; Rule 8(c) permits correcting a defense designation when justice requires. Under §11706(g), accrual occurs upon delivery or tender, without prior payment. A negotiated rate alone supplies no common-law defense to the filed tariff. The two-year affirmative limitation in §11706(c)(2) does not bar same-transaction recoupment up to the carrier's asserted liability, but supplies no extension for unrelated claims or excess affirmative recovery. The court must allow a reasonable opportunity to obtain the ICC's rate determination. It retains jurisdiction and ordinarily stays proceedings; dismissal without prejudice is permitted only if it would not unfairly disadvantage the parties. Neither payment nor a completed ICC ruling is required to file.

**Authority by component:**

- The statutory claim may be asserted before payment: [Reiter v. Cooper — The statutory claim may be asserted before payment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Reiter_v_Cooper_merits_1993-03-08.md), 1993-03-08. Scalia's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; eight Justices adopt this statutory rule and application.
- Same-transaction recoupment survives the affirmative limitation: [Reiter v. Cooper — Same-transaction recoupment survives the affirmative limitation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Reiter_v_Cooper_merits_1993-03-08.md), 1993-03-08. Scalia's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; eight Justices support this independent limitation ruling.
- Primary jurisdiction does not withdraw judicial jurisdiction: [Reiter v. Cooper — Primary jurisdiction does not withdraw judicial jurisdiction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Reiter_v_Cooper_merits_1993-03-08.md), 1993-03-08. Scalia's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; eight Justices adopt this allocation and its qualifications.

### Separate judgment while a related counterclaim remains

**Governing question:** What instructions govern separate judgment while a related counterclaim remains?

**Current rule:** A separate final judgment on the carrier's claim requires Rule 54(b)'s express no-just-reason-for-delay determination, considering judicial administration and actual equities. Prompt suit by a solvent carrier ordinarily favors separate judgment. Actual or threatened insolvency of the party seeking judgment weighs against it without creating an absolute bar. Appropriate security and a Rule 62(h) enforcement stay remain available where other equities justify them.

**Authority by component:**

- Separate judgment requires the ordinary discretionary determination: [Reiter v. Cooper — Separate judgment requires the ordinary discretionary determination](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Reiter_v_Cooper_merits_1993-03-08.md), 1993-03-08. Scalia's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; eight Justices support the rule and remand rather than deciding discretion initially.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-08; Reiter v. Cooper, for the components identified above.

### Live patent-invalidity counterclaims after noninfringement affirmance

**Governing question:** What instructions govern live patent-invalidity counterclaims after noninfringement affirmance?

**Current rule:** An intermediate appellate affirmance of noninfringement, itself subject to Supreme Court review, does not terminate jurisdiction over a live independent invalidity counterclaim. Once an actual controversy exists, the party asserting mootness must show a change eliminating it. Noninfringement affirmance alone neither supplies that change nor authorizes automatic vacatur of invalidity. Relabeling automatic avoidance as discretion does not save it. Actual mootness and unusual lawful case-specific grounds for declining review remain available; there is no universal duty to decide validity in every patent appeal.

- An intermediate noninfringement ruling does not terminate counterclaim jurisdiction: [Cardinal Chemical Co. v. Morton International, Inc. — An intermediate noninfringement ruling does not terminate counterclaim jurisdiction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/cardinal_merits_1993-05-17.md), 1993-05-17. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.
- An established invalidity controversy does not disappear without a change: [Cardinal Chemical Co. v. Morton International, Inc. — An established invalidity controversy does not disappear without a change](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/cardinal_merits_1993-05-17.md), 1993-05-17. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.
- The automatic practice cannot be saved as discretion on this record: [Cardinal Chemical Co. v. Morton International, Inc. — The automatic practice cannot be saved as discretion on this record](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/cardinal_merits_1993-05-17.md), 1993-05-17. ### Filing-time bar for overlapping Claims Court litigation

**Governing question:** What instructions govern filing-time bar for overlapping Claims Court litigation?

**Current rule:** Section 1500 bars a Claims Court action when, at filing, a suit for or in respect to the same claim is pending elsewhere against the United States or a covered federal actor. Later dismissal of the other action does not cure the original defect. Claims arising from substantially the same operative facts and seeking overlapping relief are not separated merely by different legal theories or the inability to plead them in one forum. The Court leaves open the reverse filing order and suits seeking wholly different relief; no general tolling rule follows.

**Authority by component:**

- Section1500 jurisdiction is measured when the action is filed: [Keene Corp. v. United States — Section1500 jurisdiction is measured when the action is filed](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/keene_merits_1993-05-24.md), 1993-05-24. Souter’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (8 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.
- Overlapping facts and relief cannot be separated merely by changing legal theories: [Keene Corp. v. United States — Overlapping facts and relief cannot be separated merely by changing legal theories](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/keene_merits_1993-05-24.md), 1993-05-24. Souter’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (8 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.

### Federal rules applied to parties govern still-open direct review

**Governing question:** What instructions govern federal rules applied to parties govern still-open direct review?

**Current rule:** When this Court applies a federal rule to the parties before it, that rule governs all still-open direct-review cases concerning events within its substantive scope, whether before or after announcement. Selective prospectivity cannot withhold the rule from another such case. Chevron Oil is displaced to that extent. Final judgments are not reopened; genuine remedial rules remain distinct, and every conceivable prospective disposition is not decided.

- An applied federal rule governs still-open direct review: [Harper v. ### Unequal opportunity to compete and associational standing

**Governing question:** What instructions govern unequal opportunity to compete and associational standing?

**Current rule:** A governmental barrier preventing persons able and ready to bid from competing on equal terms supplies injury without proof that they would have won a contract. An association may sue when a member has individual standing, the interest is germane to its purpose, and neither the claim nor relief requires individual-member participation. Accepted allegations suffice only at the appropriate stage; genuinely disputed elements require stage-appropriate proof. Replacing an ordinance does not moot the dispute when the successor continues the challenged exclusion from particular competitions; a replacement actually ending that injury may have a different effect.

Thomas’s Opinion of the Court, Equal opportunity to compete is a concrete injury portion; Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (7 Justices). ### Intervention denial and statutory party status for Supreme Court review

**Governing question:** Whether an unsuccessful intervention applicant may seek review of the parties’ underlying judgment.

**Current rule:** Under §1254(1), an unsuccessful intervention applicant may seek review of the denial itself, but cannot obtain review of the parties’ underlying judgment merely through an unreviewed intervention denial or financial interest. The applicant does not become a party before that denial is overturned. Article III interest, statutory party status and proper question presentation remain distinct.

**Authority by component:**

- Review route and party-status prerequisite: [Izumi Seimitsu Kogyo Kabushiki Kaisha v. U.S. Philips Corp — Review of a denied intervention application is distinct from review as a party](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Izumi_Seimitsu_Kogyo_Kabushiki_Kaisha_v_US_Philips_Corp_DIG_1993-11-30.md#public-projection), 1993-11-30. Per curiam opinion of the Court supported by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg, seven Justices; Blackmun and Stevens dissent. This stated threshold ground supports the dismissal and carries procedural authority, without a settlement-vacatur holding.

**Present operation effective:** Izumi Seimitsu Kogyo Kabushiki Kaisha v. U.S. Philips Corp, 1993-11-30.

### Presented questions, subsidiary arguments and exceptional Supreme Court review

**Governing question:** When an issue outside the stated certiorari question may be considered.

**Current rule:** Rule 14.1(a) creates a strong prudential presumption against deciding questions neither stated in the petition nor fairly included, subject to exceptional circumstances. A distinct intervention denial is not fairly included merely because reversing it would permit reaching a settlement-vacatur question; discussion in the petition’s body and merits briefing does not alone suffice. A consistently asserted Teague defense may be subsidiary where it limits application of the very constitutional rule and habeas relief challenged by the petition. Geographic tailoring is fairly included in a challenge to exclusion from designated speech zones. An exceptional extension requires its own supported basis; adversarial briefing, preservation, logical priority or usefulness alone does not enlarge the questions. The duty to notice jurisdictional defects remains separate. A respondent may defend the existing favorable judgment on a record-supported alternative rejected below when doing so neither enlarges its own rights nor reduces the adversary’s rights under that judgment. The Court may also elect to consider a deliberately disavowed legal argument supporting the same preserved claim when it falls within the question presented and can be resolved on the established record without a new factual theory or additional findings. That discretion imposes no duty to consider abandoned grounds and does not erase limits on distinct claims, relief, factual stipulations or missing facts.

**Authority by component:**

- Strong prudential presumption and independent-question boundary: [Izumi Seimitsu Kogyo Kabushiki Kaisha v. U.S. Philips Corp — The intervention question was not fairly included and no exception is warranted](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Izumi_Seimitsu_Kogyo_Kabushiki_Kaisha_v_US_Philips_Corp_DIG_1993-11-30.md#public-projection), 1993-11-30. Per curiam opinion of the Court supported by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg, seven Justices; Blackmun and Stevens dissent. The reasoned dismissal establishes this question-presentation ruling, not the merits of the judgment below.
- Confined subsidiary Teague defense: [Caspari v. Bohlen — The preserved nonretroactivity defense is fairly included here](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Caspari_v_Bohlen_merits_1994-02-23.md#public-projection), 1994-02-23. O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; eight Justices. Stevens disagrees with fair inclusion and would decline the defense under Rule 14.1(a).
- Geographic inclusion and independently justified exceptional review: [Madsen v. Women's Health Center, Inc — The limited scope of geographic and paragraph (4) review; The independent conduct-language claims remain undecided](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Madsen_v_Womens_Health_Center_Inc_merits_1994-06-30.md#public-projection), 1994-06-30. O'Connor's Part I, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg: eight. Stevens declines these distinct merits questions. O'Connor's Part IV-A, joined by Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg: eight on the Rule 14 ground. Stone-Zsela agrees only in the nonreach disposition for these independently disputed terms and applications and adds no ground.

- The two renewed alternatives may defend the existing favorable judgment: [The two renewed alternatives may defend the existing favorable judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/United_States_v_X_Citement_Video_Inc_merits_1994-11-29.md#public-projection), 1994-11-29. Part II of Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Breyer, supplies six direct joins to this reach ruling. Stone-Zsela joins only the dispositions on the two alternatives and adds no reach ground. Scalia and Thomas accept the legal availability of such defenses but do not decide either alternative because their independent ground would sustain the appellate judgment.
- The Court may consider this included legal argument despite its earlier disavowal: [The Court may consider this included legal argument despite its earlier disavowal](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Lebron_v_National_Railroad_Passenger_Corp_merits_1995-02-21.md#public-projection), 1995-02-21. Justice Scalia's reachability portion, joined by Justices Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer. Seven Justices adopt the stated discretionary-review rationale; Chief Justice Stone-Zsela reaches the question but does not join this portion, and Justice O'Connor declines the argument as a prudential matter.

**Present operation effective:** Lebron, February 21, 1995, for the bounded deliberate-disavowal component; X-Citement Video, November 29, 1994, for the favorable-judgment defense; the earlier authorities retain their separate components.

**Exceptions and limits:** Madsen’s separate noise-and-images review rested together on a continuing categorical restraint, direct connection to the clinic remedy, existing medical record, adversarial presentation and severable legal correction. It declined further independent conduct-language questions despite briefing. Those circumstances are not a universal mandatory checklist or permission to review every related clause.

**Holdings navigation:** [The intervention question was not fairly included and no exception is warranted](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-intervention-question-was-not-fairly-included-and-no-exception-is-warranted); [The preserved nonretroactivity defense is fairly included here](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-preserved-nonretroactivity-defense-is-fairly-included-here); [The limited scope of geographic and paragraph (4) review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-limited-scope-of-geographic-and-paragraph-4-review); [The independent conduct-language claims remain undecided](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-independent-conduct-language-claims-remain-undecided); [The two renewed alternatives may defend the existing favorable judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-two-renewed-alternatives-may-defend-the-existing-favorable-judgment); [The Court may consider this included legal argument despite its earlier disavowal](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-court-may-consider-this-included-legal-argument-despite-its-earlier-disavowal).

### Customs forfeiture promptness duties and dismissal

**Governing question:** Whether breach of §§1602–1604 requires dismissal of a timely forfeiture action.

**Current rule:** Breach of §§1602–1604’s separate reporting and commencement duties does not require dismissal of a forfeiture action filed within §1621’s five-year limitations period. Absence of that dismissal sanction does not make the duties optional. An independently supported constitutional-delay claim or other source-authorized relief remains available. This consequence rule does not replace the provisions’ separate reporting and commencement inquiries.

**Authority by component:**

- Mandatory duties, limitations period and absent automatic dismissal: [United States v. James Daniel Good Real Property — Mandatory administrative promptness does not supply an unstated dismissal sanction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/United_States_v_James_Daniel_Good_Real_Property_merits_1993-12-13.md#public-projection), 1993-12-13. Kennedy's opinion for the Court, Statutory timing portion, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg. All nine adopt this construction and reversal of the contrary ground below.

**Present operation effective:** United States v. James Daniel Good Real Property, 1993-12-13.

**Holdings navigation:** [Mandatory administrative promptness does not supply an unstated dismissal sanction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#mandatory-administrative-promptness-does-not-supply-an-unstated-dismissal-sanction).

### Mine Act channeling and meaningful judicial review

**Governing question:** Whether anticipatory Mine Act representation-and-posting disputes may bypass the Commission and appellate review.

**Current rule:** Where Congress’s intent is fairly discernible from the Mine Act’s language, structure and purpose and the claims can receive meaningful review, an anticipatory challenge to representation and posting requirements must proceed through the independent Commission and then the court of appeals. Connection to enforcement, relevant agency expertise and actual review availability inform that scheme-specific inquiry, not three mandatory elements for every administrative statute. Review adequacy must reflect actual injury and access; collection only after review, expedition and bounded temporary relief supported the conclusion on the absence of demonstrated irreversible compliance injury. Delayed payment alone does not establish adequate review.

**Authority by component:**

- Statutory channel and inquiry form: [Thunder Basin Coal Co. v. Reich — This Mine Act dispute must follow the statutory review channel](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Thunder_Basin_Coal_Co_v_Reich_merits_1994-01-19.md#public-projection), 1994-01-19. Blackmun's Opinion of the Court, statutory-channel portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg; all nine participants adopt this proposition at the stated level of generality.
- Meaningful-review qualification and bounded interim routes: [Thunder Basin Coal Co. v. Reich — The available route does not impose an intolerable choice on this record](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Thunder_Basin_Coal_Co_v_Reich_merits_1994-01-19.md#public-projection), 1994-01-19. Blackmun's Opinion of the Court, meaningful-review and due-process portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, and Ginsburg; seven participants adopt this ground. Scalia and Thomas agree with the judgment on a distinct constitutional formulation and are not counted as joining this explanation.

**Present operation effective:** Thunder Basin Coal Co. v. Reich, 1994-01-19.

**Exceptions and limits:** Commission temporary relief under §815(b)(2) requires a hearing opportunity for each party, substantial likelihood of favorable findings and no adverse health-and-safety effect; relief is forbidden for §814(a) or (f) citations. Appellate relief under §816(a)(2) requires notice and hearing opportunity, substantial likelihood of success and protection of miners’ health and safety; it excludes §817(a) imminent-danger withdrawal orders and §814(a) or (f) citations. Section 816(c) makes review nonstaying. No stay of every posting citation, immunity from penalties or exclusion of every collateral constitutional claim is established.

**Holdings navigation:** [This Mine Act dispute must follow the statutory review channel](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#this-mine-act-dispute-must-follow-the-statutory-review-channel); [The available route does not impose an intolerable choice on this record](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-available-route-does-not-impose-an-intolerable-choice-on-this-record).

### Objective appearance of judicial impartiality under §455(a)

**Governing question:** When judicial or outside conduct requires federal judicial disqualification.

**Current rule:** A judge must disqualify when an informed, detached observer considering relevant conduct and circumstances together would reasonably question impartiality. A reasonable conclusion that a fair and impartial hearing is unlikely suffices, without proof of actual bias or impossibility of fair judgment. Judicial origin is relevant context, not a bar or exceptional-intensity requirement; outside origin is neither necessary nor sufficient. Ordinary adjudicative views, adverse rulings, criticism and trial management alone do not establish improper partiality. Section 455(b)’s particular limits do not automatically narrow §455(a)’s independent appearance protection.

**Authority by component:**

- Single objective appearance standard across sources: [Liteky v. United States — Section 455(a) applies the same objective appearance standard regardless of source](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Liteky_v_United_States_merits_1994-03-07.md#public-projection), 1994-03-07. Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Souter. These five Justices adopt this rule at the same level of generality. Scalia, O'Connor, Thomas and Ginsburg agree with the judgment but do not join the ordinary-appearance formulation without their additional judicial-source requirement. No narrower-rationale aggregation is necessary.

**Present operation effective:** Liteky v. United States, 1994-03-07.

**Open question:** Section 144 procedure, independent §455(b)(1) claims and the remedy for an established violation remain unresolved. The four-Justice additional judicial-source threshold is not controlling.

**Holdings navigation:** [Section 455(a) applies the same objective appearance standard regardless of source](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#section-455a-applies-the-same-objective-appearance-standard-regardless-of-source).

### Statutory temporal reach and new consequences for completed conduct

**Governing question:** How courts decide whether new legislation governs events preceding enactment.

**Current rule:** First determine whether Congress prescribed the statute’s temporal reach. If it did, that direction governs subject to independent constitutional limits. Otherwise ask whether applying the provision would attach new legal consequences to completed events by impairing existing rights, increasing liability for past conduct or imposing new duties concerning completed transactions. Such operation requires clear congressional intent. Pending litigation alone does not answer the question. Procedural, jurisdictional and prospective-relief labels are not substitutes for examining the provision’s actual operation. Legislative correction of a judicial construction, or a purpose to restore an earlier understanding, does not by itself establish retrospective reach; this legislative inquiry differs from the temporal operation of judicial decisions.

**Authority by component:**

- Sequential temporal inquiry: [Landgraf v. USI Film Products — Determine statutory temporal reach before applying the presumption against new retrospective consequences](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Landgraf_v_USI_Film_Products_merits_1994-04-26.md#public-projection), 1994-04-26. Stevens’s general-framework portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Souter and Ginsburg; five Justices adopt this organizing rule. Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas concur on a distinct relevant-activity approach, not as additional joins at this level of generality.
- Corrective purpose and distinction from judicial interpretation: [Rivers v. Roadway Express, Inc — Corrective legislative purpose does not itself give an amendment retrospective reach](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Rivers_v_Roadway_Express_merits_1994-04-26.md#public-projection), 1994-04-26. Stevens’s opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Souter and Ginsburg, controls by five votes at this explanatory level. Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas accept the judicial/legislative distinction through their separate textual method, not through a presumed full join.

**Present operation effective:** Rivers v. Roadway Express, Inc, 1994-04-26.

**Holdings navigation:** [Determine statutory temporal reach before applying the presumption against new retrospective consequences](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#determine-statutory-temporal-reach-before-applying-the-presumption-against-new-retrospective-consequences); [Corrective legislative purpose does not itself give an amendment retrospective reach](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#corrective-legislative-purpose-does-not-itself-give-an-amendment-retrospective-reach).

### Jurisdiction to enforce a settlement after dismissal

**Governing question:** When a federal court may enforce a settlement after dismissing the original action.

**Current rule:** Enforcement of the settlement is a separate contract controversy requiring an independent jurisdictional basis unless the dismissal order lawfully incorporates the settlement obligation or expressly retains jurisdiction to enforce it. Mere awareness, approval or party consent does not confer jurisdiction. Under Rule 41(a)(2), the court may make compliance or retention a condition of its discretionary dismissal. For a stipulated Rule 41(a)(1)(ii) dismissal, the parties must agree to the retained enforcement authority and the court must timely embody it in its order; a private agreement alone is insufficient.

**Authority by component:**

- Independent basis or operative dismissal order: [Kokkonen v. Guardian Life Insurance Co. of America — Postdismissal contract enforcement requires a jurisdictional basis; A lawful dismissal order may make settlement compliance enforceable](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Kokkonen_v_Guardian_Life_merits_1994-05-16.md#public-projection), 1994-05-16. Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg; all nine adopt the rule, application, and reversal with remand. Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg; all nine adopt this qualification defining the boundary of the jurisdictional holding.

**Present operation effective:** Kokkonen v. Guardian Life Insurance Co. of America, 1994-05-16.

**Open question:** Possible reopening under Rule 60(b), as distinct from exercising jurisdiction over a separate contract-enforcement action, is not decided.

**Holdings navigation:** [Postdismissal contract enforcement requires a jurisdictional basis](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#postdismissal-contract-enforcement-requires-a-jurisdictional-basis); [A lawful dismissal order may make settlement compliance enforceable](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#a-lawful-dismissal-order-may-make-settlement-compliance-enforceable).

### Review preclusion for the approved 1990 base-closure package

**Governing question:** Whether implementation litigation may reopen the approved package under the 1990 base-closure statute.

**Current rule:** The 1990 base-closure statute’s integrated selection, recommendation, presidential approval and congressional-review process, with its constrained choices and strict deadlines, precludes an APA challenge to the approved closure selection through litigation nominally directed at implementation and antecedent selection defects. The holding is tied to that completed statutory package. It does not establish a categorical rule that every presidential recommendation is nonfinal, or resolve presidential APA status as a controlling rationale.

**Authority by component:**

- Scheme-specific preclusion of the completed selection challenge: [Dalton v. Specter — The approved closure selection cannot be reopened through this implementation challenge](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Dalton_v_Specter_merits_1994-05-23.md#public-projection), 1994-05-23. Souter's Opinion of the Court is joined by all nine Justices. The statutory-preclusion ground alone commands the Court; a four-Justice concurrence on recommendation finality is not controlling.

**Present operation effective:** Dalton v. Specter, 1994-05-23.

**Open question:** Distinct timely claims to compel an unperformed statutory duty, constitutional claims and genuinely ultra vires conduct remain outside the holding. The broader four-Justice rationale is not governing law.

**Holdings navigation:** [The approved closure selection cannot be reopened through this implementation challenge](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-approved-closure-selection-cannot-be-reopened-through-this-implementation-challenge).

### Sanctions for preserving a substantial question for higher review

**Governing question:** When controlling circuit precedent can justify a sanction for pursuing further review.

**Current rule:** A litigant’s preservation of a substantial question unresolved by the Supreme Court is not frivolous solely because existing circuit precedent forecloses relief below. A sanction resting only on that foreclosure cannot stand. The rule does not excuse an independently sanctionable manner or purpose of litigation, authorize endless repetition or reopen a final merits judgment. Reversal of the sanction leaves an otherwise undisturbed merits disposition in place.

**Authority by component:**

- Legitimate preservation and separation of sanction from merits: [McKnight v. General Motors Corp — Preserving a substantial question for further review is not itself frivolous; Sanction relief leaves the underlying employment disposition undisturbed](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/McKnight_v_General_Motors_Corp_merits_1994-05-23.md#public-projection), 1994-05-23. Per curiam Opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; nine direct joins.

**Present operation effective:** McKnight v. General Motors Corp, 1994-05-23.

**Holdings navigation:** [Preserving a substantial question for further review is not itself frivolous](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#preserving-a-substantial-question-for-further-review-is-not-itself-frivolous); [Sanction relief leaves the underlying employment disposition undisturbed](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#sanction-relief-leaves-the-underlying-employment-disposition-undisturbed).

### Congressional reopening of completed private federal judgments

**Governing question:** Whether §27A(b) may compel reopening of an already final, unappealed private securities judgment.

**Current rule:** Article III forbids compelling a federal court to reopen a completed, unappealed judgment in a private securities action through §27A(b)’s retroactive reinstatement command. That command addresses a private §10(b) action commenced on or before June 19, 1991, dismissed as time-barred after that date, timely under the jurisdiction’s law as it stood on that date, and the subject of a reinstatement motion within sixty days after enactment. Meeting those statutory conditions does not overcome the constitutional finality of the completed private judgment. Pending litigation, prospective relief, government waiver of its own protection and established Rule 60 mechanisms are distinct questions; the holding does not decide them by analogy alone.

**Authority by component:**
