- Final private judgment and the limited reinstatement command: [Morgan Stanley & Co. v. Pacific Mutual Life Insurance Co — Congress may not command reopening of this completed private federal judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Morgan_Stanley_v_Pacific_Mutual_merits_1994-05-23.md#public-projection), 1994-05-23. Thomas's Opinion of the Court is joined by Stone-Zsela, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter. Five participating Justices directly join this as-applied Article III rule and the limited remedy; no aggregation of separate rationales is necessary.

**Present operation effective:** Morgan Stanley & Co. v. Pacific Mutual Life Insurance Co, 1994-05-23.

**Holdings navigation:** [Congress may not command reopening of this completed private federal judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#congress-may-not-command-reopening-of-this-completed-private-federal-judgment).

### Private settlement rights and collateral-order appeals

**Governing question:** Whether rejection of a private settlement defense permits an immediate collateral-order appeal.

**Current rule:** Ordinary private settlement rights do not have the public importance required for immediate appeal under the collateral-order doctrine. Independently, final-judgment review adequately vindicates this category of contractual rights despite the expense of the intervening litigation. Describing the bargain as a right not to stand trial does not establish appealability. These independently sufficient limits govern the category presented, not every conceivable private agreement; other authorized interlocutory routes, including §1292 certification when its conditions are met, remain distinct.

**Authority by component:**

- Nine-Justice importance ground: [Digital Equipment Corp. v. Desktop Direct, Inc — A private settlement does not supply the required collateral-order importance](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Digital_Equipment_Corp_v_Desktop_Direct_merits_1994-06-06.md#public-projection), 1994-06-06. Part I of Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine adopt this independently sufficient ground for affirmance.
- Eight-Justice independent adequate-final-review ground: [Digital Equipment Corp. v. Desktop Direct, Inc — Independently, final review adequately vindicates this category of settlement rights](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Digital_Equipment_Corp_v_Desktop_Direct_merits_1994-06-06.md#public-projection), 1994-06-06. Part II of Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. Eight Justices adopt this independently sufficient alternative; Stone-Zsela does not join Part II.

**Present operation effective:** Digital Equipment Corp. v. Desktop Direct, Inc, 1994-06-06.

**Holdings navigation:** [A private settlement does not supply the required collateral-order importance](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#a-private-settlement-does-not-supply-the-required-collateral-order-importance).

### State-law incidents of claims acquired by the FDIC

**Governing question:** Whether FDIC receivership creates a federal rule displacing state imputation defenses to acquired state tort claims.

**Current rule:** When the FDIC acquires an institution’s state-law tort claims, state law ordinarily supplies the associated imputation rules and defenses. Federal financial interests or a preference for uniformity alone do not justify new federal common law; a significant conflict with an identified federal policy is required. For pre-FIRREA receivership claims the ordinary rule applies independently of that Act. If FIRREA applies, §1821’s succession to the institution’s rights carries their legal incidents subject to Congress’s enacted exceptions; it supplies no additional categorical anti-imputation rule.

**Authority by component:**

- State-law rule and federal-common-law limit: [O’Melveny & Myers v. FDIC — These acquired California claims carry California imputation law](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/OMelveny_Myers_v_FDIC_merits_1994-06-13.md#public-projection), 1994-06-13. Part I of Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine adopt the same pre-FIRREA ground, independently sufficient to reject the proposed rule without deciding FIRREA's temporal reach.
- Independent conditional FIRREA ground: [O’Melveny & Myers v. FDIC — FIRREA, if applicable, supplies no additional anti-imputation rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/OMelveny_Myers_v_FDIC_merits_1994-06-13.md#public-projection), 1994-06-13. Part II of Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine adopt this conditional statutory construction and its temporal reservation. It is not a finding that FIRREA applies retrospectively.

**Present operation effective:** O’Melveny & Myers v. FDIC, 1994-06-13.

**Open question:** The decision leaves FIRREA’s temporal applicability and the eventual operation of the particular state-law defense for the lower courts.

**Holdings navigation:** [These acquired California claims carry California imputation law](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#these-acquired-california-claims-carry-california-imputation-law); [FIRREA, if applicable, supplies no additional anti-imputation rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#firrea-if-applicable-supplies-no-additional-anti-imputation-rule).

### Criminal contempt fines and an effective opportunity to purge

**Governing question:** When coercive terminology does not make an out-of-court contempt fine civil.

**Current rule:** A fixed, noncompensatory public fine for completed out-of-court disobedience is criminal when the contemnor has no effective opportunity to purge the imposed obligation through compliance. Announcing a schedule prospectively does not by itself make later irrevocable assessments civil. Compensation measured by actual loss and genuinely conditional coercion, including a daily fine that can be avoided through compliance, remain distinct; ability to comply must be real. Serious criminal contempt requires criminal procedure, including a jury, even if a judge found disobedience beyond a reasonable doubt. The need to enforce an injunction does not replace those protections.

**Authority by component:**

- Character of fines, inadequate enforcement justification and serious-contempt procedure: [International Union, United Mine Workers of America v. Bagwell — The surviving fixed public fines are criminal punishment; Necessity for enforcement does not excuse criminal procedure on this record; Serious criminal contempt requires a jury and these civil assessments cannot stand](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/International_Union_United_Mine_Workers_of_America_v_Bagwell_merits_1994-06-30.md#public-projection), 1994-06-30. Part I of Justice Kennedy's Opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine directly adopt the classification and application. Part II of Justice Kennedy's opinion, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter and Thomas. Eight Justices adopt this additional bounded procedural explanation. Ginsburg joins the common classification and remedy but not this account. Part III of Justice Kennedy's opinion, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine adopt the jury rule and bounded reversal/remand.

**Present operation effective:** International Union, United Mine Workers of America v. Bagwell, 1994-06-30.

**Exceptions and limits:** Size and complexity alone do not define every contempt. The decision creates no universal numerical seriousness threshold, erases no properly accrued coercive fine automatically, and leaves the distinct procedures for petty and direct contempt outside its holding.

**Holdings navigation:** [The surviving fixed public fines are criminal punishment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-surviving-fixed-public-fines-are-criminal-punishment); [Necessity for enforcement does not excuse criminal procedure on this record](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#necessity-for-enforcement-does-not-excuse-criminal-procedure-on-this-record); [Serious criminal contempt requires a jury and these civil assessments cannot stand](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#serious-criminal-contempt-requires-a-jury-and-these-civil-assessments-cannot-stand).

### Equitable vacatur after settlement moots review

**Governing question:** Whether mootness from voluntary settlement warrants vacating a judgment lawfully brought for review.

**Current rule:** Subsequent mootness prevents a merits decision but leaves authority under 28 U.S.C. §2106 to make a just disposition of the judgment and proceeding. Voluntary settlement ordinarily does not establish entitlement to vacatur: the movant must supply a further sufficient equitable reason. Exceptional settlement circumstances can warrant relief, but a settlement term requiring vacatur is not itself such a circumstance. Section 2106 permits appropriate relief; it does not command vacatur whenever review becomes moot.

**Authority by component:**

- Mootness ends merits review but leaves authority to dispose of the judgment: [Mootness ends merits review but leaves authority to dispose of the judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/US_Bancorp_Mortgage_Co_v_Bonner_Mall_Partnership_mootness_vacatur_1994-11-08.md#public-projection), 1994-11-08. Justice Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. All nine directly adopt this proposition.
- Voluntary settlement ordinarily does not justify vacatur: [Voluntary settlement ordinarily does not justify vacatur](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/US_Bancorp_Mortgage_Co_v_Bonner_Mall_Partnership_mootness_vacatur_1994-11-08.md#public-projection), 1994-11-08. Justice Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. All nine directly adopt this proposition.

**Present operation effective:** 1994-11-08; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** No heightened proof quantum or exhaustive list of exceptional circumstances is adopted. This settlement rule does not turn loss of review through circumstances outside the parties’ control into automatic vacatur.

**Holdings navigation:** [Mootness ends merits review but leaves authority to dispose of the judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#mootness-ends-merits-review-but-leaves-authority-to-dispose-of-the-judgment); [Voluntary settlement ordinarily does not justify vacatur](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#voluntary-settlement-ordinarily-does-not-justify-vacatur).

### State immunity of bistate compact entities

**Governing question:** When a bistate compact entity shares its constituent States’ Eleventh Amendment immunity.

**Current rule:** Exercise of governmental power alone does not confer the States’ immunity. The entity’s actual legal, financial and institutional relationship must give good reason to conclude that the States structured it as their arm and Congress concurred. Exposure of the state treasuries is highly probative but not categorically indispensable; a separate, self-supporting enterprise responsible for its own obligations cannot claim immunity merely from its public functions.

**Authority by component:**

- PATH’s actual compact and financial structure does not confer the States’ immunity: [PATH’s actual compact and financial structure does not confer the States’ immunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Hess_v_Port_Authority_Trans_Hudson_Corp_merits_1994-11-14.md#public-projection), 1994-11-14. Justice Stevens's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer. Six of nine participants directly support this entity-specific rule and its application to both employee actions. O'Connor, Scalia and Thomas dissent; no fragmented-rationale analysis is needed.

**Present operation effective:** 1994-11-14; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** This compact-entity inquiry does not establish a universal sole-treasury test, decide abrogation or consent, or equate immunity with governmental status for constitutional-rights purposes.

**Holdings navigation:** [PATH’s actual compact and financial structure does not confer the States’ immunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#paths-actual-compact-and-financial-structure-does-not-confer-the-states-immunity).

### FEC certiorari authority and timely ratification

**Governing question:** Who may invoke Supreme Court review of an ordinary FECA enforcement action and when later authorization can cure an unauthorized filing.

**Current rule:** The Commission’s general litigation-and-appeal power under §437d(a)(6) does not displace 28 U.S.C. §518(a)’s allocation of Supreme Court representation. The Attorney General may direct otherwise in a particular case; express certiorari authority under 26 U.S.C. §§9010(d) and 9040(d) is confined to actions under those respective funding provisions. Authorization first supplied after the period for an authorized petition has expired cannot relate back to validate an unauthorized invocation. Section 2101(c) allows ninety days, with a Justice permitted for good cause to extend the application period by no more than sixty days.

**Authority by component:**

- The Commission lacks independent certiorari authority for this FECA enforcement action: [The Commission lacks independent certiorari authority for this FECA enforcement action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Federal_Election_Commission_v_NRA_Political_Victory_Fund_jurisdictional_dismissal_1994-12-06.md#public-projection), 1994-12-06. Part I of Justice Thomas's opinion of the Court, joined by O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Breyer: six of eight participating Justices directly adopt the rule. Stone-Zsela and Stevens dissent; Ginsburg does not participate.
- Authorization after the statutory petition period does not ratify this filing: [Authorization after the statutory petition period does not ratify this filing](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Federal_Election_Commission_v_NRA_Political_Victory_Fund_jurisdictional_dismissal_1994-12-06.md#public-projection), 1994-12-06. Part II of Justice Thomas's opinion of the Court, joined by O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Breyer: six direct votes. Together with Part I, this ground requires dismissal for want of jurisdiction; it is not a constitutional merits disposition.

**Present operation effective:** 1994-12-06; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** The rule decides neither every timely ratification nor the merits of the agency’s underlying enforcement action.

**Holdings navigation:** [The Commission lacks independent certiorari authority for this FECA enforcement action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-commission-lacks-independent-certiorari-authority-for-this-feca-enforcement-action); [Authorization after the statutory petition period does not ratify this filing](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#authorization-after-the-statutory-petition-period-does-not-ratify-this-filing).

### Qualified-immunity legal appeals and factual sufficiency

**Governing question:** The boundary of immediate review under §1291 when qualified immunity is denied.

**Current rule:** Immediate review may address the separable legal question whether conduct on the relevant assumed facts violates clearly established law, including the legality of a categorical direct-evidence condition. It may not determine only whether pretrial evidence creates a genuine dispute about the official’s participation, or choose between disputed motive, causation or factual accounts. A legal appeal uses the district court’s assumed facts; if not stated, the appellate court may identify likely assumptions favorable to the nonmovant without deciding truth or reweighing sufficiency. An abstract legal contention does not bring the factual dispute within immediate review.

**Authority by component:**

- Factual sufficiency is not an immediate qualified-immunity appeal: [Factual sufficiency is not an immediate qualified-immunity appeal](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Johnson_v_Jones_merits_1995-06-12.md#public-projection), 1995-06-12. Justice Breyer's opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; all nine adopt this bounded rule.
- Separable legal review of the categorical evidence rule: [Separable legal review of the categorical evidence rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Kimberlin_v_Quinlan_merits_1995-06-12.md#public-projection), 1995-06-12. Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Breyer; all nine support this legal-review proposition.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-12; the component authorities identified above.

**Open question:** Genuinely necessary connected review and a categorical rule for every mixed order remain unresolved. Johnson and Kimberlin use their common entering baseline; neither is treated as earlier same-day law for the other.

**Holdings navigation:** [Factual sufficiency is not an immediate qualified-immunity appeal](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#factual-sufficiency-is-not-an-immediate-qualified-immunity-appeal); [Separable legal review of the categorical evidence rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#separable-legal-review-of-the-categorical-evidence-rule).

### Municipal liability defenses and connected interlocutory review

**Governing question:** Whether an officers’ immunity appeal permits immediate review of a separate municipal liability defense.

**Current rule:** An ordinary municipal liability defense remains effectively reviewable after final judgment and does not acquire immunity from trial through the officers’ immunity. An expressly reserved, tentative final-policymaker ruling also lacks the conclusiveness required by Cohen. The court of appeals may not decide a separate party’s nonappealable defense merely because it is related to a valid immunity appeal and its resolution would save litigation.

**Authority by component:**

- Ordinary municipal liability remains reviewable after final judgment: [Ordinary municipal liability remains reviewable after final judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Swint_v_Chambers_County_Commission_merits_1995-03-01.md#public-projection), 1995-03-01. Part I of Justice Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Breyer; nine Justices adopt this independently sufficient failure of collateral-order jurisdiction.
- The expressly tentative ruling also lacks conclusiveness: [The expressly tentative ruling also lacks conclusiveness](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Swint_v_Chambers_County_Commission_merits_1995-03-01.md#public-projection), 1995-03-01. Part III of Justice Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined by Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Breyer; eight Justices adopt this independent ground. Chief Justice Stone-Zsela does not join this additional ground.
- A proper immunity appeal does not authorize this separate county appeal: [A proper immunity appeal does not authorize this separate county appeal](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Swint_v_Chambers_County_Commission_merits_1995-03-01.md#public-projection), 1995-03-01. Part II of Justice Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Breyer; nine Justices adopt this bounded rule and its application. Part IV, joined by the eight Associate Justices, leaves the availability and scope of genuinely necessary connected review undecided; the Chief's affirmative view of that possibility is separate and noncontrolling.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-03-01; the component authorities identified above.

**Open question:** Swint finds the county issue neither inextricably intertwined with nor necessary to meaningful immunity review. It leaves genuinely necessary connected review unresolved rather than creating either general permission or a categorical ban.

**Exceptions and limits:** A separate §1292(b) route requires a written district-court statement of a controlling legal question, substantial ground for difference of opinion and material advancement of termination, application within ten days and discretionary appellate permission. Application alone does not stay proceedings. Rulemaking authority in §§1292(e) and 2072(c) is not itself an operative appeal rule.

**Holdings navigation:** [Ordinary municipal liability remains reviewable after final judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#ordinary-municipal-liability-remains-reviewable-after-final-judgment); [The expressly tentative ruling also lacks conclusiveness](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#ordinary-municipal-liability-remains-reviewable-after-final-judgment); [A proper immunity appeal does not authorize this separate county appeal](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#a-proper-immunity-appeal-does-not-authorize-this-separate-county-appeal).

### Administrative interest and LHWCA appellate aggrievement

**Governing question:** Whether the Director may independently seek §921(c) review of a claimant’s unappealed award.

**Current rule:** A general interest in correct administration of the LHWCA does not alone make the Director adversely affected or aggrieved under §921(c) by an employee’s unappealed award. Congress may confer an express review right, and genuine financial or independently statutory injury requires its own analysis; this construction is not categorical governmental incapacity to seek review.

**Authority by component:**

- The Director's general administrative interest does not confer independent review: [The Director's general administrative interest does not confer independent review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Director_Office_of_Workers_Compensation_Programs_v_Newport_News_Shipbuilding_and_Dry_Dock_Co_merits_1995-03-21.md#public-projection), 1995-03-21. Justice O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Breyer: eight direct rationale votes. Justice Ginsburg concurs in the judgment and agrees with the case-specific statutory result, but does not join this explanation. No fractured-opinion synthesis is needed.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-03-21; the component authorities identified above.

**Open question:** Respondent status, claims involving genuine Special Fund injury and the compensation merits are not resolved by this general-administrative-interest ruling.

**Holdings navigation:** [The Director's general administrative interest does not confer independent review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-directors-general-administrative-interest-does-not-confer-independent-review).

### Habeas custody in a continuous consecutive sentence series

**Governing question:** Custody under an earlier conviction after its first consecutive segment has been served.

**Current rule:** A prisoner serving a continuous, unexpired series of consecutive sentences remains in custody under an earlier conviction when invalidating it would advance release from the aggregate term, even though that first segment has been served. This establishes only the custody threshold; procedural gates and the conviction’s merits remain separate.

**Authority by component:**

- Custody under a continuous consecutive series: [Custody under a continuous consecutive series](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Garlotte_v_Fordice_merits_1995-05-30.md#public-projection), 1995-05-30. Ginsburg’s Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Breyer; eight Justices directly support this rule.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-05-30; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Maleng’s rule for an independently expired sentence survives. Collateral consequences after all custody ends do not supply jurisdiction through this rule.

**Holdings navigation:** [Custody under a continuous consecutive series](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#custody-under-a-continuous-consecutive-series).

### Discretion to stay a declaration-only coverage action

**Governing question:** A federal declaratory action addressing state-law coverage alongside adequate parallel state proceedings.

**Current rule:** Section 2201 and Brillhart permit a reasoned stay of a declaration-only state-law coverage action with adequate parallel state proceedings without Colorado River’s exceptional-circumstances showing. The court must consider the state case’s scope, available defenses, interested and necessary parties and amenability to process. Jurisdiction is retained, allowing resumption if the state case fails to resolve the controversy. The lawful exercise of this discretion receives abuse-of-discretion review, which does not insulate a mistaken legal source or unsupported adequacy assessment.

**Authority by component:**

- Discretion to stay this declaratory action: [Discretion to stay this declaratory action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Wilton_v_Seven_Falls_Co_merits_1995-06-12.md#public-projection), 1995-06-12. Justice O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; all eight participants adopt the rule.
- Appellate review of lawful declaratory discretion: [Appellate review of lawful declaratory discretion](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Wilton_v_Seven_Falls_Co_merits_1995-06-12.md#public-projection), 1995-06-12. Justice O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; eight Justices adopt this separate review rule.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-12; the component authorities identified above.

**Open question:** Federal substantive declaratory questions, independent coercive claims and absence of parallel proceedings remain unresolved. No automatic abstention, first-filed rule, obligatory stay or unreviewable discretion follows.

**Holdings navigation:** [Discretion to stay this declaratory action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#discretion-to-stay-this-declaratory-action); [Appellate review of lawful declaratory discretion](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#appellate-review-of-lawful-declaratory-discretion).

## Civil rights and constitutional remedies

### Individual-capacity liability for official conduct under §1983

**Governing question:** Whether use of official authority prevents a state officer from being a §1983 person when sued individually.

**Current rule:** A state officer sued in an individual capacity is a person under §1983 even when the challenged act was undertaken through official authority; sovereign immunity does not independently bar a judgment payable by the officer personally. The plaintiff must prove the officer's own federal violation under color of law, and applicable personal immunities remain available. The capacity in which the officer is sued, rather than whether the conduct was official, distinguishes this action from a claim against the State or an official-capacity damages claim.

**Authority by component:**

- Personhood, capacity distinction, personal-payment immunity limit, and retained defenses: [Hafer v. Melo — Individual-capacity §1983 coverage of official conduct](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Hafer_v_Melo_merits_1991-11-05.md), November 5, 1991; O'Connor's unanimous participating opinion.

**Present operation effective:** *Hafer*, November 5, 1991.

**Holdings navigation:** [Hafer v. Melo, 502 U.S. 21 (1991) — Individual-capacity liability](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#hafer-v-melo-502-us-21-1991).

### Municipal responsibility and the underlying federal deprivation

**Governing question:** Whether a municipal employee must prove an additional freestanding abuse of governmental power to recover under §1983.

**Current rule:** A municipal employee who proves deprivation of a federal right under color of state law and the required municipal responsibility need not prove an additional, independent abuse-of-governmental-power element. The federal deprivation and its attribution to municipal policy or custom remain separate required inquiries. Employment alone establishes neither municipal liability nor a substantive right; *Canton*'s training analysis supplies attribution and causation, not the underlying constitutional guarantee.

**Authority by component:**

- Rejection of the additional element and separation of deprivation from attribution: [Collins v. City of Harker Heights — No extra governmental-abuse element under §1983](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Collins_v_City_of_Harker_Heights_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992; Part A of Stevens's opinion, joined by all nine Justices.

**Present operation effective:** *Collins*, February 26, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [Collins v. City of Harker Heights, 503 U.S. 115 (1992) — Municipal liability and public employment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#collins-v-city-of-harker-heights-503-us-115-1992).

### Voluntary public employment and substantive workplace safety duties

**Governing question:** Whether a voluntary public-employment relationship creates a general substantive-due-process duty to provide workplace safety.

**Current rule:** Voluntary municipal employment does not itself create the custody or deprivation of liberty that supports affirmative constitutional duties of care, and substantive due process imposes no independent general obligation to provide such employees a workplace free from unreasonable risks. A state-law safety duty, even assuming it creates a protected liberty interest, does not convert its breach alone into a substantive constitutional wrong. The underlying federal right and constitutional arbitrariness remain distinct from state-law entitlement and municipal attribution.

**Authority by component:**

- No employment-based general safety duty or custody: [Collins v. City of Harker Heights — Voluntary public employment creates no general constitutional safety duty](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Collins_v_City_of_Harker_Heights_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992; Part B of Stevens's unanimous opinion.
- State entitlement does not alone establish arbitrary deprivation: [Collins — These pleaded omissions do not establish constitutional arbitrariness](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Collins_v_City_of_Harker_Heights_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992; Part C joined by White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Thomas with Stevens. Stone's distinct concurrence is not part of this component's authority.

**Present operation effective:** *Collins*, February 26, 1992.

**Open question:** Actual compulsion, deliberately inflicted bodily injury, procedural due process, and a properly alleged affirmative-danger claim are not resolved. *Collins* creates neither a general affirmative-danger test nor a categorical rejection of one.

**Holdings navigation:** [Collins v. City of Harker Heights, 503 U.S. 115 (1992) — Municipal liability and public employment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#collins-v-city-of-harker-heights-503-us-115-1992).

### Compensatory remedies for intentional Title IX discrimination

**Governing question:** Whether remedial silence or Spending Clause notice bars compensatory damages in the established Title IX private action.

**Current rule:** When a cognizable federal statutory cause of action exists, courts may award appropriate relief absent clear congressional direction limiting it; recognizing relief does not itself create a new cause of action. Title IX's established private action permits compensatory damages for proved intentional discrimination, subject to proof of recipient responsibility, causation, and compensable loss. The Spending Clause notice principle does not categorically bar that compensation for intentional breach of Title IX's express nondiscrimination condition.

**Authority by component:**

- Appropriate relief in an existing action and availability of Title IX compensation: [Franklin v. Gwinnett County Public Schools — Compensatory relief in the established Title IX action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Franklin_v_Gwinnett_County_Public_Schools_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992.
- Rejection of the categorical funding-notice bar: [Franklin — Notice does not bar damages for the pleaded intentional violation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Franklin_v_Gwinnett_County_Public_Schools_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992. Both holdings rest on White's opinion joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, and Souter; Scalia and Thomas supply judgment votes only on a narrower statutory ground.

**Present operation effective:** *Franklin*, February 26, 1992.

**Open question:** The decision supplies no final recipient-attribution rule, punitive remedy, liability for unintended discriminatory effects, or resolution of genuinely ambiguous funding conditions.

**Holdings navigation:** [Franklin v. Gwinnett County Public Schools, 503 U.S. 60 (1992) — Statutory discrimination remedies](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#franklin-v-gwinnett-county-public-schools-503-us-60-1992).

### Private enforcement of Adoption Assistance Act reasonable-efforts and reporting clauses

**Governing question:** Private enforcement of 42 U.S.C. §671(a)(15)'s reasonable-efforts clause and §671(a)(9)'s reporting clause through §1983 or an action implied directly under the Act.

**Current rule:** Read within the Act's plan and administrative-enforcement structure, §671(a)(15) creates no individual right enforceable under §1983 to compel the asserted reasonable efforts to prevent removal or reunify families. An approved plan's commitment, §671(a)(3)'s statewide-operation requirement and caseworker deficiencies do not supply that missing right. Nor does §671(a)(15) imply a separate private action: beneficial purpose does not establish congressional intent to create the requested remedy. Section 671(a)(9) requires a plan to provide reporting to the appropriate court or law-enforcement agency when a state agency has reason to believe a child's home or institution is unsuitable because of neglect, abuse or exploitation and the child's care is paid for in whole or in part under Title IV-E or IV-B. That administrative direction does not create the asserted private §1983 right or an implied action to obtain caseworker relief. The §1983-right and implied-action inquiries remain distinct; failure to imply an action does not bar an otherwise available enacted §1983 remedy.

**Authority by component:**
- No individual §671(a)(15) right: [Suter v. Artist M.](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Suter_v_Artist_M_merits_1992-03-25.md), March 25, 1992, “Section 671(a)(15) supplies no individual reasonable-efforts right enforceable under §1983,” Part II.
- No separate implied (15) action: the same record, “Section 671(a)(15) supplies no separate implied action,” Part III.
- Reporting trigger and absence of asserted §1983 right: the same record, “The asserted §671(a)(9) reporting claim is not enforceable through §1983,” Part IV-A.
- No implied reporting action: the same record, “The asserted §671(a)(9) claim supplies no separate implied action,” Part IV-B. White for O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas supplies six controlling joins for each component.

**Present operation effective:** Suter, March 25, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** No general rule makes state-plan provisions unenforceable. Administrative funding review does not necessarily foreclose an otherwise existing §1983 right, and the State's administrative obligations remain. Appropriate relief presupposes a cognizable action and cannot create the missing right or action.

**Open question:** Private enforceability of §671(a)(16), read with §675(1) and §675(5)(B), remains undecided, as do the preserved claim's merits and proper relief.

**Holdings navigation:** [Suter v. Artist M., 503 U.S. 347 (1992) — Child-welfare statutory enforcement](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#suter-v-artist-m-503-us-347-1992-1).

### Private use of state seizure procedures and qualified immunity

**Governing question:** Whether private litigants using state replevin, garnishment or attachment procedures receive public-official immunity under Harlow.

**Current rule:** Private litigants invoking those procedures do not receive Harlow's objective immunity from suit merely because qualifying joint action is attributable to the State under §1983. State attribution and immunity eligibility remain separate; reasonable reliance on legislation does not itself establish this immunity. Each defendant's attribution and other liability predicates still require independent determination.

**Authority by component:**
- Private-immunity exclusion and separate attribution: [Wyatt v. Cole, “Private litigants and immunity from suit”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Wyatt_v_Cole_merits_1992-05-18.md), May 18, 1992; O'Connor, joined in full by White, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia and Kennedy (six); Stone adds a join only for replevin and the reservations (seven at that scope).

**Present operation effective:** Wyatt v. Cole, May 18, 1992.

**Open question:** The existence and scope of a distinct good-faith or probable-cause defense, any additional plaintiff's elements and allocation of any such burden remain undecided. Eligible public officials' immunity is unchanged.

**Holdings navigation:** [Wyatt v. Cole, 504 U.S. 158 (1992) — Section 1983 immunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#wyatt-v-cole-504-us-158-1992).

### Municipal civil-rights pleading under the Federal Rules

**Governing question:** What instructions govern municipal civil-rights pleading under the Federal Rules?

**Current rule:** Courts may not impose a judge-created heightened factual-particularity requirement on municipal §1983 complaints beyond the then-operative Federal Rules. Rule 8 governs; Rule 9(b)'s fraud-or-mistake categories do not add municipal liability. Monell and Canton retain their substantive attribution and causation requirements. Rule 11, discovery control and Rule 56 remain distinct. No individual-officer pleading standard is decided.

**Authority by component:**

- The Federal Rules supply the pleading standard for municipal § 1983 claims: [Leatherman v. Tarrant County Narcotics Intelligence and Coordination Unit — The Federal Rules supply the pleading standard for municipal § 1983 claims](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/leatherman-v-tarrant-county-merits-1993-03-03.md), 1993-03-03. Stone's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; unanimous controlling authority.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-03; Leatherman v. Tarrant County Narcotics Intelligence and Coordination Unit, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Leatherman v. Tarrant County Narcotics Intelligence and Coordination Unit, 507 U.S. 163 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#leatherman-v-tarrant-county-narcotics-intelligence-and-coordination-unit-507-us-163-1993).

### Private-conspiracy deprivation claims under §1985(3)

**Governing question:** What instructions govern private-conspiracy deprivation claims under §1985(3)?

**Current rule:** A purely private conspiracy under §1985(3)'s deprivation clause must aim to interfere with a right protected against private as well as official encroachment; incidental effects do not suffice. Interstate-travel interference must be a conscious objective, not simply an effect on persons who traveled. Constitutional abortion liberty does not itself protect against purely private obstruction without necessary state involvement or another privately protected right. The full nonracial-class boundary, whether women qualify and whether abortion opposition proves or excludes sex-based animus remain unresolved. The separate hindrance clause is not construed, and no categorical intrastate-obstruction rule is adopted.

**Authority by component:**

- Private conspiracy requires purposeful interference with a privately protected right: [Bray v. Alexandria Women’s Health Clinic — Private conspiracy requires purposeful interference with a privately protected right](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Bray_v_Alexandria_Womens_Health_Clinic_merits_1993-01-13.md), 1993-01-13. Scalia Part II, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Kennedy and Thomas: five direct supporters. Souter separately agrees with rejection of this theory without a formal join.
- Constitutional abortion liberty does not itself supply the private-right element: [Bray v. Alexandria Women’s Health Clinic — Constitutional abortion liberty does not itself supply the private-right element](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Bray_v_Alexandria_Womens_Health_Clinic_merits_1993-01-13.md), 1993-01-13. Scalia Part II, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Kennedy and Thomas: five direct supporters of this distinct sufficient ground addressing the alternative privacy theory. Souter separately accepts Scott’s private-right limitation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-01-13; Bray v. Alexandria Women’s Health Clinic, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Bray v. Alexandria Women’s Health Clinic, 506 U.S. 263 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#bray-v-alexandria-womens-health-clinic-506-us-263-1993); [Bray v. Alexandria Women’s Health Clinic, 506 U.S. 263 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#bray-v-alexandria-womens-health-clinic-506-us-263-1993-1).

### Ministerial court reporting and absolute judicial immunity

**Governing question:** What instructions govern ministerial court reporting and absolute judicial immunity?

**Current rule:** Accurate recording and production of a verbatim transcript are ministerial functions and do not carry absolute judicial immunity merely because they are important or integral to adjudication. The claimant of immunity must establish its historical and functional justification; discretionary adjudication supplies a different function. Denial of absolute immunity establishes neither a cause of action nor liability, and other defenses remain open.

**Authority by component:**

- The ministerial transcript function does not carry absolute judicial immunity: [Antoine v. Byers & Anderson, Inc. — The ministerial transcript function does not carry absolute judicial immunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/antoine_merits_1993-06-07.md), 1993-06-07. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-07; Antoine v. Byers & Anderson, Inc., for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Antoine v. Byers & Anderson, Inc., 508 U.S. 429 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#antoine-v-byers--anderson-inc-508-us-429-1993).

### Prosecutorial investigation, advocacy and press statements

**Governing question:** What instructions govern prosecutorial investigation, advocacy and press statements?

**Current rule:** Ordinary investigative work to identify a perpetrator before probable cause has no absolute prosecutorial immunity for alleged fabrication; later courtroom use does not retrospectively transform it into advocacy. Genuine evaluation and preparation in an advocate's role remain protected; neither all pre-indictment nor all post-indictment acts share one classification. Statements to the press announcing an indictment also lack absolute prosecutorial immunity merely because they concern prosecution or cause injury through subsequent proceedings. Qualified immunity, an actionable constitutional deprivation and other liability predicates remain separate.

**Authority by component:**

- Investigative fabrication is not protected by absolute prosecutorial immunity: [Buckley v. Fitzsimmons — Investigative fabrication is not protected by absolute prosecutorial immunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Buckley_v_Fitzsimmons_merits_1993-06-24.md), 1993-06-24. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.
- A press conference does not carry an advocate’s absolute immunity: [Buckley v. Fitzsimmons — A press conference does not carry an advocate’s absolute immunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Buckley_v_Fitzsimmons_merits_1993-06-24.md), 1993-06-24. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-24; Buckley v. Fitzsimmons, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Buckley v. Fitzsimmons, 509 U.S. 259 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#buckley-v-fitzsimmons-509-us-259-1993).

### Prospective parole-consideration claims under §1983

**Governing question:** Whether a prisoner must use habeas to restore the required frequency of discretionary parole consideration.

**Current rule:** A state prisoner may seek prospective declaratory and injunctive relief under §1983 against responsible officials to restore the legally required frequency of discretionary parole consideration when the judgment neither orders release nor establishes entitlement to release or shorter custody. Habeas is not the exclusive channel for that opportunity-only claim, and no general state-remedy exhaustion requirement attaches to the properly brought §1983 action.

**Authority by component:**

- Opportunity-only channel and absence of general exhaustion: [Cavanaugh v. Roller — Section 1983 permits this opportunity-only claim](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Cavanaugh_v_Roller_merits_1993-11-30.md#public-projection), 1993-11-30. Part II of O'Connor's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, and Souter; six Justices adopt the same rule and affirm this remedial channel.

**Present operation effective:** Cavanaugh v. Roller, 1993-11-30.

**Related current rules:** “Conviction-negating damages claims and favorable invalidation” governs the separate in-custody damages prerequisite; Cavanaugh’s opportunity-only relief remains available.

**Holdings navigation:** [Section 1983 permits this opportunity-only claim](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#section-1983-permits-this-opportunity-only-claim).

### Relevant precedent in appellate qualified-immunity review

**Governing question:** What legal sources an appellate court must consider on a preserved clearly-established-law issue.

**Current rule:** Whether a federal right was clearly established when officials acted is reviewed independently using all relevant precedent, including cases neither cited to nor discovered by the district court. This does not admit a new claim or factual record, make each considered precedent controlling or permit later decisions to supply notice retrospectively. The notice inquiry stays fixed to the conduct date. Ordinary claim and evidence preservation, particularized objective immunity and proper summary-judgment treatment remain distinct; no mandatory merits-first sequence follows.

**Authority by component:**

- Independent legal review, contemporaneous notice and preservation limits: [Elder v. Holloway — Appellate review must consider all relevant law on the preserved immunity issue](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Elder_v_Holloway_merits_1994-02-23.md#public-projection), 1994-02-23. Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas. All nine adopt the legal-authority rule and the limited reversal and remand.

**Present operation effective:** Elder v. Holloway, 1994-02-23.

**Holdings navigation:** [Appellate review must consider all relevant law on the preserved immunity issue](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#appellate-review-must-consider-all-relevant-law-on-the-preserved-immunity-issue).

### No direct federal-agency Bivens damages action

**Governing question:** Whether sovereign consent itself permits constitutional damages against a federal agency.

**Current rule:** A waiver removes sovereign immunity but does not create constitutional damages liability. Bivens’s individual-officer action does not extend directly to a federal agency: that extension would create a new defendant and public obligation. Independently, even assuming compensation and deterrence benefits, the expansion of direct federal fiscal liability and redesign of the remedial structure counsel against judicial creation of that agency action. Existing individual Bivens, Davis and Carlson actions and genuinely authorized statutory remedies retain their own scope; no adequate substitute remedy is presumed.

**Authority by component:**

- Separation of waiver from agency cause: [FDIC v. Meyer — Waiver does not create a direct agency Bivens action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/FDIC_v_Meyer_merits_1994-02-23.md#public-projection), 1994-02-23. The cause-of-action portion of Thomas's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg. All nine adopt this independently sufficient ground for rejecting the agency award.
- Independent fiscal-and-remedial reason against extension: [FDIC v. Meyer — Independent reason to leave this fiscal and remedial extension to Congress](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/FDIC_v_Meyer_merits_1994-02-23.md#public-projection), 1994-02-23. The independent fiscal-and-remedial portion of Thomas's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg. Nine Justices adopt this independently sufficient alternative ground.

**Present operation effective:** FDIC v. Meyer, 1994-02-23.

**Holdings navigation:** [Waiver does not create a direct agency Bivens action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#waiver-does-not-create-a-direct-agency-bivens-action).

### The 1991 Civil Rights Act’s new liabilities and jury election

**Governing question:** Whether the 1991 Act’s new damages and contractual coverage reach preenactment conduct.

**Current rule:** Section 102’s new compensatory and punitive damages do not apply to conduct before November 21, 1991. Its jury election exists only when statutory damages under that section are available and requested; either party may then demand a jury, which must not be told the statutory caps. Section 102 damages supplement rather than duplicate §706(g) relief and exclude backpay, interest on backpay and other relief authorized there. Punitive damages require malice or reckless indifference to federally protected rights and are unavailable against governments, government agencies and political subdivisions. Section 101’s expansion of §1981 to contractual performance, modification, termination and enjoyment of benefits likewise does not govern preenactment conduct. Corrective purpose alone supplies no contrary temporal command; a defendant need not prove actual reliance on the former interpretation.

**Authority by component:**

- New damages, their limits and dependent jury election: [Landgraf v. USI Film Products — Section 102 does not add its new damages to this preenactment conduct; The jury election depends on an available statutory damages claim](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Landgraf_v_USI_Film_Products_merits_1994-04-26.md#public-projection), 1994-04-26. Stevens’s §102 application, joined by Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Souter and Ginsburg, controls by five votes. Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas independently reach the same unavailability of new damages through their concurrence. Blackmun disagrees about compensation and reserves punitive damages. Stevens’s jury portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Souter and Ginsburg, supplies the controlling five-Justice explanation. Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas reach the same conditional conclusion in their concurrence; Blackmun would permit a jury election because he would make compensation available.
- Expanded contractual coverage and temporal reach: [Rivers v. Roadway Express, Inc — Section 101’s new contractual coverage does not govern the earlier conduct; Corrective legislative purpose does not itself give an amendment retrospective reach](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Rivers_v_Roadway_Express_merits_1994-04-26.md#public-projection), 1994-04-26. Stevens’s opinion, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Souter and Ginsburg; these five adopt the complete explanation. Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas reach the same temporal result through their separate relevant-activity and textual-command method. Stevens’s opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Souter and Ginsburg, controls by five votes at this explanatory level. Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas accept the judicial/legislative distinction through their separate textual method, not through a presumed full join.

**Present operation effective:** Rivers v. Roadway Express, Inc, 1994-04-26.

**Exceptions and limits:** Claims already available under the earlier law remain available on their own terms. Neither section supplies a new remedy for every earlier Title VII or §1981 wrong.

**Holdings navigation:** [Section 102 does not add its new damages to this preenactment conduct](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#section-102-does-not-add-its-new-damages-to-this-preenactment-conduct); [The jury election depends on an available statutory damages claim](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-jury-election-depends-on-an-available-statutory-damages-claim); [Section 101’s new contractual coverage does not govern the earlier conduct](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#section-101s-new-contractual-coverage-does-not-govern-the-earlier-conduct); [Corrective legislative purpose does not itself give an amendment retrospective reach](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#corrective-legislative-purpose-does-not-itself-give-an-amendment-retrospective-reach).

### Conviction-negating damages claims and favorable invalidation

**Governing question:** When a prisoner’s §1983 damages claim must await invalidation of the conviction or sentence.

**Current rule:** For an in-custody claimant with a real lawful opportunity to challenge the conviction, a §1983 damages claim whose success necessarily establishes the invalidity of that conviction or sentence cannot proceed before favorable invalidation. Qualifying invalidation is reversal on direct appeal, executive expungement, a declaration of invalidity by an authorized state tribunal, or a federal writ of habeas corpus calling it into question. The court compares the actual civil theory and criminal basis, not labels. Independent injuries whose success is compatible with the criminal judgment remain separately cognizable. The barred claim accrues upon qualifying invalidation; the rule is not a direction to file now and seek a stay or an invented tolling regime.

**Authority by component:**

- Limited in-custody necessary-invalidity prerequisite: [Heck v. Humphrey — I. Necessary invalidity on the present in-custody record; II. Statutory coordination supports this limited prerequisite](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Heck_v_Humphrey_merits_1994-06-24.md#public-projection), 1994-06-24. Part I of Justice O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine directly adopt this bounded rule; the distinct sufficient rationales are not added together to obtain its support. Part II of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Souter; five Justices adopt this sufficient statutory explanation at this limited scope. Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg do not join Part II.
- Qualifying invalidation, accrual and independent injuries: [Heck v. Humphrey — III. Qualifying invalidation and deferred accrual; IV. Independent injuries, claim-specific analysis and this dismissal](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Heck_v_Humphrey_merits_1994-06-24.md#public-projection), 1994-06-24. Part III of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; all nine directly adopt the four invalidation routes and the accrual consequence for the barred claim. Part IV of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; all nine directly adopt this boundary, application and remedy.

**Present operation effective:** Heck v. Humphrey, 1994-06-24.

**Open question:** Two distinct populations remain unresolved: former prisoners no longer in custody, and persons who never had a realistic opportunity for habeas review regardless of custody status. Neither reservation is an exemption already granted. Patsy’s ordinary no-exhaustion rule and Cavanaugh’s opportunity-only prospective claim remain effective.

**Holdings navigation:** [Necessary invalidity on the present in-custody record](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#necessary-invalidity-on-the-present-in-custody-record); [Statutory coordination supports this limited prerequisite](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#statutory-coordination-supports-this-limited-prerequisite); [Qualifying invalidation and deferred accrual](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#qualifying-invalidation-and-deferred-accrual); [Independent injuries, claim-specific analysis and this dismissal](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#independent-injuries-claim-specific-analysis-and-this-dismissal).

### Meaningful review of punitive-damage amounts

**Governing question:** What due process requires when a State removes traditional judicial review of punitive-damage amounts.

**Current rule:** A State may not remove meaningful judicial review of the amount of a punitive-damage award without an adequate substitute protecting against arbitrary deprivation. Review limited to whether any evidence supports an award, or safeguards directed only to entitlement rather than amount, do not necessarily fill that gap. The inquiry concerns meaningful protection against arbitrary magnitude; it does not establish a fixed constitutional ratio, mandatory remittitur, a prescribed written-opinion format or a required number of reviewing courts.

**Authority by component:**

- Amount review and inadequate substitutes: [Honda Motor Co. v. Oberg — Removing amount review without adequate replacement violates due process; Oregon's actual safeguards do not cure the amount-review gap](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Honda_Motor_Co_v_Oberg_merits_1994-06-24.md#public-projection), 1994-06-24. Justice Blackmun's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; eight Justices adopt this bounded procedural rule, without a substantive numerical rule. Justice Blackmun's opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; eight Justices adopt the application and limited review remedy.

**Present operation effective:** Honda Motor Co. v. Oberg, 1994-06-24.

**Open question:** The decision does not itself determine the permissible amount of the award or prescribe the only constitutionally sufficient system of review.

**Holdings navigation:** [Removing amount review without adequate replacement violates due process](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#removing-amount-review-without-adequate-replacement-violates-due-process); [Oregon's actual safeguards do not cure the amount-review gap](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#oregons-actual-safeguards-do-not-cure-the-amount-review-gap).

### Special federal corporations as governmental actors

**Governing question:** First Amendment responsibility of a corporation specially created and governed by the Federal Government.

**Current rule:** A corporation is governmental for First Amendment purposes when the Federal Government creates it by special law to advance governmental objectives and permanently retains power to appoint a majority of its governing directors. A statutory nonagency label cannot defeat that constitutional status, and ordinary managerial discretion does not require a separate external federal command for each challenged act. Charter, subsidy, regulation, governmental property, public benefit, a single appointment, temporary investment or present share ownership alone does not satisfy the combined rule.

**Authority by component:**

- Special federal creation and permanent majority appointment make Amtrak governmental: [Special federal creation and permanent majority appointment make Amtrak governmental](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Lebron_v_National_Railroad_Passenger_Corp_merits_1995-02-21.md#public-projection), 1995-02-21. Justice Scalia's constitutional-status portion, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer. Eight Justices directly support the same permanent-majority-appointment rule and its application; Justice O'Connor's conditional view of a question she declines to reach is not a join.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-02-21; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** This rule decides constitutional actor status, not the underlying speech claim, forum classification or relief. It supplies no universal agency, immunity, Treasury-liability, tort-liability or charter-jurisdiction classification.

**Holdings navigation:** [Special federal creation and permanent majority appointment make Amtrak governmental](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#special-federal-creation-and-permanent-majority-appointment-make-amtrak-governmental).

### Circumstantial proof of unconstitutional motive despite qualified immunity

**Governing question:** Whether qualified immunity requires direct evidence when motive is an element of the claim.

**Current rule:** Qualified immunity imposes no categorical direct-evidence requirement for unconstitutional motive. The plaintiff must establish the actual constitutional elements, personal responsibility and causation through legally competent direct or circumstantial evidence under ordinary procedural burdens. Specific nonconclusory support, lawful discovery limits and the clearly-established-law defense remain available; the rule abolishes neither all heightened specificity requirements nor a burden supplied by the underlying claim.

**Authority by component:**

- Circumstantial proof remains available in motive-dependent claims: [Circumstantial proof remains available in motive-dependent claims](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Kimberlin_v_Quinlan_merits_1995-06-12.md#public-projection), 1995-06-12. Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg, Breyer; nine votes support the rule and its operative limits.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-12; the component authorities identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Circumstantial proof remains available in motive-dependent claims](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#circumstantial-proof-remains-available-in-motive-dependent-claims).

## Criminal procedure, evidence, and punishment

### Confrontation and firmly rooted spontaneous-declaration and medical-treatment exceptions

**Governing question:** Whether production of the declarant or proof of unavailability is a prerequisite to admission under these accepted hearsay exceptions.

**Current rule:** When statements satisfy the firmly rooted spontaneous-declaration or medical-treatment exception, the Confrontation Clause does not require the prosecution first to produce the declarant or establish unavailability. The exception's actual foundations remain necessary; naming an exception cannot establish a disputed foundation or validate every police interview. The Clause is not categorically confined to formal affidavits or equivalent trial substitutes, so that proposed limitation cannot supply an alternative ground for admission. The received exception-sensitive confrontation framework remains in force.

**Authority by component:**

- Exception-specific absence of a production or unavailability gate: [White v. Illinois — No universal production or unavailability prerequisite](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/White_v_Illinois_merits_1992-01-15.md), January 15, 1992; Part I of Stone's opinion joined by all nine Justices.
- Rejection of a formal-accusation-only boundary: [White — The government's categorical definition of witness is rejected](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/White_v_Illinois_merits_1992-01-15.md), January 15, 1992; Part II joined by White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, and Souter with Stone. Scalia and Thomas do not join that component.

**Present operation effective:** *White v. Illinois*, January 15, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Former-testimony unavailability analysis, constitutional reliability outside firmly rooted exceptions, and safeguards for altered presentation of trial witnesses remain distinct. No later testimonial or primary-purpose test is adopted.

**Holdings navigation:** [White v. Illinois, 502 U.S. 346 (1992) — Confrontation and hearsay](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#white-v-illinois-502-us-346-1992).

### Physical injury in a nontrivial malicious custodial beating

**Governing question:** Whether significant physical injury is an additional necessary element where a restrained convicted prisoner suffers a nontrivial malicious beating satisfying *Whitley*.

**Current rule:** A nontrivial beating inflicted maliciously and without disciplinary justification on a restrained convicted prisoner violates the Eighth Amendment despite limited resulting physical injury when the established conduct satisfies *Whitley*'s demanding malicious-and-sadistic inquiry. Injury remains relevant evidence and bears on compensation; accidental harm or merely technical battery is insufficient. This rule identifies a sufficient constitutional violation and does not impose a universal mental-state standard for all prison-force claims.

**Authority by component:**

- Rejection of a significant-injury floor at the adopted narrow scope: [Hudson v. McMillian — Limited injury does not defeat this established nontrivial malicious beating](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Hudson_v_McMillian_merits_1992-02-25.md), February 25, 1992; Part A of O'Connor's opinion joined by Stone, White, Stevens, Kennedy, and Souter. The proposed universal *Whitley* extension has only four votes and is noncontrolling.

**Present operation effective:** *Hudson*, February 25, 1992.

**Open question:** A comprehensive mental-state rule for other force settings, psychological injury alone, and a general definition of punishment covering isolated unauthorized assaults remain unresolved. Medical-care, confinement-condition, personal-defense, and compensation rules remain distinct.

**Holdings navigation:** [Hudson v. McMillian, 503 U.S. 1 (1992) — Prisoners’ rights](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#hudson-v-mcmillian-503-us-1-1992).

### Review of Guidelines departures resting on invalid grounds

**Governing question:** When a reviewing court may affirm a Guidelines departure supported in part by an impermissible factor under 18 U.S.C. §3742(f).

**Current rule:** A departure based on a factor forbidden by the Commission, or already fully considered in the applicable range, is an incorrect Guidelines application under §3742(f)(1). An express prohibitory policy statement is authoritative guidance in applying the relevant Guideline; the arrest record itself cannot supply a departure ground. Once the challenger establishes reliance on an invalid factor, remand is required unless the party defending the sentence persuades the reviewing court, from the whole record, that the district court would have imposed the same sentence without it. The challenger need not prove that the error determined the sentence. Even if that error was harmless, §3742(f)(2) independently requires a reasonable departure extent justified by valid grounds the sentencing court actually identified. That the court could lawfully have imposed the sentence does not establish either actual causal harmlessness or permission for an appellate court to invent departure grounds.

**Authority by component:**
- Invalid-factor error and express policy prohibition: [Williams v. United States](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Williams_v_United_States_merits_1992-03-09.md), March 9, 1992, “An impermissible departure ground is a Guidelines application error.”
- Same-sentence inquiry and burden sequence: the same record, “Harmlessness requires the same sentence without the invalid factor.”
- Independent reasonableness inquiry and actual-ground limit: the same record, “Departure reasonableness remains a separate condition of affirmance.” O'Connor's opinion has seven joins at each level: Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** Williams, March 9, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** This is nonconstitutional error governed by Rule 52(a); no Chapman beyond-reasonable-doubt burden is adopted. The decision does not make every policy statement binding for every purpose or decide whether old nonsimilar convictions support a departure.

**Holdings navigation:** [Williams v. United States, 503 U.S. 193 (1992) — Guidelines departure review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#williams-v-united-states-503-us-193-1992).

### Adult Guidelines ceiling for juvenile detention

**Governing question:** The maximum detention authorized by 18 U.S.C. §5037(c)(1)(B) for a juvenile compared with the analogous adult offender and offense.

**Current rule:** The maximum authorized adult sentence used in §5037(c)(1)(B) includes the applicable adult Guidelines ceiling, subject to a properly supported lawful departure. It supplies an upper limit only: the Guidelines minimum does not become a juvenile floor, juvenile discretion below the cap remains, and the other statutory limits continue to apply. The construction does not require plenary adult Guidelines sentencing of the juvenile.

**Authority by component:**
- Upper-limit construction, departure qualification and preserved juvenile discretion: [United States v. R. L. C.](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_RLC_merits_1992-03-24.md), March 24, 1992, “The juvenile ceiling incorporates the lawful adult Guidelines maximum”; Souter's Parts I, II-A and III for Stone, White, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** R. L. C., March 24, 1992.

**Related current rules:** “Review of Guidelines departures resting on invalid grounds” supplies the lawful-departure limitation; R. L. C. approves no new departure ground.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. R. L. C., 503 U.S. 291 (1992) — Juvenile sentencing](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-v-r-l-c-503-us-291-1992-1).

### Federal detention credit, initial computation and conditional release

**Governing question:** Who initially computes credit under 18 U.S.C. §3585(b), when the computation occurs, and the treatment of time credited to another sentence.

**Current rule:** The Attorney General through the Bureau of Prisons must initially calculate §3585(b) credit after the federal sentence commences; a district court may not calculate that credit at sentencing. Time already credited against another sentence when the federal term begins cannot also receive federal credit merely because the federal sentencing hearing occurred first. Administrative review and proper subsequent judicial review remain available. Under the federal Bail Reform Act, a conditional-release order is not official detention under §3585(b), however severe its restrictions; no conditions-based credit inquiry converts that release into detention. Qualifying uncredited detention resulting from the offense sentenced or another charge for which arrest occurred after that offense retains the statutory credit command.

**Authority by component:**
- Actor, timing and review: [United States v. Wilson](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_Wilson_merits_1992-03-24.md), March 24, 1992, “BOP makes the initial credit computation after commencement.”
- Commencement-based no-double-credit construction: the same record, “Credit already applied to another sentence cannot be counted again.” Thomas's opinion for Stone, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter controls both components.

- Official detention and federal conditional release: [Official detention and federal conditional release](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Reno_v_Koray_merits_1995-06-05.md#public-projection), 1995-06-05. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court, joined by O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer; seven directly adopt the status rule and its application without an independently sufficient administrative-deference holding.

**Present operation effective:** Koray, June 5, 1995, for the federal conditional-release boundary; Wilson, March 24, 1992, for initial computation and the no-double-credit rule.

**Open question:** State custody and other unaddressed forms of official detention remain unresolved. Koray does not universally require physical BOP custody for every creditable day and establishes no independent administrative-deference or bail-choice notice rule.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Wilson, 503 U.S. 329 (1992) — Detention credit](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-v-wilson-503-us-329-1992); [Official detention and federal conditional release](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#official-detention-and-federal-conditional-release).

### Successive prosecutions, evidentiary overlap and criminal contempt

**Governing question:** When evidentiary overlap, conspiracy or completed criminal contempt bars a successive prosecution.

**Current rule:** Evidentiary use of an uncharged crime does not itself place the defendant in jeopardy for that crime.
