### Denominational selection for general governmental authority

**Governing question:** When creating a special public district impermissibly allocates civic power on a denominational basis.

**Current rule:** The State may not select a religious denomination as the recipient of general governmental authority, even when lay officials formally exercise the power. Independently, an anomalous particular grant aligned with a denomination and lacking a neutral basis assuring comparable treatment violates the required governmental neutrality. Religious accommodation may preserve equal educational access but does not cure denominational allocation of civic power. Demographic religious concentration alone does not establish the violation, and neither rationale makes every special statute or every accommodation invalid.

**Authority by component:**

- Five-Justice denominational-allocation ground: [Board of Education of Kiryas Joel Village School District v. Grumet — The State may not select a denomination as the recipient of general governmental authority](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Board_of_Education_of_Kiryas_Joel_Village_School_District_v_Grumet_merits_1994-06-27.md#public-projection), 1994-06-27. Souter's opinion, Part II introduction and II-A, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Ginsburg: five Justices. This is an independently sufficient ground. O'Connor does not join that portion, and Kennedy's separate judgment rationale is not counted as a join.
- Six-Justice particular-grant ground and accommodation limit: [Board of Education of Kiryas Joel Village School District v. Grumet — The particular denominational grant lacks a neutral basis for equal treatment; Accommodation preserves educational access but does not cure this allocation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Board_of_Education_of_Kiryas_Joel_Village_School_District_v_Grumet_merits_1994-06-27.md#public-projection), 1994-06-27. Souter's opinion, Part II-B, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor and Ginsburg: six Justices. This is an independently sufficient alternative ground; Kennedy expressly declines it. Souter's opinion, Part II-C and the implementing limits in Part III, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor and Ginsburg: six Justices. This holding rejects the accommodation defense and defines the remedy's limits; Kennedy reaches the judgment on his separate ground.

**Present operation effective:** Board of Education of Kiryas Joel Village School District v. Grumet, 1994-06-27.

**Exceptions and limits:** The existing Lemon framework is not overruled. The decision supplies no categorical prohibition on neutral arrangements serving religious communities.

**Holdings navigation:** [The State may not select a denomination as the recipient of general governmental authority](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-state-may-not-select-a-denomination-as-the-recipient-of-general-governmental-authority); [Accommodation preserves educational access but does not cure this allocation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#accommodation-preserves-educational-access-but-does-not-cure-this-allocation).

### RFRA review of prospective enforcement

**Governing question:** Application of original RFRA to threatened enforcement, including preexisting laws and commercial religious exercise.

**Current rule:** The claimant must establish a substantial burden on religious exercise. The government must then produce evidence and persuade that applying that burden to this person furthers a compelling governmental interest by the least restrictive means. The inquiry applies to threatened future implementation of a preexisting law. Commercial participation does not forfeit its protection, and labeling a law antidiscrimination does not itself justify its application. Direct transactional and dignitary injury and legally material alternatives or exemptions may be considered; availability of alternative housing is not an automatic answer to direct injury.

**Authority by component:**

- RFRA governs future enforcement and requires application-specific adjudication: [RFRA governs future enforcement and requires application-specific adjudication](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Church_of_Scientology_Flag_Service_Organization_Inc_v_City_of_Clearwater_merits_1994-12-05.md#public-projection), 1994-12-05. Justice O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer: eight direct rationale votes. Chief Justice Stone-Zsela agrees that RFRA governs, but does not join the opinion or its disposition of these adverse determinations.
- RFRA requires a distinct inquiry into this application of the housing laws: [RFRA requires a distinct inquiry into this application of the housing laws](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Swanner_v_Anchorage_Equal_Rights_Commission_merits_1995-03-27.md#public-projection), 1995-03-27. Justice O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Justices Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Thomas: five direct rationale and judgment votes. Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, Ginsburg, and Breyer dissent from the vacatur. Their agreement that RFRA governs does not turn their distinct application into Court law.

**Present operation effective:** Church of Scientology v. Clearwater, December 5, 1994; Swanner, March 27, 1995, clarifies the commercial-participation and application-specific limits without changing the burden sequence.; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Ordinary standing and appropriate-relief limits remain. RFRA protects religious belief and preserves the Establishment Clause; this statutory inquiry does not replace the constitutional free-exercise or establishment standards. A remand under it establishes neither an exemption nor failure of the government’s eventual showing.

**Holdings navigation:** [RFRA governs future enforcement and requires application-specific adjudication](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#rfra-governs-future-enforcement-and-requires-application-specific-adjudication); [RFRA requires a distinct inquiry into this application of the housing laws](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#rfra-requires-a-distinct-inquiry-into-this-application-of-the-housing-laws).

### Ministerial selection as a substantive constitutional defense

**Governing question:** Title VII claims whose adjudication or relief controls selection of religious leaders.

**Current rule:** The Religion Clauses bar applying Title VII to ministerial advancement and retention claims when adjudication would subject a church’s selection of religious leadership to civil review. The bar includes reinstatement, compelled reconsideration and damages resting on that same selection, including race, sex and retaliation theories. It is a substantive defense: a court with jurisdiction over a substantial Title VII claim under §706(f)(3) and §1331 must enter a merits dismissal, not deny subject-matter jurisdiction.

**Authority by component:**

- Ministerial selection claims encounter a constitutional defense: [Ministerial selection claims encounter a constitutional defense](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Young_v_Northern_Illinois_Conference_merits_1995-01-17.md#public-projection), 1995-01-17. Justice Souter's opinion, this proposition, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer. All nine adopt this claim-specific rule; their formal descriptions of the judgment are distinguished below.
- The defense defeats the claims on their merits, not statutory jurisdiction: [The defense defeats the claims on their merits, not statutory jurisdiction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Young_v_Northern_Illinois_Conference_merits_1995-01-17.md#public-projection), 1995-01-17. Justice Souter's opinion, this proposition, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer. All nine adopt the jurisdiction-and-merits distinction; eight vacate the jurisdictional judgment as a whole, while the Chief would vacate the jurisdictional characterization and affirm the dismissal on the merits.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-01-17; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** The rule neither enlarges §702’s religious-preference permission nor exempts every religious employee or unrelated secular conduct. An independently pleaded salary, property, tort or contract claim remains outside this defense if it can be resolved without doctrine, ministerial fitness or selection. No comprehensive minister definition, fraud-or-collusion exception, RFRA disposition or new requirement to exhaust the denomination’s ultimate hierarchy is adopted.

**Holdings navigation:** [Ministerial selection claims encounter a constitutional defense](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#ministerial-selection-claims-encounter-a-constitutional-defense); [The defense defeats the claims on their merits, not statutory jurisdiction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-defense-defeats-the-claims-on-their-merits-not-statutory-jurisdiction).

## Reproductive liberty and abortion regulation

### Protected choice and the six-step standard for previability regulation

**Governing question:** Whether a legal obligation regulating previability abortion impermissibly interferes with the patient's protected decision or access to timely, clinically appropriate care.

**Current rule:** Before viability the patient decides whether to continue pregnancy without proving medical necessity or obtaining official approval of her reasons. The State may not prohibit that choice, confer effective control on another person, or prevent timely necessary life-and-health care. After viability, prohibition requires the life-and-health safeguard, including individualized physical and mental health considerations. Viability is individualized and medically supported, not a fixed constitutional week. Clinical appropriateness concerns safe and suitable care, not official approval of the patient's reasons; not every preference is a medical emergency. The following complete standard governs throughout previability:

1. **Identify the duty and protected baseline.** Identify the enacted command, authoritative construction, exceptions, enforcement consequences and relevant medical circumstances. Distinguish an actual obligation from assumed future administration. A previability prohibition, effective third-party veto or denial of timely necessary life-and-health care violates the baseline and cannot be rescued by justification. Necessary care must remain available before imminent death or catastrophic deterioration. Defining all noncompliant treatment as unsafe does not establish medical necessity.
2. **Identify actual function and purpose.** Identify what the obligation does and the asserted permissible objective, including safety, informed consent, truthful information, permissible parental involvement, health administration or financial accountability. The State may prefer childbirth and communicate truthful information. Moral disapproval does not independently justify obstruction, punishment of protected choice or burdens imposed for their deterrent effect. An obstructive purpose established by objective evidence of text, design, context and operation independently invalidates the requirement; a legislator's opposition to abortion alone does not establish that purpose. Voluntary persuasion differs from prevention through barriers. Evaluate each justification on its terms: medical claims need medical support, and deliberative claims need an explanation linking the particular obligation to informed decisionmaking.
3. **Establish actual interference, comparison and affected population.** Challengers must establish concrete additional interference caused by the challenged obligation. Relevant effects include extra appointments, material expense, consequential delay, loss of usable provider capacity, clinical risk, coercion and disclosure consequences affecting choice or access. Compare the actual system with and without the obligation while holding other relevant law and circumstances constant. For a challenged interacting package, identify the package and compare the system without it. Consider cumulative effects without counting the same burden twice. Define the affected population from the duty's operation before deciding constitutional injury, include evidence-supported subgroups, and keep the population stable. Do not average the burden across everyone pregnant or define the group as only those who prevail. Poverty, distance, transportation, employment and abuse matter insofar as they explain the State's added condition; identify that causal link. Exceptions, bypasses and alternative providers count only if actually usable. Meaningful interference means concrete, nontrivial additional burden on obtaining care or exercising the protected decision. It triggers review rather than establishing invalidity. No universal dollar, mileage or hourly threshold applies. Reliable clinical evidence, records, testimony and reasonable predictions can establish sufficiently demonstrated prospective injury before access is lost.
4. **Apply the substantive protections in order.** First, a baseline violation, unconstitutional obstructive purpose or substantial obstacle is independently invalid. A substantial obstacle seriously obstructs the affected population's practical opportunity for timely, clinically appropriate protected care; absolute impossibility is unnecessary, and success by some patients does not establish adequate access for others. A compelling interest cannot rescue an established obstacle. Second, remaining severe interference requires a compelling permissible justification and narrow tailoring. Identify the severe intrusion and explain why the independent prohibitions did not already decide the issue; overlapping protections require no artificial boundary, but an obstacle may not be relabeled as justifiable severe interference. Serious intrusion into clinical judgment, health or protected confidentiality may require this scrutiny despite usable access. Third, for remaining meaningful interference the State must substantiate a permissible justification and its fit to the added obligation. Abstract interests and conclusory assertions are insufficient. When challengers identify a concrete, practicable, substantially less burdensome alternative adequately serving the objective, the State must answer it; inadequacy, unavailability or material countervailing consequences may answer that showing. The State need not select the cheapest imaginable policy or disprove every hypothetical alternative. Reliable experience and reasonable predictions may suffice; particular legislative findings, a new study in every case, scientific certainty and numerical benefit calculations are not required. Once justification and fit are established, courts may not substitute their preferred policy. Below meaningful interference this heightened access inquiry ends, while prohibited-purpose claims, independent constitutional protections and ordinary lawful-regulation requirements remain available.
5. **Protect clinical judgment and truthful communication.** Evaluate medical justifications against competent evidence, accepted professional standards, the patient's circumstances and substantial contrary evidence. Substantial professional agreement receives serious evidentiary weight, without a professional association's veto or conclusive weight for a clinician's bare assertion or an official's unsupported preference. Necessary care must remain timely; unnecessary committee vetoes and documentation functioning as prior permission may not displace appropriate clinical judgment. Duties must be sufficiently definite and applicable fault requirements respected. Later reasonable medical disagreement alone does not establish every element of a punishable offense; lawful enforcement against fraud, malpractice and professional misconduct remains. Required medical information must be accurate, nonmisleading and material to informed consent. Concise factual assistance notices may inform choice without ideological assent; clinicians may explain limitations and correct misinformation. Accuracy does not alone validate burdensome timing, delivery or certification. Distinguish useful confidential oversight records from public disclosure and records enforcing invalid obligations.
6. **Identify the violation and tailor relief.** Specify the duty or application that fails, why, and the corresponding relief. Preserve independently lawful requirements under applicable severability law and expressly address dependent certifications, report fields and sanctions. An individual claim does not fail because others comply; broader relief requires a corresponding showing. An indivisible mandatory condition may be invalidated when its ordinary operation imposes demonstrated meaningful interference and the State fails to justify it. Voluntary compliance in some circumstances does not alone validate compulsory compliance. Otherwise confine relief to the established applications or groups. This qualification of a strict no-valid-applications approach does not remove proof of causation, scope or inadequate justification. For cumulative burdens, remedy the challenged obligation's or package's contribution without automatically invalidating all regulation. Replacing the trimester timetable does not dissolve injunctions or authorize enforcement of every challenged provision; the mandate must specify the resulting duties.

**Authority by component:**

- Protected previability choice, individualized viability and necessary life-and-health protection: [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, “The framework retaining protected choice”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Planned_Parenthood_of_Southeastern_Pennsylvania_v_Casey_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; Stone's opinion joined by Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter.
- All six steps, including the independent obstacle bar, residual severe-interference scrutiny, meaningful lesser-burden substantiation, proof allocation, clinical safeguards and relief: *Casey*, “The standard governing previability regulation after this decision”; Stone's opinion joined by Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor and Souter. These five direct joins supply the whole rule; no *Marks* synthesis is needed.
- Integrated displacement of the independent trimester timetable: *Casey*, “The trimester timetable and the timing of regulation”; the same five-Justice rationale. The other four votes against continuing the timetable do not join the replacement framework.

**Present operation effective:** *Casey*, June 29, 1992.

**Burdens and proof:** Challengers establish the duty, population, causation and triggering interference. The State then bears the applicable justification and tailoring or fit burden. This does not alter ordinary proof of disputed historical facts or the enacted burden for a separate statutory defense. No State substantiation burden arises merely because a law concerns abortion.

**Exceptions and limits:** Gestational age may support a particular medically justified safety requirement; crossing a trimester boundary does not itself change the standard. The rule creates no general professional immunity, funding entitlement, constitutional fetal-personhood rule, conscience ruling, general right to a particular drug, or universal numerical facial-review formula. It does not decide every postviability restriction.

**Material development:** The independent trimester timing restrictions are displaced only with adoption of this complete substitute. Protected choice, viability and necessary life-and-health care survive. Absence of a substantial obstacle no longer ends the inquiry into demonstrated meaningful interference.

**Holdings navigation:** [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Scope of prospective relief for abortion requirements](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992); [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Reproductive liberty and constitutional precedent](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-5).

### Spousal notification that gives practical control over protected choice

**Governing question:** Whether compulsory spousal notice and certification may condition an adult patient's protected previability decision.

**Current rule:** A notification requirement is invalid when its proved operation gives spouses practical control over protected decisions for a large fraction of the independently defined women compelled to disclose against their wishes, after accounting for usable exceptions. Voluntary consultation by other patients does not cure that restriction. Identify the compelled group before deciding injury; do not count willing disclosure as a constitutionally valid restrictive application or impose a universal numerical threshold. Formal notice rather than consent does not answer practical prevention. Severance and relief against dependent certification, enforcement and notice-compliance reporting must address the unconstitutional duty; a separately lawful confidential demographic field does not fall merely because it shares a subsection.

**Authority by component:**

- Effective control, affected-group review and notice-specific facial qualification: [Casey, “Spousal notice and effective control over the protected decision”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Planned_Parenthood_of_Southeastern_Pennsylvania_v_Casey_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter directly support the rationale.
- Dependent reporting versus independently severable demographic information: *Casey*, “Notice-compliance and nonnotification-reason reports” (the same six) and “The independently severable marital-status field” (all nine).

**Present operation effective:** *Casey*, June 29, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** The invalidated §3209 allowed substitute signed statements that the spouse was not the father, could not be located after diligent effort, that pregnancy resulted from spousal sexual assault under §3128 reported to an agency with jurisdiction, or that the woman had reason to believe notification was likely to cause bodily injury to her by her spouse or another person; medical emergency separately excused compliance. The incorporated §3128(c) condition required personal reporting by the victim or her agent within 90 days of the assault. Statements required no notarization but had to warn against punishable false statements. These exceptions did not generally reach psychological coercion, financial retaliation, harm to children or others, exposure of private information, or divorce and custody threats. The holding does not independently adjudicate §3128(c)'s criminal-law reporting requirement. That reporting condition does not limit the distinct rape-omission permission for paternal-support information.

**Related current rules:** “Protected choice and the six-step standard for previability regulation” supplies the five-Justice independently dispositive obstacle rule. Kennedy joins this notice-specific rationale without joining that whole standard.

**Holdings navigation:** [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Abortion reporting and public disclosure](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-3); [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Spousal control of reproductive choice](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-6).

### Medical emergency and the separate disclosure-health defense

**Governing question:** How Pennsylvania's emergency, emergency-explanation and harmful-information provisions operate independently.

**Current rule:** Section 3203 defines emergency through a physician's good-faith clinical judgment that immediate abortion is needed to avert death or that delay creates serious risk of substantial and irreversible impairment of a major bodily function. It is sustained under the prospective construction that compliance cannot create a significant threat to life or health, permitting treatment of preeclampsia, inevitable abortion and prematurely ruptured membranes at onset rather than after catastrophic deterioration. Under §3205(b), the physician must explain the medical indications before emergency abortion only if possible; explanation is not advance permission or a reason to delay necessary care. Separately, §3205(c) protects a physician from liability for omitting §3205(a) information upon proof by a preponderance of a reasonable belief that furnishing it would severely adversely affect the patient's physical or mental health. Actual occurrence of the feared injury is unnecessary. That defense neither supplies a waiting-period waiver nor replaces emergency treatment protection.

**Authority by component:**

- Emergency construction: [Casey, “Medical emergency and the protective construction of §3203”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Planned_Parenthood_of_Southeastern_Pennsylvania_v_Casey_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992.
- Conditional prior explanation: *Casey*, “Emergency explanation under §3205(b).”
- Separate defense and enacted proof burden: *Casey*, “The distinct information-omission defense in §3205(c).” All three are unanimous direct holdings.

**Present operation effective:** *Casey*, June 29, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** The significant-threat interpretation is the Court's adoption of the Third Circuit's prospective construction, not extra statutory language or a binding Pennsylvania Supreme Court construction. No separate emergency-vagueness holding or decision about every clinical risk or postviability restriction follows. The statutory defense's burden remains distinct from the constitutional proof allocation.

**Holdings navigation:** [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Abortion emergency care](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-1); [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Abortion informed consent](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-2).

### Accurate abortion-consent information, optional materials and receipt certification

**Governing question:** What Pennsylvania may require patients to be told, offered or asked to certify in obtaining informed consent, including independent First Amendment limits.

**Current rule:** Section 3205(a)(1) may require accurate, medically material information about the procedure, material risks and alternatives, probable gestational age and childbirth risks. The three §3205(a)(2) notices may state truthfully: state materials concerning fetal development and alternatives are available and a free copy will be supplied if chosen; medical assistance for prenatal care, childbirth and neonatal care may be available; and the father is liable to assist in support even if he offered to pay for abortion. Assistance and legal liability are not promises of eligibility, payment or collection. Paternal-support information **may** be omitted in a rape pregnancy, including spousal rape, without a police-report condition; omission is permitted, not required, and changes no support obligation. Section 3208 publication and §3205(a)(3) chosen receipt are permissible at their objective, accurate, nonjudgmental scope. Notice of availability is distinct from receipt: patients need not accept, read or endorse materials or listed agencies. Clinicians may correct or criticize them. Section 3205(a)(4) certification may record actual receipt of lawful information, not ideological agreement, unchosen reading or compliance with an invalid prerequisite. The §3205(c) disclosure-health defense remains available on its own terms.

The First Amendment independently permits these bounded medical and ancillary disclosures, voluntary receipt and factual documentation. Payment does not strip professional speech of protection, and truth alone does not justify every compulsory statement. False, irrelevant, coercive or ideological scripts and materially different implementation remain outside the sustained duties. Accurate content does not establish the validity of a timing or exclusive-speaker condition; invalid timing and delivery obligations cannot survive through a certificate.

**Authority by component:**

- Medical content, objective publication/chosen receipt and basic certification: [Casey, “Accurate material medical information,” “Statutory publication and chosen receipt of materials,” and “Certification of lawful receipt”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Planned_Parenthood_of_Southeastern_Pennsylvania_v_Casey_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; unanimous direct holdings at those scopes.
- Each ancillary notice and its qualification: *Casey*, “Notice of available free materials,” “Notice of potentially available medical assistance,” and “Paternal-support notice and the rape omission permission”; Stone, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter supply five rationale joins. The additional judgment votes do not broaden that rationale.
- Independent speech rules: *Casey*, the three “Independent speech challenge” holdings; Souter with White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas for medical content, chosen materials and basic certification; the same group without Blackmun for ancillary notices. Stone's judgment-only agreements supply no speech rationale.
- Dependent timing and delivery links: *Casey*, “Certification of lawful receipt,” with the five-Justice invalidation grounds in “Physician delivery of medical information” and “The mandatory 24-hour interval.”

**Present operation effective:** *Casey*, June 29, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** The rape omission permission has no §3209/§3128 reporting or 90-day condition. No finding establishes universal accuracy of actual publications or agencies. No general commercial-speech classification or professional-speech immunity is adopted. Required content remains subject to the separate access framework.

**Material development:** Akron and Thornburgh are qualified only insofar as they categorically prohibit the sustained concise, accurate ancillary notices because they may encourage childbirth; their protection against misleading, coercive and unjustifiably burdensome implementation remains.

**Holdings navigation:** [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Abortion informed consent](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-2); [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Abortion information and compelled speech](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-7).

### One-parent informed consent with an independent judicial bypass

**Governing question:** The permissible operation of Pennsylvania §3206's parental-consent structure for unemancipated minors under eighteen.

**Current rule:** The minor's informed consent and one parent's informed consent ordinarily suffice, with emergency treatment excused from compliance. Only the mother's consent is required when the father participated in incest. A guardian may consent when both parents are deceased or unavailable within reasonable time and manner; after divorce the custodial parent's consent suffices; if neither parent nor guardian is so available, an adult standing in place of a parent may consent. The parent or guardian considers only the minor's best interests. The minor may elect court authorization without first seeking parental consent. Maturity and capacity with actual informed consent, or alternatively best interests, require authorization through a confidential, expedited process. Counsel is provided absent private counsel or knowing, intelligent waiver; the trial ruling is due within three business days of application and appeal ruling within five business days of filing. Proceedings and records are confidential. Necessary emergency care cannot await those proceedings.

Parental informed consent may use the accurate medical content and qualified materials, assistance and paternal-support notices described in “Accurate abortion-consent information, optional materials and receipt certification,” including optional materials and permission to omit paternal-support information after rape without a police report. This construction creates no parent-specific waiting period, physician-only delivery requirement or compulsory clinic visit. A bypass is not a universal answer to the burdens of a separate obligation.

**Authority by component:**

- Structure, statutory substitutions, emergency exception and alternate bypass routes: [Casey, “One-parent informed consent and independent judicial bypass”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Planned_Parenthood_of_Southeastern_Pennsylvania_v_Casey_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; unanimous rationale.
- Parental information and nonincorporation of additional timing, speaker or attendance obligations: *Casey*, “Accurate information used for informed parental consent”; Souter, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas supply seven rationale joins. Stone agrees only in the judgment on this component.
- No bypass-based approval of an unestablished extra visit: *Casey*, “The parental-attendance objection and the denial of special relief”; Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor and Souter supply the controlling ground.

**Present operation effective:** *Casey*, June 29, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** These are the sustained statutory safeguards, not a holding that every one is constitutionally indispensable everywhere. The lower court's use of §3205 to explain parental information is a construction, not enacted incorporation of all adult mechanisms. No independent adult-incapacity claim or hypothetical compulsory-visit operation is decided.

**Holdings navigation:** [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Minor consent and independent authorization](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-4).

### Confidential abortion reporting and distinct medical predicates

**Governing question:** The lawful scope of Pennsylvania's confidential medical-basis reporting duties and severable demographic collection.

**Current rule:** Section 3214(a)(8) may require the basis for a physician's judgment that abortion under §3211(b)(1)'s rule for twenty-four or more weeks was necessary to prevent death or substantial and irreversible impairment of a major bodily function. The report does not create a new substantive necessity test or decide every other later-abortion restriction. Section 3214(a)(10) may require an after-the-fact account of the judgment that a §3203 emergency excused compliance; it creates no advance-permission or pretreatment-report condition and does not narrow the prospective emergency construction. Section 3214(a)(11), incorporating §3210(a), may require the actual inquiries, examinations, tests and diagnostic basis used to determine probable gestational age under the prudent-physician standard. It does not command additional medically unnecessary tests, and §3210(a)'s exception when medical emergency prevents compliance remains. Individual reports omit patient names and retain the confidentiality safeguards accepted in the decision: the reports are not public records; law-enforcement access requires a common pleas court order after an application showing good cause; and the Department's statistical publication must not disclose any physician's, facility's, or patient's identity. Recording clinical judgment neither establishes a new offense from later professional disagreement alone nor provides general immunity from lawful enforcement. Separately, §3214(a)(12)'s confidential marital-status field may survive severance of the invalid spousal-notice and nonnotification-reason fields, without reconstructing those duties.

**Authority by component:**

- Each distinct medical field and its construction: [Casey, “Confidential reporting of medical necessity under §3214(a)(8),” “Confidential reporting of an emergency judgment under §3214(a)(10),” and “Confidential reporting of the diagnostic basis under §3214(a)(11)”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Planned_Parenthood_of_Southeastern_Pennsylvania_v_Casey_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; unanimous direct holdings.
- Independent demographic field: *Casey*, “The independently severable marital-status field”; unanimous direct holding.

**Present operation effective:** *Casey*, June 29, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** The separate gestational-age result field and unappealed underlying ascertainment/collection duties are not newly adjudicated. Confidential collection does not itself justify public release, and confidentiality alone does not answer a demonstrated access burden. The public-release relief concerning §§3207(b) and 3214(f) is confined to the plaintiff facilities' covered reports; extension requires its own evidentiary and procedural basis. No general immunity from financial accountability or governmental entitlement to intimate information follows.

**Holdings navigation:** [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Abortion reporting and public disclosure](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-3).

### North Dakota definitions and separate enforcement conditions

**Governing question:** The preserved facial notice challenges to North Dakota’s abortion and medical-emergency definitions and general penalty.

**Current rule:** Section 14-02.1-02(1)’s termination of pregnancy with an intention other than producing a live birth or removing a dead embryo or fetus identifies purposeful termination; diagnostic amniocentesis followed by unintended loss is not abortion merely because its diagnostic purpose is neither exclusion. Section 14-02.1-02(7) supplies a prospective best-clinical-judgment criterion: immediate abortion to avert death, or a twenty-four-hour delay creating grave peril of immediate and irreversible loss of major bodily function. Those terms require professional forecast, not certainty, a numerical prognosis or waiting until harm occurs, and survive the presented facial vagueness claim.

**Authority by component:**

- The abortion definition excludes an unintended diagnostic termination: [The abortion definition excludes an unintended diagnostic termination](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Fargo_Womens_Health_Organization_v_Schafer_merits_1995-02-13.md#public-projection), 1995-02-13. Justice Souter's Part II, joined by Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. Eight Justices directly adopt this textual ground; Chief Justice Stone-Zsela would vacate and remand this component.
- The specified emergency terms supply a prospective clinical criterion: [The specified emergency terms supply a prospective clinical criterion](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Fargo_Womens_Health_Organization_v_Schafer_merits_1995-02-13.md#public-projection), 1995-02-13. Justice Souter's Part III, joined by Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. Eight Justices directly adopt the bounded clarity and enforcement ground; Chief Justice Stone-Zsela would vacate and remand this component.
- A doubtful default-fault rationale does not invalidate the general penalty by itself: [A doubtful default-fault rationale does not invalidate the general penalty by itself](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Fargo_Womens_Health_Organization_v_Schafer_merits_1995-02-13.md#public-projection), 1995-02-13. Justice Souter's Part IV, joined by Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. Eight Justices adopt this limited rejection of the penalty theory and preserve the stated element-specific questions. Chief Justice Stone-Zsela would remand the affected definitions and enforcement questions.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-02-13; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Clarity does not establish that the narrow emergency exception protects all substantively necessary care under Casey. The separate emergency undue-burden claim was not pressed below. The federal appellate significant-threat construction is neither enacted text nor an authoritative state construction and does not delete immediacy, irreversibility or major function. Section 14-02.1-03(1) excuses its ordinary duties in an emergency but requires explanation of medical indications before abortion if possible and written certification; neither is advance permission to delay necessary care.

**Controlling construction:** Section 12.1-02-02 alone supplies no default willfulness for §14-02.1-11’s silent misdemeanor clause. The latter applies to a chapter violation without another specifically prescribed penalty; its separate willful rule-or-regulation infraction does not amend it or supply every criminal or civil element. Silence alone does not establish facial invalidity, and actual element-specific fault remains open. The separate informed-consent civil remedy specifies $10,000 punitive plus treble actual damages for a completed abortion and $5,000 punitive plus treble actual damages for an attempt; no civil scienter defense or liability is established. Later reasonable medical disagreement alone does not establish every offense element, while fraud, malpractice and professional misconduct remain subject to lawful enforcement.

**Related current rules:** Protected choice and the six-step standard for previability regulation remains controlling without a separate numerical facial threshold. Pennsylvania’s disclosure-health defense and permissive rape-related omission of paternal-support information are not inserted into North Dakota law; their absence requires assessment of properly supported applications, not automatic invalidation. The definition rulings confer no general clinical immunity.

**Holdings navigation:** [The abortion definition excludes an unintended diagnostic termination](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-abortion-definition-excludes-an-unintended-diagnostic-termination); [The specified emergency terms supply a prospective clinical criterion](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-specified-emergency-terms-supply-a-prospective-clinical-criterion); [A doubtful default-fault rationale does not invalidate the general penalty by itself](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#a-doubtful-default-fault-rationale-does-not-invalidate-the-general-penalty-by-itself).

## Education and equal protection

### Partial withdrawal of school-desegregation supervision

**Governing question:** Release of completed portions of a school-desegregation decree while obligations elsewhere remain unfinished.

**Current rule:** A court may withdraw supervision from a completed component while retaining jurisdiction and supervision elsewhere only after the school district shows full and satisfactory compliance and elimination of the relevant vestiges to the extent practicable. The court must assess demonstrated good faith toward the whole decree and whether retaining control of that component is necessary or practicable to obtain compliance elsewhere; release may not obstruct the remaining remedy. The district bears the burden of showing that imbalance for which it disclaims responsibility is not traceable to the constitutional violation. Genuinely independent demographic changes do not require perpetual balancing after required remedial work is completed. Existing findings receive clear-error review, but a finding on one question does not decide unexamined causal or remedial relationships. Withdrawal orders must define obligations released, remaining duties and retained supervision.

**Authority by component:**
- Partial-release conditions, burden and integrated assessment: [Freeman v. Pitts](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Freeman_v_Pitts_merits_1992-03-31.md), March 31, 1992, holding 1, “Partial withdrawal under an unfinished decree.”
- Causation, clear-error review and defined retained obligations: the same record, holding 2, “Causation and the limits of the remand.” Stone for White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter controls; Thomas did not participate.

**Present operation effective:** Freeman, March 31, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Racial imbalance alone establishes neither a present constitutional violation nor entitlement to release. Neither good intentions alone, an automatic demographic exemption nor categorical simultaneous compliance across all components replaces the specified showing. Rufo's consent-decree modification standard is distinct and imposes no additional changed-circumstances prerequisite for finding a school-decree obligation fulfilled. Separate opinions' specific factual applications and proposed changes to causation presumptions do not control.

**Holdings navigation:** [Freeman v. Pitts, 503 U.S. 467 (1992) — School desegregation decrees](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#freeman-v-pitts-503-us-467-1992).

### Dismantling a formerly segregated public university system

**Governing question:** Discharge of a State's affirmative duty after it maintained a legally segregated university system.

**Current rule:** Good faith and facially neutral policies alone do not discharge the duty. The State must eliminate policies traceable to the dual system that continue to foster segregation when their effects can practicably be removed consistently with sound educational practices. No fresh discriminatory intent is needed for each such remnant; current racial imbalance alone is insufficient. The State bears the burden of demonstrating dismantlement. Examine present effects, educational justification and practical alternatives, including interacting admissions policies, program duplication, mission assignments, institutional organization and properly challenged funding features. A later neutral purpose does not by itself remove an inherited policy's continuing effects. Tie remedies to the established violation and practicable removal of its consequences: neither racial identifiability nor fiscal inefficiency alone requires closure or merger, and improved funding alone does not establish dismantlement while unlawful barriers remain.

**Authority by component:**

- Continuing duty, burden, combined-effects inquiry and violation-linked remedies: [United States v. Fordice and Ayers v. Fordice, holdings 1–4](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_Fordice_and_Ayers_v_Fordice_merits_1992-06-26.md), June 26, 1992; White's opinion joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas. The related Title VI disposition uses that same established discrimination duty.

**Present operation effective:** *Fordice*, June 26, 1992, specifying the established dismantlement duty in higher education.

**Exceptions and limits:** New policies without roots in the dual system remain subject to ordinary equal-protection analysis. No proportional-enrollment command, universal test-score ban, closure order, general Title VI disparate-impact cause or new heightened proof quantum follows. Freeman's separate school-decree rules and Rufo's consent-decree modification rule retain their distinct functions.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Fordice; Ayers v. Fordice, 505 U.S. 717 (1992) — University desegregation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-v-fordice-ayers-v-fordice-505-us-717-1992).

### Rational-basis review of ordinary economic classifications

**Governing question:** What instructions govern rational-basis review of ordinary economic classifications?

**Current rule:** For an economic classification involving neither a suspect class nor a fundamental right under the question reviewed, a reasonably conceivable legitimate basis suffices and the challenger must negate it. Formal legislative findings are unnecessary. Conceivable differences in bargaining arrangements, regulatory costs or competition can justify a line without proof that every regulated entity shares a monopoly. Moreno and Cleburne retain their prohibition of irrational or impermissibly motivated classifications; this rule supplies no resolution of a distinct heightened-scrutiny or First Amendment claim.

**Authority by component:**

- Common-owner bargaining and regulatory costs sustain the economic line: [FCC v. Beach Communications, Inc. — Common-owner bargaining and regulatory costs sustain the economic line](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/beach_merits_1993-06-01.md), 1993-06-01. Thomas’s Parts I and II-A; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (8 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.
- Competitive entry independently supports the ownership distinction: [FCC v. Beach Communications, Inc. — Competitive entry independently supports the ownership distinction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/beach_merits_1993-06-01.md), 1993-06-01. Thomas’s Part II-B; White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (7 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-01; FCC v. Beach Communications, Inc., for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [FCC v. Beach Communications, Inc., 508 U.S. 307 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#fcc-v-beach-communications-inc-508-us-307-1993); [FCC v. Beach Communications, Inc., 508 U.S. 307 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#fcc-v-beach-communications-inc-508-us-307-1993-1).

### Appropriate private placement and IDEA reimbursement

**Governing question:** When parents who reject an inadequate public program may obtain reimbursement for a private placement.

**Current rule:** When the public offer violates IDEA and the parents' unilateral private placement provides an education otherwise appropriate for the child under the Act, §1415(e)(2) permits equitable reimbursement even if the placement lacks state approval or does not satisfy every requirement in §1401(a)(18)'s definition of publicly provided free appropriate education. Parents act at financial risk and must establish both public inadequacy and private appropriateness. Neither parental choice nor progress alone guarantees relief. The court must consider relevant equitable circumstances and a reasonable amount; equity may support reduction or denial, and full reimbursement is inappropriate if private cost was unreasonable. No mandatory checklist replaces that discretion.

**Authority by component:**

- Private-placement eligibility, parental predicates and equitable amount: [Florence County School District Four v. Carter — Appropriate parental placement and equitable reimbursement](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Florence_County_School_District_Four_v_Carter_merits_1993-11-09.md#public-projection), 1993-11-09. O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg; O'Connor and those eight Justices supply nine votes for this rule and affirmance. Direct unanimous authority; no fractured-opinion inference.

**Present operation effective:** Florence County School District Four v. Carter, 1993-11-09.

**Holdings navigation:** [Appropriate parental placement and equitable reimbursement](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#appropriate-parental-placement-and-equitable-reimbursement).

### Violation-linked educational salary support

**Governing question:** Salary funding in a decree remedying established school segregation.

**Current rule:** A desegregation court may require instructional and noninstructional salary support reasonably necessary to deliver educational measures repairing established segregation-caused injury without finding low salaries themselves a separate constitutional wrong. Supported educational-repair and personnel-necessity findings supply the required connection; incidental voluntary attraction does not defeat that ground.

**Authority by component:**

- Violation-linked salary support is permissible on the preserved findings: [Violation-linked salary support is permissible on the preserved findings](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Missouri_v_Jenkins_merits_1995-06-12.md#public-projection), 1995-06-12. Souter's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Ginsburg and Breyer; five Justices adopt this holding and affirm both reviewed salary judgments.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-12; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** The rule supplies no right to general improvement, perpetual funding or reopening of a broader magnet foundation outside review. Partial withdrawal still requires the complete Freeman showing; low scores alone prove neither continuing injury nor completed compliance, and national-score parity is not indispensable.

**Holdings navigation:** [Violation-linked salary support is permissible on the preserved findings](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#violation-linked-salary-support-is-permissible-on-the-preserved-findings).

### Program-wide athletic opportunity and independent sex-classification review

**Governing question:** Title IX accommodation and constitutional review during public-university athletic restructuring.

**Current rule:** Title IX does not guarantee a particular varsity team or sport-for-sport counterparts. Lawful separate teams remain subject to equal opportunity. Effective accommodation may be shown by any one of substantial proportionality, a history and continuing responsive expansion for the underrepresented sex, or full and effective accommodation of that sex’s interests and abilities; other regulatory duties remain independently binding. A public university’s sex-conscious restructuring separately must serve an actual important objective and be substantially related to it. Genuine fiscal or programmatic change may preserve opportunities against demonstrated inequality, but ratios, budget pressure or attempted federal compliance alone are insufficient. Stereotypes, pretext and measures materially broader than demonstrated need are forbidden.

**Authority by component:**

- Program-wide participation opportunity: [Program-wide participation opportunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Kelley_v_Board_of_Trustees_of_the_University_of_Illinois_merits_1995-05-22.md#public-projection), 1995-05-22. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, Part I; Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer directly join this proposition.
- Independent constitutional scrutiny: [Independent constitutional scrutiny](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Kelley_v_Board_of_Trustees_of_the_University_of_Illinois_merits_1995-05-22.md#public-projection), 1995-05-22. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, Part II; Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer directly join this proposition.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-05-22; the component authorities identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Program-wide participation opportunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#program-wide-participation-opportunity); [Independent constitutional scrutiny](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#independent-constitutional-scrutiny).

## Apportionment and representation

### Constitutional review of interstate equal-proportions apportionment

**Governing question:** Whether allocation of a fixed House among States through equal proportions complies with Article I, §2 and the Fourteenth Amendment's population command.

**Current rule:** Courts may review the constitutional population challenge to equal proportions; Congress's responsibility to choose a method and the availability of more than one constitutional method do not make that challenge a political question. When allocating whole Representatives within a statutorily fixed House while respecting state boundaries and each State's constitutional minimum, Congress may choose the population-based relative representational equality supplied by equal proportions. Improvement in selected absolute population disparities does not establish invalidity where the proposed change worsens other legitimate measures of equality. This permits equal proportions without uniquely requiring it, validating every population-related formula or creating a percentage tolerance. Intrastate district-equality requirements remain distinct and unchanged.

**Authority by component:**
- Constitutional reviewability: [United States Department of Commerce v. Montana](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_Department_of_Commerce_v_Montana_merits_1992-03-31.md), March 31, 1992, “Congress's apportionment choice remains subject to constitutional review,” Part II.
- Permissible relative-equality method and limits: the same record, “Equal proportions satisfies the presented interstate population challenge,” Part III. Stevens's unanimous opinion controls both components.

**Present operation effective:** Montana, March 31, 1992.

**Open question:** The separate Article I, §§2 and 7 challenge to automatic decennial apportionment remains undecided, including its standing, justiciability, merits and relief. This construction does not constitutionalize a House of 435 or decide counting-rule challenges.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States Department of Commerce v. Montana, 503 U.S. 442 (1992) — Political questions and constitutional review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-department-of-commerce-v-montana-503-us-442-1992); [United States Department of Commerce v. Montana, 503 U.S. 442 (1992) — Interstate congressional apportionment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-department-of-commerce-v-montana-503-us-442-1992-3).

### Counting temporarily overseas federal personnel with enduring state ties

**Governing question:** The constitutional residence connection needed to include temporarily absent federal personnel in a State's apportionment population.

**Current rule:** The constitutional count may include federal personnel temporarily overseas whose enduring ties support residence in a particular State. Literal physical presence on census day is unnecessary; attribution without a supportable residence connection is not authorized. Interstate population counting does not import every intrastate district-equality rule.

**Authority by component:**

- Permissible residence connection: [Franklin v. Massachusetts, “Temporarily absent residents may be included in the constitutional count”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Franklin_v_Massachusetts_merits_1992-06-26.md), June 26, 1992; Stone's Part III joined by all eight Associates.

**Present operation effective:** *Franklin*, June 26, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** No dataset is universally required or approved. The record-specific APA explanation and methodology ruling adds no new standard of administrative review or tolerance for unequal districts within a State.

**Holdings navigation:** [Franklin v. Massachusetts, 505 U.S. 788 (1992) — Interstate congressional apportionment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#franklin-v-massachusetts-505-us-788-1992-1).

### Timely state redistricting and superseding state judgments

**Governing question:** What instructions govern timely state redistricting and superseding state judgments?

**Current rule:** Federal courts must defer redistricting while a State's legislature or judiciary is timely undertaking it unless the record shows no lawful plan will be produced in time for election. A reasonable deadline and retained jurisdiction may safeguard federal relief, but preference for another plan cannot displace or obstruct timely state work; every state appeal need not finish first. A final state judgment replacing the challenged plan must receive legal effect, not treatment as an optional proposal. The challenge to the old plan is moot unless it also reaches the replacement; old-plan allegations alone do not establish a new-plan violation. This supplies no broader party-preclusion rule.

**Authority by component:**

- 1. Timely state redistricting includes the State's courts: [Growe v. Emison — 1. Timely state redistricting includes the State's courts](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Growe_v_Emison_merits_1993-02-23.md), 1993-02-23. Scalia's opinion on state priority, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; all nine adopt this rule. The separate eight-Justice dismissal direction concerns this completed record, not the general availability of retained jurisdiction.
- 2. A superseding state judgment must receive legal effect: [Growe v. Emison — 2. A superseding state judgment must receive legal effect](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Growe_v_Emison_merits_1993-02-23.md), 1993-02-23. Scalia's opinion on the live-plan issue, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; all nine adopt the stated rule. No party-preclusion rule beyond that necessary legal effect is announced.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-02-23; Growe v. Emison, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Growe v. Emison, 507 U.S. 25 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#growe-v-emison-507-us-25-1993).

### Section 2 fragmentation, state districting choice and influence claims

**Governing question:** What instructions govern section 2 fragmentation, state districting choice and influence claims?

**Current rule:** A §2 single-member fragmentation claim requires a minority sufficiently large and geographically compact to form a district majority, political cohesion and majority bloc voting ordinarily sufficient to defeat its preferred candidate, absent special circumstances such as an unopposed minority candidate. These are necessary, not alone sufficient; totality remains. Distinct groups' numbers cannot replace missing cohesion or bloc-voting proof. Coalition cognizability and the population denominator remain open. A State need not prove remedial necessity before choosing majority-minority districts; challengers initially must show unequal opportunity caused by the arrangement under the totality, while a federal court may order such districts only as necessary to remedy an established violation. Independent constitutional limits remain. Assuming an influence claim exists, majority bloc voting remains necessary; modification or elimination of the district-majority prerequisite and influence cognizability remain unresolved. The necessary voting conditions do not require maximization of majority-minority districts. Unequal electoral opportunity under the totality does not require discriminatory purpose. Relevant proportionality compares the group’s share of the relevant population with the share of districts in which it has an effective opportunity to elect preferred candidates; its weight depends on the other evidence. It is neither a conclusive safe harbor nor a burden-shifting affirmative defense, and disproportionality alone does not establish liability. Nominal district labels and officeholders’ race do not replace demonstrated opportunity. No proportional-election entitlement, racial floor or ceiling, universal geographic scale or population denominator follows. Opportunity elsewhere cannot excuse proved local packing, fragmentation or another electoral barrier.

**Authority by component:**

- 3. Fragmentation claims require proof of the causal voting conditions: [Growe v. Emison — 3. Fragmentation claims require proof of the causal voting conditions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Growe_v_Emison_merits_1993-02-23.md), 1993-02-23. Scalia's statutory opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; all nine adopt the stated single-member extension and record application. No fractured-opinion or Marks inference is required.
- H1. Section 2 does not make a State prove remedial necessity before choosing majority-minority districts: [Voinovich v. Quilter — H1. Section 2 does not make a State prove remedial necessity before choosing majority-minority districts](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Voinovich_v_Quilter_merits_1993-03-02.md), 1993-03-02. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, Part II.B, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas. All nine adopt the complete proposition; it directly controls the rejection of the lower court's statutory prohibition and burden allocation.
- H2. The assumed influence-dilution claim fails for lack of the required majority bloc voting: [Voinovich v. Quilter — H2. The assumed influence-dilution claim fails for lack of the required majority bloc voting](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Voinovich_v_Quilter_merits_1993-03-02.md), 1993-03-02. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, Parts II.A and II.C, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas. All nine adopt this independently dispositive rejection of the presented Section 2 claim and each stated reservation.

- No maximization duty and proportionality within the totality: [Johnson v. De Grandy — 3. Necessary voting conditions do not require maximization; 4. Proportional opportunity is relevant evidence, not a safe harbor](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Johnson_v_De_Grandy_merits_1994-06-30.md#public-projection), 1994-06-30. Souter's Opinion of the Court, Part II, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Ginsburg; seven Justices directly adopt the complete necessary-versus-sufficient distinction and rejection of maximization. Thomas and Scalia's statutory noncoverage position is a different rationale and supplies no join. Souter's Opinion of the Court, Part III, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor and Ginsburg; six Justices directly adopt this contextual rule and its explanation. Kennedy separately accepts the exact limited propositions that proportionality is relevant, that the necessary conditions alone do not establish liability, and that proportionality supplies no safe harbor; his concurrence does not join Part III's full rationale or Parts IV and V's affirmative applications. No combined rationale under Marks is needed.

**Present operation effective:** Johnson v. De Grandy, 1994-06-30, for the maximization and proportionality limits; the earlier Growe and Voinovich components remain effective on their original dates.

### Justification of state legislative population deviations

**Governing question:** What instructions govern justification of state legislative population deviations?

**Current rule:** A maximum total deviation exceeding ten percent establishes a prima facie case requiring the State to justify its legislative plan. County preservation remains a possible rational policy, not automatic justification. Courts must assess whether the plan reasonably advances it, with substantiated connection to the deviations and consistency of implementation, and whether resulting disparities nevertheless exceed constitutional limits. Crossing ten percent supplies neither automatic invalidity nor approval of the offered policy.

**Authority by component:**

- H4. Population deviations above ten percent require a genuine justification inquiry: [Voinovich v. Quilter — H4. Population deviations above ten percent require a genuine justification inquiry](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Voinovich_v_Quilter_merits_1993-03-02.md), 1993-03-02. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, Part IV, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas. All nine adopt this standard and the population-only remand; neither the State's success nor a particular remedial map receives a controlling vote.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-02; Voinovich v. Quilter, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Voinovich v. Quilter, 507 U.S. 146 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#voinovich-v-quilter-507-us-146-1993).

### Territorial racial design and independently cognizable injury

**Governing question:** What instructions govern territorial racial design and independently cognizable injury?

**Current rule:** Deliberate or predominant racial consideration in territorial district design, including departures from ordinary districting criteria, does not alone state the design-only equal-protection claim without racial exclusion, intentional dilution, materially unequal participation, legally inferior civic membership or another independently cognizable constitutional deprivation. This substantive boundary is not heightened pleading, a universal downstream-loss requirement or an additional injury element for independent statutory claims. Other claims retain their own elements; no predominance scrutiny rule or general replacement for Croson or Metro Broadcasting follows. This remains so when race actually predominates and traditional criteria are subordinated. A cognizable status or participation injury can be complete in government’s own unequal treatment without a second governmental act, monetary loss or later electoral defeat; shared-electorate composition alone does not establish imposed political beliefs, racial ineligibility, inferior civic status or a representative legally responsible only to one race. Absence of a statutory command or prior adjudication requiring a particular district does not itself prohibit otherwise lawful voluntary consideration of racial conditions. Shape is evidence, not a necessary gateway or sufficient proof. Purpose findings receive ordinary clear-error review; their legal consequences remain separate.

**Authority by component:**

- Territorial racial design alone does not state the asserted deprivation: [Shaw v. Reno — Territorial racial design alone does not state the asserted deprivation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Shaw_v_Reno_merits_1993-06-28.md), 1993-06-28. Stone-Zsela’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, Souter (5 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.

- 3. Racial predominance alone does not establish the asserted constitutional violation: [3. Racial predominance alone does not establish the asserted constitutional violation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Miller_and_consolidated_merits_1995-06-29.md#public-projection), 1995-06-29. Stone, Part II, joined by Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer: five votes, applying Shaw.
- 4. The asserted sorting, status and representation inferences do not establish a distinct deprivation here: [4. The asserted sorting, status and representation inferences do not establish a distinct deprivation here](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Miller_and_consolidated_merits_1995-06-29.md#public-projection), 1995-06-29. Stone, Part III, joined by Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer: five votes.
- 5. Absence of federal compulsion does not itself prohibit voluntary inclusion: [5. Absence of federal compulsion does not itself prohibit voluntary inclusion](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Miller_and_consolidated_merits_1995-06-29.md#public-projection), 1995-06-29. Stone, Part IV, joined by Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: nine votes for the limited permission-versus-compulsion proposition. Only Stone, Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer conclude that this plan survives the asserted constitutional challenge.
- 6. District shape is evidence, not a threshold requirement: [6. District shape is evidence, not a threshold requirement](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Miller_and_consolidated_merits_1995-06-29.md#public-projection), 1995-06-29. Stone, Part V-A, joined by Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: nine votes at this evidentiary level; they disagree about the substantive effect of proved predominance.
- 7. The supported predominance finding is accepted without dictating its legal consequence: [7. The supported predominance finding is accepted without dictating its legal consequence](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Miller_and_consolidated_merits_1995-06-29.md#public-projection), 1995-06-29. Stone, Part V-B, joined by Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: nine votes on acceptance and review of the finding; the constitutional consequence divides 5–4 as stated above.
