### The designated channel for preclearance review

**Governing question:** What instructions govern the designated channel for preclearance review?

**Current rule:** A district court outside the District of Columbia cannot review the Attorney General's Voting Rights Act preclearance determination through a challenge to a State's plan; §14(b)'s designated channel and Morris's review restriction govern. Dismissal on that basis does not immunize the State's plan from an independently cognizable constitutional or statutory challenge.

**Authority by component:**

- Federal preclearance dismissal remains separate: [Shaw v. Reno — Federal preclearance dismissal remains separate](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Shaw_v_Reno_merits_1993-06-28.md), 1993-06-28. Stone-Zsela’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-28; Shaw v. Reno, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Shaw v. Reno, 509 U.S. 630 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#shaw-v-reno-509-us-630-1993); [Shaw v. Reno, 509 U.S. 630 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#shaw-v-reno-509-us-630-1993-1).

### Section 2 challenges to governing-body size

**Governing question:** What comparison permits a vote-dilution claim directed at the size of a local governing body.

**Current rule:** A local governing body’s size is within Voting Rights Act §2 coverage. A vote-dilution challenge must supply a reasonable comparison with independent legal and practical roots, not a number chosen solely to produce desired racial winners. No uniquely ideal size is required. Accepting the comparison does not establish liability: the claimant must still satisfy the applicable Gingles predicates and the statutory totality inquiry into unequal electoral opportunity. A remedy for an established violation must address the violation while considering genuine governmental interests; the decision supplies no automatic judicial entitlement to a particular number of offices.

**Authority by component:**

- Six-Justice statutory coverage: [Holder v. Hall — 1. The county's governing-body size is within Section 2's coverage](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Holder_v_Hall_merits_1994-06-30.md#public-projection), 1994-06-30. Blackmun's Opinion of the Court, Part I, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter and Ginsburg; six Justices directly adopt this threshold rule. O'Connor joins this part while opposing statutory liability; Kennedy reserves the affirmative coverage question, and Scalia and Thomas reject coverage of dilution.
- Five-Justice comparison, liability and remedy requirements: [Holder v. Hall — 2. A reasonable comparison must have legal and practical roots independent of a desired racial tally; 3. The accepted comparison does not replace the required proof of unequal opportunity; 4. The existing remand must provide a lawful remedy for the established violation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Holder_v_Hall_merits_1994-06-30.md#public-projection), 1994-06-30. Blackmun's Opinion of the Court, Part II, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Souter and Ginsburg; five Justices directly adopt the complete comparison rule and its application. O'Connor and Kennedy reject that comparison; Thomas and Scalia reject dilution coverage on a different, broader ground. Blackmun's Opinion of the Court, Part III, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Souter and Ginsburg; five Justices directly adopt the stated application and preservation of the existing proof requirements. There is no separate holding that the Gingles predicates alone establish liability or shift the ultimate statutory burden. Blackmun's Opinion of the Court, Part IV, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Souter and Ginsburg; the five-Justice judgment coalition adopts these remedial limits and the affirmance of the Eleventh Circuit's existing remedy remand.

**Present operation effective:** Holder v. Hall, 1994-06-30.

**Related current rules:** “Section 2 fragmentation, state districting choice and influence claims” supplies the related preconditions and the limits on maximization and proportionality.

### Exclusive congressional qualifications and ballot restrictions

**Governing question:** State ballot exclusions that add substantive personal qualifications for Congress.

**Current rule:** A State may not deny an otherwise constitutionally qualified congressional candidate ordinary ballot access because of prior congressional service when that restriction adds a substantive personal qualification. Article I’s exclusive qualifications cannot be supplemented indirectly through the ballot. Genuine time, place and manner administration remains distinct; no general power to require competitive elections or redesign Congress follows.

**Authority by component:**

- States cannot add congressional qualifications through the ordinary ballot: [States cannot add congressional qualifications through the ordinary ballot](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/US_Term_Limits_v_Thornton_Bryant_v_Hill_merits_1995-05-22.md#public-projection), 1995-05-22. Stevens's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer: six direct votes, in both consolidated dockets.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-05-22; the component authorities identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [States cannot add congressional qualifications through the ordinary ballot](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#states-cannot-add-congressional-qualifications-through-the-ordinary-ballot).

### Personal districting exposure and direct apportionment review

**Governing question:** Standing and the statutory appeal route for a substantial congressional-districting challenge.

**Current rule:** Voters personally subjected to the challenged electoral assignment may litigate asserted unequal treatment when their own exposure, causation and redressability are established, even if their substantive theory fails. Residence alone does not automatically establish standing. A substantial constitutional apportionment challenge adjudicated by the required three-judge district court through a merits denial of injunctive relief falls within §§2284 and 1253.

**Authority by component:**

- 1. Personal exposure and standing are distinct from success on the merits: [1. Personal exposure and standing are distinct from success on the merits](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Miller_and_consolidated_merits_1995-06-29.md#public-projection), 1995-06-29. Stone, Part I-A, joined by Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: nine votes.
- 2. The merits denial of an injunction is directly reviewable: [2. The merits denial of an injunction is directly reviewable](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Miller_and_consolidated_merits_1995-06-29.md#public-projection), 1995-06-29. Stone, Part I-B, joined by Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: nine votes.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-29; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** A nonmerits dismissal requires its own review-channel analysis; an available appeal establishes neither a cognizable merits deprivation nor entitlement to an injunction.
