Personal exposure and standing are distinct from success on the merits](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#personal-exposure-and-standing-are-distinct-from-success-on-the-merits); [2. The merits denial of an injunction is directly reviewable](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-merits-denial-of-an-injunction-is-directly-reviewable).

## Federalism

### Compulsory state regulation or ownership of private obligations

**Governing question:** An exercise of ordinary federal commerce power making a State choose between administering federal instructions for private actors and assuming their property and liabilities.

**Current rule:** Congress may not require an unwilling State to choose between regulating private waste generators under federal instructions and accepting ownership and possession of their waste with associated liability. Two unauthorized commands do not create a lawful option. State officials' advocacy for the federal statute neither enlarges congressional powers nor estops a noncompacting State from challenging the command. This differs from authorized monetary or access inducements leaving the State free to choose whether and how to govern, where consequences of refusal fall on private generators rather than transferring their responsibilities to the State.

**Authority by component:**
- Compulsory-ownership alternative and officials' assent: [New York v. United States, “5. Compulsory state ownership or regulation”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/New_York_v_United_States_merits_1992-06-19.md), June 19, 1992; O'Connor, joined by Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas (five), extending Hodel's legislative-compulsion boundary.
- Distinct actual monetary and access inducements: New York, “3. Monetary incentives” and “4. Access incentives,” June 19, 1992; O'Connor Parts III-A and III-B, supported by all eight Associate Justices including the author, applying the received spending and commerce doctrines without creating a numerical coercion threshold.

**Controlling construction:** The 1985 Act's general state-responsibility language states an objective implemented through the specified incentives; it supplies no independently enforceable command to regulate. This is the five-Justice holding in New York, “2. No independent command in the responsibility clause,” not a generic avoidance rule.

**Present operation effective:** New York v. United States, June 19, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Generally applicable regulation of state activity under Garcia and Baker remains distinct and undisturbed. Actual compact obligations, state market conduct, state-court duties and judicial enforcement of federal law are reserved. The decision establishes no general Guarantee Clause justiciability rule or power to direct other States to accept waste.

**Holdings navigation:** [New York v. United States, 505 U.S. 144 (1992) — Federal incentives and compulsory state administration](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#new-york-v-united-states-505-us-144-1992).

### Required commercial connection and distinct economic-class regulation

**Governing question:** The commerce connection required for the original school-zone possession prohibition and the distinct armed-vehicle-taking class.

**Current rule:** A stand-alone local school-zone possession prohibition without an enacted commerce connection cannot be sustained by a generalized chain through crime costs, education and productivity to the national economy. Courts cannot save the unambiguous original §922(q)(1)(A) offense by adding an unexpressed commerce element. This bounded rule does not categorically exclude noneconomic intrastate conduct or require formal legislative findings in every case. Regulation of an economic vehicle-taking class within an interstate motor-vehicle theft-prevention scheme supplies a distinct sufficient commerce ground for original §2119, whose enacted particular-vehicle interstate-history requirement must also be proved.

**Authority by component:**

- The original possession offense lacks the required commerce connection: [The original possession offense lacks the required commerce connection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/United_States_v_Lopez_merits_1995-04-26.md#public-projection), 1995-04-26. Justice Kennedy’s opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O’Connor, Scalia and Thomas: five direct votes for this bounded ground and affirmance. The Chief Justice’s additional six-route framework has no additional joins and is not the Court’s exhaustive test. No Marks synthesis is needed.
- The possession text cannot be saved by adding a new commerce element: [The possession text cannot be saved by adding a new commerce element](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/United_States_v_Lopez_merits_1995-04-26.md#public-projection), 1995-04-26. Kennedy’s opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Scalia and Thomas: five direct votes for the no-insertion disposition and the precise reservation. No Justice’s conditional willingness to consider the indictment issue is counted as a separate holding deciding it.
- The vehicle-taking economic class supplies the commerce connection: [The vehicle-taking economic class supplies the commerce connection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/United_States_v_Harris_merits_1995-04-27.md#public-projection), 1995-04-27. Justice O’Connor's opinion of the Court, joined on this ground by Justices Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer: eight direct votes. Chief Justice Stone-Zsela agrees with the judgment on a distinct instrumentality ground and does not join the economic-class rationale.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-04-27; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Remote movement alone and generalized social costs independently establish neither holding. Wickard and Perez retain their economic-class scope; appropriate inclusion of intrastate conduct necessary to a valid interstate regime remains possible. Garcia’s generally applicable regulation of state activity is undisturbed. No exhaustive six-route test, automatic vehicle-instrumentality rule, Second Amendment holding, amended-statute ruling or independent indictment ruling follows.

**Holdings navigation:** [The original possession offense lacks the required commerce connection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-original-possession-offense-lacks-the-required-commerce-connection); [The possession text cannot be saved by adding a new commerce element](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-possession-text-cannot-be-saved-by-adding-a-new-commerce-element); [The vehicle-taking economic class supplies the commerce connection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-vehicle-taking-economic-class-supplies-the-commerce-connection).

## Federal preemption

### Airline rates, routes and services under the express preemption clause

**Governing question:** State enforcement under 49 U.S.C. App. §1305(a)(1), including generally applicable laws and the general aviation remedies savings clause.

**Current rule:** Section 1305(a)(1) preempts state enforcement having a connection with or reference to covered air-carrier rates, routes or services. General applicability does not avoid that express relation, and §1506's general remedies clause preserves remedies outside the specific prohibition without undoing it. Fare-marketing requirements that directly regulate fare descriptions, restrictions, availability, surcharges and round-trip pricing fall within that construction. Independently, the identified package of compelled disclosures and advertising restrictions is preempted because it substantially constrains the marketing of restricted fares and significantly affects fares themselves. Directly covered requirements need not conflict with an existing federal rule; consistency alone does not defeat the express prohibition. State-imposed consumer-fraud standards directly governing earned travel awards and redemption restrictions fall within the prohibition despite a damages remedy or general applicability. Courts may, however, use ordinary contract law to identify and enforce the airline’s actual self-imposed undertaking and ordinary remedies. They may not enlarge or reform that bargain through external state policy. Formation, interpretation and generally applicable defenses remain distinct; the surviving contract route creates no federal contract code.

**Authority by component:**
- Connection-or-reference construction, savings and direct-reference ground: [Morales v. Trans World Airlines, Inc., “Express relation to rates and the savings clause”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Morales_v_Trans_World_Airlines_Inc_merits_1992-06-01.md), June 1, 1992; Scalia, joined by White, O'Connor, Kennedy and Thomas (five).
- Independent significant-effects ground at the identified fare-marketing scope: Morales, “Independently sufficient effect of these fare-marketing requirements,” June 1, 1992; same five Justices.

- State consumer-fraud standards governing these airline benefits are preempted: [State consumer-fraud standards governing these airline benefits are preempted](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/American_Airlines_Inc_v_Wolens_merits_1995-01-18.md#public-projection), 1995-01-18. Justice Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court, consumer-fraud portion, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Kennedy, Souter and Breyer. Five of the eight participating Justices directly adopt the rule and explanation. Justices O'Connor and Thomas also vote to preempt this claim on their separate broader ground; Justice Stevens dissents on this component.
- An airline's actual voluntary bargain remains enforceable: [An airline's actual voluntary bargain remains enforceable](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/American_Airlines_Inc_v_Wolens_merits_1995-01-18.md#public-projection), 1995-01-18. Justice Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court, contract portion, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, Kennedy, Souter and Breyer. Six of eight participants directly adopt this rationale; no synthesis of narrower separate opinions is required. Justices O'Connor and Thomas dissent on this component.

**Present operation effective:** American Airlines v. Wolens, January 18, 1995, resolves the voluntary-bargain boundary; Morales retains its earlier express-relation and significant-effects components.

**Exceptions and limits:** Tenuous, remote or peripheral connections, materially different nonprice duties and every indirect business cost are outside the resolved boundary. The effects holding is not a freestanding economic-effects threshold for all state laws. Federal unfair-practices authority is not an independent field-preemption holding. The decision creates neither general airline immunity nor complete displacement of ordinary state remedies.

**Holdings navigation:** [Morales v. Trans World Airlines, Inc., 504 U.S. 374 (1992) — Federal preemption](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#morales-v-trans-world-airlines-inc-504-us-374-1992); [State consumer-fraud standards governing these airline benefits are preempted](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#state-consumer-fraud-standards-governing-these-airline-benefits-are-preempted); [An airline's actual voluntary bargain remains enforceable](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#an-airlines-actual-voluntary-bargain-remains-enforceable).

### Occupational-safety supplementation without an approved state plan

**Governing question:** Whether OSH Act §18 automatically preempts a supplementary state occupational-safety standard on an issue covered by a federal standard when the State lacks an approved plan.

**Current rule:** Occupational overlap and lack of an approved state plan do not alone establish preemption. The provisions require examination for an actual basis of displacement; neither overlap nor a dual worker-and-public-safety purpose is conclusive. This is a limited negative holding: it supplies no complete replacement test for conflict, no universal permission whenever simultaneous compliance is physically possible and no approval of every compatible state license. Surviving pre-divergence conflict principles, including Hines and Florida Lime, remain operative on their own terms.

**Authority by component:**
- Rejection of automatic displacement and provision-specific inquiry: [Gade v. National Solid Wastes Management Association, “1. Section 18 does not automatically displace compatible supplementation”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Gade_v_National_Solid_Wastes_Management_Association_merits_1992-06-18.md), June 18, 1992; expressly shared proposition of Souter, Blackmun, Stevens, Thomas and Stone-Zsela (five). Souter's complete opinion has only four joins; no broader Marks rule is extracted.

**Present operation effective:** Gade v. National Solid Wastes Management Association, June 18, 1992.

**Unresolved interaction:** Morales construes a different express airline clause and supplies no general anti-duplication objective for §18. Gade does not replace the independent force of an actual statutory ceiling, exclusive arrangement, savings provision or proved incompatibility; the competing writings do not establish a unified new positive conflict test.

**Holdings navigation:** [Gade v. National Solid Wastes Management Association, 505 U.S. 88 (1992) — Federal preemption](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#gade-v-national-solid-wastes-management-association-505-us-88-1992).

### Cigarette-labeling preemption by statutory period and predicate duty

**Governing question:** Whether the 1965 or amended 1969 cigarette-labeling provisions displace common-law damages obligations.

**Current rule:** Original §5 of the 1965 Act bars positive enactments requiring particular cautionary statements on cigarette packages or in advertising; it does not displace the presented common-law damages claims. Federal warning compliance does not itself confer immunity.

For federally compliant packages, amended §5(b) reaches common-law duties imposing additional or clearer smoking-health warnings in advertising or promotion, including liability premised on advertising's neutralization of the mandated warning. Identify the duty necessary for liability rather than its pleading label. The clause preserves: voluntarily undertaken express warranties, because the manufacturer supplies their substantive promise; claims resting solely on testing, research or other nonpromotional conduct or an independently actionable nonpromotional disclosure duty; and general intentional material falsehood, including advertising falsehood, when liability does not prescribe additional warning content or punish warning neutralization. A conspiracy claim survives only insofar as its predicate deceptive duty survives. Advertising as evidence does not necessarily make the underlying duty promotional, but assigning consumer warnings to an alternative channel does not by itself establish an independent duty.

**Authority by component:**

- Original-Act construction: [Cipollone v. Liggett Group, Inc., “The 1965 Act does not displace these common-law damages claims”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Cipollone_v_Liggett_Group_Inc_merits_1992-06-24.md), June 24, 1992; Stevens, Stone, White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter.
- Covered warning and neutralization duties under the successor clause: *Cipollone*, “The successor clause reaches covered warning and warning-neutralization duties”; Stevens, Stone, White and O'Connor plus Scalia and Thomas expressly support that operative proposition.
- Warranty, independent nonpromotional duties, intentional falsehood and surviving conspiracy: *Cipollone*, “Voluntarily undertaken express warranties survive,” “Independently actionable nonpromotional conduct remains outside the clause,” and “General intentional falsehood and surviving concealment conspiracies are not barred.” The record identifies Stevens's four-member narrower grounds as controlling under Marks, with Blackmun, Kennedy and Souter's broader no-common-law-preemption grounds; each surviving category is a logical subset producing the same result, with seven judgment supporters. No whole amended-Act opinion or blanket common-law exemption is adopted.

**Present operation effective:** *Cipollone*, June 24, 1992, construing each Act for its own statutory period.

**Exceptions and limits:** Preserved claims still require an actual state-law duty and all applicable elements. No agency-disclosure duty, design-defect ruling, later-amendment construction, cigarette field immunity or generic bar on independently established actual-conflict preemption is supplied. Gade's negative overlap rule and Morales's separate airline language retain their own scopes.

**Holdings navigation:** [Cipollone v. Liggett Group, Inc., 505 U.S. 504 (1992) — Federal preemption](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#cipollone-v-liggett-group-inc-505-us-504-1992).

### ERISA plan commands and indirect economic effects

**Governing question:** When state benefit mandates or indirect price effects relate to covered plans under §514(a).

**Current rule:** ERISA §514(a) preempts a replacement-coverage obligation insofar as it uses a covered plan’s benefit content as the mandatory legal measure, even if replacement is separately administered through exempt workers’ compensation arrangements. Requirements measured solely by exempt governmental or church plans remain outside that holding. By contrast, the reviewed commercial-insurer hospital differentials and conditional HMO assessment are not preempted merely because price or participation effects influence plan choices: the measures neither use ERISA status or plan benefits as their legal benchmark nor command benefits, eligibility, uniform administration or an alternative enforcement remedy. Ordinary indirect economic influence is not itself such a command.

**Authority by component:**

- Covered-plan content as a mandatory legal measure: [District of Columbia v. Greater Washington Board of Trade — Covered-plan content as a mandatory legal measure](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/District_of_Columbia_v_Greater_Washington_Board_of_Trade_merits_1992-12-14.md), 1992-12-14. Thomas’s opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter; eight Justices adopt the same operative rule.

- The reviewed 13% hospital differential is not preempted: [The reviewed 13% hospital differential is not preempted](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/New_York_State_Conference_of_Blue_Cross_Blue_Shield_Plans_v_Travelers_Insurance_Co_merits_1995-04-26.md#public-projection), 1995-04-26. Justice Souter’s opinion of the Court, joined on this proposition by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: nine direct votes. The direct self-funded application is outside this merits holding.
- The additional commercial-insurer 11% charge is not preempted: [The additional commercial-insurer 11% charge is not preempted](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/New_York_State_Conference_of_Blue_Cross_Blue_Shield_Plans_v_Travelers_Insurance_Co_merits_1995-04-26.md#public-projection), 1995-04-26. Souter’s Court opinion and the same nine named Justices directly adopt this distinct application. It is not a holding that the additional 11% applies to every payor or that the reproduced period fixes liability under a later extension.
- The conditional HMO assessment is not preempted: [The conditional HMO assessment is not preempted](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/New_York_State_Conference_of_Blue_Cross_Blue_Shield_Plans_v_Travelers_Insurance_Co_merits_1995-04-26.md#public-projection), 1995-04-26. Souter’s Court opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: nine direct votes for this separately analyzed assessment. The decision supplies no insurance-saving holding for the distinct 9% measure.

**Present operation effective:** Travelers, April 26, 1995, clarifies the previously reserved indirect-economic-effects boundary; Board of Trade, December 14, 1992, continues to govern covered-plan benchmarks.

**Holdings navigation:** [District of Columbia v. Greater Washington Board of Trade, 506 U.S. 125 (1992)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#district-of-columbia-v-greater-washington-board-of-trade-506-us-125-1992); [The reviewed 13% hospital differential is not preempted](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-reviewed-13-hospital-differential-is-not-preempted); [The additional commercial-insurer 11% charge is not preempted](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-additional-commercial-insurer-11-charge-is-not-preempted); [The conditional HMO assessment is not preempted](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-conditional-hmo-assessment-is-not-preempted).

**Exceptions and limits:** No rate or combined-charge safe harbor is created. The possibility of effects sufficiently acute to operate as a substantive coverage command or effectively restrict insurer choice remains open. This is a rule about the ERISA connection, not a new hospital-payment code or permission to disregard a measure’s enacted conditions.

**Open question:** Direct self-funded application, insurance saving and deemer questions remain unresolved. The insurer/HMO rulings do not decide them. Laws specifically referring to covered plans, mandating benefits, interfering with uniform administration or supplying alternative enforcement remain outside the nonpreemption holding.

### Proprietary public purchases and labor preemption

**Governing question:** What instructions govern proprietary public purchases and labor preemption?

**Current rule:** Garmon and Machinists preemption do not bar a project-specific contracting condition when the State acts for its proprietary interests as owner and purchaser, analogous private conduct is lawful, and Congress has not expressly or impliedly displaced the proprietary conduct. Public ownership or a procurement label alone is insufficient; purchasing cannot regulate unrelated conduct or impose general labor policy. The holding creates no general NLRA-employer status for a State. Distinct §§8(e) and 8(f) limits remain, including §8(f)'s preservation of employee elections under §§9(c) and 9(e).

**Authority by component:**

- Genuine proprietary project purchases are not state labor regulation: [Building & Construction Trades Council v. Associated Builders & Contractors (Boston Harbor) — Genuine proprietary project purchases are not state labor regulation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/building-construction-trades-v-associated-builders-boston-harbor-merits-1993-03-08.md), 1993-03-08. Blackmun's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas: unanimous controlling authority in both dockets.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-08; Building & Construction Trades Council v. Associated Builders & Contractors (Boston Harbor), for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Building & Construction Trades Council v. Associated Builders & Contractors (Boston Harbor), 507 U.S. 218 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#building--construction-trades-council-v-associated-builders--contractors-boston-harbor-507-us-218-1993).

### Federal railroad coverage and separate warning and speed duties

**Governing question:** What instructions govern federal railroad coverage and separate warning and speed duties?

**Current rule:** FRSA §434 reaches common-law railroad duties only when operative federal rules substantially subsume the relevant subject, rather than merely touch it. Additional or more stringent covered requirements survive only when necessary to reduce an essentially local safety hazard, compatible with federal requirements and not an undue interstate-commerce burden. General crossing planning and MUTCD compliance alone do not preempt warning negligence. Sections 646.214(b)(3)-(4) displace independent device-selection duties when their federally participating installation predicates apply; abandoned gates, redirected funds or nonwarning circuitry do not alone establish them. Under (b)(3)(i), gates with flashing lights are required for multiple main-line tracks; nearby tracks allowing one train to obscure another; high speed with limited sight distance or moderately high highway and rail traffic; high vehicular or train volume, substantial schoolbus or hazardous-material traffic, unusually restricted sight distance, continuing accidents or combinations; or diagnostic-team recommendation. Under (b)(3)(ii), a diagnostic-team justification that gates are inappropriate permits FHWA to find those requirements inapplicable, without commanding omission. Otherwise (b)(4) requires FHWA approval of device choice. The separate (b)(2) highway-project route is unadjudicated. Track-class speed rules cover ordinary time-and-place excessive-speed negligence despite compliance with the federal maximum; local facts alone do not invoke the saving clause. A duty to slow or stop for a specific individual hazard remains unresolved.

**Authority by component:**

- Coverage requires substantial subsumption of the actual duty: [CSX Transportation, Inc. v. Easterwood — Coverage requires substantial subsumption of the actual duty](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/easterwood_merits_1993-04-21.md), 1993-04-21. White’s Opinion of the Court, I; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.
- Warning-device preemption requires the operative project predicates: [CSX Transportation, Inc. v. Easterwood — Warning-device preemption requires the operative project predicates](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/easterwood_merits_1993-04-21.md), 1993-04-21. White’s Opinion of the Court, II; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.
- The ordinary excessive-speed claim is covered: [CSX Transportation, Inc. v. Easterwood — The ordinary excessive-speed claim is covered](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/easterwood_merits_1993-04-21.md), 1993-04-21. White’s Opinion of the Court, III–IV; White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy (6 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-04-21; CSX Transportation, Inc. v. Easterwood, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [CSX Transportation, Inc. v. Easterwood, 507 U.S. 658 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#csx-transportation-inc-v-easterwood-507-us-658-1993).

### Insurance liquidation priorities against federal priority

**Governing question:** What instructions govern insurance liquidation priorities against federal priority?

**Current rule:** Where the federal statute does not specifically relate to insurance, McCarran-Ferguson §1012(b)'s first clause protects state liquidation priority to the extent it regulates fulfillment of policyholder obligations. Administration reasonably necessary to implement those payments shares that protection. Insurance labeling alone is insufficient: ordinary employee wages and general-creditor preferences yield to §3713, in the state-insurer insolvency/act-of-bankruptcy setting; §3713(a)(2) excludes Title 11 cases. Expenses merely labeled administrative acquire no immunity. The second-clause antitrust exemption and §1013(b) boycott provision remain separate.

**Authority by component:**

- The first clause protects the policy-performance priority: [United States Department of Treasury v. Fabe — The first clause protects the policy-performance priority](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/fabe_merits_1993-06-11.md), 1993-06-11. Blackmun’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor (5 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.
- Necessary administration shares the protected function: [United States Department of Treasury v. Fabe — Necessary administration shares the protected function](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/fabe_merits_1993-06-11.md), 1993-06-11. Blackmun’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor (5 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.
- Ordinary creditor preferences remain subject to federal priority: [United States Department of Treasury v. Fabe — Ordinary creditor preferences remain subject to federal priority](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/fabe_merits_1993-06-11.md), 1993-06-11. Blackmun’s Opinion of the Court; the four dissenters agree in this disposition on their broader ground; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor (5 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-11; United States Department of Treasury v. Fabe, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States Department of Treasury v. Fabe, 508 U.S. 491 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-department-of-treasury-v-fabe-508-us-491-1993).

### Published tariff cancellation and mileage-based undercharges

**Governing question:** Whether a carrier can collect undercharges using a tariff whose essential mileage incorporation was publicly canceled.

**Current rule:** When a carrier’s rate depends on incorporated mileage material and its required participation is canceled through an effective public tariff filing, the incomplete combination supplies no enforceable rate for later transportation unless a legally sufficient replacement applies. This is not a private lapse that leaves a filed rate effective until properly revised: the published cancellation is itself operative. Bankruptcy does not restore the canceled tariff. The ruling does not defeat charges for transportation before cancellation or authorize equitable disregard of an otherwise effective filed rate.

**Authority by component:**

- Effective public cancellation of an essential tariff component: [Security Services, Inc. v. Kmart Corp — Published cancellation of essential mileage participation defeats these undercharges](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Security_Services_v_Kmart_merits_1994-05-16.md#public-projection), 1994-05-16. Souter's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, and Kennedy; seven Justices adopt the rule and affirmance. Thomas and Ginsburg separately dissent from the statutory-authority and remedy conclusions.

**Present operation effective:** Security Services, Inc. v. Kmart Corp, 1994-05-16.

**Holdings navigation:** [Published cancellation of essential mileage participation defeats these undercharges](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#published-cancellation-of-essential-mileage-participation-defeats-these-undercharges).

### Independent state rights and actual labor-contract interpretation

**Governing question:** When a collective-bargaining agreement displaces an otherwise independent state-law claim under §301 or the Railway Labor Act.

**Current rule:** A state claim enforcing an independent, nonnegotiable right is not preempted merely because the workplace has a collective-bargaining agreement, the agreement supplies an undisputed wage figure, the same facts support a contract grievance, or a defense refers to the agreement. The claim’s actual legal elements and necessary interpretation govern, not its label. Section 301 does not displace a wage-penalty claim requiring only undisputed wage arithmetic. Under the Railway Labor Act, a claim turning on conduct, motive or another purely factual question likewise remains independent unless resolving it requires interpretation of disputed contract terms. Contractual disputes remain subject to their prescribed processes; parallel arbitration does not itself remove independent rights.

**Authority by component:**

- Section 301 and undisputed wage arithmetic: [Livadas v. Bradshaw — An independent wage-penalty claim is not preempted by §301](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Livadas_v_Bradshaw_merits_1994-06-13.md#public-projection), 1994-06-13. Stevens's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; all nine adopt this antecedent holding.
- Railway Labor Act and independent factual inquiry: [Hawaiian Airlines, Inc. v. Norris — Independent state rights remain enforceable without interpreting the labor agreement](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Hawaiian_Airlines_v_Norris_merits_1994-06-20.md#public-projection), 1994-06-20. Justice Stevens's opinion for a unanimous Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg. All nine adopt the complete rule, application, precedent treatment, and bounded affirmance.

**Present operation effective:** Hawaiian Airlines, Inc. v. Norris, 1994-06-20.

**Holdings navigation:** [An independent wage-penalty claim is not preempted by §301](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#an-independent-wage-penalty-claim-is-not-preempted-by-301); [Independent state rights remain enforceable without interpreting the labor agreement](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#independent-state-rights-remain-enforceable-without-interpreting-the-labor-agreement).

### Motor-vehicle standards and independent actual conflict

**Governing question:** Preemption of state defect claims when no applicable federal safety standard remains in effect.

**Current rule:** Section 1392(d)’s express restriction requires an in-effect federal motor-vehicle standard governing the same aspect of performance. Judicial invalidation of a standard and absence of an operative requirement do not themselves establish impossibility or an affirmative federal no-regulation objective. An express clause does not categorically foreclose proof of an independent actual conflict with operative federal law or a demonstrated federal objective, and a savings clause is not immunity from every actual conflict.

**Authority by component:**

- No in-effect federal standard supplies the express-preemption predicate here: [No in-effect federal standard supplies the express-preemption predicate here](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Freightliner_Corp_v_Myrick_merits_1995-04-18.md#public-projection), 1995-04-18. Justice Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Breyer: eight direct rationale votes. Justice Scalia concurs only in the judgment and gives no separate explanation.
- Judicial invalidation alone establishes no actual federal conflict: [Judicial invalidation alone establishes no actual federal conflict](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Freightliner_Corp_v_Myrick_merits_1995-04-18.md#public-projection), 1995-04-18. Justice Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Breyer: eight direct rationale votes. Scalia's judgment-only agreement supplies no additional rationale vote.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-04-18; the component authorities identified above.

**Open question:** Whether common-law duties themselves are safety standards under §1392(d) is not decided. Valid operative standards and proved affirmative no-regulation policies require their own analysis.

**Holdings navigation:** [No in-effect federal standard supplies the express-preemption predicate here](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#no-in-effect-federal-standard-supplies-the-express-preemption-predicate-here); [Judicial invalidation alone establishes no actual federal conflict](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#judicial-invalidation-alone-establishes-no-actual-federal-conflict).

## Immigration

### Administrative EAJA coverage of deportation hearings

**Governing question:** Whether an INA deportation hearing qualifies as an adjudication under §554 for administrative fees under EAJA §504.

**Current rule:** A deportation hearing governed by the INA's exclusive procedure instead of 5 U.S.C. §554 is outside EAJA §504's administrative fee authorization, even if it resembles a formal APA adjudication. Actual statutory governance controls; functional similarity, agency regulations, and remedial purpose do not replace the enacted cross-reference. Failure of coverage makes it unnecessary to decide substantial justification.

**Authority by component:**

- Actual §554 governance and exclusion of the independent INA proceeding: [Ardestani v. INS — Law of the decision, Holding and operative rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Ardestani_v_INS_merits_1991-12-10.md), December 10, 1991; O'Connor's opinion joined by Stone, White, Scalia, Kennedy, and Souter.

**Present operation effective:** *Ardestani*, December 10, 1991.

**Exceptions and limits:** This construction does not restrict a qualifying judicial EAJA action or administrative work integral to it; that distinct predicate must be established independently.

**Holdings navigation:** [Ardestani v. INS, 502 U.S. 129 (1991) — Administrative fee awards](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#ardestani-v-ins-502-us-129-1991).

### Unauthorized-employment conditions on immigration release

**Governing question:** Whether INA §242(a) permits a general unauthorized-work condition on release pending deportation proceedings.

**Current rule:** Section 242(a) authorizes a generally applicable condition forbidding unauthorized employment where the regulation excludes authorized workers and preserves individualized status determinations and discretionary relief. Officials must determine existing work authorization, address colorable citizenship or status claims, and consider the regulatory avenue for discretionary relief. Authority to impose release conditions is not categorically confined to securing physical appearance, but remains connected to the statutory immigration scheme.

**Authority by component:**

- Narrowed condition and individual-status safeguards: [INS v. National Center for Immigrants' Rights — Law of the decision, Holding and operative rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/INS_v_NCIR_merits_1991-12-16.md), December 16, 1991; Stevens's unanimous opinion.

**Present operation effective:** *NCIR*, December 16, 1991.

**Open question:** Constitutional objections and misapplication when represented procedures are unavailable or misused remain unresolved. The holding does not authorize interference with lawful work or presume disputed deportability.


**Exceptions and limits:** This authorization rule preserves the existing individualized relief avenue; it does not define eligibility or decision criteria for discretionary work-authorization relief. No entitlement to such relief is inferred.

**Holdings navigation:** [INS v. National Center for Immigrants' Rights, Inc., 502 U.S. 183 (1991) — Release conditions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#ins-v-national-center-for-immigrants-rights-inc-502-us-183-1991).

### Political-opinion nexus in coercive recruitment claims

**Governing question:** Whether coercive recruitment by a politically motivated organization establishes persecution on account of political opinion.

**Current rule:** The applicant-specific protected reason for persecution must be supported by evidence; the organization's political agenda and the applicant's refusal do not create a categorical presumption of the required nexus. Direct evidence of motive is unnecessary, and circumstantial evidence may establish it. Under §1105a(a)(4), a reviewing court may displace a supported Board factual determination only when the record compels the contrary finding, rather than merely allowing it. This statutory nexus inquiry does not merge asylum's well-founded-fear threshold with withholding's higher threshold or make every reopening ground subject to one review standard.

**Authority by component:**

- Applicant-specific nexus and direct or circumstantial motive evidence: [INS v. Elias-Zacarias — Political purpose of the recruiting group does not itself establish nexus](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/INS_v_Elias_Zacarias_merits_1992-01-22.md), January 22, 1992.
- Review of the supported nexus determination and limited reopening reach: [Elias-Zacarias — The existing record does not warrant overturning the Board](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/INS_v_Elias_Zacarias_merits_1992-01-22.md), January 22, 1992. Scalia's opinion joined by White, Kennedy, Souter, and Thomas controls both; Stone's agreement with rejecting the categorical premise does not join the opinion.

**Present operation effective:** *Elias-Zacarias*, January 22, 1992.

**Open question:** Political neutrality is not categorically excluded, but the Court does not adopt an unqualified rule resolving imputed political opinion. No new substantive reopening rule is supplied by the earlier fractured *Doherty* dispositions.

**Holdings navigation:** [INS v. Elias-Zacarias, 502 U.S. 478 (1992) — Persecution and review of agency findings](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#ins-v-elias-zacarias-502-us-478-1992).

### Juvenile release avenues and facial custody review

**Governing question:** What instructions govern juvenile release avenues and facial custody review?

**Current rule:** Section 1252(a)(1) permits temporary juvenile custody under §242.24 without mandatory placement with every willing unrelated adult. Under (b)(1), release is required in preference order to a nondetained parent, guardian or adult sibling, aunt, uncle or grandparent unless detention is necessary for timely appearance or the juvenile's or others' safety. If the only listed custodian is detained, (b)(2) permits discretionary case-by-case simultaneous release. Under (b)(3), a parent or guardian detained or abroad may designate a capable willing custodian by sworn affidavit before an immigration or consular officer; the custodian must agree to care and ensure future appearances. Other agreeing adults qualify under (b)(4) only in unusual and compelling circumstances at the district director's or chief patrol agent's discretion. The family classifications and temporary nonpunitive care survive the facial liberty and equality challenges, subject to the binding conditions agreement. Examination, notice and requested immigration-judge review are not facially inadequate on the record establishing no general inability to invoke them. Section 1252(a)(1) permits habeas upon a conclusive showing that deportability is not being determined with reasonable dispatch warranted by the facts. There is no thirty-day cap or categorical approval of ineffective notice, delayed or denied review, unconstitutional individual confinement or systemic implementation defects; voluntary-departure safeguards are not universally adjudicated.

**Authority by component:**

- 1. Statutory authority for the juvenile release regulation: [Reno v. Flores — 1. Statutory authority for the juvenile release regulation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Reno_v_Flores_merits_1993-03-23.md), 1993-03-23. Scalia's opinion for the Court, joined by White, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas: six votes for this rule. Stone-Zsela concurs in the judgment on a narrower statutory and facial-review ground.
- 2. The facial substantive-due-process challenge fails: [Reno v. Flores — 2. The facial substantive-due-process challenge fails](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Reno_v_Flores_merits_1993-03-23.md), 1993-03-23. Scalia's Court opinion, joined by White, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas: six. O'Connor and Souter also explain the substantial liberty interest in avoiding institutional confinement in a separate concurrence; Stone-Zsela does not join this formulation.
- 3. Request-based review is not facially inadequate: [Reno v. Flores — 3. Request-based review is not facially inadequate](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Reno_v_Flores_merits_1993-03-23.md), 1993-03-23. Scalia's Court opinion, joined by White, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas: six. Stone-Zsela agrees with setting aside the universal automatic-hearing remedy but reserves a distinct Mathews-based implementation inquiry.
- 4. The challenged classifications do not establish facial unequal treatment: [Reno v. Flores — 4. The challenged classifications do not establish facial unequal treatment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Reno_v_Flores_merits_1993-03-23.md), 1993-03-23. Scalia's Court opinion, joined by White, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas: six. Stone-Zsela concurs in the judgment on this component and adds no ground.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-23; Reno v. Flores, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Reno v. Flores, 507 U.S. 292 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#reno-v-flores-507-us-292-1993).

### Legalization review channels and concrete application barriers

**Governing question:** What instructions govern legalization review channels and concrete application barriers?

**Current rule:** Section 1255a(f) channels individual legalization determinations but does not categorically bar district-court challenges to generally applicable regulations where no individual determination supplies effective review. A label alone establishes neither ripeness nor district review of an individual denial. Actual refusal to accept a timely application under the challenged rule supplies concrete application where front-desking blocks the individual channel. Mere awareness of an adverse rule is insufficient; further facts may establish a comparable inability substantially caused by front-desking without personal rejection. Processing a timely tender differs from extending a deadline; no automatic extension or general equitable-tolling resolution follows.

**Authority by component:**

- The individual-determination channel does not foreclose all rule challenges: [Reno v. Catholic Social Services, Inc. — The individual-determination channel does not foreclose all rule challenges](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Reno_v_Catholic_Social_Services_Inc_merits_1993-06-18.md), 1993-06-18. Souter’s Opinion of the Court, The individual-determination channel does not foreclose all rule challenges portion; Stone-Zsela, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (5 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.
- A concrete application barrier must be established: [Reno v. Catholic Social Services, Inc. — A concrete application barrier must be established](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Reno_v_Catholic_Social_Services_Inc_merits_1993-06-18.md), 1993-06-18. Souter’s Opinion of the Court, A concrete application barrier must be established portion; Stone-Zsela, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (5 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-18; Reno v. Catholic Social Services, Inc., for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Reno v. Catholic Social Services, Inc., 509 U.S. 43 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#reno-v-catholic-social-services-inc-509-us-43-1993).

### High-seas repatriation under the asserted statutory and treaty restraints

**Governing question:** What instructions govern high-seas repatriation under the asserted statutory and treaty restraints?

**Current rule:** Section 243(h)'s restriction on deporting or returning an alien to threatened persecution does not govern the challenged coordinated interdiction and repatriation beyond the territorial sea; removal of the words within the United States in 1980 does not extend that domestic provision to this operation. Independently, Refugee Convention Article 33 through the 1967 Protocol does not extend its return prohibition to this high-seas operation. Article 33(2) withholds benefit where reasonable grounds identify a danger to the security of the country of presence, or final conviction of a particularly serious crime establishes danger to its community. The territorial holding leaves independent enforceability unresolved and creates no general executive exemption from law.

**Authority by component:**

- Section 243(h) does not govern this offshore return operation: [Sale v. Haitian Centers Council — Section 243(h) does not govern this offshore return operation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Sale_v_Haitian_Centers_Council_merits_1993-06-21.md), 1993-06-21. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, Section 243(h) does not govern this offshore return operation portion; White, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (7 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.
- Article 33 does not independently limit the high-seas operation: [Sale v. Haitian Centers Council — Article 33 does not independently limit the high-seas operation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Sale_v_Haitian_Centers_Council_merits_1993-06-21.md), 1993-06-21. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, Article 33 does not independently limit the high-seas operation portion; White, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (7 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-21; Sale v. Haitian Centers Council, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Sale v. Haitian Centers Council, 509 U.S. 155 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#sale-v-haitian-centers-council-509-us-155-1993).

### Separate review periods for deportation and reconsideration

**Governing question:** Finality and consolidation under former 8 U.S.C. §1105a.

**Current rule:** A motion to reopen or reconsider does not postpone the original deportation order’s finality or judicial-review filing period. A petition directed to that order must satisfy its own statutory period. A separately reviewable reconsideration disposition has its own period, and §1105a(a)(6) requires consolidation when both reviews are properly pending. Optional reconsideration is not a prerequisite to judicial review, and the consolidation rule does not make every reconsideration refusal reviewable.

**Authority by component:**

- Reconsideration does not postpone the original-order filing period under this INA scheme: [Reconsideration does not postpone the original-order filing period under this INA scheme](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Stone_v_Immigration_and_Naturalization_Service_merits_1995-04-19.md#public-projection), 1995-04-19. Justice Kennedy’s opinion of the Court, joined by Stevens, Scalia, Thomas and Ginsburg: five direct votes for the same statutory ground. The four dissenters adopt a different construction, not a narrower version of this rule.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-04-19; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Filing finality, agency enforceability, exhaustion and administrative or judicial stays remain distinct. No general equitable-tolling rule or immigration merits rule is decided; Ibarra’s criminal-reconsideration and Darby’s APA-exhaustion instructions remain in their own settings.

**Holdings navigation:** [Reconsideration does not postpone the original-order filing period under this INA scheme](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#reconsideration-does-not-postpone-the-original-order-filing-period-under-this-ina-scheme).

## Labor, employment, and maritime law

### Jones Act seaman status and LHWCA overlap

**Governing question:** The substantial vessel connection for seaman status and the effect of longshore classification or voluntary benefits.

**Current rule:** Employment in an occupation listed in the LHWCA does not foreclose seaman status because the LHWCA excludes a vessel's master or crew. Receipt of voluntary LHWCA benefits without a final status adjudication does not preclude a Jones Act suit, and overlapping recovery remains subject to statutory credit. The worker must independently establish the required substantial employment-related connection to a vessel in navigation and contribution to the vessel's function; traditional navigational duties are unnecessary. Status requires an employment-related connection to a vessel in navigation or identifiable fleet substantial in both duration and nature. Actual assignment governs rather than a job label or isolated moment; a genuine change of essential assignment is assessed on the new position rather than mechanically diluted by earlier shoreside service. For ordinary arrangements, about thirty percent vessel-connected service is a rule of thumb, not a rigid cutoff or jurisdictional condition, with justified departures possible. Courts may decide status as law only when properly assessed facts permit one reasonable conclusion. Temporary repair does not invariably remove a vessel from navigation, although major reconstruction can on a sufficient record. Withdrawn-vessel work is not automatically qualifying service but can illuminate the actual employment relationship; later work does not retroactively create status.

**Authority by component:**

- Occupational classification, unadjudicated-benefits nonpreclusion, and credit: [Southwest Marine v. Gizoni — Law of the decision, Holding and operative rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Southwest_Marine_v_Gizoni_merits_1991-12-04.md), December 4, 1991; White's unanimous participating opinion, applying the preserved *Wilander* status requirements.

- Contribution and substantial employment-related vessel connection: [Contribution and substantial employment-related vessel connection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Chandris_Inc_v_Latsis_merits_1995-06-14.md#public-projection), 1995-06-14. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined at this level by Stone-Zsela, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg; six support the general connection rule. Stevens, Thomas and Breyer concur only in the judgment on their different actual-crew approach.
- A nonabsolute temporal guide for ordinary assignments: [A nonabsolute temporal guide for ordinary assignments](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Chandris_Inc_v_Latsis_merits_1995-06-14.md#public-projection), 1995-06-14. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, this temporal-guide discussion, joined by Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg; five directly support it. Stone-Zsela does not join this discussion. No judgment-concurrence vote is aggregated into its authority.
- The preserved categorical drydock exclusion requires a new trial: [The preserved categorical drydock exclusion requires a new trial](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Chandris_Inc_v_Latsis_merits_1995-06-14.md#public-projection), 1995-06-14. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined on this preserved instructional holding by Stone-Zsela, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg; six support this rationale. The three judgment concurrences supply no full-framework join.

**Present operation effective:** Chandris, June 14, 1995, for the substantial-nature-and-duration formulation, qualified temporal guide and repair boundaries; Gizoni retains its classification, voluntary-benefits and credit rules.

**Open question:** The preclusive effect of a final award, settlement or ultimate status determination remains unresolved. The thirty-percent discussion has five direct joins; the general connection rule has six and does not rest on judgment concurrences.

**Holdings navigation:** [Southwest Marine, Inc. v. Gizoni, 502 U.S. 81 (1991) — Seaman status and compensation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#southwest-marine-inc-v-gizoni-502-us-81-1991); [Contribution and substantial employment-related vessel connection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#contribution-and-substantial-employment-related-vessel-connection); [A nonabsolute temporal guide for ordinary assignments](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#a-nonabsolute-temporal-guide-for-ordinary-assignments); [The preserved categorical drydock exclusion requires a new trial](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-preserved-categorical-drydock-exclusion-requires-a-new-trial).

### Member enforcement of interunion contracts under §301

**Governing question:** Whether an individual union member may sue for violation of a qualifying international-local union constitution.

**Current rule:** LMRA §301 permits an individual member who is a beneficiary of a qualifying contract between labor organizations to sue for its violation. An international constitution binding the international and its local remains an interunion contract when a member invokes it. The plaintiff must satisfy ordinary contract predicates; the statute does not federalize every internal union dispute or classify standalone local bylaws as covered contracts.

**Authority by component:**

- Beneficiary standing and continued interunion-contract character: [Wooddell v. International Brotherhood of Electrical Workers, Local 71 — Section 301 plaintiff and contract coverage](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Wooddell_v_IBEW_Local_71_merits_1991-12-04.md), December 4, 1991; White's unanimous participating opinion.

**Present operation effective:** *Wooddell*, December 4, 1991.

**Holdings navigation:** [Wooddell v. International Brotherhood of Electrical Workers, Local 71, 502 U.S. 93 (1991) — Union-constitution enforcement](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#wooddell-v-international-brotherhood-of-electrical-workers-local-71-502-us-93-1991-1).

### Duration of protected military-service leave under §2024(d)

**Governing question:** Whether otherwise covered leave may be denied because a court considers its duration unreasonable.

**Current rule:** Section 2024(d) contains no implied reasonableness limit on the duration of otherwise covered military service. Neither a per se three-year exclusion nor a free-ranging judicial reasonableness balance may supply an omitted duration cap. The holding removes only that implied cap; the enacted coverage, notice, service, and return requirements remain independently applicable and are not excused by duration protection.

**Authority by component:**

- Rejection of judicial duration limits: [King v. St. Vincent's Hospital — Law of the decision, Holding and operative rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/King_v_St_Vincents_Hospital_merits_1991-12-16.md), December 16, 1991; Souter's unanimous participating opinion.

**Present operation effective:** *King*, December 16, 1991.

**Holdings navigation:** [King v. St. Vincent's Hospital, 502 U.S. 215 (1991) — Military leave and reemployment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#king-v-st-vincents-hospital-502-us-215-1991).

### Nonemployee organizers' access under a nondiscriminatory exclusion policy

**Governing question:** When the Board may balance a nonemployee organizer's need for entry against the employer's property rights.

**Current rule:** Nonemployee organizers must demonstrate that employees are practically beyond reasonable offsite communication before balancing may begin. Greater convenience or improved organizing success does not establish that threshold. The Board may assess actual obstacles through its expertise, but it may not replace the controlling statutory inaccessibility requirement with universal balancing that treats alternatives as merely another factor. The inquiry requires neither organizing success nor exhaustion of every imaginable contact technique. Employee solicitation, discriminatory exclusion, and lawful public-property advocacy remain governed by their distinct rules; genuine isolation by living or working location remains within the inaccessibility inquiry.

**Authority by component:**

- Antecedent inaccessibility, communication-versus-success distinction, and rejection of universal *Jean Country* balancing: [Lechmere v. NLRB — Nonemployee access requires demonstrated inaccessibility before balancing](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Lechmere_v_NLRB_merits_1992-01-27.md), January 27, 1992; Stone's opinion joined by O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** *Lechmere*, January 27, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [Lechmere, Inc. v. NLRB, 502 U.S. 527 (1992) — Nonemployee organizing access](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#lechmere-inc-v-nlrb-502-us-527-1992).

### ERISA employee status under general common-law agency

**Governing question:** Classification as an employee under ERISA's circular employee definition.

**Current rule:** ERISA incorporates general common-law agency, rather than any particular State's law or a substitute based on benefit expectations, reliance and bargaining power. Courts must weigh all incidents of the relationship, including control over the manner and means of work, skill, tools, work location, duration, assignment rights, scheduling discretion, payment, assistants, integration into the regular business, the hiring party's business, benefits and tax treatment. No factor, contractual label or expectation of benefits is decisive. Employee classification precedes the resulting vesting or forfeiture inquiry; this construction does not extend to statutes with broader express definitions, including the FLSA's suffer-or-permit definition.

**Authority by component:**
- Statutory construction and all-incidents inquiry: [Nationwide Mutual Insurance Co. v. Darden](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Nationwide_Mutual_Insurance_Co_v_Darden_merits_1992-03-24.md), March 24, 1992, “ERISA employee status follows general common-law agency”; Souter's unanimous opinion.

**Present operation effective:** Darden, March 24, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [Nationwide Mutual Insurance Co. v. Darden, 503 U.S. 318 (1992) — ERISA employee classification](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#nationwide-mutual-insurance-co-v-darden-503-us-318-1992).

### Vested LHWCA entitlement and third-party settlement approval

**Governing question:** When LHWCA §33(g)'s approval and notification conditions attach to a worker's third-party recovery.

**Current rule:** A worker whose substantive compensation right has vested is a person entitled to compensation despite disputed employer liability, absence of payments or absence of an award. For a covered third-party settlement below the statutory compensation entitlement, §33(g)(1) requires prescribed written employer-and-carrier approval before execution and filing within thirty days. Failure to obtain and file required approval terminates compensation and medical benefits under §33(g)(2). Judgments and settlements at least equal to the compensation entitlement require notification rather than that prior approval.

**Authority by component:**

- Vested entitlement, approval/filing and notification relationship: [Estate of Cowart v. Nicklos Drilling Co., “Vested entitlement exists before payment or an award”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Estate_of_Cowart_v_Nicklos_Drilling_Co_merits_1992-06-22.md), June 22, 1992; Kennedy, Stone, White, Scalia, Souter and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** *Cowart*, June 22, 1992.

**Open question:** Whether employer participation independently satisfies or excuses approval, any preserved alternative-approval or waiver issue, the gross-versus-net comparison and effects on other final awards remain unresolved. Injury alone does not establish vested entitlement, and not every settlement requires approval. No new deference rule follows.

**Holdings navigation:** [Estate of Cowart v. Nicklos Drilling Co., 505 U.S. 469 (1992) — Longshore compensation and third-party settlements](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#estate-of-cowart-v-nicklos-drilling-co-505-us-469-1992).

### Scheduled hearing loss and the separate audiogram filing trigger

**Governing question:** What instructions govern scheduled hearing loss and the separate audiogram filing trigger?

**Current rule:** Occupational hearing loss sustained during exposure is a scheduled presumptively disabling injury under §908(c)(13), complete at last exposure, which fixes injury time for the wage basis even if claimed after retirement. Neither later awareness nor filing moves it to §910(i)'s delayed-disability category or §908(c)(23)'s retiree formula. Scheduled compensation does not require actual earning loss; bilateral total loss uses two-thirds of the applicable average weekly wage for 200 weeks, with proportional partial-loss treatment. Separately, §908(c)(13)(D) delays running of §912 notice and §913 claim periods until receipt of an audiogram and accompanying report indicating loss. That filing protection does not move injury time; the distinct delayed-disease and retiree systems cannot be hybridized with the hearing-loss schedule.

**Authority by component:**

- Holding 1 — compensation branch and injury date.: [Bath Iron Works Corp. v. Director, Office of Workers’ Compensation Programs — Holding 1 — compensation branch and injury date.](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/bath-iron-works-v-director-owcp-merits-1993-01-12.md), 1993-01-12. Stevens’s unanimous Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas; nine votes for the rule and application.
- Holding 2 — the distinct filing protection.: [Bath Iron Works Corp. v. Director, Office of Workers’ Compensation Programs — Holding 2 — the distinct filing protection.](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/bath-iron-works-v-director-owcp-merits-1993-01-12.md), 1993-01-12. The same unanimous Court opinion; all nine Justices join this independently sufficient answer to the employer’s legislative-history argument as well as the text-based classification in Holding 1.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-01-12; Bath Iron Works Corp. v. Director, Office of Workers’ Compensation Programs, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Bath Iron Works Corp. v. Director, Office of Workers’ Compensation Programs, 506 U.S. 153 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#bath-iron-works-corp-v-director-office-of-workers-compensation-programs-506-us-153-1993).

### Age motivation, pension tenure and willfulness

**Governing question:** What instructions govern age motivation, pension tenure and willfulness?

**Current rule:** ADEA disparate treatment requires age actually to motivate and have a determinative influence on the employment decision. Service-based pension interference alone does not establish it; deliberate use of pension status as an age proxy or mixed age and pension motivation may. Age-triggered vesting and disparate impact remain unresolved. A disparate-treatment violation is willful if the employer knew or recklessly disregarded whether conduct violated the Act, for individual decisions as well as formal policies. Once shown, liquidated damages require no additional outrageousness, direct evidence or predominant-age motive. A good-faith nonreckless mistake about a statutory permission is not willful.

**Authority by component:**

- Service-based vesting is distinct from age motivation: [Hazen Paper Co. v. Biggins — Service-based vesting is distinct from age motivation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/hazen_merits_1993-04-20.md), 1993-04-20. O'Connor’s Opinion of the Court, I; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.
- Thurston governs individual acts as well as formal policies: [Hazen Paper Co. v. Biggins — Thurston governs individual acts as well as formal policies](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/hazen_merits_1993-04-20.md), 1993-04-20. O'Connor’s Opinion of the Court, II; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-04-20; Hazen Paper Co. v. Biggins, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Hazen Paper Co. v. Biggins, 507 U.S. 604 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#hazen-paper-co-v-biggins-507-us-604-1993).

### Public-sector compensatory time and legally capable representatives

**Governing question:** What instructions govern public-sector compensatory time and legally capable representatives?

**Current rule:** For employees within §207(o)(2)(A)(ii) hired before April 15, 1986, the regular compensatory-time practice then in effect supplies the required agreement or understanding without a further individual or collective agreement. For later hires whose chosen organization cannot under state law enter a binding subsection (i) agreement, the employer may use individual subsection (ii) agreements or understandings instead of losing compensatory time entirely. No consent is presumed outside those predicates; legally capable representatives, later-adopted practices and ordinary overtime requirements retain their distinct treatment. No general federal public-sector bargaining right follows.

**Authority by component:**

- The earlier-hire component: the transition rule supplies the agreement: [Moreau v. Klevenhagen — The earlier-hire component: the transition rule supplies the agreement](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Moreau_v_Klevenhagen_merits_1993-05-03.md), 1993-05-03. Stevens's opinion for the Court, joined by all nine participating Justices.
- The later-hire component: individual agreements where no representative could bind: [Moreau v. Klevenhagen — The later-hire component: individual agreements where no representative could bind](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Moreau_v_Klevenhagen_merits_1993-05-03.md), 1993-05-03. Stevens's opinion for the Court, joined by all nine participating Justices.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-05-03; Moreau v. Klevenhagen, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Moreau v. Klevenhagen, 508 U.S. 22 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#moreau-v-klevenhagen-508-us-22-1993).

### Discharge of pension funding obligations as an exchange

**Governing question:** What instructions govern discharge of pension funding obligations as an exchange?

**Current rule:** Under §4975(c)(1)(A), an employer's property transfer satisfying an enforceable pension-funding obligation is a sale or exchange under the established tax meaning. Independently, it is an ordinary exchange because the plan receives property and the employer receives release. An unencumbered contribution is not excluded. Section 4975(f)(3)'s inclusion of property subject to a mortgage or similar lien assumed by the plan or placed by a disqualified person within ten years does not exclude other exchanges for consideration. Exemption, correction and tax computation remain distinct; no deference holding follows.

**Authority by component:**

- The established tax meaning includes property exchanged for release of a funding duty: [Commissioner v. Keystone Consolidated Industries, Inc. — The established tax meaning includes property exchanged for release of a funding duty](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/keystone_merits_1993-05-24.md), 1993-05-24. Blackmun’s Part III-A; White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (7 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.
- Discharge supplies consideration even without importing a technical tax meaning: [Commissioner v. Keystone Consolidated Industries, Inc. — Discharge supplies consideration even without importing a technical tax meaning](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/keystone_merits_1993-05-24.md), 1993-05-24. Blackmun’s Parts III-C and III-D; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (8 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-05-24; Commissioner v. Keystone Consolidated Industries, Inc., for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Commissioner v. Keystone Consolidated Industries, Inc., 508 U.S. 152 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#commissioner-v-keystone-consolidated-industries-inc-508-us-152-1993).

### ERISA equitable categories and nonfiduciary compensation

**Governing question:** What instructions govern ERISA equitable categories and nonfiduciary compensation?

**Current rule:** Section 502(a)(3)'s appropriate equitable relief means traditionally equitable categories, not compensatory damages merely because equity could award them in trust litigation. Compensation from an admitted nonfiduciary for plan losses is unavailable under that provision. The rule does not prohibit every equitable monetary remedy or decide the expressly disclaimed antecedent cause-of-action question; restitution is not reopened.

**Authority by component:**

- The reviewed compensatory award is not appropriate equitable relief: [Mertens v. Hewitt Associates — The reviewed compensatory award is not appropriate equitable relief](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/mertens_merits_1993-06-01.md), 1993-06-01. Scalia’s Opinion of the Court; Blackmun, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (5 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-01; Mertens v. Hewitt Associates, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Mertens v. Hewitt Associates, 508 U.S. 248 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#mertens-v-hewitt-associates-508-us-248-1993).

### Neutral adjudication and withdrawal-liability rebuttal

**Governing question:** What instructions govern neutral adjudication and withdrawal-liability rebuttal?

**Current rule:** A plan sponsor's withdrawal-liability assessment and demand are enforcement acts, not adjudication; due process permits the preliminary demand when first adjudication is by a neutral arbitrator under lawful burdens. Section 1401(a)(3)(A) assigns the employer ordinary preponderance persuasion to disprove factual determinations, without extra appellate deference, clear-and-convincing proof or conclusiveness. Under (B), the employer may rebut actuarial calculations by a preponderance showing either aggregate unreasonableness of assumptions and methods considering plan experience and reasonable expectations, or significant error in application. Fraud is unnecessary. The judicial-review presumption remains separate.

- The preliminary assessment is not an adjudication: [Concrete Pipe & Products of California, Inc. v. Souter’s Opinion of the Court, The factual presumption assigns ordinary persuasion portion; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter (8 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.
- Actuarial rebuttal retains both statutory routes: [Concrete Pipe & Products of California, Inc. v. Construction Laborers Pension Trust for Southern California — Actuarial rebuttal retains both statutory routes](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Concrete_Pipe_and_Products_of_California_Inc_v_Construction_Laborers_Pension_Trust_for_Southern_California_merits_1993-06-14.md), 1993-06-14.
