### Section 302 transaction prohibitions and trust administration

**Governing question:** What instructions govern section 302 transaction prohibitions and trust administration?

**Current rule:** Section 302(e) authorizes restraint of prohibited payment, demand, receipt and related transactions under §302(a)-(b). Section 302(c)(5) identifies qualifying trusts; it does not create a general federal supervisory remedy for subsequent administration. Reserve retention alone supplies no transfer remedy without an actual prohibited transaction. Payments to a nonqualifying trust remain regulated; ERISA asset entitlement is not decided.

**Authority by component:**

- Section 302 restrains prohibited transactions: [Local 144 Nursing Home Pension Fund v. Demisay — Section 302 restrains prohibited transactions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Local_144_Nursing_Home_Pension_Fund_v_Demisay_merits_1993-06-14.md), 1993-06-14. Scalia’s Opinion of the Court, Section 302 restrains prohibited transactions portion; Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-14; Local 144 Nursing Home Pension Fund v. Demisay, for the components identified above.

### Rejected employment explanations and circumstantial discrimination proof

**Governing question:** What instructions govern rejected employment explanations and circumstantial discrimination proof?

**Current rule:** After an employer produces admissible nondiscriminatory explanations, a proven prima-facie case and rejection of those explanations do not automatically command judgment for the employee. They may support intentional discrimination without a mandatory additional category of direct or independent proof. Burdine's persuasion allocation is unchanged; Hicks adopts no replacement allocation, universal Title VII/§1983 equivalence or determination of the ultimate finding. Stone's proposed shifted burden is noncontrolling.

**Authority by component:**

- Pretext alone does not command judgment, and circumstantial evidence need not be supplemented by a special category of proof: [St. Mary's Honor Center v. Hicks — Pretext alone does not command judgment, and circumstantial evidence need not be supplemented by a special category of proof](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/St_Mary_s_Honor_Center_v_Hicks_merits_1993-06-25.md), 1993-06-25. Scalia’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas (5 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-25; St. Mary's Honor Center v. Hicks, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [St. Mary's Honor Center v. Hicks, 509 U.S. 502 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#st-marys-honor-center-v-hicks-509-us-502-1993).

### Hostile employment conditions without a psychological-injury prerequisite

**Governing question:** When discriminatory harassment alters employment conditions under Title VII.

**Current rule:** Sex-based harassment violates Title VII when, considering all circumstances, it is sufficiently severe or pervasive to alter employment conditions and create an environment a reasonable person would find hostile or abusive and the employee actually perceives as abusive. Serious psychological injury, treatment, diagnosis and lost productivity are not prerequisites. Relevant circumstances include frequency, severity, physical threat or humiliation rather than merely offensive utterances, and unreasonable interference with work; none is indispensable. Psychological effects may inform subjective abuse. Mere offense without the objective and subjective predicates remains insufficient.

**Authority by component:**

- Objective and subjective requirements, totality and absence of an injury floor: [Harris v. Forklift Systems, Inc — Hostile employment conditions do not require psychological injury](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Harris_v_Forklift_Systems_Inc_merits_1993-11-09.md#public-projection), 1993-11-09. O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg; O'Connor and those eight Justices supply nine votes for the complete rule and reversal with remand. Direct unanimous authority; no fractured-opinion inference.

**Present operation effective:** Harris v. Forklift Systems, Inc, 1993-11-09.

**Open question:** A sex-specific reasonable-person formulation, constructive discharge and employer-attribution questions are not resolved by Harris.

**Holdings navigation:** [Hostile employment conditions do not require psychological injury](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#hostile-employment-conditions-do-not-require-psychological-injury).

### ERISA guaranteed-benefit exclusions and insurer fiduciary functions

**Governing question:** Which insurer assets are excluded from plan-asset status by ERISA §401(b)(2).

**Current rule:** A guaranteed-benefit policy itself is a plan asset; the insurer’s underlying assets are not deemed plan assets solely because it issued the policy only to the extent the policy genuinely guarantees the amount of benefits. A nonguaranteed component does not gain the exclusion from general-account placement. Plan-asset status subjects the insurer to §3(21)(A) fiduciary duties to the extent it exercises the specified authority or control over management or disposition; breach, causation, loss and remedy remain separate. McCarran-Ferguson does not displace ERISA’s express insurance-related duties. Compatible state insurance regulation survives the saving clause, but a concrete conflicting obligation cannot control over ERISA itself. An agency interpretation cannot erase the exclusion’s component limitation.

**Authority by component:**

- Component-specific exclusion and asset-and-function inquiry: [John Hancock Mutual Life Insurance Co. v. Harris Trust & Savings Bank — The guaranteed-benefit exclusion operates by component](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/John_Hancock_Mutual_Life_Insurance_Co_v_Harris_Trust_Savings_Bank_merits_1993-12-13.md#public-projection), 1993-12-13. Ginsburg's opinion for the Court, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, and Souter. Six Justices adopt the component-specific construction and its application to the disputed free funds.
- Federal duties and compatible state insurance regulation: [John Hancock Mutual Life Insurance Co. v. Harris Trust & Savings Bank — Insurance regulation does not displace ERISA's express direction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/John_Hancock_Mutual_Life_Insurance_Co_v_Harris_Trust_Savings_Bank_merits_1993-12-13.md#public-projection), 1993-12-13. Ginsburg's opinion for the Court, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, and Souter. This six-Justice ground rejects the asserted state-insurance obstacle to affirmance.
- Statutory limit on the categorical agency exclusion: [John Hancock Mutual Life Insurance Co. v. Harris Trust & Savings Bank — An agency interpretation cannot erase the qualified exclusion](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/John_Hancock_Mutual_Life_Insurance_Co_v_Harris_Trust_Savings_Bank_merits_1993-12-13.md#public-projection), 1993-12-13. Ginsburg's opinion for the Court, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, and Souter. These six adopt the statutory-limit ground; the dissenters separately reject the categorical agency position without adopting the Court's entire investment-risk analysis.

**Present operation effective:** John Hancock Mutual Life Insurance Co. v. Harris Trust & Savings Bank, 1993-12-13.

**Open question:** No universal guarantee formula, illiquidity-only rule, categorical rejection of changed agency views or general rule for agency litigation positions is adopted.

**Holdings navigation:** [An agency interpretation cannot erase the qualified exclusion](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#an-agency-interpretation-cannot-erase-the-qualified-exclusion); [The guaranteed-benefit exclusion operates by component](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-guaranteed-benefit-exclusion-operates-by-component); [Insurance regulation does not displace ERISA's express direction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#insurance-regulation-does-not-displace-erisas-express-direction).

### Hearing misconduct and reviewable NLRB remedial discretion

**Governing question:** Whether deliberate employee falsehood at a remedial hearing forfeits reinstatement or backpay under NLRA §10(c).

**Current rule:** Deliberate hearing falsehood does not automatically forfeit reinstatement or backpay for an established unfair labor practice. The Board may withhold relief for supported misconduct, but need not impose categorical forfeiture. The separate prohibition for an employee actually suspended or discharged for cause concerns the actual employment ground; later testimony does not establish that earlier ground. The Board must recognize material misconduct and provide reasons connecting its remedy to the Act. Review remains available for arbitrariness, inadequate explanation or departure from statutory purpose, without requiring a new sanctions formula or separate express forfeiture discussion in every case.

**Authority by component:**

- No automatic forfeiture and distinct discharge-for-cause bar: [ABF Freight System, Inc. v. NLRB — Hearing falsehood and the statutory remedy](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/ABF_Freight_System_v_NLRB_merits_1994-01-24.md#public-projection), 1994-01-24. Stevens's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; seven Justices. Scalia and O'Connor agree with the lawfulness of the result in a separate judgment concurrence.
- Explanation and review of delegated remedy: [ABF Freight System, Inc. v. NLRB — Review of this remedial choice](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/ABF_Freight_System_v_NLRB_merits_1994-01-24.md#public-projection), 1994-01-24. Stevens's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; seven Justices. Scalia and O'Connor find the action lawful on their narrower statutory-discretion ground, without endorsing the Court's public-remedy assessment.

**Present operation effective:** ABF Freight System, Inc. v. NLRB, 1994-01-24.

**Open question:** A falsehood undermining the violation finding or a remedial predicate may present a different case; no automatic relief entitlement is created.

**Holdings navigation:** [Hearing falsehood and the statutory remedy](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#hearing-falsehood-and-the-statutory-remedy); [Review of this remedial choice](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#review-of-this-remedial-choice).

### State maritime forum administration and substantive uniformity

**Governing question:** Whether a competent state court must apply federal forum non conveniens to a domestic in personam maritime action.

**Current rule:** A competent state court hearing a domestic Jones Act and accompanying in personam maritime action may retain the case without applying federal forum non conveniens. State forum policy must remain impartial toward federal claims and sister-state citizens; treating nonresidents alike is not itself citizenship discrimination. Independently, that forum-management choice neither materially prejudices a characteristic feature of maritime law nor impairs its necessary harmony and uniformity. A procedural label does not protect a rule that impairs substantive maritime rights or integral burdens or remedies.

**Authority by component:**

- Saving-clause and Jones Act forum choice: [American Dredging Co. v. Miller — State forum administration under the saving clause and Jones Act](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/American_Dredging_Co_v_Miller_merits_1994-02-23.md#public-projection), 1994-02-23. The statutory/state-forum portion of Scalia's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter, and Ginsburg; seven Justices adopt this sufficient ground for affirmance. Stevens joins this portion and the judgment, but not the separate Jensen analysis.
- Independent maritime-uniformity ground: [American Dredging Co. v. Miller — Independent maritime-uniformity ground](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/American_Dredging_Co_v_Miller_merits_1994-02-23.md#public-projection), 1994-02-23. The Jensen portion of Scalia's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Souter, and Ginsburg; six Justices adopt this independently sufficient ground. Stevens does not join it. Souter also supplies a separate close-classification qualification without withdrawing this join.

**Present operation effective:** American Dredging Co. v. Miller, 1994-02-23.

**Exceptions and limits:** Federal courts retain their own forum practice. Jurisdiction, venue, substantive choice of law and discretionary dismissal remain distinct. Foreign-party, independent discrimination and Commerce Clause questions are not decided.

**Holdings navigation:** [State forum administration under the saving clause and Jones Act](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#state-forum-administration-under-the-saving-clause-and-jones-act).

### Maritime settlement credit and contribution protection

**Governing question:** How a settlement with one jointly liable maritime tortfeasor affects the remaining claim and contribution.

**Current rule:** The plaintiff’s claim against nonsettling maritime tortfeasors is reduced by the settling tortfeasor’s equitable proportion of responsibility, not by settlement dollars. A settler is protected from contribution when the nonsettler has a meaningful opportunity to litigate and obtain a reliable determination of that proportionate share. The nonsettler then pays its own adjudicated obligation and cannot recover the plaintiff’s voluntary settlement shortfall from the settler. A reliable proportionate credit is the premise for the contribution bar, not a fact established merely by identifying a settlement. Settlement risk belongs to the settling parties; the rule does not abolish surviving joint liability or transfer ordinary insolvency risk wholesale to the injured plaintiff.

**Authority by component:**

- Proportionate-share rule and settlement risk: [McDermott, Inc. v. AmClyde — Proportionate-share credit for maritime settlements; Settlement risk, surviving joint liability and this remand](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/McDermott_Inc_v_AmClyde_merits_1994-04-20.md#public-projection), 1994-04-20. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone, Blackmun, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; all nine adopt the rule and its settlement-specific limits. Stevens’s unanimous Opinion of the Court; all nine adopt this application and the bounded reversal and remand.
- Reliable allocation prerequisite to contribution protection: [Boca Grande Club, Inc. v. Florida Power & Light Co — Proportionate-share credit supplies the protection that makes contribution against the settler unavailable](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Boca_Grande_Club_v_Florida_Power_Light_merits_1994-04-20.md#public-projection), 1994-04-20. Stevens’s opinion for the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; nine Justices adopt this application of McDermott.

**Present operation effective:** Boca Grande Club, Inc. v. Florida Power & Light Co, 1994-04-20.

**Holdings navigation:** [Proportionate-share credit supplies the protection that makes contribution against the settler unavailable](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#proportionate-share-credit-supplies-the-protection-that-makes-contribution-against-the-settler-unavailable); [Proportionate-share credit for maritime settlements](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#proportionate-share-credit-for-maritime-settlements); [Settlement risk, surviving joint liability and this remand](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#settlement-risk-surviving-joint-liability-and-this-remand).

### Professional direction and genuine NLRA supervisory authority

**Governing question:** How §2(11) distinguishes skilled patient-care direction from supervisory status.

**Current rule:** The Board may distinguish a professional employee’s skilled direction of care from genuine authority in the employer’s interest to perform any of §2(11)’s listed supervisory functions: hire, transfer, suspend, lay off, recall, promote, discharge, assign, reward, discipline, responsibly direct, adjust grievances or effectively recommend such action. The authority must involve independent judgment and not merely routine or clerical action. No hiring-or-firing power is indispensable, and genuinely held authority may qualify despite infrequent exercise. Professional judgment may be independent judgment, but skilled care alone does not establish the required supervisory authority. This is a permissible distinction, not a mandatory professional exemption or a holding that patient care never serves the employer’s interest.

**Authority by component:**

- Permissible functional distinction and all statutory predicates: [NLRB v. Health Care & Retirement Corp. of America — The Board may distinguish skilled direction from genuine supervisory authority](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/NLRB_v_Health_Care_Retirement_Corp_merits_1994-05-23.md#public-projection), 1994-05-23. Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court is joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Souter. All five directly adopt the permissible-construction ground; Stone-Zsela's proposed mandatory construction appears only in his concurrence.

**Present operation effective:** NLRB v. Health Care & Retirement Corp. of America, 1994-05-23.

**Holdings navigation:** [The Board may distinguish skilled direction from genuine supervisory authority](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-board-may-distinguish-skilled-direction-from-genuine-supervisory-authority).

### Public-employee speech and the employer’s mistaken factual account

**Governing question:** Which employer beliefs about employee speech protect a disciplinary decision, and which do not.

**Current rule:** An employer acting on a genuine, reasonable account of speech that would be disciplinable under the governing public-employee-speech rules is protected even if that factual account is mistaken. Conversely, an unreasonable and incorrect account does not defeat a claim when the employee actually spoke protected speech and that speech caused the adverse action. The employee must show protected speech was a substantial or motivating factor; the employer may establish by a preponderance that it would have taken the same action absent the protected speech. These limited propositions do not impose liability for every investigation defect or adopt the plurality’s complete investigative procedure.

**Authority by component:**

- Seven-Justice genuine-and-reasonable-account safe harbor: [Waters v. Churchill — A genuine, reasonable account of disciplinable speech supplies a limited safe harbor](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Waters_v_Churchill_merits_1994-05-31.md#public-projection), 1994-05-31. O'Connor's plurality opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Souter and Ginsburg, adopts this sufficient condition; Scalia's concurrence in the judgment, joined by Kennedy and Thomas, expressly accepts it under their broader genuine-ground rule. Seven Justices agree at this precise level; only four adopt the full objective-inquiry framework.
- Six-Justice unreasonable-and-false-account limit: [Waters v. Churchill — An unreasonable false account cannot defeat actually protected speech](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Waters_v_Churchill_merits_1994-05-31.md#public-projection), 1994-05-31. O'Connor, Stone-Zsela, Souter and Ginsburg accept this proposition under objective factual care; Stevens's dissent, joined by Blackmun, expressly accepts it under their broader actual-speech rule. Six Justices agree on this proposition, but the two dissenters do not join the reasonable-error safe harbor or the judgment vacating the appellate rule.
- Motive and same-decision proof: [Waters v. Churchill — The motive dispute requires remand without restoring defendants' summary judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Waters_v_Churchill_merits_1994-05-31.md#public-projection), 1994-05-31. O'Connor's four-Justice plurality and Scalia's three-Justice concurrence in the judgment support this common vacatur and limited remand. Blackmun and Stevens would affirm the appellate reversal under their different actual-speech rule; their votes are not counted as support for vacatur.

**Present operation effective:** Waters v. Churchill, 1994-05-31.

**Open question:** Unreasonable belief where the actual speech was unprotected, and a universal investigation checklist, are not resolved. The four-Justice complete reasonableness framework is noncontrolling.

**Holdings navigation:** [A genuine, reasonable account of disciplinable speech supplies a limited safe harbor](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#a-genuine-reasonable-account-of-disciplinable-speech-supplies-a-limited-safe-harbor); [An unreasonable false account cannot defeat actually protected speech](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#an-unreasonable-false-account-cannot-defeat-actually-protected-speech); [The motive dispute requires remand without restoring defendants' summary judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-motive-dispute-requires-remand-without-restoring-defendants-summary-judgment).

### The vessel’s cargo-turnover warning duty

**Governing question:** Which cargo hazards a vessel must warn a competent stevedore about at turnover.

**Current rule:** The vessel must warn of cargo hazards known to it, or that it should know through the care assigned to it, likely to be encountered in unloading and neither known to nor obvious to or reasonably anticipated by a competent stevedore. The vessel has no general duty to supervise loading or inspect completed stowage for concealed defects absent a contract, law or custom imposing that duty. Knowledge and obviousness must be assessed for the correct actor at the relevant turnover time; a supplier’s knowledge is not automatically the vessel’s knowledge.

**Authority by component:**

- Assigned care, competent stevedore and actor-specific inquiry: [Howlett v. Birkdale Shipping Co., S.A — The cargo turnover-warning duty is limited by the vessel’s assigned care; Knowledge and obviousness require the correct actor and time on remand](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Howlett_v_Birkdale_Shipping_Co_SA_merits_1994-06-13.md#public-projection), 1994-06-13. Kennedy's unanimous Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg adopt the limited turnover-warning rule and rejection of a general cargo-inspection duty. Kennedy's unanimous Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg adopt this application and bounded vacatur and remand.

**Present operation effective:** Howlett v. Birkdale Shipping Co., S.A, 1994-06-13.

**Holdings navigation:** [The cargo turnover-warning duty is limited by the vessel’s assigned care](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-cargo-turnover-warning-duty-is-limited-by-the-vessels-assigned-care); [Knowledge and obviousness require the correct actor and time on remand](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#knowledge-and-obviousness-require-the-correct-actor-and-time-on-remand).

### Equal state enforcement for employees who collectively bargain

**Governing question:** Whether a State may withhold ordinary labor-law enforcement solely because employees completed protected collective bargaining.

**Current rule:** A State may not deny enforcement of an otherwise independent wage right solely because the employee’s collective-bargaining agreement contains an arbitration clause. Conditioning state assistance on avoiding completed collective bargaining impermissibly penalizes the federally protected activity. Section 1983 supplies a remedy for the identified individual federal labor right infringed by that policy; a generalized assertion of federal preemption alone is not thereby made an enforceable individual right.

**Authority by component:**

- Protected bargaining and the specific §1983 channel: [Livadas v. Bradshaw — California may not penalize completion of protected collective bargaining; Section 1983 supplies a channel for this identified federal labor right](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Livadas_v_Bradshaw_merits_1994-06-13.md#public-projection), 1994-06-13. Stevens's unanimous Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg adopt this statutory labor-right holding. Stevens's unanimous Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg adopt the right, nonforeclosure and bounded-remedy analysis.

**Present operation effective:** Livadas v. Bradshaw, 1994-06-13.

**Holdings navigation:** [Section 1983 supplies a channel for this identified federal labor right](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#section-1983-supplies-a-channel-for-this-identified-federal-labor-right); [California may not penalize completion of protected collective bargaining](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#california-may-not-penalize-completion-of-protected-collective-bargaining).

### FELA negligent emotional injury within the zone of danger

**Governing question:** When an employee may recover under FELA for negligently caused emotional injury.

**Current rule:** A negligently caused emotional injury can be actionable on either of two alternative routes: the employee sustained actual physical impact from the employer's negligence, or the employee was placed by that negligence in immediate risk of physical harm and the emotional injury arises from fear of harm to the employee's own safety — that fear-based qualification attaches only to the threatened-impact route, not to the physical-impact route. Ordinary negligence, genuine injury and legally sufficient causation remain necessary. Bystander distress outside that zone and ordinary work-stress claims do not qualify. The rule neither guarantees recovery from exposure alone nor requires a judicially invented numerical proximity threshold.

**Authority by component:**

- Zone-of-danger gate and surviving negligence elements: [Consolidated Rail Corp. v. Gottshall — Negligent emotional injury is actionable within the employee's zone of danger](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Consolidated_Rail_Corp_v_Gottshall_merits_1994-06-24.md#public-projection), 1994-06-24. Justice Thomas's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter. Five of nine participating Justices directly adopt the same operative rule; no combination of separate rationales supplies its force.
- Exclusion of the ordinary work-stress category: [Consolidated Rail Corp. v. Gottshall — Carlisle's work-stress claim is excluded and requires judgment for Conrail](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Consolidated_Rail_Corp_v_Gottshall_merits_1994-06-24.md#public-projection), 1994-06-24. Justice Thomas's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter. Five Justices adopt both the legal exclusion and mandatory defendant-judgment remedy.

**Present operation effective:** Consolidated Rail Corp. v. Gottshall, 1994-06-24.

**Open question:** The Court does not decide whether every qualifying claim additionally requires physical manifestations of the emotional injury.

**Holdings navigation:** [Negligent emotional injury is actionable within the employee's zone of danger](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#negligent-emotional-injury-is-actionable-within-the-employees-zone-of-danger); [Carlisle's work-stress claim is excluded and requires judgment for Conrail](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#carlisles-work-stress-claim-is-excluded-and-requires-judgment-for-conrail).

### After-acquired misconduct and age-discrimination remedies

**Governing question:** The effect of misconduct discovered after an allegedly age-discriminatory discharge.

**Current rule:** Misconduct unknown to the employer at discharge does not erase an ADEA violation or categorically eliminate all relief. The employee retains the liability burden. To limit relief, the employer must prove misconduct so serious that it would in fact have discharged the employee for that reason alone, not merely that discharge would have been permissible. Once proved, reinstatement and front pay are ordinarily inappropriate; backpay’s starting measure runs from unlawful discharge to actual discovery, subject to extraordinary equitable circumstances affecting either party’s legitimate interests.

**Authority by component:**

- Later-discovered misconduct does not erase an earlier ADEA violation: [Later-discovered misconduct does not erase an earlier ADEA violation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/McKennon_v_Nashville_Banner_Publishing_Co_merits_1995-01-23.md#public-projection), 1995-01-23. O’Connor’s opinion for the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer. All nine adopt this proposition and explanation; it is a direct majority holding.
- Proven independently sufficient misconduct limits appropriate relief: [Proven independently sufficient misconduct limits appropriate relief](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/McKennon_v_Nashville_Banner_Publishing_Co_merits_1995-01-23.md#public-projection), 1995-01-23. O’Connor’s opinion for the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer. All nine adopt the proof requirement and bounded remedial rule.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-01-23; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Actual age motivation, ultimate persuasion and separate willfulness requirements remain unchanged. A litigation assumption of discrimination does not establish liability.

**Holdings navigation:** [Later-discovered misconduct does not erase an earlier ADEA violation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#later-discovered-misconduct-does-not-erase-an-earlier-adea-violation); [Proven independently sufficient misconduct limits appropriate relief](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#proven-independently-sufficient-misconduct-limits-appropriate-relief).

### Withdrawal-liability installment assumptions and separate collection triggers

**Governing question:** The computational interest starting point under §1399(c)(1)(A)(i).

**Current rule:** The installment computation assumes first payment on the first day of the plan year after the withdrawal year and does not capitalize interest for the preceding withdrawal year. That assumption does not postpone actual payment: payments begin no later than sixty days after demand notwithstanding review or appeal and are quarterly unless the plan specifies other intervals. Overdue interest runs from the actual due date until payment.

**Authority by component:**

- The assumed payment date fixes the computational starting point: [The assumed payment date fixes the computational starting point](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Milwaukee_Brewery_Workers_Pension_Plan_v_Jos_Schlitz_Brewing_Co_merits_1995-02-21.md#public-projection), 1995-02-21. Breyer’s opinion for the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg. All nine adopt this construction and its limits.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-02-21; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Ordinary liability is limited to the first twenty annual payments when the calculated period is longer; the cap does not apply when termination occurs through withdrawal of all employers or substantially all under an agreement or arrangement to withdraw. Prepayment of remaining annual payments and accrued interest is permitted without penalty, subject to redetermination for later qualifying mass withdrawal. Default acceleration may include interest from the first missed payment’s due date. Payment default requires failure to cure within sixty days after written notice; other adopted-plan defaults must indicate substantial likelihood of inability to pay withdrawal liability. Market-rate collection interest and amortization assumptions remain separate. The decision changes neither actuarial nor factual-review burdens.

**Holdings navigation:** [The assumed payment date fixes the computational starting point](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-assumed-payment-date-fixes-the-computational-starting-point).

### Corporate procedures for ERISA plan amendments

**Governing question:** Sufficiency and implementation of a welfare plan’s reservation of amendment power to a corporation.

**Current rule:** A written reservation of amendment to the Company can satisfy ERISA §402(b)(3)’s procedure and authorized-person requirements because §3(9) includes corporations and applicable corporate law identifies who can bind them. A list of named officers is not invariably required. Any additional procedure the plan actually adopts remains binding. Sufficiency of the reservation does not prove that an authorized actor adopted an amendment: actual express or implied authority, approval and any legally effective ratification, with its timing and consequences, require separate determination.

**Authority by component:**

- The company-amendment clause satisfies §402(b)(3): [The company-amendment clause satisfies §402(b)(3)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Curtiss_Wright_Corp_v_Schoonejongen_merits_1995-03-06.md#public-projection), 1995-03-06. Souter’s unanimous opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer; all nine adopt the statutory construction.
- A sufficient procedure still requires authorized corporate action: [A sufficient procedure still requires authorized corporate action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Curtiss_Wright_Corp_v_Schoonejongen_merits_1995-03-06.md#public-projection), 1995-03-06. Souter’s unanimous opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer; all nine adopt this required remand and its limits.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-03-06; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** A summary plan description is not proof of authorized amendment. Sponsor amendment power remains distinct from fiduciary duties, written-instrument and disclosure requirements. No universal procedural-violation remedy, vesting rule or amendment immunity follows.

**Holdings navigation:** [The company-amendment clause satisfies §402(b)(3)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-company-amendment-clause-satisfies-402b3); [A sufficient procedure still requires authorized corporate action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#a-sufficient-procedure-still-requires-authorized-corporate-action).

### Admiralty locality and connection for vessel-caused land injury

**Governing question:** The location and maritime-connection requirements under §1333(1) and the Admiralty Extension Act.

**Current rule:** Damage consummated on land meets locality when proximately caused by a vessel on navigable waters; fixed distance or elapsed-time cutoffs do not replace ordinary causation. Connection asks both whether the general type of incident potentially disrupts maritime commerce and whether the activity giving rise to it substantially relates to traditional maritime activity. Vessel damage to an underwater structure and vessel-based repair or maintenance of waterway structures satisfy those respective inquiries without making actual closure of navigation necessary.

**Authority by component:**

- Vessel-caused land injury satisfies admiralty locality through ordinary proximate causation: [Vessel-caused land injury satisfies admiralty locality through ordinary proximate causation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Jerome_B_Grubart_Inc_v_Great_Lakes_Dredge_Dock_Co_merits_1995-02-22.md#public-projection), 1995-02-22. Justice Souter's Part I, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O'Connor, Kennedy and Ginsburg, has five votes, more than the four required on this seven-member Court. Justices Thomas and Scalia agree that vessel causation and locality support jurisdiction but join the judgment on their separate locality rule, not this Court opinion.
- This underwater-structure incident and vessel-based repair activity satisfy Sisson: [This underwater-structure incident and vessel-based repair activity satisfy Sisson](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Jerome_B_Grubart_Inc_v_Great_Lakes_Dredge_Dock_Co_merits_1995-02-22.md#public-projection), 1995-02-22. Justice Souter's Part II is joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O'Connor, Kennedy and Ginsburg. These five directly adopt both connection inquiries and their application. Justices Thomas and Scalia reject imposing the additional inquiries as necessary conditions in this vessel setting.
- An alleged maritime proximate cause is not erased by a contributing nonmaritime actor: [An alleged maritime proximate cause is not erased by a contributing nonmaritime actor](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Jerome_B_Grubart_Inc_v_Great_Lakes_Dredge_Dock_Co_merits_1995-02-22.md#public-projection), 1995-02-22. Justice Souter's Part III, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O'Connor, Kennedy and Ginsburg, supplies five direct votes. The two locality-route Justices concur in the jurisdictional judgment without joining this application of Sisson's activity inquiry.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-02-22; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** For the activity branch, at least one alleged tortfeasor’s relevant maritime activity as an alleged proximate cause suffices despite another actor’s nonmaritime contribution. Mere joinder supplies no jurisdiction over independent claims. Jurisdiction does not establish negligence, liability, limitation, a damages cap or exclusive federal adjudication.

**Holdings navigation:** [Vessel-caused land injury satisfies admiralty locality through ordinary proximate causation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#vessel-caused-land-injury-satisfies-admiralty-locality-through-ordinary-proximate-causation); [This underwater-structure incident and vessel-based repair activity satisfy Sisson](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#this-underwater-structure-incident-and-vessel-based-repair-activity-satisfy-sisson); [An alleged maritime proximate cause is not erased by a contributing nonmaritime actor](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#an-alleged-maritime-proximate-cause-is-not-erased-by-a-contributing-nonmaritime-actor).

### Economic-capacity modification of nonscheduled disability awards

**Governing question:** Modification under LHWCA §22 without a change in physical impairment.

**Current rule:** Section 22 permits modification of a nonscheduled disability award upon a demonstrated change in injury-related earning capacity although physical impairment is unchanged. Changed conditions or mistake in a determination of fact must fall within one year after the last compensation payment, whether or not a compensation order issued, or within one year after claim rejection. The employer seeking modification must prove the qualifying change. Increased wages are conclusive only insofar as they fairly and reasonably represent capacity under §8(h); otherwise the decisionmaker must consider injury, impairment, usual employment, other capacity circumstances and natural future disability effects. Findings retain statutory substantial-evidence review.

**Authority by component:**

- Economic change can support timely nonscheduled modification: [Economic change can support timely nonscheduled modification](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Metropolitan_Stevedore_Co_v_Rambo_merits_1995-06-12.md#public-projection), 1995-06-12. Justice Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer; eight Justices adopt the construction and bounded reversal/remand.
- Capacity, proof and the remand: [Capacity, proof and the remand](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Metropolitan_Stevedore_Co_v_Rambo_merits_1995-06-12.md#public-projection), 1995-06-12. Justice Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer; eight Justices adopt this application/remedy qualification.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-12; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** True §8(i) settlements are distinct. Scheduled compensation, medical benefits, untimely reopening, future recurrence and nominal-award doctrine remain undecided; no automatic termination or unrestricted revision of final awards follows.

**Holdings navigation:** [Economic change can support timely nonscheduled modification](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#economic-change-can-support-timely-nonscheduled-modification); [Capacity, proof and the remand](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#capacity-proof-and-the-remand).

### Borrowing limitations periods for WARN claims

**Governing question:** Selection of a limitations source for the reviewed 1988 WARN cause of action.

**Current rule:** An appropriate analogous state limitations period governs WARN claims when NLRA §10(b) is neither a clearly closer analogy nor shown significantly more suitable in light of federal policy and litigation practicalities. The narrow federal-borrowing exception remains dependent on those actual conditions. Section 1658 supplies no period for this 1988 cause.

**Authority by component:**

- Source and application of the WARN limitations period: [Source and application of the WARN limitations period](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/North_Star_Steel_v_Thomas_and_Crown_Cork_merits_1995-05-30.md#public-projection), 1995-05-30. Souter’s Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O’Connor, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer; eight Justices directly support the rule and application. Scalia joins only the judgment.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-05-30; the component authorities identified above.

**Open question:** No universal state period or guarantee of adequacy for every state rule is adopted. Exact state selection where necessary, accrual, tolling, arbitration, exhaustion and deferral remain separate.

**Holdings navigation:** [Source and application of the WARN limitations period](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#source-and-application-of-the-warn-limitations-period).

### Compensatory maritime prejudgment interest

**Governing question:** When an admiralty court may deny ordinary prejudgment interest.

**Current rule:** Prejudgment interest ordinarily compensates loss of use of an otherwise compensable amount. Departure requires a legally sufficient exceptional circumstance supported by the record. Mutual fault already reflected in principal damages and an ordinary genuine liability dispute, without more, do not justify denial; undue claimant-caused delay can qualify.

**Authority by component:**

- Mutual fault and ordinary liability uncertainty do not defeat maritime interest: [Mutual fault and ordinary liability uncertainty do not defeat maritime interest](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/City_of_Milwaukee_v_Cement_Division_National_Gypsum_Co_merits_1995-06-12.md#public-projection), 1995-06-12. Stevens's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; all eight participating Justices adopt the complete rule and affirm the calculation remand.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-12; the component authorities identified above.

**Open question:** No exhaustive exception list, liquidated-claim prerequisite, rate, period, compounding method or categorical municipal-financing exception is adopted.

**Holdings navigation:** [Mutual fault and ordinary liability uncertainty do not defeat maritime interest](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#mutual-fault-and-ordinary-liability-uncertainty-do-not-defeat-maritime-interest).

## Bankruptcy

### Ordinary-course eligibility of payments on long-term debt

**Governing question:** Whether debt maturity categorically excludes a payment from the contemporaneous §547(c)(2) preference exception.

**Current rule:** Payments on long-term debt may qualify; maturity supplies no additional categorical exclusion or presumption of ordinariness. The creditor bears §547(g)'s burden to prove all three then-current elements: the debt was incurred in the ordinary course of both parties, payment occurred in their ordinary course, and payment accorded with ordinary business terms. Eligibility alone does not establish any element or exempt all interest or commitment fees.

**Authority by component:**

- Long-term-debt eligibility and all retained statutory elements and burden: [Union Bank v. Wolas — Law of the decision, Holding and operative rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Union_Bank_v_Wolas_merits_1991-12-11.md), December 11, 1991; Stevens's unanimous opinion.

**Present operation effective:** *Wolas*, December 11, 1991.

**Holdings navigation:** [Union Bank v. Wolas, 502 U.S. 151 (1991) — Ordinary-course preference exception](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#union-bank-v-wolas-502-us-151-1991).

### Chapter 7 strip-down of allowed real-property liens under §506(d)

**Governing question:** Whether §506(d) reduces an allowed, lien-backed real-property debt to its current judicially determined collateral value in the Chapter 7 setting decided in *Dewsnup*.

**Current rule:** When the claim is allowed under §502 and secured by the real-property lien, §506(d) does not strip that lien down merely because §506(a) values the collateral below the allowed debt. Section 506(a) continues to value secured and unsecured amounts for treatment of the claim; that valuation does not automatically avoid the corresponding lien under §506(d). The rule distinguishes personal discharge from lien survival and does not immunize liens from an independently applicable express avoidance provision.

**Authority by component:**

- Limited Chapter 7 construction and continued valuation function: [Dewsnup v. Timm — Allowed real-property liens in the presented Chapter 7 setting](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Dewsnup_v_Timm_merits_1992-01-15.md), January 15, 1992; Blackmun's opinion joined by White, Stevens, O'Connor, and Kennedy.

**Present operation effective:** *Dewsnup*, January 15, 1992.

**Open question:** Other statutory settings, reorganization restrictions, express statutory exceptions, and estate-administration issues are not resolved by this construction. It does not adopt an abandonment rationale.

**Holdings navigation:** [Dewsnup v. Timm, 502 U.S. 410 (1992) — Liens and claim valuation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#dewsnup-v-timm-502-us-410-1992).

### Federal sovereign immunity from affirmative bankruptcy money judgments

**Governing question:** Whether original §106(c), estate-property jurisdiction, or property and trust characterizations authorize an affirmative monetary recovery from the United States under §550.

**Current rule:** Original §106(c) does not unequivocally waive immunity from an affirmative personal money judgment recovering an unauthorized transfer under §550. A plausible reading under which its application-and-binding language governs nonmonetary or claim determinations defeats clear consent to that remedy; legislative history cannot supply missing enacted clarity. Sections 106(a) and (b) retain their own claim-linked and offset operations. General estate-property jurisdiction under §1334(d), an asserted trust interest, and the money's origin in estate assets do not supply the missing consent. An order requiring payment of a sum, rather than adjudicating identified property under the court's control, cannot be preserved by calling it a property proceeding or defensive equitable recoupment.

**Authority by component:**

- Original §106(c) monetary-waiver construction: [United States v. Nordic Village — Original §106(c) does not clearly authorize this monetary recovery](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_Nordic_Village_merits_1992-02-25.md), February 25, 1992.
- Distinction between jurisdiction and consent; inadequacy of property, trust, and recoupment labels: [Nordic Village — Jurisdictional and property labels do not sustain this money judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_Nordic_Village_merits_1992-02-25.md), February 25, 1992. Scalia's opinion joined by Stone, White, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, and Thomas controls both.

**Present operation effective:** *Nordic Village*, February 25, 1992.

**Open question:** Genuinely distinct specific-property proceedings require their actual facts and independent authority; their validity is not decided. State immunity and a different express statutory waiver remain outside the decision.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Nordic Village, Inc., 503 U.S. 30 (1992) — Federal sovereign immunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-v-nordic-village-inc-503-us-30-1992).

### Ordinary-check transfer date for preference avoidance

**Governing question:** When an ordinary check transfers the debtor's bank-account interest for the preference period in 11 U.S.C. §547(b).

**Current rule:** The transfer occurs when the drawee honors the ordinary check, because delivery gives the payee no assigned interest in the debtor's account. Section 547(e) does not relate the transfer back to a time before that interest passed. The trustee must still establish the other preference elements, and properly presented statutory defenses retain their separate operation. The construction does not decide transfer timing under §547(c) or for nonordinary payment instruments.

**Authority by component:**
- Honor-date construction and preserved boundaries: [Barnhill v. Johnson](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Barnhill_v_Johnson_merits_1992-03-25.md), March 25, 1992, controlling holding 1, “Honor determines the §547(b) transfer”; Stone for White, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** Barnhill, March 25, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [Barnhill v. Johnson, 503 U.S. 393 (1992) — Preference transfer timing](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#barnhill-v-johnson-503-us-393-1992).

### Finality of an unchallenged scheduled exemption

**Governing question:** Whether a trustee may contest a scheduled exemption after the applicable Bankruptcy Rule 4003(b) deadline because the claim lacked a colorable statutory basis.

**Current rule:** Under 11 U.S.C. §522(l) and the applicable Rule 4003(b), property claimed as exempt becomes exempt if no party in interest objects within 30 days after the creditors' meeting concludes or a relevant amendment to the exemption list is filed, whichever is later, and no further time is granted within that period. Rule 9006(b)(3) allows enlargement only on Rule 4003(b)'s conditions. Lack of a colorable substantive exemption supplies no exception permitting a later objection. Uncertainty about a scheduled lawsuit's value permits timely inquiry and objection; it does not require waiting for collection. Properly supported misconduct remedies retain their own conditions and do not supply an adjudicated late-objection exception here.

**Authority by component:**
- Deadline, extension, no-objection consequence and rejection of a colorability exception: [Taylor v. Freeland & Kronz](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Taylor_v_Freeland_and_Kronz_merits_1992-04-21.md), April 21, 1992, “Untimely objection to a claimed exemption,” Thomas for Stone, White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter, eight votes.

**Present operation effective:** Taylor, April 21, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** The applicable historical deadline governs; the later supplemental-schedule wording does not revive its expiry. Rule 4003(c)'s objector burden remains for timely, properly presented proceedings. This decision concerns the scheduled proceeds claimed and does not determine the scope of differently limited exemption language.

**Open question:** Section 105(a), unpreserved fraud, unscheduled assets, respondents' alternative valuation/lien/good-faith theories, sanctions and retroactivity remain unresolved.

**Holdings navigation:** [Taylor v. Freeland & Kronz, 503 U.S. 638 (1992) — Exemption objections](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#taylor-v-freeland--kronz-503-us-638-1992).

### Federal-law transfer restrictions and exclusion of trust interests

**Governing question:** Exclusion of a debtor's beneficial trust interest from the estate under 11 U.S.C. §541(c)(2).

**Current rule:** Applicable nonbankruptcy law includes federal law. A transfer restriction on a debtor's beneficial interest in a trust is preserved when enforceable under that law; an established ERISA-qualified trust with an ERISA-enforceable antialienation restriction is therefore excluded from the estate. Exclusion is distinct from exemption and does not change exemption deadlines or adjudicate statutory avoidance remedies.

**Authority by component:**
- Federal-law construction and qualifying trust exclusion: [Patterson v. Shumate, “Holding 1: An enforceable federal pension restriction excludes this beneficial trust interest”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Patterson_v_Shumate_merits_1992-06-15.md), June 15, 1992; Blackmun, joined by White, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas (eight).

**Present operation effective:** Patterson v. Shumate, June 15, 1992.

**Open question:** Other plans, nontrust interests, statutory exceptions, distributed benefits, §522(b)(2)(A) and the payment-versus-corpus scope of §522(d)(10)(E) are not decided.

**Holdings navigation:** [Patterson v. Shumate, 504 U.S. 753 (1992) — Estate exclusions for pension trust interests](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#patterson-v-shumate-504-us-753-1992).

### Excusable neglect under Rule 9006(b)(1)

**Governing question:** What instructions govern excusable neglect under Rule 9006(b)(1)?

**Current rule:** For a Chapter 11 claim deadline subject to Rule 9006(b)(1), neglect includes inadvertence, mistake and carelessness as well as events beyond control. Post-expiration relief requires a motion and excusability assessed from all relevant circumstances, including debtor prejudice, delay and administrative effects, reason including reasonable control, and good faith. No consideration invariably decides the issue. Freely chosen counsel's conduct is fully attributed to the client; reliance alone is no exception. Under then-effective (b)(2), enlargement is barred for Rules 1007(d), 1017(b)(3), 2003(a) and (d), 7052, 9023 and 9024. Under (b)(3), enlargement is limited to the terms of Rules 1006(b)(2), 1017(e), 3002(c), 4003(b), 4004(a), 4007(c), 8002 and 9033. Thus Chapter 7 claim limits and Taylor's exemption-objection rule gain no general equitable exception.

**Authority by component:**

- 1. Excusable neglect can include carelessness: [Pioneer Investment Services Co. v. Brunswick Associates Limited Partnership — 1. Excusable neglect can include carelessness](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Pioneer_Investment_Services_Co_v_Brunswick_Associates_Limited_Partnership_merits_1993-03-24.md), 1993-03-24. Stone-Zsela's opinion for the Court, joined by White, Blackmun, Stevens and Kennedy: five.
- 2. Counsel's conduct is attributed to the creditor: [Pioneer Investment Services Co. v. Brunswick Associates Limited Partnership — 2. Counsel's conduct is attributed to the creditor](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Pioneer_Investment_Services_Co_v_Brunswick_Associates_Limited_Partnership_merits_1993-03-24.md), 1993-03-24. Stone-Zsela's Court opinion, joined by White, Blackmun, Stevens and Kennedy: five; the dissent does not dispute attorney attribution, but its disagreement about excusability does not create another rationale majority.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-24; Pioneer Investment Services Co. v. Brunswick Associates Limited Partnership, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Pioneer Investment Services Co. v. Brunswick Associates Limited Partnership, 507 U.S. 380 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#pioneer-investment-services-co-v-brunswick-associates-limited-partnership-507-us-380-1993).

### Principal-residence mortgage rights in Chapter 13

**Governing question:** What instructions govern principal-residence mortgage rights in Chapter 13?

**Current rule:** When an allowed claim secured only by the debtor's principal-residence real property retains a secured portion, §1322(b)(2) protects the holder's mortgage rights as a whole despite §506(a) valuation and bifurcation. The debtor cannot modify the deficiency portion by altering protected repayment or enforcement rights. Independent restrictions and §1322(b)(5) remain: cure within a reasonable time and maintenance during the case are permitted when the last claim payment falls after the final plan payment, not general rewriting. The stay remains distinct. Wholly unsecured liens and classification of ancillary deed provisions are undecided; Dewsnup's Chapter 7 rule remains separate.

**Authority by component:**

- Valuation does not remove the protected rights of this mortgage holder: [Nobelman v. American Savings Bank — Valuation does not remove the protected rights of this mortgage holder](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/nobelman_merits_1993-06-01.md), 1993-06-01. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-01; Nobelman v. American Savings Bank, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Nobelman v. American Savings Bank, 508 U.S. 324 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#nobelman-v-american-savings-bank-508-us-324-1993).

### Oversecured interest and present value of deferred cure arrearages

**Governing question:** What instructions govern oversecured interest and present value of deferred cure arrearages?

**Current rule:** Section 506(b) allows interest on an allowed secured claim within collateral value exceeding the claim after §506(c) recovery, even without contractual arrearage interest; reasonable fees, costs and charges separately require agreement support. Where §1325(a)(5)(B) governs a deferred secured-arrearage cure over creditor objection with retained collateral, lien retention and effective-date present value of at least the allowed claim are required. Section 1322(b)(5)'s cure-and-maintenance authority does not exempt arrearages; acceptance and surrender are separate alternatives. The parties in Rake accepted §506(b) through confirmation; the Court selects no rate or universal undersecured-mortgage rule.

**Authority by component:**

- Section 506(b) supplies interest on these oversecured arrearages: [Rake v. Wade — Section 506(b) supplies interest on these oversecured arrearages](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/rake_merits_1993-06-07.md), 1993-06-07. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.
- Deferred cure payments must preserve present value under the applicable confirmation alternative: [Rake v. Wade — Deferred cure payments must preserve present value under the applicable confirmation alternative](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/rake_merits_1993-06-07.md), 1993-06-07. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-07; Rake v. Wade, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Rake v. Wade, 508 U.S. 464 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#rake-v-wade-508-us-464-1993).

### Reasonably equivalent value in a forced real-estate sale

**Governing question:** How §548(a)(2) measures value at a mortgage foreclosure.

**Current rule:** Compliance with state foreclosure procedures does not conclusively establish reasonably equivalent value. Compare the price with value under realistically comparable forced-sale conditions, accounting for legally and practically relevant sale constraints. Procedure is relevant evidence, but ordinary open-market appraisal alone is not the comparison and hindsight alone does not establish avoidability. The challenger retains the burden of proving the statutory avoidance predicates, including inadequate value and the independently applicable timing and financial-condition requirements. No mandatory evidence checklist, fixed percentage, categorical price floor or presumption from a low appraisal ratio replaces that inquiry.

**Authority by component:**

- Independent federal value inquiry and comparable forced-sale conditions: [BFP v. Resolution Trust Corp — Regular foreclosure procedure does not conclusively establish federal value; Value is compared under realistically comparable forced-sale conditions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/BFP_v_Resolution_Trust_Corp_merits_1994-05-23.md#public-projection), 1994-05-23. Souter's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Ginsburg; five Justices directly adopt the proposition and explanation. No fractured-decision synthesis is needed.

**Present operation effective:** BFP v. Resolution Trust Corp, 1994-05-23.

**Open question:** Tax sales, personal-property foreclosures and actual-fraud theories are not resolved. Regular foreclosure is neither a conclusive safe harbor nor automatically avoidable.

**Holdings navigation:** [Regular foreclosure procedure does not conclusively establish federal value](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#regular-foreclosure-procedure-does-not-conclusively-establish-federal-value); [Value is compared under realistically comparable forced-sale conditions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#value-is-compared-under-realistically-comparable-forced-sale-conditions).

### Interim restraint and orderly review in related bankruptcy proceedings

**Governing question:** Collateral displacement of an operative §105 restraint in a proceeding actually related to Chapter 11.

**Current rule:** In a related proceeding supported by an actual bond, retained collateral and reorganization connection, §1334(b) supplies nonexclusive subject-matter jurisdiction; §105 supplies remedial authority, not independent jurisdiction. Section 157(c)(1)’s limit on final noncore adjudication does not eliminate sufficient authority for an interlocutory preservation restraint. Where actual relatedness, sufficient interim authority, substantial support for the applicable restraint and a concrete relief-and-review mechanism are established, a party must challenge it through the issuing proceeding and authorized review rather than execute contrary to it elsewhere while it remains effective.

**Authority by component:**

- Immediate execution on this bond is related to the Chapter 11 case: [Immediate execution on this bond is related to the Chapter 11 case](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Celotex_Corp_v_Edwards_merits_1995-04-19.md#public-projection), 1995-04-19. This portion of Justice Thomas's opinion of the Court is joined by Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Breyer: six direct full-rationale votes, including the author. Chief Justice Stone-Zsela does not join this stronger resolution and concurs in the judgment on his separate colorable-jurisdiction ground. Stevens and Ginsburg's acceptance of district-court relatedness is not a join in this complete rationale.
- Section 157 permits this interim restraint without a final core determination: [Section 157 permits this interim restraint without a final core determination](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Celotex_Corp_v_Edwards_merits_1995-04-19.md#public-projection), 1995-04-19. This portion of Justice Thomas's opinion of the Court is joined by Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Breyer: six direct full-rationale votes, including the author. Chief Justice Stone-Zsela does not join this stronger resolution and concurs in the judgment on his separate colorable-jurisdiction ground. Stevens and Ginsburg's acceptance of district-court relatedness is not a join in this complete rationale.
- This still-operative restraint must be challenged through orderly relief and review: [This still-operative restraint must be challenged through orderly relief and review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Celotex_Corp_v_Edwards_merits_1995-04-19.md#public-projection), 1995-04-19. This portion of Justice Thomas's opinion of the Court is joined by Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Breyer: six direct full-rationale votes, including the author. Chief Justice Stone-Zsela does not join this stronger resolution and concurs in the judgment on his separate colorable-jurisdiction ground. Stevens and Ginsburg's acceptance of district-court relatedness is not a join in this complete rationale.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-04-19; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Relatedness is not final core authority, a core label is not dispositive and management difficulty alone is insufficient. Final classification, consent, ultimate statutory and equitable validity, evidentiary support for continuation and duration remain separate. United Mine Workers preserves its exclusion for a frivolous and insubstantial jurisdictional claim; Walker preserves transparent-invalidity or frivolous-pretense and timely-but-frustrated-review reservations. They are not universal licenses to disregard orders. No contempt or sanction-survival rule is adjudicated.

**Sequence or decisionmaker:** For a noncore related proceeding, §157(c)(1) reserves final order to the district judge after considering proposed findings and reviewing de novo matters timely and specifically objected to. All-party consent under (c)(2) is a separate route. Applicable §158 finality and leave requirements and an independently qualifying §1292 route remain; an available motion for relief is not a promise of immediate circuit review. Statutory withdrawal of reference retains its own conditions.

**Holdings navigation:** [Immediate execution on this bond is related to the Chapter 11 case](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#immediate-execution-on-this-bond-is-related-to-the-chapter-11-case); [Section 157 permits this interim restraint without a final core determination](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#section-157-permits-this-interim-restraint-without-a-final-core-determination); [This still-operative restraint must be challenged through orderly relief and review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#this-still-operative-restraint-must-be-challenged-through-orderly-relief-and-review).

## Civil RICO and securities liquidation

### Direct relation between a civil RICO violation and injury

**Governing question:** Causation for damages under 18 U.S.C. §1964(c).

**Current rule:** A plaintiff must establish factual causation and proximate cause, including some direct relation between the injury asserted and the alleged §1962 violation. Directness is a contextual requirement informed by attribution difficulties, overlapping recovery and available directly injured plaintiffs; those considerations explain the inherited requirement and are not separate newly required elements. Factual connection, foreseeable loss and an obligation to advance money alone are insufficient. Subrogation changes the claimant's identity without making the underlying injury more direct. The construction imports no antitrust-injury requirement, special racketeering injury or prior-conviction requirement, and creates no exceptionless bar to every third-party injury.

**Authority by component:**
- Factual and proximate cause, contextual directness and preserved limits: [Holmes v. Securities Investor Protection Corp.](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Holmes_v_Securities_Investor_Protection_Corp_merits_1992-03-24.md), March 24, 1992, “Civil RICO requires a sufficiently direct causal relation,” Part II.
- Subrogation cannot improve the directness of the asserted injury: the same record, “The nonpurchasing customers' derivative losses are too remote,” Part III-A. Souter for Stone, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Thomas controls both propositions; Scalia's judgment-only rationale is distinct.

**Present operation effective:** Holmes, March 24, 1992.

**Open question:** Whether securities-fraud RICO plaintiffs must be purchasers or sellers remains undecided; separate opinions rejecting that restriction do not create a Court holding.

**Holdings navigation:** [Holmes v. Securities Investor Protection Corp., 503 U.S. 258 (1992) — Civil RICO causation and SIPA remedies](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#holmes-v-securities-investor-protection-corp-503-us-258-1992).

### SIPA participation is not an independent damages action

**Governing question:** Whether 15 U.S.C. §78eee(d) authorizes SIPC's recovery of liquidation administration advances as damages.

**Current rule:** Section 78eee(d) provides party-in-interest status, a right to be heard and deemed intervention in liquidation matters; it does not itself create an independent damages right for administration advances. The duty to fund administration under §78fff-3(b)(2) does not turn participation into a reimbursement action. Combining §78eee(d) with §1964(c) neither supplies a missing substantive recovery right nor dispenses with RICO's own conditions. Participation remains effective despite failure of that damages theory, and independently grounded claims under other law are not categorically foreclosed.

**Authority by component:**
- Provision-specific participation/recovery distinction: [Holmes v. Securities Investor Protection Corp.](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Holmes_v_Securities_Investor_Protection_Corp_merits_1992-03-24.md), March 24, 1992, “SIPA participation rights do not create damages liability,” Part III-B; Souter for Stone, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** Holmes, March 24, 1992.

**Open question:** The merits of administration-expense subrogation to trustees were not decided.

**Holdings navigation:** [Holmes v. Securities Investor Protection Corp., 503 U.S. 258 (1992) — Civil RICO causation and SIPA remedies](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#holmes-v-securities-investor-protection-corp-503-us-258-1992).

### Direction of enterprise affairs under §1962(c)

**Governing question:** What instructions govern direction of enterprise affairs under §1962(c)?

**Current rule:** Section 1962(c) requires some role in operating or managing the enterprise itself through the prohibited racketeering pattern, not merely one's own affairs or service provision. Primary responsibility and formal executive status are unnecessary. An outsider who actually directs enterprise affairs can qualify, as can lower participants operating under upper management; the exact lower-rung boundary remains open. No professional immunity, general aiding-and-abetting rule, conspiracy construction or lenity alternative is adopted. Holmes's civil direct-causation requirement remains independent.

**Authority by component:**

- Some role in directing the enterprise is necessary, without an executive-status requirement: [Reves v. Ernst & Young — Some role in directing the enterprise is necessary, without an executive-status requirement](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/reves-v-ernst-young-merits-1993-03-03.md), 1993-03-03. The statutory-construction portion of Blackmun's Opinion of the Court is joined by Stone, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas: seven Justices adopt the stated rule at the same level of generality. Souter and White reject the directional requirement.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-03; Reves v. Ernst & Young, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Reves v. Ernst & Young, 507 U.S. 170 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#reves-v-ernst--young-507-us-170-1993).

### Contribution within the received Rule 10b-5 action

**Governing question:** What instructions govern contribution within the received Rule 10b-5 action?

**Current rule:** Persons jointly liable for the same violation under the established implied Rule 10b-5 action may seek contribution subject to proof of shared underlying liability and applicable allocation requirements. This is an incident of that received action, not new professional liability, automatic aiding-and-abetting liability, indemnity or contribution under unrelated statutes.

**Authority by component:**

- Contribution is available among persons jointly liable under the received Rule10b-5 action: [Musick, Peeler & Garrett v. Employers Insurance of Wausau — Contribution is available among persons jointly liable under the received Rule10b-5 action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/musick_merits_1993-06-01.md), 1993-06-01. Kennedy’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter (6 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-01; Musick, Peeler & Garrett v. Employers Insurance of Wausau, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Musick, Peeler & Garrett v. Employers Insurance of Wausau, 508 U.S. 286 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#musick-peeler--garrett-v-employers-insurance-of-wausau-508-us-286-1993).

### No economic-motive element in §1962(c)

**Governing question:** Whether a noneconomic enterprise or purpose falls categorically outside §1962(c).

**Current rule:** Section 1962(c) adds no economic or profit-seeking motive requirement for the enterprise or predicate acts. Enterprise, commerce, direction, predicate offenses, pattern, causation, individual responsibility and constitutional limits remain independently necessary; a dependent §1962(d) claim cannot be rejected on that invented motive requirement. Obstruction, coercion and extortion remain distinct, including the Hobbs Act’s obtaining requirement when invoked.

**Authority by component:**

- Absence of economic-motive element and unchanged predicates: [National Organization for Women, Inc. v. Scheidler — Section 1962(c) has no added economic-motive element](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/National_Organization_for_Women_Inc_v_Scheidler_merits_1994-01-24.md#public-projection), 1994-01-24.
