Blackmun's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; nine Justices adopt the same statutory construction and its limited remedial consequence.

**Present operation effective:** National Organization for Women, Inc. v. Scheidler, 1994-01-24.

**Related current rules:** “Direction of enterprise affairs under §1962(c)” and “Direct relation between a civil RICO violation and injury” retain their distinct requirements.

**Open question:** Private RICO injunctive relief, actual First Amendment defenses and §1962(a)’s income requirement are not decided.

**Holdings navigation:** [Section 1962(c) has no added economic-motive element](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#section-1962c-has-no-added-economic-motive-element).

### Culpable affirmative assistance to a securities fraud

**Governing question:** When the received private §10(b) and Rule 10b-5 action reaches a person assisting another’s fraud.

**Current rule:** A person who knowingly or with severe recklessness substantially assists an underlying securities fraud through affirmative conduct can be privately liable. The defendant must know or severely recklessly disregard both the activity’s fraudulent character and its conduct’s assisting role. Severe recklessness is an extreme departure from ordinary care presenting a specific danger of deception known to the defendant or so obvious that the defendant must have appreciated it. An independent disclosure duty is unnecessary for affirmative assistance. The plaintiff must prove the underlying manipulation or deception, qualifying assistance and culpability, legally sufficient reliance on the underlying fraud, causation and recoverable loss by the ordinary civil preponderance standard. Investors need not have known of or relied on concealed assistance itself; no reliance presumption follows merely from this rule.

**Authority by component:**

- Received action, mental state, underlying-fraud reliance and proof: [Central Bank of Denver, N.A. v. First Interstate Bank of Denver, N.A — The received private action includes culpable substantial assistance; Knowledge or severe recklessness in affirmative substantial assistance; Triable issues require further proceedings, not a liability award](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Central_Bank_of_Denver_v_First_Interstate_Bank_merits_1994-04-19.md#public-projection), 1994-04-19. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Stone, Blackmun, Souter and Ginsburg; five Justices adopt the existence of the confined action and its received-remedy rationale. No fractured-decision synthesis is necessary. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Souter and Ginsburg; all five adopt this confined mental-state and affirmative-assistance rule, not a universal recklessness rule for every securities claim. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Souter and Ginsburg; five Justices adopt the record application, preserved proof requirements and limited affirmance and remand.

**Present operation effective:** Central Bank of Denver, N.A. v. First Interstate Bank of Denver, N.A, 1994-04-19.

**Exceptions and limits:** Negligence, valuation concerns alone, professional status, ordinary services without qualifying culpability and assisting effect, and mere duty-free silence are insufficient. Relabeling inaction does not make it affirmative. No primary misrepresentation by the assistant, general investigation duty, liability from default, universal securities scienter rule or comprehensive SEC-enforcement rule follows.

**Open question:** The full rules for duty-based omissions and consciously intended assistance through duty-free inaction remain open. Contribution separately requires established shared liability under Musick, Peeler.

**Holdings navigation:** [The received private action includes culpable substantial assistance](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-received-private-action-includes-culpable-substantial-assistance); [Knowledge or severe recklessness in affirmative substantial assistance](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#knowledge-or-severe-recklessness-in-affirmative-substantial-assistance); [Triable issues require further proceedings, not a liability award](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#triable-issues-require-further-proceedings-not-a-liability-award).

### Bespoke business acquisitions outside §12(2)’s prospectus setting

**Governing question:** Section 12(2) coverage of an individually negotiated closely held business acquisition.

**Current rule:** A bespoke, privately negotiated agreement through which sophisticated purchasers with direct enterprise access acquire substantially all stock in a closely held business and allocate disclosure and valuation risk through individualized terms falls outside §§2(10), 10 and 12’s prospectus-disclosure setting. Sophistication alone, registration exemption alone or a price-adjustment remedy creates no exemption. Merely speaking that agreement’s terms does not evade the common prospectus setting.

**Authority by component:**

- The negotiated acquisition agreement is outside section 12(2): [The negotiated acquisition agreement is outside section 12(2)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Gustafson_v_Alloyd_Co_merits_1995-02-28.md#public-projection), 1995-02-28. Souter's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor and Kennedy. These five directly adopt this confined proposition and its contextual explanation. The broader public-offering-only construction has four supporters and is not the Court's rule. No Marks inference supplies the five joins.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-02-28; the component authorities identified above.

**Open question:** Standardized exempt or institutional distributions and other oral-solicitation settings remain unresolved; there is no controlling public-offering-only rule.

**Exceptions and limits:** Where §12(2) applies, its interstate-facilities or mails nexus, material falsehood or contextually misleading omission, purchaser ignorance and purchase from the statutory seller remain. The seller bears proof of no knowledge and inability to discover through reasonable care. Tender permits consideration plus interest less income; damages are available if the security is no longer owned. Coverage of exempt securities retains §3(a)(2)’s cross-referenced exclusion, whose precise scope is not construed here. No fraud, actual-reliance or sophistication element is added. Independent contract, fraud and securities claims retain their own requirements.

**Holdings navigation:** [The negotiated acquisition agreement is outside section 12(2)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-negotiated-acquisition-agreement-is-outside-section-122).

### Direct enterprise engagement in interstate commerce

**Governing question:** The direct-engagement alternative under §1962(a).

**Current rule:** An enterprise engages in interstate commerce when it directly participates in interstate acquisition, production or distribution of goods or services. Actual cross-border procurement and repeated interstate recruitment and movement for enterprise work, together with carriage across state lines of the enterprise’s output, can establish that operation without proving that the particular enterprise substantially affects interstate commerce.

**Authority by component:**

- The mine’s own interstate operations satisfy the enacted enterprise-commerce condition: [The mine’s own interstate operations satisfy the enacted enterprise-commerce condition](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/United_States_v_Robertson_merits_1995-05-01.md#public-projection), 1995-05-01. Justice Breyer’s opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg: nine direct joins in the rule, cumulative application and bounded remedy.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-05-01; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** The affecting-commerce alternative and its constitutional reach remain undecided. No procurement-only sufficient rule or attribution of every proprietor’s private act to the enterprise is adopted. Section 1962(a)’s separate income, participation and investment elements remain.

**Controlling construction:** The separate open-market securities-investment qualification requires no intent to control, participate in control or assist control; postpurchase aggregate holdings of the purchaser, immediate family and racketeering or unlawful-debt accomplices below one percent of any outstanding class; and no legal or actual power to elect a director. It is not an exemption for ordinary ownership and operation of an enterprise.

**Holdings navigation:** [The mine’s own interstate operations satisfy the enacted enterprise-commerce condition](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-mines-own-interstate-operations-satisfy-the-enacted-enterprise-commerce-condition).

## Antitrust

### Equipment competition and single-brand aftermarket power

**Governing question:** Whether equipment-market competition or brand identity categorically defeats Sherman Act claims concerning parts and service aftermarkets.

**Current rule:** Equipment competition is relevant but does not as a matter of law foreclose power in parts or service markets. Summary judgment cannot rest solely on an economic prediction contradicted by evidence supporting a rational inference of power, including material information or switching costs and actual price or exclusion effects; unsupported speculation remains insufficient. One brand's parts or service may constitute a relevant market when actual substitutes and competitive restraints support that definition. Brand identity alone neither establishes nor defeats the market. Sufficient separate consumer demand may distinguish functionally related parts and service; the ordinary tying and monopolization elements and Rule 56 burdens remain independently necessary.

**Authority by component:**
- Separate-demand background: [Eastman Kodak Co. v. Image Technical Services, Inc., “1. Distinct parts and service demand and conditional sales present a triable tying claim”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Eastman_Kodak_Co_v_Image_Technical_Services_Inc_merits_1992-06-08.md), June 8, 1992, applying Jefferson Parish.
- No categorical equipment-competition defense: Eastman Kodak, “2. Equipment-market competition is relevant but not a categorical defense,” June 8, 1992.
- Single-brand market possibility: Eastman Kodak, “3. A single brand may define a relevant market when actual substitutes do not constrain it,” June 8, 1992.
- Each proposition: Blackmun, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, Kennedy and Souter (six).

**Present operation effective:** Eastman Kodak Co. v. Image Technical Services, Inc., June 8, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** No presumption of monopoly arises from a patent, proprietary design, brand identity, numerical share alone or imperfect information. Power, exclusionary acquisition or maintenance, antitrust injury and damages remain separate. No general duty to supply competitors is adopted.

**Holdings navigation:** [Eastman Kodak Co. v. Image Technical Services, Inc., 504 U.S. 451 (1992) — Tying and aftermarket monopolization](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#eastman-kodak-co-v-image-technical-services-inc-504-us-451-1992).

### Actual state supervision of private rate fixing

**Governing question:** State-action immunity for privately agreed rates that become effective absent a state veto.

**Current rule:** The private claimant must establish both a clearly articulated state policy and actual supervision of the particular restraint. State officials must exercise enough independent substantive judgment and control to make the rate scheme a product of deliberate state intervention; a legally available veto, functioning regulatory machinery, nominal approval or the legal effect of nonobjection alone does not suffice. The inquiry addresses actual substantive examination, not a mandatory regulatory form.

**Authority by component:**
- Active-supervision requirement: [FTC v. Ticor Title Insurance Co., “1. Private rate fixing requires actual substantive state supervision”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/FTC_v_Ticor_Title_Insurance_Co_merits_1992-06-12.md), June 12, 1992; Kennedy Parts II–III, joined by White, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia and Souter (six).

**Present operation effective:** FTC v. Ticor Title Insurance Co., June 12, 1992.

**Open question:** Distinct sovereign action, materially different comprehensive supervision, infrequent lapses, other insurance-law exemptions and any difference in FTC and Sherman Act immunity remain undecided. The existing statutory conclusiveness of supported Commission findings is not changed.

**Holdings navigation:** [FTC v. Ticor Title Insurance Co., 504 U.S. 621 (1992) — State-action immunity for private rate fixing](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#ftc-v-ticor-title-insurance-co-504-us-621-1992-1).

### Independent dangerous probability in attempted monopolization

**Governing question:** What instructions govern independent dangerous probability in attempted monopolization?

**Current rule:** Sherman Act §2 attempted monopolization requires anticompetitive or predatory conduct, specific intent to monopolize and dangerous probability of monopoly power, evaluated through relevant product and geographic market and economic power there. Conduct may support permissible inferences, but conduct and intent cannot replace independent dangerous-probability proof. No market-share minimum, small-firm immunity or prohibition of circumstantial proof follows; Kodak's empirical market inquiry remains separate and operative.

**Authority by component:**

- Conduct and intent cannot replace dangerous probability: [Spectrum Sports v. McQuillan — Conduct and intent cannot replace dangerous probability](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Spectrum_Sports_v_McQuillan_merits_1993-01-25.md), 1993-01-25. White’s opinion of the Court for Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas; nine Justices adopt the rule and its explanation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-01-25; Spectrum Sports v. McQuillan, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Spectrum Sports v. McQuillan, 506 U.S. 447 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#spectrum-sports-v-mcquillan-506-us-447-1993).

### Objective baselessness before single-suit sham motive

**Governing question:** What instructions govern objective baselessness before single-suit sham motive?

**Current rule:** Under the presented single-lawsuit sham theory, Noerr protection fails only if the action is objectively baseless so no reasonable litigant could realistically expect merits success, and then is shown to use governmental process itself rather than its outcome as an anticompetitive weapon. An objectively reasonable suit ends that inquiry without motive discovery. Even a sham requires ordinary antitrust elements. Fraud, serial proceedings and independent conduct remain unresolved.

**Authority by component:**

- A single suit must be objectively baseless before motive defeats petitioning protection: [Professional Real Estate Investors, Inc. v. Columbia Pictures Industries, Inc. — A single suit must be objectively baseless before motive defeats petitioning protection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/pre_merits_1993-05-03.md), 1993-05-03. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (7 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-05-03; Professional Real Estate Investors, Inc. v. Columbia Pictures Industries, Inc., for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Professional Real Estate Investors, Inc. v. Columbia Pictures Industries, Inc., 508 U.S. 49 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#professional-real-estate-investors-inc-v-columbia-pictures-industries-inc-508-us-49-1993).

### Below-cost pricing and prospective recoupment

**Governing question:** What instructions govern below-cost pricing and prospective recoupment?

**Current rule:** A primary-line predatory-pricing claim requires pricing below an appropriate measure of the defendant's costs and a reasonable prospect of recouping that investment through anticompetitive returns. Tacit oligopolistic coordination can provide the mechanism; actual success, express conspiracy and single-firm monopoly are not indispensable. Intent, concentration and harm to a rival alone are insufficient. The appropriate cost measure is not comprehensively selected; attempted monopoly, secondary-line discrimination and other exclusionary conduct retain distinct elements.

**Authority by component:**

- Below-cost pricing must carry a prospect of recoupment: [Brooke Group v. Brown & Williamson — Below-cost pricing must carry a prospect of recoupment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Brooke_Group_v_Brown_and_Williamson_merits_1993-06-21.md), 1993-06-21. Kennedy’s Opinion of the Court, Below-cost pricing must carry a prospect of recoupment portion; O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (5 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-21; Brooke Group v. Brown & Williamson, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Brooke Group v. Brown & Williamson, 509 U.S. 209 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#brooke-group-v-brown--williamson-509-us-209-1993).

### Activity-specific insurance exemption and collateral boycott

**Governing question:** What instructions govern activity-specific insurance exemption and collateral boycott?

**Current rule:** Otherwise qualifying, state-regulated business of insurance does not lose §2(b) McCarran-Ferguson antitrust protection merely through agreement with foreign reinsurers. Activity and regulation must qualify; nationality alone is not decisive. Under §3(b), concerted refusal of collateral or unrelated transactions to compel terms in a targeted transaction may be boycott; joint insistence on terms of the very transaction refused is not without more. Whether purportedly other business genuinely affects the reinsured risk remains relevant. Exemption and boycott do not establish antitrust liability.

**Authority by component:**

- Insurance exemption follows activity rather than association: [Hartford Fire Insurance v. California — Insurance exemption follows activity rather than association](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Hartford_Fire_Insurance_v_California_merits_1993-06-28.md), 1993-06-28. Souter’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.
- Collateral refusals distinguish boycott from agreement on terms: [Hartford Fire Insurance v. California — Collateral refusals distinguish boycott from agreement on terms](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Hartford_Fire_Insurance_v_California_merits_1993-06-28.md), 1993-06-28. Scalia’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas (5 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-28; Hartford Fire Insurance v. California, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Hartford Fire Insurance v. California, 509 U.S. 764 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#hartford-fire-insurance-v-california-509-us-764-1993).

### American-market effects and asserted foreign-law permission

**Governing question:** What instructions govern american-market effects and asserted foreign-law permission?

**Current rule:** Foreign conduct intended to produce and producing substantial American insurance-market effects may be adjudicated under the Sherman Act; foreign permission without an incompatible duty or another established ground requiring nonapplication does not warrant the comity dismissal asserted in Hartford. No universal comity rule or impossibility-only test is adopted. Section 6a's precise applicability remains reserved. Where applicable, it excludes conduct involving trade or commerce with foreign nations other than import trade or import commerce unless the conduct has a direct, substantial, and reasonably foreseeable effect either on domestic or import trade or commerce, or on export trade or export commerce with foreign nations of a person engaged in that trade or commerce in the United States. Under paragraph (2), the qualifying effect must give rise to a claim under 15 U.S.C. §§1–7 other than §6a itself. If those sections apply only through the export-effect alternative in paragraph (1)(B), they apply only for injury to export business in the United States.

**Authority by component:**

- The asserted British permission does not bar these American-market claims: [Hartford Fire Insurance v. California — The asserted British permission does not bar these American-market claims](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Hartford_Fire_Insurance_v_California_merits_1993-06-28.md), 1993-06-28. Souter’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, Souter (5 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.

- Retained statutory terms, without deciding applicability: [15 U.S.C. §6a(1)(A)–(B), (2) and the final export-only limitation](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/15/6a), enacted by Pub. L. 97-290, title IV, §402, October 8, 1982, 96 Stat. 1246; the export alternative identifies the affected person and trade, paragraph (2) requires the claim connection, and the final sentence limits an export-only basis to injury to export business in the United States.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-28; Hartford Fire Insurance v. California, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Hartford Fire Insurance v. California, 509 U.S. 764 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#hartford-fire-insurance-v-california-509-us-764-1993).

## Federal taxation and governmental liability

### Federal income-tax duties of corporate assignees and separate liquidating trusts

**Governing question:** Who files and pays income tax after a bankruptcy plan transfers corporate property or property into a separate liquidating trust, and whether plan silence excuses later duties.

**Current rule:** Under §6012(b)(3), an assignee holding all or substantially all of a corporation's property must file its income-tax returns whether or not the corporation dissolves or the assignee operates the business; §6151(a) requires payment of tax due with the returns. A separate liquidating trust organized to liquidate and distribute transferred property has its own filing obligation under §6012(b)(4), carried out by a fiduciary whose powers satisfy §7701(a)(6) and Regulation §301.7701-6; restrictions on discretion alone do not eliminate fiduciary status. When estate property passes directly into the separate trust without revesting in the debtor followed by a debtor contribution, the asserted grantor-trust theory does not displace that duty. Confirmation under §1141(a) of a plan silent about taxes and government failure to object do not exempt the assignee or fiduciary from statutory return and payment obligations arising after appointment.

**Authority by component:**

- Corporate assignment category and accompanying payment: [Holywell Corp. v. Smith — Corporate assignee's filing and payment duty](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Holywell_Corp_v_Smith_merits_1992-02-25.md), February 25, 1992.
- Separate liquidating trust, fiduciary status, and direct-transfer boundary: [Holywell — Fiduciary of the separate liquidating trust](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Holywell_Corp_v_Smith_merits_1992-02-25.md), February 25, 1992.
- No prospective tax exemption from silent confirmation: [Holywell — A silent confirmed plan does not excuse prospective tax duties](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Holywell_Corp_v_Smith_merits_1992-02-25.md), February 25, 1992. Thomas's unanimous opinion controls all three components.

**Present operation effective:** *Holywell*, February 25, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Grantor-trust provisions retain force where their actual predicates exist. This rule does not prescribe identical duties for every bankruptcy trustee or decide taxes already due before appointment, express adjudication of existing tax claims, amounts, penalties, deductions, or collection details.

**Holdings navigation:** [Holywell Corp. v. Smith, 503 U.S. 47 (1992) — Bankruptcy fiduciaries’ filing and payment duties](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#holywell-corp-v-smith-503-us-47-1992).

### Separate assets and enduring benefits in capitalization

**Governing question:** Whether a separate and distinct asset is necessary to classify an expenditure as capital under §263.

**Current rule:** Creating or enhancing a separate and distinct asset suffices for capitalization but is not indispensable. The character, duration, and extent of the transaction's benefits also matter. Professional expenses directly facilitating a transaction with established substantial enduring corporate restructuring benefits may be capital even without a separately created asset. Mere incidental future usefulness does not compel capitalization of a routine expense; commercial reasonableness and necessity do not by themselves establish current deductibility under §162(a).

**Authority by component:**

- Nonexclusive asset ground, enduring-benefit inquiry, and incidental-benefit limit: [INDOPCO v. Commissioner — A separate asset is sufficient, but not necessary, for capitalization](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/INDOPCO_v_Commissioner_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992; Blackmun's unanimous opinion.

**Present operation effective:** *INDOPCO*, February 26, 1992.

**Open question:** No automatic classification of all acquisition or professional fees, hostile acquisitions, failed transactions, or particular later cost-recovery period is supplied.

**Holdings navigation:** [INDOPCO, Inc. v. Commissioner, 503 U.S. 79 (1992) — Capital expenditures](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#indopco-inc-v-commissioner-503-us-79-1992).

### The FTCA's punitive-damages exclusion

**Governing question:** What damages §2674 excludes as punitive.

**Current rule:** Section 2674 excludes damages whose legal nature is punishment for culpable wrongdoing, rather than every compensatory award claimed to be excessive, duplicative, or difficult for the injured person to perceive. Alleged duplication or unconsciousness alone does not turn compensation into punitive damages. Avoiding the federal exclusion does not establish entitlement or an amount; governing state compensation law and applicable offsets remain independently controlling.

**Authority by component:**

- Traditional punitive category, rejection of a broader punitive-effect test, and independent state-law entitlement: [Molzof v. United States — Law of the decision, Holding and operative rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Molzof_v_United_States_merits_1992-01-14.md), January 14, 1992; Thomas's unanimous opinion.

**Present operation effective:** *Molzof*, January 14, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [Molzof v. United States, 502 U.S. 301 (1992) — Federal Tort Claims Act damages](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#molzof-v-united-states-502-us-301-1992).

### Comparative principal place of business under §280A

**Governing question:** What instructions govern comparative principal place of business under §280A?

**Current rule:** Under then-effective §280A(c)(1)(A), principal place requires comparing relative importance of activities across locations and time spent. Customer contact or service delivery has great but nonconclusive weight; special facilities may add weight, and time is particularly useful when functions do not settle the comparison. Necessary administration, no other office and general §162 deductibility alone do not suffice. No location need qualify and the home does not qualify by default. Exclusive regular business use remains necessary. Client visits at home are not indispensable to subsection (A); normal-course dealings under (B) and qualifying unattached structures under (C) remain distinct. Employer convenience is an employee-only condition.

**Authority by component:**

- Controlling holding: [Commissioner v. Soliman — Controlling holding](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/commissioner-v-soliman-merits-1993-01-12.md), 1993-01-12. Kennedy’s Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O’Connor and Souter: six votes for this formulation and its application. Thomas and Scalia agree only with the judgment on their separately stated approach; Stevens dissents. The Court opinion controls without a fractured-decision inquiry.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-01-12; Commissioner v. Soliman, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Commissioner v. Soliman, 506 U.S. 168 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#commissioner-v-soliman-506-us-168-1993).

### The shareholder return and the assessment period

**Governing question:** What instructions govern the shareholder return and the assessment period?

**Current rule:** For individual income-tax assessment of an S corporation shareholder, the shareholder's return starts ordinary §6501(a) time, subject to applicable agreements and statutory exceptions. Expiration associated with the corporation's §6037 information return does not bar pass-through adjustment in a timely shareholder assessment. That return retains its corporate-tax function. Special audit regimes keep their terms; no enlargement beyond a lawful extension or exception follows.

**Authority by component:**

- Controlling holding: [Bufferd v. Commissioner of Internal Revenue — Controlling holding](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Bufferd_v_Commissioner_merits_1993-01-25.md), 1993-01-25. White’s opinion for the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Thomas. All nine Justices adopt this rule and its explanation; it controls by a direct majority.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-01-25; Bufferd v. Commissioner of Internal Revenue, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Bufferd v. Commissioner of Internal Revenue, 506 U.S. 523 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#bufferd-v-commissioner-of-internal-revenue-506-us-523-1993).

### Mineral-depletion basis and the prospective statutory exception

**Governing question:** What instructions govern mineral-depletion basis and the prospective statutory exception?

**Current rule:** For liabilities governed by former §57(a)(8), the percentage-depletion offset uses adjusted basis of the §614 mineral-deposit interest without that year's depletion deduction. Separately depreciable tangible drilling and development improvements under Regulation §1.612-4(c)(1) are excluded; lawfully capitalized intangible costs recoverable by depletion remain included. Under Energy Policy Act §1915(a), (d), for taxable years beginning after December 31, 1992, §57(a)(1) does not apply to deductions computed under §613A(c). Section 56(g)(4)(F)(i) otherwise uses §611 cost depletion for property placed in service in taxable years beginning after December 31, 1989; under (ii), for taxable years beginning after December 31, 1992, clause (i) and subparagraph (C)(i) do not apply to §613A(c) deductions. The year-beginning condition is not the return or decision date. No unrelated deduction exemption or individual eligibility is inferred, and the former rule survives for earlier governed liabilities.

**Authority by component:**

- Controlling holding: [United States v. Hill — Controlling holding](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/United_States_v_Hill_merits_1993-01-25.md), 1993-01-25. Souter’s opinion for the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, and Thomas. All nine Justices adopt this rule and its explanation; it controls by a direct majority.
- Prospective depletion exceptions and transition: [Energy Policy Act of 1992, §1915(a), (d)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Energy_Policy_Act_1992_depletion_amendment_1992-10-24.md), enacted October 24, 1992, applicable to taxable years beginning after December 31, 1992; §57(a)(1) and §56(g)(4)(F) retain distinct conditions.

**Present operation effective:** United States v. Hill, January 25, 1993, for construction of the former provision. Energy Policy Act §1915(a), (d), enacted October 24, 1992, governs its stated deductions for taxable years beginning after December 31, 1992; the enactment and taxable-year transition are distinct.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Hill, 506 U.S. 546 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-v-hill-506-us-546-1993).

### Federal lien notice and after-acquired property priority

**Governing question:** What instructions govern federal lien notice and after-acquired property priority?

**Current rule:** A filed federal tax lien has priority over an earlier docketed judgment lien in land acquired only after federal notice, because the judgment lien becomes choate in that land upon acquisition. Both attach on acquisition; priority follows the federal notice rule, not fictional prior attachment. Existing-property choateness and other statutory creditor protections remain distinct.

**Authority by component:**

- Federal notice precedes choateness in after-acquired property: [United States v. McDermott — Federal notice precedes choateness in after-acquired property](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/mcdermott_merits_1993-03-24.md), 1993-03-24. Scalia’s Opinion of the Court, I; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter (6 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-24; United States v. McDermott, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. McDermott, 507 U.S. 447 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-v-mcdermott-507-us-447-1993).

### Proof of a depreciable acquired intangible

**Governing question:** What instructions govern proof of a depreciable acquired intangible?

**Current rule:** Under §167 and Regulation §1.167(a)-3, an acquired intangible is depreciable when the taxpayer proves ascertainable value and a limited useful life estimable with reasonable accuracy, despite resemblance to expected continued patronage. The substantial proof burden remains with the taxpayer. Unsupported assertions, indefinite or self-regenerating value and double allocation fail; goodwill remains nondepreciable. INDOPCO's capitalization rule is unchanged.

**Authority by component:**

- Proof controls depreciation of the acquired customer intangible: [Newark Morning Ledger Co. v. United States — Proof controls depreciation of the acquired customer intangible](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/newark_merits_1993-04-20.md), 1993-04-20. Blackmun’s Opinion of the Court, I; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-04-20; Newark Morning Ledger Co. v. United States, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Newark Morning Ledger Co. v. United States, 507 U.S. 546 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#newark-morning-ledger-co-v-united-states-507-us-546-1993).

### Antarctic claims under the FTCA foreign-country exception

**Governing question:** What instructions govern antarctic claims under the FTCA foreign-country exception?

**Current rule:** Section 2680(k), read with the FTCA's liability and place-of-law provisions, excludes claims entirely based on Antarctic acts or omissions despite absence of another recognized sovereign. Sections 1346(b) and 2674 do not substitute the claimant's home-state law merely because residence supplies proper venue. No universal territorial bar to constitutional, maritime or other statutory channels follows; those waivers retain their terms.

**Authority by component:**

- The FTCA does not authorize this Antarctic tort action: [Smith v. United States (Antarctica) — The FTCA does not authorize this Antarctic tort action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/smith-v-united-states-antarctica-merits-1993-03-08.md), 1993-03-08. The statutory portion of White's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas: eight Justices adopt this holding. Stevens dissents.

### Prior administrative exhaustion of an FTCA action

**Governing question:** What instructions govern prior administrative exhaustion of an FTCA action?

**Current rule:** Before instituting an FTCA action the claimant must present the claim to the proper agency and receive final written denial sent by certified or registered mail; after six months without final disposition, the claimant may elect to treat inaction as denial. Later exhaustion does not cure the original premature filing even if little litigation occurred. Section 2675(a) excepts Federal Rules third-party complaints, crossclaims and counterclaims. New-action and limitations questions remain open.

**Authority by component:**

- Administrative exhaustion must precede the original FTCA action: [McNeil v. United States — Administrative exhaustion must precede the original FTCA action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/mcneil_merits_1993-05-17.md), 1993-05-17. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-05-17; McNeil v. United States, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [McNeil v. United States, 508 U.S. 106 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#mcneil-v-united-states-508-us-106-1993).

### Reimbursed contractor taxes and derivative federal recovery

**Governing question:** What instructions govern reimbursed contractor taxes and derivative federal recovery?

**Current rule:** Federal reimbursement or advance funding of an independent contractor's state taxes alone creates no federal common-law money-had-and-received action for alleged state-law assessment errors. Economic Treasury cost does not make the contractor's tax a direct federal tax. Subrogation takes a derivative right subject to pre-existing infirmities; where governing state law requires active assertion and the contractor's claim lapses before that assertion, later federal suit does not revive it. No universal choice between state and federal limitations for federal plaintiffs is decided.

**Authority by component:**

- Reimbursement does not create the asserted federal refund right: [United States v. California — Reimbursement does not create the asserted federal refund right](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/california_merits_1993-04-26.md), 1993-04-26. O'Connor’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.
- Later subrogation does not revive the expired claim: [United States v. California — Later subrogation does not revive the expired claim](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/california_merits_1993-04-26.md), 1993-04-26. O'Connor’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-04-26; United States v. California, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. California, 507 U.S. 746 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-v-california-507-us-746-1993).

### Common-law delay compensation on assumed federal program debt

**Governing question:** What instructions govern common-law delay compensation on assumed federal program debt?

**Current rule:** A State's matured contractual assumption of federal food-stamp reimbursement obligations carries applicable federal common-law compensation for delayed payment. The decision leaves the lawful rate, accrual period and any applicable qualification for determination under the underlying obligation; it adopts no formula or new exemption. State-debtor status alone supplies no free-ranging equitable balance. Sections 3701(c) and 3717 exclude state agencies from the Debt Collection Act's mandatory mechanism without extinguishing that independent common-law obligation. Statutory minimum rates, collection fees and penalties do not thereby apply, and unilaterally imposed penalties remain distinct.

**Authority by component:**

- Compensation for delayed payment of the assumed debt: [United States v. Texas — Compensation for delayed payment of the assumed debt](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/texas_merits_1993-04-05.md), 1993-04-05. White’s Opinion of the Court, I; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (8 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.
- State exclusion from the statutory mechanism does not extinguish common law: [United States v. Texas — State exclusion from the statutory mechanism does not extinguish common law](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/texas_merits_1993-04-05.md), 1993-04-05. White’s Opinion of the Court, II; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (8 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-04-05; United States v. Texas, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Texas, 507 U.S. 529 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-v-texas-507-us-529-1993).

### Agency FTCA exclusivity and sue-and-be-sued immunity

**Governing question:** How FTCA agency exclusivity interacts with an express sue-and-be-sued clause.

**Current rule:** Section 2679(a) makes FTCA relief exclusive against agency sue-and-be-sued authority only for claims cognizable under the whole of §1346(b), including its private-person and state-law requirements. A federal constitutional tort lies outside that category. Former §1725(c)(4)’s broad FSLIC clause waives immunity for such a claim; an implied restriction requires a clear showing of inconsistency with the statutory or constitutional scheme, necessity to avoid grave interference with governmental functions, or plain congressional intent to use the clause narrowly. Waiver does not supply a substantive cause of action or establish recovery.

**Authority by component:**

- Complete §1346(b) cognizability and agency exclusivity: [FDIC v. Meyer — FTCA exclusivity does not encompass this federal constitutional claim](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/FDIC_v_Meyer_merits_1994-02-23.md#public-projection), 1994-02-23. The statutory-exclusivity portion of Thomas's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg. All nine adopt this separate threshold holding.
- Former FSLIC waiver and three implied-restriction grounds: [FDIC v. Meyer — Former FSLIC's sue-and-be-sued clause waives immunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/FDIC_v_Meyer_merits_1994-02-23.md#public-projection), 1994-02-23. The waiver portion of Thomas's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg. Nine Justices adopt the specific waiver and its stated limits.

**Present operation effective:** FDIC v. Meyer, 1994-02-23.

**Exceptions and limits:** The separate employee-exclusivity rule in §2679(b)(1) is unchanged. The former clause governs the covered agency claims; no universal federal waiver or successor-clause construction is supplied.

**Holdings navigation:** [FTCA exclusivity does not encompass this federal constitutional claim](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#ftca-exclusivity-does-not-encompass-this-federal-constitutional-claim); [Former FSLIC's sue-and-be-sued clause waives immunity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#former-fslics-sue-and-be-sued-clause-waives-immunity).

### Gratuitous disclaimers and federal gift-tax timing

**Governing question:** When a later disclaimer of an older property interest constitutes a federally taxable gift.

**Current rule:** Federal transfer-tax law independently identifies a gratuitous disposition; state-law relation back does not erase an otherwise completed later transfer. The exclusion of transfers before the 1932 gift tax does not immunize every later disposition of an interest created earlier. If the pre-1977 regulatory disclaimer exception applies, it requires an unequivocal refusal effective under local law, made within a reasonable time after knowledge of the interest and before acceptance; a partial refusal must still be complete and unqualified as to the part refused. No fixed period replaces the knowledge-based inquiry. On the record governed by that exception, failure to disclaim within a reasonable time supports the acceptance presumption absent contrary facts.

**Authority by component:**

- Independent federal disposition, conditional regulatory exception and temporal limit: [United States v. Irvine — Federal law taxes the later gratuitous transfer, independently of state relation back; If the regulatory exception applies, this disclaimer was not timely; The 1932 limitation protects earlier transfers, not every later disposition of an old interest](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/United_States_v_Irvine_merits_1994-04-20.md#public-projection), 1994-04-20. Souter's statutory-transfer portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg; all eight participating Justices adopt this statutory ground and the alternative for regulatory inapplicability. Souter's conditional reasonable-time portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg; seven Justices adopt this independently sufficient conditional ground and its explanation. Scalia does not join this portion, though he agrees with taxability on his separate acceptance-and-transfer rationale. Souter's prospective-application portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg; eight Justices adopt this answer to the independent statutory defense.

**Present operation effective:** United States v. Irvine, 1994-04-20.

**Open question:** The decision does not resolve whether that regulation covers this 1917 inter vivos trust, created before the 1932 gift-tax enactment, or supply a rule for later §2518 qualified disclaimers. Its conditional timeliness holding must not be converted into universal regulatory coverage.

**Holdings navigation:** [Federal law taxes the later gratuitous transfer, independently of state relation back](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#federal-law-taxes-the-later-gratuitous-transfer-independently-of-state-relation-back); [The 1932 limitation protects earlier transfers, not every later disposition of an old interest](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-1932-limitation-protects-earlier-transfers-not-every-later-disposition-of-an-old-interest).

### Refund jurisdiction for lien-backed payment of another’s assessment

**Governing question:** A property claimant’s refund action after payment to release a federal tax lien.

**Current rule:** Section 1346(a)(1) permits a person not personally liable for another’s assessment to seek refund of her own payment made under the coercive force of a federal lien to free property claimed as hers, alleging wrongful collection. A duly filed administrative claim under §7422(a) and applicable §6511 timing remain required; its taxpayer terminology does not silently withdraw the express waiver for this collection claim. Independently, this property-and-payment relationship is within §7701(a)(14)’s taxpayer definition for the connected refund provisions.

**Authority by component:**

- Express refund consent includes this lien-backed collection claim: [Express refund consent includes this lien-backed collection claim](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/United_States_v_Williams_merits_1995-04-25.md#public-projection), 1995-04-25. Justice Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined on this ground by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Souter, and Breyer: seven direct votes for the stated plaintiff category and jurisdiction-only disposition.
- Williams also falls within taxpayer for these refund provisions: [Williams also falls within taxpayer for these refund provisions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/United_States_v_Williams_merits_1995-04-25.md#public-projection), 1995-04-25. Ginsburg's separately identified alternative holding, joined by Stevens, O’Connor, Souter, and Breyer: five direct votes. Stone-Zsela and Scalia do not join or decide this independent definitional ground. Kennedy and Thomas reject it. The alternative controls through its own five-Justice coalition, without combining distinct opinions under Marks.
- Distinct third-party mechanisms do not foreclose this refund action: [Distinct third-party mechanisms do not foreclose this refund action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/United_States_v_Williams_merits_1995-04-25.md#public-projection), 1995-04-25. Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined on this construction by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Souter, and Breyer: seven direct votes. This ground answers the asserted statutory obstacle; remedial hardship does not independently waive immunity.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-04-25; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Quiet title, wrongful levy and substitute-fund mechanisms do not impose an additional exhaustion requirement or exclusively displace this refund route. A lien is not a levy; substitute-fund discharge is permissive and requires agreement, and fund remedies presuppose that fund. Volunteers, strangers, universal taxpayer status and unrestricted challenges to another’s assessment remain outside the holding. Jurisdiction establishes no wrongful collection or refund amount.

**Holdings navigation:** [Express refund consent includes this lien-backed collection claim](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#express-refund-consent-includes-this-lien-backed-collection-claim); [Williams also falls within taxpayer for these refund provisions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#express-refund-consent-includes-this-lien-backed-collection-claim); [Distinct third-party mechanisms do not foreclose this refund action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#distinct-third-party-mechanisms-do-not-foreclose-this-refund-action).

### Receipt-specific personal-injury income exclusion

**Governing question:** The return-year §104(a)(2) conditions for excluding damages from gross income.

**Current rule:** The taxpayer must satisfy both the regulation’s tort-or-tort-type-right requirement and the independent statutory requirement that the particular damages be received on account of personal injuries or sickness. Amounts attributable to §213 deductions allowed in a prior year remain excepted. ADEA backpay and willfulness-based liquidated damages do not satisfy the required causal connection merely because the underlying claim is assumed tort-type.

**Authority by component:**

- Receipt-specific causation under the return-year exclusion: [Receipt-specific causation under the return-year exclusion](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Commissioner_v_Schleier_merits_1995-06-14.md#public-projection), 1995-06-14. Stevens's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Kennedy, Ginsburg and Breyer; five directly support the causal holding. Scalia concurs only in the judgment and supplies no vote for this explanation.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-14; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** This is the personal-injuries-or-sickness text, without a later physical-injury amendment. No categorical ADEA tort classification, physical-or-mental-health-only test, general punitive-damages rule or allocation for a distinct settlement is decided.

**Holdings navigation:** [Receipt-specific causation under the return-year exclusion](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#receipt-specific-causation-under-the-return-year-exclusion).

### Judicial review of Westfall Act certification

**Governing question:** The effect of a scope-of-employment certification on substitution and removal.

**Current rule:** A Westfall Act scope-of-employment certification is judicially reviewable before substitution of the United States becomes final. Initial substitution does not conclusively establish employment scope; §2679(d)(2) makes certification conclusive for removal only. The proper defendant must be determined through lawful scope review and appropriate evidence and procedure before the applicable immunity and merits issues.

**Authority by component:**

- Review of the scope certification: [Review of the scope certification](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Gutierrez_de_Martinez_v_Lamagno_merits_1995-06-14.md#public-projection), 1995-06-14. Ginsburg’s Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O’Connor, Kennedy and Breyer; six Justices adopt the statutory review rule.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-14; the component authorities identified above.

**Open question:** The decision creates no rule about retained jurisdiction after certification is rejected in a removed case. The foreign-country exception and other actual defenses remain intact.

**Holdings navigation:** [Review of the scope certification](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#review-of-the-scope-certification).

## State taxation and interstate commerce

### Separate due-process and Commerce Clause connections for remote use-tax collection

**Governing question:** A State's imposition of sales-or-use-tax collection duties on a remote seller.

**Current rule:** Purposeful and continuous solicitation of a State's consumers may supply due process's minimum connection without physical presence when the collection obligation is rationally related to benefits received through the market. Bellas Hess's indispensable physical-presence requirement for due process no longer governs this collection duty. The separate Commerce Clause rule remains: a seller whose customer connections are limited to mail or common-carrier commerce without the requisite local presence cannot be required to collect sales or use tax merely because purposeful market access satisfies due process. Complete Auto's substantial-nexus requirement remains distinct from its other requirements. Congress may alter dormant-Commerce constraints through valid legislation but cannot authorize a due-process violation.

**Authority by component:**
- Revised due-process connection: [Quill Corp. v. North Dakota, “1. Purposeful contacts can satisfy due process without physical presence”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Quill_Corp_v_North_Dakota_merits_1992-05-26.md), May 26, 1992; Stevens Parts I–III, supported by all eight Associate Justices including the author; Stone does not reach it.
- Surviving specific Commerce collection boundary: Quill, “2. The specific Commerce collection precedent remains controlling,” May 26, 1992; express common narrower ground of Stevens, Blackmun, O'Connor, Souter, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Stone (eight), with the record's stated common-ground authority rather than the four-Justice Part IV policy discussion.

**Present operation effective:** Quill Corp. v. North Dakota, May 26, 1992, separating the revised due-process rule from the surviving specific Commerce collection rule.

**Exceptions and limits:** The rule does not govern every state tax, immunize consumers from use tax, set numerical sales or property thresholds, or establish a general software-nexus rule. The particular software application does not supply an additional reusable property rule. Potential retroactive effects of overruling the Commerce rule were excluded from the grant.

**Holdings navigation:** [Quill Corp. v. North Dakota, 504 U.S. 298 (1992) — State-tax collection and interstate commerce](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#quill-corp-v-north-dakota-504-us-298-1992).

### Operational assets and the unitary-business limitation on apportioned income

**Governing question:** Whether a State may apportion income from an asset held by a nondomiciliary corporation doing business there.

**Current rule:** The State may apportion income connected to the corporation's unitary business but may not include discrete, unrelated investment value merely because the corporation does business in the State or owns the asset. A unitary relationship between payor and recipient is sufficient but unnecessary when the income-producing asset or capital transaction itself serves an operational function in the taxpayer's unitary business, subject to territorial connection and fair apportionment. A general acquisition strategy and intended later use of sale proceeds cannot replace an identifiable operational function where the enterprises lack functional integration, centralized management and economies of scale. A valid taxable base and a fair allocation formula remain separate requirements; the taxpayer's existing clear-and-cogent burden survives.

**Authority by component:**
- Unitary relationship and operational-function routes: [Allied-Signal, Inc. v. Director, Division of Taxation, “Holding 1: The unitary-business limitation and operational-asset route remain governing law”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Allied_Signal_Inc_v_Director_Division_of_Taxation_merits_1992-06-15.md), June 15, 1992; Kennedy, joined by White, Stevens, Scalia and Souter (five).
- Insufficiency of general acquisition strategy and later proceeds use without either connection: Allied-Signal, “Holding 2: The stipulated record requires exclusion of the ASARCO gain,” June 15, 1992; same five Justices.

**Present operation effective:** Allied-Signal, Inc. v. Director, Division of Taxation, June 15, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** No holding makes every state-law category of business income constitutional or reduces the inquiry to investment duration.

**Holdings navigation:** [Allied-Signal, Inc., as successor-in-interest to Bendix Corp. v. Director, Division of Taxation, 504 U.S. 768 (1992) — State-tax apportionment and investment income](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#allied-signal-inc-as-successor-in-interest-to-bendix-corp-v-director-division-of-taxation-504-us-768-1992).

### Solicitation and independently useful business functions

**Governing question:** The meaning of solicitation under 15 U.S.C. §381(a), and the separate scope of §381(c)'s independent-contractor exemption.

**Current rule:** Solicitation includes express or implicit requests for purchases and activities serving no independent business function apart from requesting orders. Customary assignment to salespeople, promotional usefulness or a connection with future sales does not protect an independent service, delivery or inventory function. No automatic before-sale/after-sale boundary governs. The statutory protection retains its out-of-state approval and fulfillment conditions; this entry addresses the functional definition, not every prerequisite of §381 protection.

**Authority by component:**
- Functional definition: [Wisconsin Department of Revenue v. William Wrigley, Jr., Co., “Solicitation includes its ancillary means, not independent business functions”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Wisconsin_Department_of_Revenue_v_William_Wrigley_Jr_Co_merits_1992-06-19.md), June 19, 1992; Scalia Parts I–II, joined by White, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter and Thomas (six).

**Present operation effective:** Wisconsin Department of Revenue v. William Wrigley, Jr., Co., June 19, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Maintaining a company-attributable office generally exceeds solicitation, except that §381(c) (Pub. L. 86–272, title I, §101, Sept. 14, 1959, 73 Stat. 555; unamended through the event date) separately exempts (1) sales or order-solicitation conducted by one or more independent contractors, and (2) an office maintained in the State by one or more independent contractors whose activities there consist solely of making sales or soliciting orders for tangible personal property — the multiple-principal definition alone does not satisfy this separate office-specific condition. "Independent contractor" is defined by §381(d)(1) as a commission agent, broker, or other independent contractor selling or soliciting orders for more than one principal and holding himself out as such in the regular course of business; §381(d)(2) excludes an independent contractor from the separate term "representative." Neither branch of this exemption reaches a company's own local representatives or employees, whose activities remain governed by the functional definition above. Ordinary use of a representative's home or temporary meeting place does not automatically establish a company-attributable office.

**Holdings navigation:** [Wisconsin Department of Revenue v. William Wrigley, Jr., Co., 505 U.S. 214 (1992) — State net-income tax and protected solicitation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#wisconsin-department-of-revenue-v-william-wrigley-jr-co-505-us-214-1992).

### Trivial departures from protected solicitation

**Governing question:** Whether activities outside protected solicitation defeat §381 protection.

**Current rule:** Genuinely trivial departures do not defeat protection when they are too slight to create a nontrivial additional connection with the taxing State. The court considers the unprotected activities together; their small proportion of the company's total sales does not settle the inquiry. No fixed dollar threshold, sales-percentage safe harbor or automatic rule for a rare isolated act is established.

**Authority by component:**
- Aggregate, nontrivial-additional-connection qualification: [Wisconsin Department of Revenue v. William Wrigley, Jr., Co., “Truly trivial departures do not destroy protection; Wrigley's combined functions are not trivial”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Wisconsin_Department_of_Revenue_v_William_Wrigley_Jr_Co_merits_1992-06-19.md), June 19, 1992; Scalia Part II, joined by White, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter and Thomas (six). Part III's five-Justice application does not make any one activity independently sufficient in every case.

**Present operation effective:** Wisconsin Department of Revenue v. William Wrigley, Jr., Co., June 19, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [Wisconsin Department of Revenue v. William Wrigley, Jr., Co., 505 U.S. 214 (1992) — State net-income tax and protected solicitation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#wisconsin-department-of-revenue-v-william-wrigley-jr-co-505-us-214-1992).

### Container importation exemptions and local lease taxation

**Governing question:** What instructions govern container importation exemptions and local lease taxation?

**Current rule:** The 1956 and 1972 Container Conventions prohibit direct or indirect taxes by reason of or in connection with importation of qualifying temporarily admitted containers, not every container transaction. The 1972 definition excludes fees and charges limited to approximate service cost. A nondiscriminatory local transfer-of-possession lease tax is not importation taxation; the conventions, §1322 and regulations neither occupy all container taxation nor preempt a tax that does not obstruct temporary admission. Complete Auto's nexus, apportionment, nondiscrimination and fair-service-relation requirements remain necessary. Foreign-commerce taxation additionally must avoid substantial risk of international multiple taxation and interference with national one-voice authority; same-transaction credits and actual federal arrangements matter, while Executive support informs but does not control. Michelin's uniformity, federal-revenue and interstate-harmony concerns remain distinct. Whether Michelin changed Richfield for direct taxation of goods in transit remains unresolved.

**Authority by component:**

- 1. The Container Conventions exempt importation charges, not every container transaction: [Itel Containers International Corp. v. Huddleston — 1. The Container Conventions exempt importation charges, not every container transaction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Itel_Containers_International_Corp_v_Huddleston_merits_1993-02-23.md), 1993-02-23. Kennedy's treaty portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter and Thomas: eight Justices adopt this construction and application.
- 2. Federal container regulation does not preempt this local lease tax: [Itel Containers International Corp. v. Huddleston — 2. Federal container regulation does not preempt this local lease tax](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Itel_Containers_International_Corp_v_Huddleston_merits_1993-02-23.md), 1993-02-23. Kennedy's regulatory-preemption portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter and Thomas: eight Justices adopt the stated rejection of both grounds.
- 3. The local lease tax satisfies the additional foreign-commerce requirements: [Itel Containers International Corp. v. Huddleston — 3. The local lease tax satisfies the additional foreign-commerce requirements](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Itel_Containers_International_Corp_v_Huddleston_merits_1993-02-23.md), 1993-02-23. Kennedy's foreign-commerce portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter and Thomas: seven Justices adopt this rule and application. Scalia agrees only in the judgment on this issue; Blackmun dissents.
- 4. A local lease transaction is not the import or export taxed in Richfield: [Itel Containers International Corp. v. Huddleston — 4. A local lease transaction is not the import or export taxed in Richfield](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Itel_Containers_International_Corp_v_Huddleston_merits_1993-02-23.md), 1993-02-23. Kennedy's Import-Export portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter and Thomas: seven Justices adopt this rule and application. Scalia concurs only in this component's judgment on a distinct export-status ground; Blackmun does not supply a separate Import-Export disposition.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-02-23; Itel Containers International Corp. v. Huddleston, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Itel Containers International Corp. v. Huddleston, 507 U.S. 60 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#itel-containers-international-corp-v-huddleston-507-us-60-1993).

### Backward-looking relief for unconstitutional taxes

**Governing question:** What instructions govern backward-looking relief for unconstitutional taxes?

**Current rule:** Applying a federal tax rule retroactively does not alone determine remedy. When the State required payment before adequate opportunity to contest, due process requires meaningful backward-looking relief; lawful refunds, retroactive nondiscriminatory assessments or a combination may suffice. Meaningful prepayment review presents a different remedial setting. State law and due process govern the correction; no automatic refund of every payment follows. Even if an adequate prepayment route existed, a State that held out a clear postpayment remedy for illegally assessed and collected taxes, including voluntary payments, may not withdraw that remedy after payment and rely on failure to use the other route. It must afford a clear and certain opportunity for a refund or constitutionally adequate equivalent relief, subject to ordinary proof of payment and amount. A prospective adequate prepayment system remains possible with fair notice.

**Authority by component:**

- Retroactivity and the lawful tax remedy are separate: [Harper v. Virginia Department of Taxation — Retroactivity and the lawful tax remedy are separate](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Harper_v_Virginia_Department_of_Taxation_merits_1993-06-18.md), 1993-06-18. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court, Retroactivity and the lawful tax remedy are separate portion; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (8 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.

- A State may not withdraw its apparent postpayment remedy after payment: [A State may not withdraw its apparent postpayment remedy after payment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Reich_v_Collins_merits_1994-12-06.md#public-projection), 1994-12-06. Justice O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer: nine direct votes for the bounded procedural rule and remedy.

**Present operation effective:** Reich v. Collins, December 6, 1994, for the after-payment withdrawal limit; Harper’s distinct remedial-choice rules remain.

**Holdings navigation:** [Harper v. Virginia Department of Taxation, 509 U.S. 86 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#harper-v-virginia-department-of-taxation-509-us-86-1993); [A State may not withdraw its apparent postpayment remedy after payment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#a-state-may-not-withdraw-its-apparent-postpayment-remedy-after-payment).

### Railroad property taxes and ordinary nonrailroad exemptions

**Governing question:** Whether the 4-R Act requires extension of ordinary property-tax exemptions to railroad property.

**Current rule:** For §11503(b)(1)–(3), the §11503(a)(4) commercial-and-industrial comparison class includes qualifying commercial or industrial property actually subject to the levy, excluding transportation property and land used primarily for agriculture or timber growing. Exempt property is outside that class, so those paragraphs do not require extension of ordinary nonrailroad exemptions. Paragraph (4) likewise does not invalidate a generally applicable value-based property tax solely because ordinary nonrailroad categories are exempt. Assessment, collection and rate protections retain their own force.

**Authority by component:**

- Taxable comparison class and exclusions: [Department of Revenue of Oregon v. ACF Industries, Inc — The ordinary property-tax comparison excludes exempt property](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Department_of_Revenue_of_Oregon_v_ACF_Industries_Inc_merits_1994-01-24.md#public-projection), 1994-01-24. Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; eight Justices adopt the statutory definition as the premise of the exemption ruling.
- Catchall construction for ordinary exemptions: [Department of Revenue of Oregon v. ACF Industries, Inc — Ordinary exemptions do not become forbidden discrimination under the catchall](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Department_of_Revenue_of_Oregon_v_ACF_Industries_Inc_merits_1994-01-24.md#public-projection), 1994-01-24. Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; eight Justices adopt this construction and the reversal of the exemption requirement.

**Present operation effective:** Department of Revenue of Oregon v. ACF Industries, Inc, 1994-01-24.

**Open question:** Taxation of railroads alone or with an isolated targeted group remains unresolved. No exemption percentage, numerical safe harbor, intent requirement or immunity for every levy called a property tax is established.

**Holdings navigation:** [The ordinary property-tax comparison excludes exempt property](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-ordinary-property-tax-comparison-excludes-exempt-property); [Ordinary exemptions do not become forbidden discrimination under the catchall](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#ordinary-exemptions-do-not-become-forbidden-discrimination-under-the-catchall).

### Reasonable aircraft-operator airport charges

**Governing question:** What the Anti-Head Tax Act requires for state or municipal airport charges to aircraft operators.

**Current rule:** Sections 1513(a) and (b), read together, permit airport-owner or operator rental, landing and other facility-service charges to aircraft operators only if reasonable. In the setting decided in Northwest Airlines, reasonableness requires fair approximation of facility use, no excess relative to benefits and no discrimination against interstate commerce. Exact equality among unlike users is unnecessary, but each requirement remains independent. A per-passenger charge is not saved merely by a service label or satisfaction of Evansville, and not every expense passed through by an airline is a prohibited passenger charge.

**Authority by component:**

- Statutory categories and three-part reasonableness instruction: [Northwest Airlines, Inc. v. County of Kent — The statutory airport-fee condition and its measure](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Northwest_Airlines_Inc_v_County_of_Kent_merits_1994-01-24.md#public-projection), 1994-01-24. Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter; seven Justices adopt both this statutory construction and the stated three-part measure. Thomas rejects this construction.

**Present operation effective:** Northwest Airlines, Inc. v. County of Kent, 1994-01-24.

**Exceptions and limits:** Subsection (a) prohibits charges imposed directly or indirectly on persons traveling in air commerce, their carriage, air-transportation sales or gross receipts from those sales. Subsection (b)’s distinct first clause saves taxes not enumerated in (a), including property, net-income, franchise and sales/use taxes on goods or services; its second saves the reasonable aircraft-operator facility charges. Concession reserves or unequal recovery percentages alone do not establish a defect. No private AHTA action, different DOT standard or displacement of independent constitutional review is decided.

**Holdings navigation:** [The statutory airport-fee condition and its measure](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-statutory-airport-fee-condition-and-its-measure).

### Discriminatory routing, charges and linked subsidies in interstate commerce

**Governing question:** When a state or local economic measure must satisfy the discrimination rule rather than ordinary incidental-burden balancing.

**Current rule:** A measure discriminates when it favors local economic interests by burdening interstate competitors, including a waste charge expressly keyed to out-of-state origin, compulsory routing that reserves a processing service to a designated local private operator, or an integrated assessment-and-distribution scheme that collects from the whole market but returns the proceeds only to local producers. The entire linked operation matters; formally equal collection does not cleanse its discriminatory distribution. The government must show a legitimate local purpose that cannot adequately be served by reasonable nondiscriminatory alternatives.
