Ordinary incidental-burden balancing does not validate discrimination. Conservation, sanitation, cost recovery and financing a favored private facility do not themselves meet that burden; contracting with a private operator does not make compulsory market regulation governmental market participation.

**Authority by component:**

- Origin-based surcharge and justification burden: [Oregon Waste Systems, Inc. v. Department of Environmental Quality — An express waste-origin surcharge triggers the discrimination rule; Conservation and cost-recovery labels do not justify this private-facility differential](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Oregon_Waste_Systems_Inc_v_Department_of_Environmental_Quality_merits_1994-04-04.md#public-projection), 1994-04-04. Thomas's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg; eight Justices directly adopt this proposition and its application.
- Private processing monopoly and neutral alternatives: [C & A Carbone, Inc. v. Town of Clarkstown — Compulsory use of this local private processor discriminates in the service market; Sanitary service and financing do not justify this discriminatory route](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/C_A_Carbone_Inc_v_Town_of_Clarkstown_merits_1994-05-16.md#public-projection), 1994-05-16. Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Scalia, Thomas and Ginsburg; six Justices adopt the discrimination classification and its stated private-service boundary. O'Connor agrees only with the judgment on a distinct Pike ground. Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Scalia, Thomas and Ginsburg; six Justices adopt this application of the discrimination rule. No independent Pike holding is adopted by that coalition.
- Integrated levy, local distribution and justification: [West Lynn Creamery, Inc. v. Healy — The assessment and dedicated local distribution must be examined together; the local preference lacks a sufficient justification](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/West_Lynn_Creamery_v_Healy_Merits_1994-06-17.md#public-projection), 1994-06-17. Justice Stevens's opinion, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, and Ginsburg: six Justices adopt this rule and its application. Justices Scalia and Thomas concur in the judgment on the narrower dedicated-industry-tax ground. Justice Stevens's opinion, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, and Ginsburg: six Justices adopt the justification analysis and its application. Eight Justices support reversal and remand; Justice Blackmun dissents.

**Present operation effective:** West Lynn Creamery, Inc. v. Healy, 1994-06-17.

**Exceptions and limits:** These decisions do not adjudicate every general-revenue subsidy, genuinely public waste system or government-owned landfill. A compensatory tax must independently satisfy the requirements in “Compensatory taxes and the actual equivalent local burden.”

**Holdings navigation:** [An express waste-origin surcharge triggers the discrimination rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#an-express-waste-origin-surcharge-triggers-the-discrimination-rule); [Conservation and cost-recovery labels do not justify this private-facility differential](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#conservation-and-cost-recovery-labels-do-not-justify-this-private-facility-differential); [Compulsory use of this local private processor discriminates in the service market](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#compulsory-use-of-this-local-private-processor-discriminates-in-the-service-market); [Sanitary service and financing do not justify this discriminatory route](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#sanitary-service-and-financing-do-not-justify-this-discriminatory-route); [The assessment and dedicated local distribution must be examined together](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-assessment-and-dedicated-local-distribution-must-be-examined-together); [The local preference lacks a sufficient justification](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-local-preference-lacks-a-sufficient-justification).

### Compensatory taxes and the actual equivalent local burden

**Governing question:** When an otherwise discriminatory tax can compensate for a tax already borne by the corresponding intrastate activity.

**Current rule:** The State must identify the actual intrastate burden for which it compensates, show that the interstate charge roughly approximates and does not exceed that burden, and establish that the taxed events are substantially equivalent, reasonably treated as mutually exclusive proxies for one another. General income taxation and waste disposal are not such equivalent events. For a use tax said to equalize local sales taxes, the comparison must use the sales tax actually imposed in the affected locality. Statewide averages and burdens elsewhere cannot cure an excess there. Equal or lower use-tax burdens in other localities are not invalidated merely because an excess exists elsewhere; relief addresses the established inequality through lawful remedial procedures.

**Authority by component:**

- Three compensatory-tax predicates: [Oregon Waste Systems, Inc. v. Department of Environmental Quality — General income taxes do not establish a compensatory disposal tax](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Oregon_Waste_Systems_Inc_v_Department_of_Environmental_Quality_merits_1994-04-04.md#public-projection), 1994-04-04. Thomas's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg; eight Justices directly adopt this proposition and its application.
- Local comparison, bounded invalidity and relief: [Associated Industries of Missouri v. Lohman — The compensating burden must exist in the affected locality; Relief must address the established inequality through lawful state procedures](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Associated_Industries_of_Missouri_v_Lohman_merits_1994-05-23.md#public-projection), 1994-05-23. Thomas's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg; eight direct joins. Blackmun agrees with the judgment without joining this full framework.

**Present operation effective:** Associated Industries of Missouri v. Lohman, 1994-05-23.

**Holdings navigation:** [General income taxes do not establish a compensatory disposal tax](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#general-income-taxes-do-not-establish-a-compensatory-disposal-tax); [The compensating burden must exist in the affected locality](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-compensating-burden-must-exist-in-the-affected-locality); [Relief must address the established inequality through lawful state procedures](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#relief-must-address-the-established-inequality-through-lawful-state-procedures).

### Worldwide unitary-income taxation and foreign-commerce safeguards

**Governing question:** How a worldwide combined-reporting tax is tested against foreign-commerce and notice objections.

**Current rule:** Worldwide combined reporting remains subject to the ordinary constitutional tax requirements and the distinct foreign-commerce inquiries into impermissible multiple taxation and national uniformity. Foreign incorporation of the parent does not alone establish a forbidden burden, and foreign use of a different accounting method does not itself establish unconstitutional multiple taxation; the actual claimed burden and available apportionment protections matter. National-uniformity review is independent: congressional silence is not affirmative authorization, and nonbinding executive policy or foreign objections do not alone invalidate the state method. Reasonable estimation of foreign-affiliate information can satisfy notice and reporting requirements when intelligible constraints, meaningful review and actual accommodations exist; administration must consider the expense of producing the information, not demand impossible precision.

**Authority by component:**

- Ordinary limits, actual reporting accommodations and notice: [Barclays Bank PLC v. Franchise Tax Board of California — Ordinary constitutional tax requirements and the apportionment challenge — Part I-A; The presented reporting-burden challenge fails under the actual accommodations — Part I-B; Reasonable approximation and available review satisfy the presented notice challenge — Part II](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Barclays_Bank_PLC_v_Franchise_Tax_Board_of_California_merits_1994-06-20.md#public-projection), 1994-06-20. Justice Kennedy's opinion, Part I-A, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg: nine Justices adopt the rule and its bounded application. Agreement on this component does not decide Barclays' separate foreign-commerce objections. Justice Kennedy's opinion, Part I-B, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg: nine direct joins to this reporting-burden application. Justice Kennedy's opinion, Part II, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg: nine direct joins. This resolves Barclays' notice and discretion objections; it does not create a separate Colgate claim.
- Domestic- and foreign-parent multiple-taxation components: [Barclays Bank PLC v. Franchise Tax Board of California — Container governs the domestic-parent multiple-taxation claim — Part III-A; The foreign-parent record does not establish the forbidden multiple-taxation burden — Part III-B](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Barclays_Bank_PLC_v_Franchise_Tax_Board_of_California_merits_1994-06-20.md#public-projection), 1994-06-20. Justice Kennedy's opinion, Part III-A, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg: nine Justices support this retained-precedent rule. O'Connor and Thomas accept its continued application, not its extension to Barclays. Justice Kennedy's opinion, Part III-B, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Souter and Ginsburg: seven direct joins. O'Connor and Thomas dissent from this component and would reverse Barclays on this independently sufficient ground.
- Independent uniformity inquiry and limited policy evidence: [Barclays Bank PLC v. Franchise Tax Board of California — Independent national-uniformity review and the limits of nonbinding policy — Part IV-A; The specific federal policy record supplies additional support, not authorization — Part IV-B](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Barclays_Bank_PLC_v_Franchise_Tax_Board_of_California_merits_1994-06-20.md#public-projection), 1994-06-20. Justice Kennedy's opinion, Part IV-A, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg: eight direct joins to this bounded national-uniformity and legal-force conclusion. Scalia agrees in the rejection of the attack on his separate method, without joining this substantive national-uniformity inquiry. Justice Kennedy's opinion, Part IV-B, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, Souter and Ginsburg: five direct joins to this additional national-uniformity ground and its limits. Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia and Thomas do not join it. It provides an additional explanation for the national-uniformity result, not an independently sufficient ground to uphold a tax that fails another constitutional requirement.

**Present operation effective:** Barclays Bank PLC v. Franchise Tax Board of California, 1994-06-20.

**Exceptions and limits:** California’s relief mechanism permits, where the ordinary formula fails fairly to represent business activity, separate accounting, exclusion of a factor, inclusion of additional factors or another reasonable method, on taxpayer petition or agency requirement. The decision does not validate every estimate, foreign information demand or demonstrated duplicative burden. The narrower five-Justice discussion of the particular federal policy record is supporting evidence, not congressional permission.

**Holdings navigation:** [Ordinary constitutional tax requirements and the apportionment challenge — Part I-A](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#ordinary-constitutional-tax-requirements-and-the-apportionment-challenge--part-i-a); [The presented reporting-burden challenge fails under the actual accommodations — Part I-B](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-presented-reporting-burden-challenge-fails-under-the-actual-accommodations--part-i-b); [Reasonable approximation and available review satisfy the presented notice challenge — Part II](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#reasonable-approximation-and-available-review-satisfy-the-presented-notice-challenge--part-ii); [Container governs the domestic-parent multiple-taxation claim — Part III-A](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#container-governs-the-domestic-parent-multiple-taxation-claim--part-iii-a); [The foreign-parent record does not establish the forbidden multiple-taxation burden — Part III-B](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-foreign-parent-record-does-not-establish-the-forbidden-multiple-taxation-burden--part-iii-b); [Independent national-uniformity review and the limits of nonbinding policy — Part IV-A](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#independent-national-uniformity-review-and-the-limits-of-nonbinding-policy--part-iv-a); [The specific federal policy record supplies additional support, not authorization — Part IV-B](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-specific-federal-policy-record-supplies-additional-support-not-authorization--part-iv-b).

### Private repurchase-agreement returns and federal-obligation immunity

**Governing question:** Whether the income-tax exemption in 31 U.S.C. §3124(a) covers returns from a separate private repo obligation.

**Current rule:** A return owed under a separate private financing obligation is not interest on a United States obligation merely because federal securities secure or are delivered in the repurchase transaction. Actual book-entry delivery does not change the source of negotiated earnings. Section 3124(a) continues to protect federal obligations and their interest against direct and indirect taxation, except nondiscriminatory corporate franchise or substitute corporate nonproperty taxes and estate or inheritance taxes.

**Authority by component:**

- The private repo return is outside the federal-obligation exemption: [The private repo return is outside the federal-obligation exemption](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Nebraska_Department_of_Revenue_v_Loewenstein_merits_1994-12-12.md#public-projection), 1994-12-12. Justice Stevens's Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer directly adopt this proposition and its stated qualifications. These 9 rationale votes control without combining separate rationales.

**Present operation effective:** 1994-12-12; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** This protected-subject boundary is not an exception authorizing individual income tax on federal interest, a universal repo-ownership classification or approval of actual discriminatory tax treatment.

**Holdings navigation:** [The private repo return is outside the federal-obligation exemption](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-private-repo-return-is-outside-the-federal-obligation-exemption).

### Local retail sales of interstate transportation

**Governing question:** Application of Complete Auto to the full price of a completed local sale of locally originating interstate transportation.

**Current rule:** A physically present carrier’s completed local ticket sale for a trip originating in the State supplies substantial nexus. Identical taxation of completed local retail sales taxes that sale once and is internally consistent; later transportation segments are not additional sales of the ticket. The full retail price may measure this consumer sales tax without mileage apportionment. A carrier-business gross-receipts tax remains distinct and subject to Central Greyhound’s apportionment rule; labels and nominal buyer incidence alone do not establish external consistency.

**Authority by component:**

- The completed local sale and carrier operations supply substantial nexus: [The completed local sale and carrier operations supply substantial nexus](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Oklahoma_Tax_Commission_v_Jefferson_Lines_Inc_merits_1995-04-03.md#public-projection), 1995-04-03. Justice Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, Souter, and Ginsburg: five direct rationale votes. Justices Scalia and Thomas concur only in the judgment and do not join this complete explanation.
- Identical taxation of the completed retail sale is internally consistent: [Identical taxation of the completed retail sale is internally consistent](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Oklahoma_Tax_Commission_v_Jefferson_Lines_Inc_merits_1995-04-03.md#public-projection), 1995-04-03. Justice Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, Souter, and Ginsburg: five direct rationale votes. Justices Scalia and Thomas concur only in the judgment and do not join this complete explanation.
- The full price measures the local consumer sale without mileage apportionment: [The full price measures the local consumer sale without mileage apportionment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Oklahoma_Tax_Commission_v_Jefferson_Lines_Inc_merits_1995-04-03.md#public-projection), 1995-04-03. Justice Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, Souter, and Ginsburg: five direct rationale votes. Justices Scalia and Thomas concur only in the judgment and do not join this complete explanation.
- The established burden does not discriminate against interstate ticket sales: [The established burden does not discriminate against interstate ticket sales](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Oklahoma_Tax_Commission_v_Jefferson_Lines_Inc_merits_1995-04-03.md#public-projection), 1995-04-03. Justice Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, Souter, and Ginsburg: five direct rationale votes. Justices Scalia and Thomas concur only in the judgment and do not join this complete explanation.
- General services supporting the local activity supply a fair relation: [General services supporting the local activity supply a fair relation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Oklahoma_Tax_Commission_v_Jefferson_Lines_Inc_merits_1995-04-03.md#public-projection), 1995-04-03. Justice Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, Souter, and Ginsburg: five direct rationale votes. Justices Scalia and Thomas concur only in the judgment and do not join this complete explanation.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-04-03; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Nondiscrimination requires absence of an established additional discriminatory burden, not facially equal rates alone. Broad governmental services supporting the local activity establish fair relation without dollar-for-dollar or mile-for-mile reimbursement. Remote purchases, wholly out-of-state trips, actual competing assessments and materially different practical burdens remain undecided.

**Holdings navigation:** [The completed local sale and carrier operations supply substantial nexus](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-completed-local-sale-and-carrier-operations-supply-substantial-nexus); [Identical taxation of the completed retail sale is internally consistent](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#identical-taxation-of-the-completed-retail-sale-is-internally-consistent); [The full price measures the local consumer sale without mileage apportionment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-full-price-measures-the-local-consumer-sale-without-mileage-apportionment); [The established burden does not discriminate against interstate ticket sales](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-established-burden-does-not-discriminate-against-interstate-ticket-sales); [General services supporting the local activity supply a fair relation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#general-services-supporting-the-local-activity-supply-a-fair-relation).

### Section 1983 tax equity and derivative fees

**Governing question:** Federal equitable relief against state taxes where an adequate legal remedy exists.

**Current rule:** Federal equitable principles informing §1983 preclude injunctive or declaratory interference with state tax administration when an adequate legal remedy actually protects the claimant. This is not direct application of §1341 to state courts and does not restrict independently lawful state-law equity. If the invoked §1983 equitable claim affords no relief, §1988 supplies no fee entitlement through that claim. Success on state law does not categorically defeat fees when an independently viable fee-eligible federal claim exists.

**Authority by component:**

- Federal tax equity and an actually adequate legal remedy: [Federal tax equity and an actually adequate legal remedy](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/National_Private_Truck_Council_Inc_v_Oklahoma_Tax_Commission_merits_1995-06-19.md#public-projection), 1995-06-19. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer; nine Justices adopt this remedial rule.
- Fees follow an available fee-eligible federal claim: [Fees follow an available fee-eligible federal claim](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/National_Private_Truck_Council_Inc_v_Oklahoma_Tax_Commission_merits_1995-06-19.md#public-projection), 1995-06-19. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court; all nine Justices join this proposition.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-19; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Actual adequacy matters; a supposed remedy cannot replace the clear and certain relief required by the tax-remedy authorities. Separate damages claims, independent state fee grounds and refund amounts are not decided.

**Holdings navigation:** [Federal tax equity and an actually adequate legal remedy](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#federal-tax-equity-and-an-actually-adequate-legal-remedy); [Fees follow an available fee-eligible federal claim](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#fees-follow-an-available-fee-eligible-federal-claim).

## Indian law

### State taxation of qualifying General Allotment Act fee land

**Governing question:** What state taxes Congress authorized on reservation land validly patented in fee under the General Allotment Act.

**Current rule:** The General Allotment Act's fee-land rule and Burke Act proviso authorize ad valorem taxation of land validly patented in fee under that Act, including qualifying land later held by the Nation. The tax follows the land, but patent source and independently applicable federal protection must be determined parcel by parcel. The Indian Reorganization Act does not automatically return every existing fee parcel to tax-protected trust status. This permission does not unmistakably authorize a tax on an Indian owner's sale of the land. The tax's legal incidence controls; securing a transactional sales tax with a lien does not convert it into an authorized land tax.

**Authority by component:**

- Ad valorem permission and parcel-specific limits: [County of Yakima v. Confederated Tribes — Ad valorem holding and operative rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/County_of_Yakima_v_Confederated_Tribes_merits_1992-01-14.md), January 14, 1992; Scalia's opinion joined by Stone, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, and Thomas.
- No implied sales-excise permission and unchanged legal incidence: [County of Yakima — Sales-excise holding and operative rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/County_of_Yakima_v_Confederated_Tribes_merits_1992-01-14.md), January 14, 1992; all nine Justices join.

**Present operation effective:** *County of Yakima*, January 14, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [County of Yakima v. Confederated Tribes and Bands of the Yakima Indian Nation, 502 U.S. 251 (1992) — State taxation of reservation fee land](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#county-of-yakima-v-confederated-tribes-and-bands-of-the-yakima-indian-nation-502-us-251-1992).

### Kansas concurrent state criminal jurisdiction

**Governing question:** What instructions govern kansas concurrent state criminal jurisdiction?

**Current rule:** Under §3243 Kansas may prosecute state offenses by or against Indians on Kansas reservations, including trust or restricted allotments, to the same extent as state offenses elsewhere, even when conduct also violates the Indian Major Crimes Act. Federal jurisdiction over federal offenses is preserved; Kansas gains no authority to adjudicate federal charges. The specific enactment gives no corresponding authority to other States and resolves neither tribal jurisdiction's extent nor successive-prosecution issues.

**Authority by component:**

- Kansas-specific concurrent authority over state offenses: [Negonsott v. Samuels — Kansas-specific concurrent authority over state offenses](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Negonsott_v_Samuels_merits_1993-02-24.md), 1993-02-24. White's unanimous opinion, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas, controls the grant, the saving clause and the disposition.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-02-24; Negonsott v. Samuels, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Negonsott v. Samuels, 507 U.S. 99 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#negonsott-v-samuels-507-us-99-1993).

### Indian-country residence, source and state member taxation

**Governing question:** What instructions govern indian-country residence, source and state member taxation?

**Current rule:** Absent express congressional authorization, a State may not tax a tribal member's Indian-country income when the member resides there; tribal employment alone is insufficient. Residence encompasses §1151 reservations, dependent Indian communities and allotments with unextinguished Indian titles, including rights-of-way. For resident members, vehicle excise and registration levies constituting prohibited property taxation cannot be saved by collecting the same charges from later purchasers. Genuine apportioned off-country road-use charges and every vehicle-sale tax are not decided. Membership, residence, source, tax character and authorization remain separate; broader outside-residence self-government questions remain open. A State may apply its generally applicable income tax to members living outside Indian country who earn wages from tribal work within it; the Treaty of Dancing Rabbit Creek does not exempt that specified employee income. Protection remains for members both residing and earning the covered tribal income in Indian country.

**Authority by component:**

- Member income protection depends on Indian-country residence and source: [Oklahoma Tax Commission v. Sac and Fox Nation — Member income protection depends on Indian-country residence and source](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/sac_fox_merits_1993-05-17.md), 1993-05-17. O'Connor’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.
- The challenged vehicle levies cannot be sustained merely as off-country use charges: [Oklahoma Tax Commission v. Sac and Fox Nation — The challenged vehicle levies cannot be sustained merely as off-country use charges](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/sac_fox_merits_1993-05-17.md), 1993-05-17. O'Connor’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.

- Resident taxation and the surviving treaty promise: [Resident taxation and the surviving treaty promise](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Oklahoma_Tax_Commission_v_Chickasaw_Nation_merits_1995-06-14.md#public-projection), 1995-06-14. Ginsburg’s income portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas; five Justices adopt the off-country-resident treaty construction.

**Present operation effective:** Chickasaw Nation, June 14, 1995, for the off-country-resident treaty construction; Sac and Fox’s residence, source and vehicle-tax components remain.

**Holdings navigation:** [Oklahoma Tax Commission v. Sac and Fox Nation, 508 U.S. 114 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#oklahoma-tax-commission-v-sac-and-fox-nation-508-us-114-1993); [Resident taxation and the surviving treaty promise](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#resident-taxation-and-the-surviving-treaty-promise).

### Regulation following statutory acquisition of treaty lands

**Governing question:** What instructions govern regulation following statutory acquisition of treaty lands?

**Current rule:** On the former Cheyenne River trust lands acquired under the Flood Control and Cheyenne River Acts, displacement of exclusive occupation also displaces non-Indian hunting-and-fishing regulation derived from exclusion. Retained tribal access and personal hunting-and-fishing privileges alone do not preserve licensing authority over non-Indian public users. Residual Montana authority remains separately available if established: consensual commercial dealings, contracts, leases or arrangements with the Tribe or members, or conduct threatening or directly affecting political integrity, economic security, health or welfare, within Montana's limits. Proper federal authorization remains possible; general inherent sovereignty alone does not restore the displaced power. No general state exclusivity or reservation-boundary ruling follows.

**Authority by component:**

- Acquisition displaces exclusion-derived regulation: [South Dakota v. Bourland — Acquisition displaces exclusion-derived regulation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/South_Dakota_v_Bourland_merits_1993-06-14.md), 1993-06-14. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court, Acquisition displaces exclusion-derived regulation portion; White, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.
- Residual authority remains a distinct inquiry: [South Dakota v. Bourland — Residual authority remains a distinct inquiry](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/South_Dakota_v_Bourland_merits_1993-06-14.md), 1993-06-14. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court, Residual authority remains a distinct inquiry portion; White, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-14; South Dakota v. Bourland, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [South Dakota v. Bourland, 508 U.S. 679 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#south-dakota-v-bourland-508-us-679-1993).

### Reasonable nonmember-tax collection through reservation traders

**Governing question:** When federal trader regulation permits state collection controls on reservation cigarette transactions.

**Current rule:** Federal trader statutes do not displace reasonable measures needed to collect valid state taxes on sales to nonmembers. Probable-demand quotas, documentation and prior approval may survive a facial challenge when they preserve an adequate supply for protected on-reservation sales to tribal members for their own use and channel other sales through lawful tax collection. The exemption does not attach automatically to every wholesale purchase. Stamped taxable sales are not capped by the exempt-sales quota. Administrative burdens must remain reasonable and cannot abolish the exemption; facial validity does not foreclose proof of deficient administration in a particular application.

**Authority by component:**

- Valid nonmember tax, reasonable collection and protected member demand: [Department of Taxation and Finance of New York v. Milhelm Attea & Bros., Inc — Federal trader regulation does not displace reasonable collection of valid nonmember taxes; Probable-demand limits and prior approval survive only at facial scope](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Department_of_Taxation_and_Finance_of_New_York_v_Milhelm_Attea_and_Bros_Inc_merits_1994-06-13.md#public-projection), 1994-06-13. O'Connor's unanimous Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg adopt the limited federal-statute and collection holding. O'Connor's unanimous Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg adopt the qualified quota-and-approval holding and reversal of the facial injunction.

**Present operation effective:** Department of Taxation and Finance of New York v. Milhelm Attea & Bros., Inc, 1994-06-13.

**Exceptions and limits:** Tribal sovereign immunity and remedies against particular actors remain separate. The ruling does not authorize taxation of the protected member’s own on-reservation use.

**Holdings navigation:** [Federal trader regulation does not displace reasonable collection of valid nonmember taxes](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#federal-trader-regulation-does-not-displace-reasonable-collection-of-valid-nonmember-taxes); [Probable-demand limits and prior approval survive only at facial scope](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#probable-demand-limits-and-prior-approval-survive-only-at-facial-scope).

### Legal incidence of taxes on Indian-country tribal sales

**Governing question:** State tax incidence on a tribe or members in Indian country.

**Current rule:** Without clear congressional authorization, a State may not enforce a tax whose legal incidence falls on the Tribe or tribal members for Indian-country sales. Incidence is determined from the enacted payment, remittance and credit arrangement, not merely economic burden or the identity of the person transmitting funds. The reviewed retailer-incidence motor-fuels tax is barred on that basis.

**Authority by component:**

- Legal incidence of the reviewed motor-fuels tax: [Legal incidence of the reviewed motor-fuels tax](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Oklahoma_Tax_Commission_v_Chickasaw_Nation_merits_1995-06-14.md#public-projection), 1995-06-14. Ginsburg’s Opinion of the Court, joined by all eight other Justices; nine Justices support the fuel-incidence rule and application.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-14; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** A proposed incidence-shifting bill is not operative law. A later enacted shift requires independent analysis; this rule does not decide the amount of any refund or a different nonmember-incidence tax.

**Holdings navigation:** [Legal incidence of the reviewed motor-fuels tax](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#legal-incidence-of-the-reviewed-motor-fuels-tax).

## Property and takings

### Boundary-preserving conditions for artificial coastal extension

**Governing question:** The Corps' authority under Rivers and Harbors Act §10, 33 U.S.C. §403, to protect federal submerged-land rights when permitting an artificial coastal extension.

**Current rule:** Section 10 permits consideration of an artificial extension's prospective effect on federal submerged-land rights and permits conditioning approval on preservation of the existing federal-state boundary for additional entitlement attributable to the project. The Submerged Lands Act does not compel unconditional approval or invalidate such a condition when existing state property and claims remain intact. The condition must address the same boundary harm supporting withholding of approval; this holding supplies no power to demand unrelated existing territory or approve different conditions.

**Authority by component:**
- Statutory power, attributable-extension limit and preserved state interests: [United States v. Alaska](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/United_States_v_Alaska_original_summary_judgment_1992-04-21.md), April 21, 1992, “Statutory authority for the boundary-preserving condition,” White's Part II for all nine Justices. The source adopts this construction through the Nome condition's stated limits; it does not grant unconditional approval to all future disclaimers.

**Present operation effective:** Alaska, April 21, 1992.

**Open question:** Natural accretion, compulsory surrender of existing state territory, different permit conditions and a general rule governing States' constitutional takings remedies remain undecided.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Alaska, 503 U.S. 569 (1992) — Federal and state submerged-land boundaries](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-v-alaska-503-us-569-1992).

### Complete deprivation of economically beneficial land use

**Governing question:** Compensation for a land-use restriction eliminating all economically beneficial use of the relevant property.

**Current rule:** Complete deprivation effects a taking requiring just compensation unless background state property or nuisance law already excluded the prohibited use from the owner's title. After the owner establishes complete deprivation, the State must identify and substantiate a background limitation that independently prohibits the use in the property's circumstances. A new legislative declaration that the use is harmful is insufficient. Apply ordinary adjudicative proof requirements, without a special heightened quantum. Changed knowledge may affect application of genuine nuisance law; legislative labeling cannot substitute for an antecedent title limit. Compensation concerns any actual taking period, not an assumed permanent loss.

**Authority by component:**

- Categorical compensation, background-law exception and allocation: [Lucas v. South Carolina Coastal Council, “Complete economic deprivation requires compensation unless background ownership law already foreclosed the use”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Lucas_v_South_Carolina_Coastal_Council_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; Scalia, Stone, White, O'Connor and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** *Lucas*, June 29, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Penn Central's contextual analysis remains for partial deprivation. No percentage shortcut, parcel-definition rule, automatic valuation, right to anticipated profits or finding that every environmental restriction is invalid follows. Valid authority to regulate remains distinct from responsibility to compensate.

**Holdings navigation:** [Lucas v. South Carolina Coastal Council, 505 U.S. 1003 (1992) — Complete economic deprivation of land](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#lucas-v-south-carolina-coastal-council-505-us-1003-1992-1).

### Review of an elapsed categorical building prohibition despite later permitting

**Governing question:** Whether a later discretionary permit route defeats review of compensation for a completed period during which no building permission was available.

**Current rule:** A later discretionary procedure does not bar review of an earlier categorical prohibition when permission during that period was unavailable and the state supreme court finally rejected the compensation claim on the merits. Ordinary finality requirements remain outside that setting. Any established taking receives compensation for its actual duration; the later procedure, factual loss, duration, valuation and unaddressed defenses remain relevant rather than being decided by reviewability alone.

**Authority by component:**

- Earlier-period reviewability and limited temporal consequence: [Lucas, “The original categorical prohibition presents a reviewable earlier-period claim”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Lucas_v_South_Carolina_Coastal_Council_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; the five-Justice Court opinion applies First English's actual-period compensation requirement while distinguishing ordinary permit finality.

**Present operation effective:** *Lucas*, June 29, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [Lucas v. South Carolina Coastal Council, 505 U.S. 1003 (1992) — Finality and earlier-period takings claims](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#lucas-v-south-carolina-coastal-council-505-us-1003-1992).

### Interstate escheat obligations and priority evidence

**Governing question:** What instructions govern interstate escheat obligations and priority evidence?

**Current rule:** Identify the entity legally obligated under governing law to pay the unclaimed distribution before allocating escheat. An issuer discharged by payment to an intermediary record owner is not debtor for the intermediary's obligation to its customer; a mere paying agent owing the issuer differs. The creditor's last known address in debtor records supplies primary priority. If unknown or the primary State cannot escheat, debtor incorporation supplies secondary priority subject to a later established primary claim; executive offices do not replace incorporation. Proper evidence may identify addresses by individual transactions or another method actually identifying them. General estimates from broker concentration do not establish addresses or substitute a new allocation rule.

**Authority by component:**

- Identify the obligation before allocating unclaimed funds: [Delaware v. New York — Identify the obligation before allocating unclaimed funds](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/delaware_original_exceptions_1993-03-30.md), 1993-03-30. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court, I; Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.
- Address first, incorporation second: [Delaware v. New York — Address first, incorporation second](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/delaware_original_exceptions_1993-03-30.md), 1993-03-30. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court, II; Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.
- Evidence may prove an address but cannot substitute a new allocation rule: [Delaware v. New York — Evidence may prove an address but cannot substitute a new allocation rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/delaware_original_exceptions_1993-03-30.md), 1993-03-30. Thomas’s Opinion of the Court, III; Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-30; Delaware v. New York, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Delaware v. New York, 507 U.S. 490 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#delaware-v-new-york-507-us-490-1993).

### Military-service exclusion from real-property redemption time

**Governing question:** What instructions govern military-service exclusion from real-property redemption time?

**Current rule:** For real-property redemption periods covered by 50 U.S.C. App. §525, qualifying military service after October 6, 1942 is excluded from time computation without proof that service impaired redemption. Career active-duty personnel qualify. This tolling does not cancel taxes or import the separate prejudice conditions governing other statutory protections.

**Authority by component:**

- Service tolls redemption without a separate prejudice showing: [Conroy v. Aniskoff — Service tolls redemption without a separate prejudice showing](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/conroy_merits_1993-03-31.md), 1993-03-31. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, I; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (8 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-03-31; Conroy v. Aniskoff, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Conroy v. Aniskoff, 507 U.S. 511 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#conroy-v-aniskoff-507-us-511-1993).

### Enforcement and modification of interstate water decrees

**Governing question:** What instructions govern enforcement and modification of interstate water decrees?

**Current rule:** Enforcement of an established water-decree right requires no new injury showing; the decree, original opinion, report, record and relevant course of conduct may identify the right. New allocation or modification requires clear and convincing real and substantial injury. Rule 17.2 uses Rule 56 and its precedents as guides: no genuine material factual dispute and entitlement as law are required. A nonmovant with the ultimate burden must sufficiently support essential elements under the applicable enforcement or clear-and-convincing modification standard. This is distinct from Rufo's institutional-consent-decree framework and creates no general transfer of water rights through silence. A supported allegation of upstream groundwater depletion or changed federal storage administration may proceed only insofar as it materially threatens the decree’s allocation, operation or supporting conditions. Downstream nonirrigation and habitat evidence may be relevant to injury and lawful modification without creating an independent environmental entitlement or priority. Location below the decree’s principal diversion point alone does not foreclose a material decree connection. A State’s own pumping is no categorical unclean-hands defense without relevant conduct and a connection to the asserted harm; independent federal contractual compliance belongs in its lawful separate channel.

**Authority by component:**

- Enforcement and modification require different showings: [Nebraska v. Wyoming — Enforcement and modification require different showings](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/nebraska_partial_summary_judgment_1993-04-20.md), 1993-04-20. O'Connor’s Opinion of the Court, I; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.
- Summary judgment uses the applicable substantive burden: [Nebraska v. Wyoming — Summary judgment uses the applicable substantive burden](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/nebraska_partial_summary_judgment_1993-04-20.md), 1993-04-20. O'Connor’s Opinion of the Court, I; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

- Groundwater and equitable connection: [Groundwater and equitable connection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Nebraska_v_Wyoming_original_exceptions_1995-05-30.md#public-projection), 1995-05-30. Souter's Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer directly join this proposition.
- Downstream and habitat evidence: [Downstream and habitat evidence](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Nebraska_v_Wyoming_original_exceptions_1995-05-30.md#public-projection), 1995-05-30. Souter's Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer directly join this proposition.
- Horse Creek and supporting conditions: [Horse Creek and supporting conditions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Nebraska_v_Wyoming_original_exceptions_1995-05-30.md#public-projection), 1995-05-30. Souter's Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer directly join this proposition.
- Federal administration as decree predicate: [Federal administration as decree predicate](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Nebraska_v_Wyoming_original_exceptions_1995-05-30.md#public-projection), 1995-05-30. Souter's Opinion of the Court, federal-administration portion; Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer directly join this proposition.

**Present operation effective:** Nebraska v. Wyoming, May 30, 1995, for these pleading, relevance and defense boundaries; April 20, 1993, for the retained enforcement/modification proof standards.

**Holdings navigation:** [Nebraska v. Wyoming, 507 U.S. 584 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#nebraska-v-wyoming-507-us-584-1993); [Nebraska v. Wyoming, 507 U.S. 584 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#nebraska-v-wyoming-507-us-584-1993-1); [Groundwater and equitable connection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#groundwater-and-equitable-connection); [Downstream and habitat evidence](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#downstream-and-habitat-evidence); [Horse Creek and supporting conditions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#horse-creek-and-supporting-conditions); [Federal administration as decree predicate](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#federal-administration-as-decree-predicate).

**Exceptions and limits:** Leave to amend proves no injury, breach, priority or right to water. The settled allocation cannot be replaced through the admitted amendments by a mass beneficial-use ceiling; discrete geographic, priority, unlawful-call and relevant waste defenses not dependent on that ceiling remain. Kansas v. Colorado does not choose a universal Compact-enforcement quantum or displace this decree-modification standard.

### Donee ownership and civil-forfeiture relation back

**Governing question:** What instructions govern donee ownership and civil-forfeiture relation back?

**Current rule:** An actual owner may invoke §881(a)(6)'s innocent-owner defense despite acquisition by gift rather than purchase. To prevent forfeiture to the extent of that interest, the owner must establish that the relevant act or omission occurred without the owner's knowledge or consent under the statute. Section 881(h) does not self-execute an adjudication extinguishing the defense: the claimant may prove it before forfeiture is determined. If forfeiture is authorized, title then relates back to the triggering act subject to protected interests. Eligibility supplies neither innocence nor title. The precise temporal knowledge measure, general knowledge-versus-consent construction, tracing, estoppel and a constitutional guarantee remain open; §881(a)(7)'s distinct defense is not construed.

**Authority by component:**

- The innocent-owner defense is available to an owner who received a gift: [United States v. A Parcel of Land Known as 92 Buena Vista Avenue — The innocent-owner defense is available to an owner who received a gift](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/United_States_v_92_Buena_Vista_Avenue_merits_1993-02-24.md), 1993-02-24. Stevens's opinion, joined by Stone, Blackmun, O’Connor and Souter, supplies five votes for this proposition; Scalia and Thomas also accept donee eligibility in their judgment concurrence. The Court opinion independently controls.
- Relation back operates after the court determines that forfeiture is authorized: [United States v. A Parcel of Land Known as 92 Buena Vista Avenue — Relation back operates after the court determines that forfeiture is authorized](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/United_States_v_92_Buena_Vista_Avenue_merits_1993-02-24.md), 1993-02-24. Stevens's opinion, joined by Stone, Blackmun, O’Connor and Souter, controls with five votes. Scalia and Thomas concur in this practical result on their separately stated relation-back analysis; no narrower-ground inference is needed.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-02-24; United States v. A Parcel of Land Known as 92 Buena Vista Avenue, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. A Parcel of Land Known as 92 Buena Vista Avenue, 507 U.S. 111 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-v-a-parcel-of-land-known-as-92-buena-vista-avenue-507-us-111-1993).

### Process before civil forfeiture seizure of real property

**Governing question:** When the Government may seize real property for civil forfeiture before notice and hearing.

**Current rule:** The Government ordinarily must provide the owner notice and a meaningful opportunity to be heard before civil forfeiture seizure of real property; a Fourth Amendment warrant does not satisfy this independent Fifth Amendment duty. To proceed without prior process for exigent circumstances, the Government must show that less restrictive measures, such as recorded notice of the claim, a restraining order or a bond, would not prevent sale, destruction or continued unlawful use. Emergency seizure remains possible on that showing with appropriate subsequent process. The protection includes control and income from rented property, not only physical occupancy.

**Authority by component:**

- Prior process and Government exigency showing: [United States v. James Daniel Good Real Property — Notice and a hearing before civil seizure of real property](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/United_States_v_James_Daniel_Good_Real_Property_merits_1993-12-13.md#public-projection), 1993-12-13. Kennedy's opinion for the Court, Pre-seizure process portion, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, Souter, and Ginsburg. These six adopt the same rule, its exigency qualification, and its application.

**Present operation effective:** United States v. James Daniel Good Real Property, 1993-12-13.

**Exceptions and limits:** Movable-property exceptions retain their actual justification. No criminal-forfeiture rule, final ownership determination, immunity waiver or automatic compensation remedy follows.

**Holdings navigation:** [Notice and a hearing before civil seizure of real property](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#notice-and-a-hearing-before-civil-seizure-of-real-property).

### Nexus and rough proportionality for individualized land dedications

**Governing question:** What a government must establish when it conditions a development permit on dedication of property interests.

**Current rule:** For an individualized permit condition demanding a real-property dedication, the government must establish an essential nexus to a legitimate regulatory interest and rough proportionality between the nature and extent of the demanded dedication and the proposed development’s impact. An individualized determination must justify each demand; a generalized possibility of benefit is insufficient. Reasonable estimates may suffice, without exact mathematical precision or proof that this property is uniquely suited. Public access must itself be justified when a less intrusive restriction would address the impact.

**Authority by component:**

- Nexus, governmental burden and nature-and-extent comparison: [Dolan v. City of Tigard — The City must justify the nature and extent of each demanded dedication; The public-greenway dedication is insufficiently justified; The pathway dedication requires more than a possible traffic benefit](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Dolan_v_City_of_Tigard_merits_1994-06-24.md#public-projection), 1994-06-24. Justice O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas; five Justices adopt this rule and its government justification burden. Justice O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas; all five support both the rule and this application. Justice O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas; five Justices adopt the application and its estimation allowance.

**Present operation effective:** Dolan v. City of Tigard, 1994-06-24.

**Exceptions and limits:** The decision does not decide legislative exactions, monetary fees or every permit restriction. Failure of the showing does not automatically entitle the applicant to a permit or compensation; lawful reconsideration remains available.

**Holdings navigation:** [The City must justify the nature and extent of each demanded dedication](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-city-must-justify-the-nature-and-extent-of-each-demanded-dedication); [The public-greenway dedication is insufficiently justified](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-public-greenway-dedication-is-insufficiently-justified); [The pathway dedication requires more than a possible traffic benefit](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-pathway-dedication-requires-more-than-a-possible-traffic-benefit).

### Arkansas River Compact protection against material depletion

**Governing question:** The protection of usable quantity and availability under Article IV-D.

**Current rule:** Article IV-D protects usable quantity and availability against material depletion by post-Compact development, including improved or prolonged operation of existing works. Article VI-A(2) preserves Colorado’s administration subject to the Compact’s other terms. A liability baseline based on supported historical actual use is not replaced by hypothetical maximum capacity or later decreed quantities. Usability includes supported timing and recharge rather than gross annual flow alone.

## Environmental and administrative law

### Downstream water-quality standards in federal discharge permitting

**Governing question:** Whether and how EPA may apply downstream state standards in an interstate federal discharge permit, including where receiving waters are already degraded.

**Current rule:** The Clean Water Act authorizes EPA under §402(a) and its valid regulations to require compliance with applicable downstream state water-quality standards; limits on the downstream State's own direct authority do not restrict that federal delegation. The Act does not categorically forbid a new discharge merely because effluent will reach already noncompliant waters irrespective of whether the discharge causes a detectable violation under applicable permit standards. For the federal interstate permit setting decided in *Arkansas*, federally approved downstream standards incorporated by valid regulation operate as federal permitting law, and EPA's reasonable, consistently held construction receives substantial deference. EPA permissibly construed Oklahoma's no-degradation requirement to prohibit an actual detectable or measurable violation, rather than every infinitesimal theoretical effect. This construction preserves enforceable standards and supplies no general tolerance for pollution labeled trivial.

**Authority by component:**

- Delegated downstream-protection authority: [Arkansas v. Oklahoma — EPA may require downstream standards in this federal permit](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Arkansas_v_Oklahoma_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992.
- Rejection of an already-degraded-waters categorical bar: [Arkansas — Existing degradation is not a categorical statutory prohibition](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Arkansas_v_Oklahoma_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992.
- Incorporated standards and permissible detectable-violation construction: [Arkansas — Reasonable construction of the incorporated downstream standards](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Arkansas_v_Oklahoma_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992. Stevens's unanimous opinion controls these components.
- Distinct administrative proof and unchanged judicial-review standards needed to apply that construction: [Arkansas — Supported findings govern review of this permit](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Arkansas_v_Oklahoma_merits_1992-02-26.md), February 26, 1992; the same unanimous opinion.

**Present operation effective:** *Arkansas v. Oklahoma*, February 26, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** The no-detectable-violation showing in this permitting proceeding uses an administrative preponderance burden; reviewing courts apply whole-record substantial-evidence review rather than reweighing merely permissible alternative findings. Those standards retain their distinct functions. Existing degradation matters where the actual governing standards make it relevant; detectable violations, unreliable findings, and failure to apply valid standards remain reviewable.

**Open question:** The Court does not hold that Congress requires identical compliance in every federal or state permitting setting, construe state law for unrelated purposes, or decide independent source-state remedies.

**Holdings navigation:** [Arkansas v. Oklahoma, 503 U.S. 91 (1992) — Federal permit interpretation and review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#arkansas-v-oklahoma-503-us-91-1992); [Arkansas v. Oklahoma, 503 U.S. 91 (1992) — Interstate water-pollution permits](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#arkansas-v-oklahoma-503-us-91-1992-1).

### Acquisition for Amtrak service and the presumption of need

**Governing question:** The acquisition, negotiation and need requirements of 45 U.S.C. §562(d), including service-promoting reconveyance.

**Current rule:** The ICC may construe property required for passenger service to include property usefully and appropriately acquired for Amtrak's rail operations, without indispensable permanent Amtrak ownership; express service-promoting reconveyance authority remains available. The independent inability-to-agree condition requires a supported finding from the parties' actual positions that further sale negotiations will not produce agreement, without an additional mandatory bargaining ritual. An unaccepted unilateral offer alone is not invariably sufficient, and the need presumption does not replace that antecedent finding. To rebut presumed need, the railroad must establish both significant impairment of its common-carrier duties and an adequate available alternative. The ICC may assess impairment from the transaction as a whole, including preserved trackage rights and compensation, and may construe alternative property as an adequate alternative route rather than a lesser interest in the same segment. Each failure to prove a conjunctive condition independently leaves the presumption unrebutted. Passenger-service connection, just compensation and separate regulatory requirements continue to constrain acquisition.

**Authority by component:**
- Service-connected acquisition and reconveyance: [National Railroad Passenger Corp. v. Boston & Maine Corp.](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/National_Railroad_Passenger_Corp_v_Boston_and_Maine_Corp_merits_1992-03-25.md), March 25, 1992, holding 1.
- Independent negotiation prerequisite and its factual showing: the same record, holding 3, “Inability to agree on sale terms.”
- Conjunctive rebuttal and whole-transaction impairment: the same record, holding 4.
- Route-based alternative-property construction: the same record, holding 5. Kennedy for Stone, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia and Souter supplies six controlling joins throughout.

**Present operation effective:** National Railroad Passenger Corp., March 25, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [National Railroad Passenger Corp. v. Boston & Maine Corp., 503 U.S. 407 (1992) — Passenger-rail acquisition and agency review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#national-railroad-passenger-corp-v-boston--maine-corp-503-us-407-1992).

### An agency interpretation necessarily embodied in its decision

**Governing question:** Whether an agency's decision contains its own sufficient statutory ground when later counsel states that ground in more explicit words.

**Current rule:** A reviewing court may recognize an interpretation necessarily embodied in the agency's own decision even when the agency did not use the exact words later employed by counsel. The actual decision must disclose that operative premise. This permits recognition of the agency's ground, not judicial invention of a new ground, missing findings or a substitute policy; appellate rationalizations cannot sustain a rationale absent from the agency decision.

**Authority by component:**
- Discernible-agency-ground boundary: [National Railroad Passenger Corp. v. Boston & Maine Corp.](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/National_Railroad_Passenger_Corp_v_Boston_and_Maine_Corp_merits_1992-03-25.md), March 25, 1992, holding 2, “The agency's discernible ground”; Kennedy for Stone, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia and Souter, applying Chenery while preserving State Farm.

**Present operation effective:** National Railroad Passenger Corp., March 25, 1992.

**Holdings navigation:** [National Railroad Passenger Corp. v. Boston & Maine Corp., 503 U.S. 407 (1992) — Passenger-rail acquisition and agency review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#national-railroad-passenger-corp-v-boston--maine-corp-503-us-407-1992).

### Reviewability of the Secretary's completed census determination

**Governing question:** APA review of a completed population count followed by presidential transmission of apportionment figures.

**Current rule:** The Secretary's completed census determination is final agency action under §704 despite the President's later statutory transmission of the resulting apportionment. The census statutes neither preclude review nor commit counting entirely to unreviewable discretion: the obligation to count the proper population supplies law to apply, while leaving substantial methodological choice. General supervision and possible correction do not make this completed decision tentative; the prescribed transmission differs from a statutorily independent presidential policy judgment.

**Authority by component:**

- Finality and reviewability: [Franklin v. Massachusetts, “The Secretary's completed census determination is reviewable”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Franklin_v_Massachusetts_merits_1992-06-26.md), June 26, 1992; Stone's Part IV joined by Blackmun, Stevens, Kennedy and Souter.

**Present operation effective:** *Franklin*, June 26, 1992.

**Open question:** The President's APA status and susceptibility to coercive judicial relief are left undecided. This rule does not turn remaining agency discretion into entitlement to a selected outcome.

**Holdings navigation:** [Franklin v. Massachusetts, 505 U.S. 788 (1992) — Census determinations and judicial review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#franklin-v-massachusetts-505-us-788-1992-2).

### Procedural invalidity and entitlement to mandatory relief on appellate-stay review

**Governing question:** An application to vacate an appellate stay of an order granting effectively final individual relief from agency action.

**Current rule:** The applicant must show a substantial likelihood of entitlement to the ordered relief and demonstrable error in the appellate stay. If the asserted procedural defect would restore agency discretion rather than compel the requested substantive outcome, a strong procedural challenge and serious irreparable injury do not alone establish entitlement to mandatory judicial selection of that outcome. Authority to require lawful agency action is distinct from authority to determine its discretionary substance.

**Authority by component:**

- Relief-specific likelihood and remedial gap: [Benten v. Kessler, “A substantial procedural challenge does not alone establish entitlement to mandatory return”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Benten_v_Kessler_A-40_application_to_vacate_stay_1992-07-17.md), July 17, 1992; per curiam joined by Stone, White, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** *Benten*, July 17, 1992, at the appellate-stay stage.

**Exceptions and limits:** No final FDA procedural ruling, categorical exhaustion requirement, substantive drug approval, constitutional access rule or bar to promptly requiring lawful agency action is adopted. Stone's separate finding of likely procedural invalidity is not a component of the Court's rule.

**Holdings navigation:** [Benten v. Kessler, 505 U.S. 1084 (1992) — Appellate stays and mandatory agency relief](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#benten-v-kessler-505-us-1084-1992).

### Comparability limits in automatic housing-rent adjustments

**Governing question:** What instructions govern comparability limits in automatic housing-rent adjustments?

**Current rule:** Under the reviewed HAP terms, the automatic annual factor is subject to §1.9(d)'s overriding prohibition of material differences from comparable unassisted rents, while preserving differences at the initial contract date and the protected initial rent floor. HUD may use independent comparability studies to enforce those terms. Authority to use studies does not determine accuracy or lawful application of a particular study; no unconditional factor entitlement exists.

**Authority by component:**

- The comparability limit overrides an unconditional factor entitlement: [Cisneros v. Alpine Ridge Group — The comparability limit overrides an unconditional factor entitlement](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/cisneros_merits_1993-05-03.md), 1993-05-03. White’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-05-03; Cisneros v. Alpine Ridge Group, for the components identified above.
