### Adjudicatory waiver and monetary assessments under the McCarran Amendment

**Governing question:** What instructions govern adjudicatory waiver and monetary assessments under the McCarran Amendment?

**Current rule:** The McCarran Amendment subjects the United States to state water-adjudication procedures but does not specifically waive immunity from the filing assessments reviewed in Idaho. Express exclusion of judgments for costs and the blurred line between those charges and former costs reinforce the absence of monetary consent. Participation does not itself authorize assessments. The controlling rationale does not definitively classify every charge as costs or withdraw adjudicatory consent.

**Authority by component:**

- Adjudicatory consent does not authorize these monetary assessments: [United States v. Idaho ex rel. Director, Idaho Department of Water Resources — Adjudicatory consent does not authorize these monetary assessments](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/idaho_merits_1993-05-03.md), 1993-05-03. O'Connor’s Opinion of the Court; White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (7 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-05-03; United States v. Idaho ex rel. Director, Idaho Department of Water Resources, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States v. Idaho ex rel. Director, Idaho Department of Water Resources, 508 U.S. 1 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-v-idaho-ex-rel-director-idaho-department-of-water-resources-508-us-1-1993).

### Confidential sources and segregable information under FOIA

**Governing question:** What instructions govern confidential sources and segregable information under FOIA?

**Current rule:** Under Exemption 7(D), confidentiality requires express assurance or circumstances reasonably supporting its inference, not absolute secrecy. Government bears the burden; no universal FBI criminal-source presumption applies. Supported rebuttable inferences may rest on particular circumstances or a properly defined category, including crime and source relationship. Identity protection applies when law-enforcement disclosure could reasonably reveal a confidential source, including an agency, authority or private institution. The provision separately protects confidential-source information furnished in a criminal investigation by criminal-law-enforcement authority, and covers lawful national-security intelligence investigations. Identity redaction alone does not eliminate covered source-information protection; reasonably segregable nonexempt material must be released. Public-testimony waiver and other exemptions remain unresolved.

**Authority by component:**

- Confidentiality requires an assurance or a supported inference, not an FBI label: [United States Department of Justice v. Landano — Confidentiality requires an assurance or a supported inference, not an FBI label](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/landano_merits_1993-05-24.md), 1993-05-24. O'Connor’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.
- Source identity and criminal-source information receive distinct statutory protection: [United States Department of Justice v. Landano — Source identity and criminal-source information receive distinct statutory protection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/landano_merits_1993-05-24.md), 1993-05-24. O'Connor’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-05-24; United States Department of Justice v. Landano, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [United States Department of Justice v. Landano, 508 U.S. 165 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-department-of-justice-v-landano-508-us-165-1993).

### Unearmarked allocation and organizational policy exceptions

**Governing question:** What instructions govern unearmarked allocation and organizational policy exceptions?

**Current rule:** Distribution of a lump-sum appropriation among authorized statutory purposes is committed to agency discretion under §701(a)(2) when Congress supplies neither a binding earmark nor another meaningful allocation standard. Legislative-history references and the general trust relationship do not supply a duty to continue a particular program. Enacted boundaries and independent constitutional claims remain. An organizational policy reallocation changing service availability, rather than imposing a new binding eligibility rule, falls within §553(b)(A)'s organization and general-policy exceptions even assuming it is a rule. The separate §552 publication question remains open.

**Authority by component:**

- Allocation of this unearmarked lump sum is committed to agency discretion: [Lincoln v. Vigil — Allocation of this unearmarked lump sum is committed to agency discretion](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/lincoln_merits_1993-05-24.md), 1993-05-24. Souter’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.
- This organizational and policy change does not require notice and comment: [Lincoln v. Vigil — This organizational and policy change does not require notice and comment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/lincoln_merits_1993-05-24.md), 1993-05-24. Souter’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support for this exact proposition; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-05-24; Lincoln v. Vigil, for the components identified above.

### Medicare corrective adjustments and permissible interpretive change

**Governing question:** What instructions govern medicare corrective adjustments and permissible interpretive change?

**Current rule:** Section 1395x(v)(1)(A)(ii) commands suitable retroactive corrective adjustments when aggregate reimbursement for a provider and period proves inadequate or excessive. The Secretary may construe that command as reconciling interim payments to sums payable under lawful reasonable-cost methods and ceilings, without an individualized exception whenever actual costs exceed the ceiling. Authorized exceptions and independent challenges to unlawful methods remain. Interpretive change can reduce weight without automatically invalidating a considered permissible construction; text, delegation and reasons must be examined. Unsupported litigation positions neither replace required agency explanation nor gain automatic controlling weight.

**Authority by component:**

- Corrective adjustments operate within the authorized cost-method system: [Good Samaritan Hospital v. Shalala — Corrective adjustments operate within the authorized cost-method system](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/good_samaritan_merits_1993-06-07.md), 1993-06-07. White’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Kennedy, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.
- A changed agency interpretation is not automatically disqualified: [Good Samaritan Hospital v. Shalala — A changed agency interpretation is not automatically disqualified](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/good_samaritan_merits_1993-06-07.md), 1993-06-07. White’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O'Connor, Kennedy, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.

### Antecedent governing law and nonpositive-law Code omissions

**Governing question:** What instructions govern antecedent governing law and nonpositive-law Code omissions?

**Current rule:** In a live case a court may address whether the asserted authorizing statute remains in force after giving the parties fair opportunity to brief that necessary antecedent question. This does not require raising every unargued issue or bypassing adversarial fairness. Governing Statutes at Large control over an omission from a nonpositive-law Code edition; the enacted structure establishes that the 1916 insurance-agency provision amended Federal Reserve Act §13 rather than Revised Statutes §5202 and survived the 1918 amendment of the latter. This is no general license for policy rewriting. Geographic reach and the Comptroller's construction remain unresolved.

**Authority by component:**

- The court may decide a necessary antecedent question of governing law: [United States National Bank of Oregon v. Independent Insurance Agents of America, Inc. — The court may decide a necessary antecedent question of governing law](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/national_bank_merits_1993-06-07.md), 1993-06-07. Souter’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.
- The enacted statutory structure establishes that the insurance authorization survived: [United States National Bank of Oregon v. Independent Insurance Agents of America, Inc. — The enacted statutory structure establishes that the insurance authorization survived](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/national_bank_merits_1993-06-07.md), 1993-06-07. Souter’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-07; United States National Bank of Oregon v. Independent Insurance Agents of America, Inc., for the components identified above.

Independent Insurance Agents of America, Inc., 508 U.S. 439 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-national-bank-of-oregon-v-independent-insurance-agents-of-america-inc-508-us-439-1993); [United States National Bank of Oregon v. Independent Insurance Agents of America, Inc., 508 U.S. 439 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#united-states-national-bank-of-oregon-v-independent-insurance-agents-of-america-inc-508-us-439-1993-1).

### APA limits on exhaustion of superior-agency appeals

**Governing question:** What instructions govern APA limits on exhaustion of superior-agency appeals?

**Current rule:** In an APA action, an otherwise final agency decision is reviewable without an optional superior-agency appeal unless a statute expressly requires exhaustion or an agency rule both requires the appeal and makes the action inoperative pending it. Courts cannot add exhaustion beyond those alternatives. APA finality, reviewability and adequate-remedy conditions remain; express statutory exhaustion and non-APA judicial exhaustion retain their own operation.

**Authority by component:**

- Section 704 controls exhaustion of superior-agency review: [Darby v. Cisneros — Section 704 controls exhaustion of superior-agency review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Darby_v_Cisneros_merits_1993-06-21.md), 1993-06-21. Blackmun’s Opinion of the Court, Section 704 controls exhaustion of superior-agency review portion; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-21; Darby v. Cisneros, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Darby v. Cisneros, 509 U.S. 137 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#darby-v-cisneros-509-us-137-1993).

### Privacy Act incorporation of FOIA in federal bargaining requests

**Governing question:** When a federal labor-information duty can compel personal-record disclosure through the Privacy Act’s FOIA exception.

**Current rule:** Section 7114(b)(4) operates only to the extent disclosure is not prohibited by law. For a record in a system of records, §552a(b) prohibits disclosure absent the subject’s written request or prior written consent or an applicable enumerated exception. Under §552a(b)(2), FOIA itself must require disclosure. Collective-bargaining usefulness does not enlarge Reporters Committee’s ordinary third-party public-interest inquiry. Exemption 6 balances concrete privacy against demonstrated incremental public understanding; it permits withholding rather than commanding secrecy, while the Privacy Act supplies the prohibition through this invoked route when FOIA does not require release.

**Authority by component:**

- Labor duty, Privacy Act trigger and incorporated FOIA inquiry: [Department of Defense v. Federal Labor Relations Authority — The labor duty incorporates the Privacy Act's FOIA exception without alteration](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Department_of_Defense_v_FLRA_merits_1994-02-23.md#public-projection), 1994-02-23. Stevens's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; nine Justices adopt this statutory coordination and precedent-based explanation.
- Distinct Exemption 6 permission and resulting Privacy Act prohibition: [Department of Defense v. Federal Labor Relations Authority — The added home addresses do not justify disclosure on this record](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Department_of_Defense_v_FLRA_merits_1994-02-23.md#public-projection), 1994-02-23. Stevens's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; nine Justices adopt the same record-specific balance and reversal of both enforcement components.

**Present operation effective:** Department of Defense v. Federal Labor Relations Authority, 1994-02-23.

**Exceptions and limits:** The labor duty concerns requested, normally maintained, reasonably available data necessary for full and proper bargaining discussion, understanding and negotiation, excluding management/supervisory bargaining guidance, advice, counsel and training. Exemption 6 covers personnel, medical and similar files and a clearly unwarranted invasion; Exemption 7(C)’s different law-enforcement threshold is not substituted. Scattered public availability does not eliminate dissemination privacy. Routine use, subject consent, other exceptions and a different demonstrated transparency use remain separate. Requester neutrality does not erase a protected person’s position when disclosure is opposed on a privilege ground.

**Holdings navigation:** [The labor duty incorporates the Privacy Act's FOIA exception without alteration](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-labor-duty-incorporates-the-privacy-acts-foia-exception-without-alteration); [The added home addresses do not justify disclosure on this record](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-added-home-addresses-do-not-justify-disclosure-on-this-record).

### Municipal resource recovery and generated hazardous ash

**Governing question:** Whether RCRA §3001(i) exempts hazardous ash generated by a qualifying energy-recovery facility.

**Current rule:** Section 3001(i) does not exempt a qualifying facility’s generation of hazardous ash from Subtitle C merely because it excludes listed treatment, storage, disposal or other management activities at the facility. The facility predicate requires recovery of energy from burning municipal solid waste; receipt and burning only of household waste and nonhazardous commercial or industrial solid waste, with no hazardous waste identified or listed under §3001; and contractual requirements, or notification or inspection procedures, assuring that hazardous waste is not received or burned. These alternative compliance means must not become cumulative mandates. Even a household-only facility satisfying those predicates must treat generated hazardous ash according to the governing hazardous-waste requirements. An agency’s reconsideration cannot expand the exclusion beyond the statute.

**Authority by component:**

- Qualifying facility, excluded activities and unexcluded generation: [City of Chicago v. Environmental Defense Fund — The facility exclusion does not exempt generated hazardous ash; Agency reconsideration does not authorize an interpretation beyond the statute](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/City_of_Chicago_v_Environmental_Defense_Fund_merits_1994-05-02.md#public-projection), 1994-05-02. Scalia’s opinion for the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; seven Justices adopt the statutory rule and its stated limits. The same seven-Justice opinion: Scalia, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. Stevens and O’Connor disagree about the statutory range, not the existence of Congress’s limiting authority.

**Present operation effective:** City of Chicago v. Environmental Defense Fund, 1994-05-02.

**Holdings navigation:** [Agency reconsideration does not authorize an interpretation beyond the statute](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#agency-reconsideration-does-not-authorize-an-interpretation-beyond-the-statute); [The facility exclusion does not exempt generated hazardous ash](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-facility-exclusion-does-not-exempt-generated-hazardous-ash).

### Water-quality certification and conditions on the licensed activity

**Governing question:** What §401 permits a certifying authority to regulate once a federally licensed activity may discharge into navigable waters.

**Current rule:** An activity requiring a federal license or permit that may result in a discharge into navigable waters triggers §401(a)(1). Certification comes from the originating State, the appropriate interstate agency, or the Administrator if the state or interstate agency lacks authority. Section 401(d) authorizes limitations and monitoring on the applicant’s activity as necessary to assure compliance with applicable §§301, 302, 306 and 307 requirements and other appropriate state-law requirements stated in the certification; authority is not confined to the triggering discharge. Section 301(b)(1)(C)’s incorporation of more stringent limitations needed to meet water-quality standards, and the separate appropriate-state-law clause, each independently include lawful §303 standards. Neither route authorizes unrelated project policy. Lawful conditions become conditions of the later federal license. State water-allocation savings preserve allocation authority and established quantity rights, but do not exempt activities from necessary water-quality flow conditions or turn certification into a water-right permit. States may preserve stricter pollution requirements, but §510(1) forbids less-stringent requirements where the corresponding federal requirements are in effect.

**Authority by component:**

- Discharge trigger, independent incorporation routes, quantity savings and license effect: [PUD — A discharge triggers certification; necessary conditions can govern the activity; Section 301 supplies one route incorporating water-quality standards; The appropriate-State-law clause independently includes these standards; Water-allocation savings do not categorically exclude necessary flow protection; This authorized certification condition survives the present licensing objection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/PUD_No_1_of_Jefferson_County_v_Washington_Department_of_Ecology_merits_1994-05-31.md#public-projection), 1994-05-31. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg; seven direct joins to this proposition and its explanation.

**Present operation effective:** PUD, 1994-05-31.

**Exceptions and limits:** Certification applications require public notice; hearings are required only to the extent the authority deems appropriate. Failure or refusal to act within a reasonable time, no longer than one year after receipt, waives certification for that application. A license cannot issue before required certification or waiver and cannot issue after certification is denied. Where the specified effluent limitations and performance standards do not apply, the State must so certify, without thereby satisfying §511(c). The decision resolves no actual conflict with a later FERC license; independent federal-license review remains available. It creates no §402 evidentiary rule.

**Holdings navigation:** [A discharge triggers certification; necessary conditions can govern the activity](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#a-discharge-triggers-certification-necessary-conditions-can-govern-the-activity); [Section 301 supplies one route incorporating water-quality standards](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#section-301-supplies-one-route-incorporating-water-quality-standards); [Water-allocation savings do not categorically exclude necessary flow protection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#water-allocation-savings-do-not-categorically-exclude-necessary-flow-protection); [This authorized certification condition survives the present licensing objection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#this-authorized-certification-condition-survives-the-present-licensing-objection).

### Designated water uses, criteria and preservation of existing uses

**Governing question:** How enforceable water-quality standards protect uses beyond numerical pollutant criteria.

**Current rule:** A §303 standard contains designated uses and criteria based on those uses; compliance with numerical criteria does not invariably satisfy the standard when an activity would defeat a protected use. A necessary minimum-flow condition can protect an approved fish use without a numerical-criteria violation. Criteria must rest on sound science and sufficiently protect the uses, including the most sensitive when there are several. Criteria may be concentrations, levels or narrative statements; the regulatory direction that States should establish numerical values is not a universal shall. Section 303(c)(2)(B) separately requires numerical criteria for listed toxic pollutants with published criteria whose presence or discharge could reasonably interfere with designated uses, as necessary to support them; where numerical criteria are unavailable, biological monitoring or assessment criteria consistent with the prescribed information apply. This does not limit or delay biological permit conditions or previously adopted numerical criteria. Narrative toxic controls also require the applicable method for regulating point-source toxic discharges on water-quality-limited segments.

**Authority by component:**

- Uses and criteria with statutory toxic-criteria qualifications: [PUD — Designated uses are enforceable components alongside criteria](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/PUD_No_1_of_Jefferson_County_v_Washington_Department_of_Ecology_merits_1994-05-31.md#public-projection), 1994-05-31. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg; seven direct joins to this proposition and its explanation.
- Independent protection of existing uses: [PUD — Protection of the existing fish use also sustains this condition](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/PUD_No_1_of_Jefferson_County_v_Washington_Department_of_Ecology_merits_1994-05-31.md#public-projection), 1994-05-31. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg; seven direct joins to this proposition and its explanation.

**Present operation effective:** PUD, 1994-05-31.

**Exceptions and limits:** A nonexisting designated use may be removed, or subcategories established, only upon demonstrated infeasibility for an authorized reason: natural pollutant concentrations; natural ephemeral, intermittent or low flows unless sufficient effluent can compensate consistently with conservation requirements; irremediable human conditions or conditions more damaging to correct; a dam, diversion or hydrologic modification where neither feasible restoration nor operation can attain the use; natural physical features unrelated to water quality precluding aquatic-life protection; or controls beyond §§301(b) and 306 causing substantial and widespread economic and social impact. Existing uses cannot be removed unless a more protective use is added; uses attainable through statutory effluent limits and cost-effective reasonable nonpoint practices cannot be removed. Notice and a hearing opportunity precede use or subcategory changes. Omission or removal of §101(a)(2) uses or a less-stringent subcategory requires attainability analysis; designation including those uses does not. Seasonal criteria cannot defeat a more protective use in another season.

**Controlling construction:** Existing instream uses and necessary water quality must be maintained and protected. Higher-quality water may be lowered only after the State finds necessity for important area economic or social development through full intergovernmental coordination and public participation; existing uses remain fully protected, the highest statutory and regulatory point-source requirements must be achieved, and all cost-effective reasonable nonpoint practices applied. Outstanding National resource waters retain their separate protection, and thermal impairment remains subject to §316. Class AA status or federal land alone does not establish an outstanding-resource designation. Washington’s separate existing-beneficial-use protection supplies an additional ground; its prohibition on degradation injuring existing uses is not merged with its separate provision requiring both such injury and long-term irreparable environmental harm. No universal dam-operation duty, optimal-fishery requirement or adjudicated infeasibility finding follows.

**Holdings navigation:** [Designated uses are enforceable components alongside criteria](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#designated-uses-are-enforceable-components-alongside-criteria).

### Private CERCLA fees and independently necessary response work

**Governing question:** Which legal-professional expenses qualify as private CERCLA response costs.

**Current rule:** Sections 107(a)(4)(B) and 101(25) do not shift private litigation attorney’s fees; a reference to enforcement activities is not the required authorization for that private award. Work performed by a lawyer can nevertheless qualify when the actual task independently constitutes a necessary response activity, such as identifying other responsible parties in a way that advances cleanup, and the private claimant proves necessity and consistency with the National Contingency Plan. Job title and billing label are not controlling. Work protecting the client’s own allocation or liability position in negotiations with EPA is not thereby a response cost; truly separable qualifying work must be established as such.

**Authority by component:**

- No private litigation fee shifting: [Key Tronic Corp. v. United States — CERCLA does not authorize these private litigation fees](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Key_Tronic_Corp_v_United_States_merits_1994-06-06.md#public-projection), 1994-06-06. Part I of Stevens's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg. Six Justices adopt the litigation-fee exclusion; Blackmun, Scalia and Thomas dissent from that component.
- Task-specific response costs and exclusion of liability negotiations: [Key Tronic Corp. v. United States — Independent identification work may be a necessary response cost; The claimed EPA-negotiation work protected the client's liability position](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Key_Tronic_Corp_v_United_States_merits_1994-06-06.md#public-projection), 1994-06-06. Part II of Stevens's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine expressly join this severable task-function portion and its remand. Part III of Stevens's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine expressly join this record-specific functional exclusion and affirmance.

**Present operation effective:** Key Tronic Corp. v. United States, 1994-06-06.

**Exceptions and limits:** The private claimant’s “consistent with” requirement is not the Government’s distinct “not inconsistent with” standard. The holding supplies neither a universal legal-work exclusion nor an award for all useful professional activity.

**Holdings navigation:** [CERCLA does not authorize these private litigation fees](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#cercla-does-not-authorize-these-private-litigation-fees); [Independent identification work may be a necessary response cost](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#independent-identification-work-may-be-a-necessary-response-cost); [The claimed EPA-negotiation work protected the client's liability position](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-claimed-epa-negotiation-work-protected-the-clients-liability-position).

### Limited modification authority and mandatory tariff filing

**Governing question:** Whether the Communications Act’s power to modify filing requirements permits eliminating them for an entire class.

**Current rule:** Section 203(b)(2) permits the Commission, in its discretion and for good cause shown, to modify requirements made by or under §203 in particular instances or by general order applicable to special circumstances or conditions; no required notice period may exceed 120 days. That authority permits adjustments to filing form, contents, timing or place, including consequential and class-wide technical changes, but not replacement of mandatory filing throughout the nondominant long-distance sector with an optional system. The inquiry examines the obligation and whether its essential operation is adjusted or removed, not a numerical market-share threshold. Good cause alone cannot authorize the replacement. The Act’s exception for connecting carriers preserves their separate inspection duties; §203(c)’s allowance for otherwise lawful authority does not confer unlimited dispensing power.

**Authority by component:**

- Bounded modification and distinct notice authority: [MCI Telecommunications Corp. v. American Telephone & Telegraph Co — The modification power does not permit this replacement of mandatory filing](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/MCI_Telecommunications_Corp_v_AT_and_T_merits_1994-06-17.md#public-projection), 1994-06-17. Chief Justice Stone-Zsela's Opinion of the Court, joined by Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg. Five of eight participating Justices directly adopt the structural modification ground, its application and its stated limits; no aggregation of separate rationales is necessary.

**Present operation effective:** MCI Telecommunications Corp. v. American Telephone & Telegraph Co, 1994-06-17.

**Exceptions and limits:** Limited deferral or waiver is not categorically foreclosed, but no particular waiver is approved. Failure of the special-circumstances-or-conditions predicate is a four-Justice alternative, not a separate controlling holding. No universal major-questions doctrine is created; the statute’s permissible flexibility remains available within the actual delegation.

**Holdings navigation:** [The modification power does not permit this replacement of mandatory filing](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-modification-power-does-not-permit-this-replacement-of-mandatory-filing).

### APA persuasion burdens and statutory departures

**Governing question:** Who loses when evidence is genuinely balanced in an adjudication governed by APA §556(d).

**Current rule:** Unless a controlling statute otherwise provides, §556(d)’s burden of proof assigns the burden of persuasion to the proponent of the order; in the proceedings governed here, the proponent must establish the claim by a preponderance and loses at genuine equipoise. Burdens of producing evidence, lawful presumptions and separately allocated defenses remain distinct. The Longshore Act’s incorporation of APA adjudication is not displaced by §23’s evidentiary flexibility or its treatment of corroborated declarations of deceased employees. In the black-lung setting, even assuming statutory authority for an otherwise valid regulatory departure, the invoked remedial-policy provision does not establish an ultimate tie-breaking rule.

**Authority by component:**

- Persuasion allocation: [Director, Office of Workers’ Compensation Programs v. Greenwich Collieries — The APA assigns persuasion to the proponent of an order](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Director_Office_of_Workers_Compensation_Programs_v_Greenwich_Collieries_merits_1994-06-20.md#public-projection), 1994-06-20. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg. Six participating Justices directly adopt this allocation, its application to both proceedings, and the limited treatment of prior precedent; no aggregation of different grounds is necessary.
- Longshore incorporation despite evidentiary flexibility: [Director, Office of Workers’ Compensation Programs v. Greenwich Collieries — The Longshore Act's evidentiary flexibility does not displace the APA allocation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Director_Office_of_Workers_Compensation_Programs_v_Greenwich_Collieries_merits_1994-06-20.md#public-projection), 1994-06-20. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg. These six adopt the Longshore incorporation holding and apply it to both the lifetime-disability and death-benefit claims in Maher Terminals.
- No established black-lung departure: [Director, Office of Workers’ Compensation Programs v. Greenwich Collieries — The invoked black-lung regulation does not establish a departure from the APA](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Director_Office_of_Workers_Compensation_Programs_v_Greenwich_Collieries_merits_1994-06-20.md#public-projection), 1994-06-20. O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg. Six Justices adopt the actual-regulation ground, the express reservation of broader delegated power, and the bounded administrative remedy.

**Present operation effective:** Director, Office of Workers’ Compensation Programs v. Greenwich Collieries, 1994-06-20.

**Exceptions and limits:** Transportation Management’s contrary description of the APA burden is disapproved; its distinct mixed-motive defense allocation is not overruled. The full scope of power to create a black-lung exception is left open.

**Holdings navigation:** [The APA assigns persuasion to the proponent of an order](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-apa-assigns-persuasion-to-the-proponent-of-an-order); [The Longshore Act's evidentiary flexibility does not displace the APA allocation](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-longshore-acts-evidentiary-flexibility-does-not-displace-the-apa-allocation); [The invoked black-lung regulation does not establish a departure from the APA](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-invoked-black-lung-regulation-does-not-establish-a-departure-from-the-apa).

### Medicare educational costs shifted from a related institution

**Governing question:** How the Medicare anti-redistribution regulation limits otherwise eligible education costs.

**Current rule:** The Secretary may reasonably apply §413.85(c)’s prohibition on redistribution of costs from educational institutions to hospitals to deny newly shifted costs formerly borne by a related college. Eligibility as an educational activity does not create an unconditional reimbursement right. Section 413.17’s cost-related-organization rule does not override the separate redistribution limitation. The validity of the actual agency ground must be assessed under the regulation and the relevant agency practice; neither an invented historical-cost ceiling nor a different rationale can replace that ground.

**Authority by component:**

- Anti-redistribution limit and review of the actual agency rationale: [Thomas Jefferson University v. Shalala — The redistribution provision reasonably limits newly shifted educational costs; The actual agency ground survives the asserted inconsistency](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Thomas_Jefferson_University_v_Shalala_merits_1994-06-24.md#public-projection), 1994-06-24. Justice Kennedy's Opinion of the Court, Part I, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Scalia and Souter. Five participating Justices directly adopt this regulatory construction, its application and its limits. Justice Kennedy's Opinion of the Court, Part II, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Scalia and Souter. The same five Justices adopt this answer to the consistency and actual-ground objections; it supports application of the redistribution holding rather than furnishing an alternative reimbursement bar.

**Present operation effective:** Thomas Jefferson University v. Shalala, 1994-06-24.

**Related current rules:** The existing rules for interpretation of an agency’s own regulations and corrective Medicare adjustments remain effective; this is a particular reimbursement limitation, not a new general deference test.

**Holdings navigation:** [The redistribution provision reasonably limits newly shifted educational costs](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-redistribution-provision-reasonably-limits-newly-shifted-educational-costs); [The actual agency ground survives the asserted inconsistency](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-actual-agency-ground-survives-the-asserted-inconsistency).

### Express public credit enforcement alongside filed-rate duties

**Governing question:** Availability of an ICC injunction against collection violating credit regulations under the applicable pre-reform provisions.

**Current rule:** Sections 10743(b)(1) and 11702(a)(4) authorize the ICC to seek a judicial injunction against collection violating valid credit regulations; §§10761–10762’s filed-rate duties do not categorically defeat that channel. Section 11702(a)(4) excludes an action under a provision governing rate reasonableness or discrimination, but enforcement of credit and billing requirements is not transformed into such a rate-review action by credit regulation’s antidiscrimination purpose.

**Authority by component:**

- The filed-rate duty does not defeat express public credit-rule enforcement: [The filed-rate duty does not defeat express public credit-rule enforcement](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Interstate_Commerce_Commission_v_Transcon_Lines_merits_1995-01-10.md#public-projection), 1995-01-10. Justice Kennedy's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. All nine participating Justices directly adopt this proposition and its qualifications; no aggregation of separate rationales is required.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-01-10; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Statutory authority alone proves neither a violation nor entitlement to every requested restraint. No general private equitable defense, cancellation of lawful freight principal, universal debt discharge or rule under later transportation reforms is created.

**Holdings navigation:** [The filed-rate duty does not defeat express public credit-rule enforcement](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-filed-rate-duty-does-not-defeat-express-public-credit-rule-enforcement).

### Medicare accounting records and lawful allocation explanations

**Governing question:** Whether GAAP recognition and an interpretive manual compel or defeat a reimbursement timing rule.

**Current rule:** Section 1395x(v)(1)(A) and §§413.20 and 413.24 make adequate, verifiable accrual records the accounting base but do not make every GAAP recognition rule conclusive for Medicare reimbursement. Lawful reasonable-cost methods may distribute relevant advance-refunding expense elements across benefited periods. A manual explaining a permissible application of existing regulations is interpretive under APA §553(b)(A), rather than a new conflicting obligation requiring notice and comment. Neither interpretive form nor adjudication permits contradiction of binding law.

**Authority by component:**

- The accounting regulations do not compel immediate recognition of this loss: [The accounting regulations do not compel immediate recognition of this loss](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Shalala_v_Guernsey_Memorial_Hospital_merits_1995-03-06.md#public-projection), 1995-03-06. Kennedy’s opinion of the Court, Parts I–III, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Ginsburg, and Breyer. Five Justices expressly adopt this specific construction; no aggregation of different rationales or Marks rule is used.
- The manual explains a lawful allocation rather than changing binding law: [The manual explains a lawful allocation rather than changing binding law](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Shalala_v_Guernsey_Memorial_Hospital_merits_1995-03-06.md#public-projection), 1995-03-06. Kennedy’s opinion of the Court, Parts I–III, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Ginsburg, and Breyer. The same five expressly adopt interpretive status, the lawful actual ground, and the bounded remedy.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-03-06; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Actual incurred cost excludes unnecessary costs in efficient delivery of needed services; allocation must prevent cross-subsidy in either direction between Medicare and non-Medicare patients. Necessary-and-proper-interest and reasonable patient-care-cost limits remain. No universal amortization period, immediate-recognition right or general deference rule follows.

**Controlling construction:** For advance refunding through new debt initiated on or after July 1, 1983, PRM §233 treats expense elements separately. New issue costs amortize from incurrence to new scheduled maturity; old unamortized discount, premium and issue costs run from new issuance until old holders receive principal, proportionately for serial bonds. Call premiums or penalties become allowable when old holders receive principal, prorated for serial payments. Interest on both debts is allowed annually as paid or accrued, with new discount/premium amortization adjusting interest and income from new proceeds offsetting it, including trust earnings. Redemption, miscellaneous expenses and annual authority/trustee fees retain their independent §2305 qualification; its exact allowance conditions are not established by this decision and no dependent entitlement is decided.

**Effective transition:** Recall without new debt is outside §233’s advance-refunding rule. Necessary borrowing accounts for available sinking funds, and excess borrowing to buy depreciable assets accounts for existing funded depreciation. Ordinary net allowable refinancing costs cannot exceed costs without refinancing; compelling factors such as necessary cash flow or removal of a restrictive covenant may justify more upon a showing satisfactory to the intermediary. Without that showing the annual ceiling remains. No equity-capital award or later §1395hh rulemaking disposition is supplied.

**Holdings navigation:** [The accounting regulations do not compel immediate recognition of this loss](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-accounting-regulations-do-not-compel-immediate-recognition-of-this-loss); [The manual explains a lawful allocation rather than changing binding law](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-manual-explains-a-lawful-allocation-rather-than-changing-binding-law).

### Habitat injury within the Endangered Species Act’s harm prohibition

**Governing question:** The permissible scope and causal limits of the regulatory habitat clause.

**Current rule:** The ESA permits “harm” to include significant habitat modification or degradation that actually kills or injures protected wildlife by significantly impairing essential behavioral patterns, including breeding, feeding or sheltering. Actual death or injury must be proximately caused with ordinary foreseeability; bare but-for linkage or speculative future harm is insufficient. An omission may count only where an independently existing legal duty supplies the obligation. This decision creates no general duty to maintain habitat. Independently, where the statutory definition leaves room on indirect injury, the Secretary’s actual-injury construction is reasonable and receives Chevron deference without becoming the only permissible construction.

**Authority by component:**

- 1. Statutory authority for habitat-caused injury: [1. Statutory authority for habitat-caused injury](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Babbitt_v_Sweet_Home_Chapter_of_Communities_for_a_Great_Oregon_merits_1995-06-29.md#public-projection), 1995-06-29. Souter's Opinion of the Court, Part I; Stone, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg, and Breyer join this statutory proposition. Seven votes make it controlling independently of Part II's deference rationale.
- 2. Reasonable delegated construction: [2. Reasonable delegated construction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Babbitt_v_Sweet_Home_Chapter_of_Communities_for_a_Great_Oregon_merits_1995-06-29.md#public-projection), 1995-06-29. Souter's Opinion of the Court, Part II; Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg, and Breyer join. Six votes independently control this rationale; Stone does not join it.
- 3. Actual injury and ordinary causal limits: [3. Actual injury and ordinary causal limits](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Babbitt_v_Sweet_Home_Chapter_of_Communities_for_a_Great_Oregon_merits_1995-06-29.md#public-projection), 1995-06-29. Souter's Opinion of the Court, Part III; Stone, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg, and Breyer join the common rule. O'Connor's concrete reproduction example and Palila reservations are her concurrence, not additional majority applications.
- 4. Distinct statutory protections and conditional permissions: [4. Distinct statutory protections and conditional permissions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Babbitt_v_Sweet_Home_Chapter_of_Communities_for_a_Great_Oregon_merits_1995-06-29.md#public-projection), 1995-06-29. Souter's Opinion of the Court, Part IV; Stone, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg, and Breyer join. The statutory qualifications below are integral limits, not new adjudications of applications.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-29; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Section 9(a)(1) governs listed endangered fish or wildlife and persons subject to United States jurisdiction, with taking prohibitions in the United States, territorial sea and high seas, subject to §§6(g)(2) and 10. Threatened-species protections arise separately under §4(d), §9(a)(1)(G) and lawful regulations, including §17.31. For resident fish or wildlife in a State with a §6(c) cooperative agreement, §4(d) rules apply only to the extent also adopted by the State. Section 7’s agency no-jeopardy and critical-habitat obligations do not replace the actual-taking inquiry. A no-jeopardy opinion alone exempts no taking: §7(o)(1) protects activity necessary to a §7(h)-exempted agency action, while §7(o)(2) protects compliance with an actual §7(b)(4) written incidental-take statement’s terms.

**Sequence or decisionmaker:** Section 10(a)(1)(A) separately permits scientific or propagation-and-survival permissions. Incidental-take permission under §10(a)(1)(B) requires taking incidental to, not the purpose of, otherwise lawful activity. The conservation plan must state likely impacts, minimization/mitigation steps and funding, considered alternatives and rejected reasons, and other necessary appropriate measures. After public comment the Secretary must find incidental taking, maximum-practicable minimization and mitigation, adequate funding, no appreciable reduction in survival-and-recovery likelihood in the wild, fulfillment of other required measures and receipt of assurances. Those findings require issuance with necessary or appropriate conditions and reporting; noncompliance requires revocation. Otherwise lawful land use alone is no exemption.

**Controlling construction:** Section 10(j)’s released experimental populations, including solely their offspring, qualify only while wholly geographically separate from nonexperimental populations of the same species. Outside-range release requires a conservation finding, regulatory identification before release and a best-available-information essentiality determination. Members are treated as threatened; a nonessential population is treated as proposed for listing under §7 except §7(a)(1), with the National Wildlife Refuge and National Park System exception. Critical habitat may not be designated for a nonessential population; earlier authorized separate releases retain the statutory regulatory-determination condition. No population application is decided.

**Open question:** Section 6(g)(2)’s exact exception and its application are not construed: the Record identifies the official text and applicable State-agreement record as necessary to decide that question. The facial rule adjudicates no enforcement scienter or universal knowledge-of-illegality requirement. Separate §11 civil and criminal channels retain applicable notice and hearing requirements and the civil good-faith protection for action believed necessary to protect a person from bodily harm.

**Holdings navigation:** [1. Statutory authority for habitat-caused injury](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#statutory-authority-for-habitat-caused-injury); [2. Reasonable delegated construction](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#statutory-authority-for-habitat-caused-injury); [3. Actual injury and ordinary causal limits](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#actual-injury-and-ordinary-causal-limits); [4. Distinct statutory protections and conditional permissions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#distinct-statutory-protections-and-conditional-permissions).

## Attorney's fees

### No contingency enhancement under the environmental fee provisions

**Governing question:** Enhancement above a properly determined lodestar for the risk of nonpayment when counsel's compensation depends on success.

**Current rule:** A reasonable fee under 42 U.S.C. §6972(e) and 33 U.S.C. §1365(d) may not be enhanced to compensate contingency risk. A market-access rationale supplies no exception. The ordinary reasonable-hours-and-rate lodestar, applicant's ordinary burden and prohibition against double counting remain in force.

**Authority by component:**

- Categorical contingency prohibition: [City of Burlington v. Dague, “Contingency does not justify an enhanced statutory fee”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/City_of_Burlington_v_Dague_merits_1992-06-24.md), June 24, 1992; Scalia, White, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas. The record adopts White's previously noncontrolling Delaware Valley II approach for these statutes.

**Present operation effective:** *Dague*, June 24, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** Delay adjustments, correction of an unreasonable underlying rate, differently worded compensation schemes and common-fund fees are not decided. The ruling adjudicates these two provisions; it does not enlarge Ardestani's administrative-eligibility rule or supply a federal-immunity premise.

**Holdings navigation:** [City of Burlington v. Dague, 505 U.S. 557 (1992) — Statutory attorney's fees](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#city-of-burlington-v-dague-505-us-557-1992).

### Nominal civil-rights relief and reasonable fees

**Governing question:** What instructions govern nominal civil-rights relief and reasonable fees?

**Current rule:** An enforceable nominal-damages judgment directly benefiting the plaintiff by changing the fee defendant's legal obligation establishes prevailing-party eligibility under §1988. Magnitude bears on amount, not that eligibility. Reasonable fees require an explained Hensley assessment of actual success and unrelated unsuccessful work, permitting reduction to zero without mechanical proportionality to damages or a presumption of substantial fees. Correcting an appellate eligibility error does not itself fix the amount or automatically reinstate a prior award.

**Authority by component:**

- Enforceable nominal relief establishes eligibility: [Farrar v. Hobby — Enforceable nominal relief establishes eligibility](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Farrar_v_Hobby_merits_1992-12-14.md), 1992-12-14. Part I of White’s opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas; all nine Justices support this controlling proposition.
- Reasonable amount requires reconsideration on this review: [Farrar v. Hobby — Reasonable amount requires reconsideration on this review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Farrar_v_Hobby_merits_1992-12-14.md), 1992-12-14. Part II of White’s opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Souter; five Justices control the remedy and stated fee principles.

**Present operation effective:** 1992-12-14; Farrar v. Hobby, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Farrar v. Hobby, 506 U.S. 103 (1992)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#farrar-v-hobby-506-us-103-1992).

### Social Security remand judgments and EAJA fees

**Governing question:** What instructions govern social Security remand judgments and EAJA fees?

**Current rule:** A sentence-four §405(g) judgment reversing a benefits denial with remand terminates the civil action and confers prevailing-party status; judgment must be entered rather than retaining the merits action pending an administrative award. Sentence-six remands are available on the Secretary's pre-answer motion for good cause, or for new material evidence with good cause for its earlier omission; these proceedings retain jurisdiction for the further administrative proceedings and return to court. Under the then-effective Rules, EAJA's thirty-day application period follows proper judgment entry and expiration of the applicable sixty-day appeal period; absence of the Rule 58 separate judgment prevents expiration. Hudson's integrated-administration fee rationale does not govern a properly entered sentence-four remand without retained jurisdiction, or retention challenged as improper; it remains available for qualifying sentence-six proceedings subject to EAJA's other conditions. No later deemed-entry rule applies.

**Authority by component:**

- Sentence-four judgment ends the action and confers prevailing status: [Shalala v. Schaefer — Sentence-four judgment ends the action and confers prevailing status](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Shalala_v_Schaefer_merits_1993-06-24.md), 1993-06-24. Scalia’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (7 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.
- The missing separate judgment prevents the fee deadline from expiring: [Shalala v. Schaefer — The missing separate judgment prevents the fee deadline from expiring](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Shalala_v_Schaefer_merits_1993-06-24.md), 1993-06-24. Scalia’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (7 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.
- Hudson does not preserve administrative-fee coverage after an ordinary terminated sentence-four action: [Shalala v. Schaefer — Hudson does not preserve administrative-fee coverage after an ordinary terminated sentence-four action](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Shalala_v_Schaefer_merits_1993-06-24.md), 1993-06-24. Scalia’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; White, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-24; Shalala v. Schaefer, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Shalala v. Schaefer, 509 U.S. 292 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#shalala-v-schaefer-509-us-292-1993).

### Evenhanded discretionary copyright fees

**Governing question:** What standard governs a prevailing copyright party’s §505 fee request.

**Current rule:** Prevailing plaintiffs and defendants are eligible under the same discretionary standard. Frivolousness or bad faith cannot be an added prerequisite imposed only on defendants. Frivolousness, motivation, objective factual or legal unreasonableness, and compensation or deterrence may be considered insofar as they serve copyright’s purposes and are used evenhandedly; they are neither cumulative elements nor an exclusive or weighted formula. Section 505 permits but does not require reasonable fees, subject to independently applicable limitations of the title. Prevailing status supplies no automatic full-reimbursement entitlement.

**Authority by component:**

- Parity and permissible considerations: [Fogerty v. Fantasy, Inc — Prevailing plaintiffs and defendants receive the same discretionary standard](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Fogerty_v_Fantasy_Inc_merits_1994-03-01.md#public-projection), 1994-03-01. Stone-Zsela's opinion of the Court, joined by Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Ginsburg; eight Justices adopt this rule and its copyright-purpose explanation. Thomas independently agrees with equal discretionary eligibility from the statutory text, but concurs in the judgment without joining that explanation. The Court's eight-vote rationale controls directly.
- Discretion, reasonableness and statutory limitations: [Fogerty v. Fantasy, Inc — Prevailing status permits a fee decision but does not command payment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Fogerty_v_Fantasy_Inc_merits_1994-03-01.md#public-projection), 1994-03-01. Stone-Zsela's opinion of the Court, joined by Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, and Ginsburg; eight Justices adopt this holding and remedy. Thomas independently adopts the same permissive statutory rule and remand in his concurrence in the judgment; no combination of unlike rationales is necessary to establish authority.

**Present operation effective:** Fogerty v. Fantasy, Inc, 1994-03-01.

**Exceptions and limits:** No §412 construction, costs ruling, contingency enhancement rule or prescribed weight for objective reasonableness is adopted.

**Holdings navigation:** [Prevailing plaintiffs and defendants receive the same discretionary standard](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#prevailing-plaintiffs-and-defendants-receive-the-same-discretionary-standard); [Prevailing status permits a fee decision but does not command payment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#prevailing-status-permits-a-fee-decision-but-does-not-command-payment).

## Trademark and trade dress

### Inherently distinctive nonfunctional restaurant trade dress

**Governing question:** Whether acquired secondary meaning is additionally necessary under pre-1988 Lanham Act §43(a).

**Current rule:** Nonfunctional restaurant trade dress that is inherently distinctive may be protected without acquired secondary meaning if the remaining infringement requirements, including likelihood of confusion, are met. Protection does not expire after an unspecified period merely because acquired meaning has not developed. Functionality, distinctiveness and confusion remain independent requirements.

**Authority by component:**

- Inherent-distinctiveness alternative and rejection of an unwritten expiration period: [Two Pesos, Inc. v. Taco Cabana, Inc., “Inherent distinctiveness suffices without acquired secondary meaning”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Two_Pesos_v_Taco_Cabana_merits_1992-06-26.md), June 26, 1992; White, Stone, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter.

**Present operation effective:** *Two Pesos*, June 26, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** No general ownership of cuisine, decorative style, useful designs or generic matter, later product-design rule, new proof burden or resolution of the excluded functionality challenge follows.

**Holdings navigation:** [Two Pesos, Inc. v. Taco Cabana, Inc., 505 U.S. 763 (1992) — Trade dress](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#two-pesos-inc-v-taco-cabana-inc-505-us-763-1992).

### Source-identifying color and functionality

**Governing question:** Eligibility of color for Lanham Act protection.

**Current rule:** Color may receive trademark protection when it acquires source-identifying significance and is nonfunctional, subject to ordinary Lanham Act requirements including likelihood of confusion where required. Bare color requires secondary meaning; the law protects neither color in the abstract nor useful features. A feature essential to use or purpose or affecting cost or quality is functional, and protection cannot foreclose color shown competitively necessary. Source significance does not overcome functionality.

**Authority by component:**

- Color may identify source under the Lanham Act: [Color may identify source under the Lanham Act](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Qualitex_Co_v_Jacobson_Products_Co_merits_1995-03-28.md#public-projection), 1995-03-28. Justice Souter's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer: nine direct rationale votes.
- Functionality preserves useful and competitively necessary color features: [Functionality preserves useful and competitively necessary color features](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Qualitex_Co_v_Jacobson_Products_Co_merits_1995-03-28.md#public-projection), 1995-03-28. Justice Souter's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg, and Breyer: nine direct rationale votes on this operative limitation.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-03-28; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** The decision does not resolve every aesthetic feature or color-market competition question or establish infringement merely from eligibility. The existing inherently distinctive trade-dress rule is not replaced by a universal secondary-meaning demand.

**Holdings navigation:** [Color may identify source under the Lanham Act](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#color-may-identify-source-under-the-lanham-act); [Functionality preserves useful and competitively necessary color features](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#functionality-preserves-useful-and-competitively-necessary-color-features).

## Constitutional structure

### Germane military judicial assignments and fixed tenure

**Governing question:** Whether commissioned military judges need another appointment or fixed terms in the statutory system reviewed in Weiss.

**Current rule:** The Appointments Clause applies, but presidential appointment and Senate confirmation to a military office suffice for assignment of germane trial and appellate judicial duties; assignment does not confer a new unrelated office. Due process does not require fixed terms merely because those assignments lack specified duration: Middendorf’s military-specific inquiry considers history and actual statutory protections against command influence. The facial holding does not require proof of actual capitulation to challenge a constitutionally intolerable structure, or preclude supported retaliation, improper reassignment or command-influence claims.

**Authority by component:**

- Existing commission and germane-duty appointment rule: [Weiss v. United States — Commissioned officers assigned germane military judicial duties](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Weiss_v_United_States_merits_1994-01-19.md#public-projection), 1994-01-19. Part A of Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; nine Justices support the confined existing-commission and germane-duty proposition.
- Contextual due-process tenure holding: [Weiss v. United States — Fixed tenure and impartial military adjudication](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Weiss_v_United_States_merits_1994-01-19.md#public-projection), 1994-01-19. Part B of Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter and Ginsburg; seven Justices. Scalia and Thomas concur in the judgment on the distinct historical-practice ground and join the historical description, but do not join Part B's contextual sufficiency analysis.

**Present operation effective:** Weiss v. United States, 1994-01-19.

**Open question:** Civilian appellate appointments, unrelated or principal-office assignments and every possible structural-bias claim remain undecided.

**Holdings navigation:** [Commissioned officers assigned germane military judicial duties](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#commissioned-officers-assigned-germane-military-judicial-duties); [Fixed tenure and impartial military adjudication](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#fixed-tenure-and-impartial-military-adjudication).

### Selective execution control by congressional agents

**Governing question:** Constitutional limits on a congressional agent’s legally operative control of executive action.

**Current rule:** Congress may advise, oversee, obtain reports and enact a fixed waiting period, but may not give its agent selective, legally operative control over execution outside the constitutional allocation of power. Power to trigger an additional legal restraint on otherwise covered airport decisions is such control. Mere influence, nominations considered alone, consultation, reporting and nonvoting attendance are not independently forbidden.

**Authority by component:**

- Congressional agents may not control the legal effectiveness of airport decisions: [Congressional agents may not control the legal effectiveness of airport decisions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Metropolitan_Washington_Airports_Authority_v_Hechinger_merits_1995-06-05.md#public-projection), 1995-06-05. Stevens's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer; nine Justices adopt the congressional-agency-plus-operative-control ground. Stone's independent Article I ground is not an additional Court holding.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-05; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** The airport statute’s §2456(h) contingency separately withdraws the Authority’s power to perform covered submitted actions when a judicial order prevents the Board from performing its statutory functions. Severance cannot restore actions that contingency prohibits. Unaffected authorized functions, the lease and compact survive; past transactions are not automatically invalidated.

**Holdings navigation:** [Congressional agents may not control the legal effectiveness of airport decisions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#congressional-agents-may-not-control-the-legal-effectiveness-of-airport-decisions).

### Timely direct challenges to adjudicators’ appointments

**Governing question:** Relief for a litigant’s timely substantial constitutional appointment objection on direct review.

**Current rule:** A litigant who timely challenges a substantial constitutional defect in the appointment of officers adjudicating his own case and pursues it on direct review is entitled to fresh adjudication by lawfully appointed officers. The de facto officer doctrine cannot validate the defective panel’s judgment against him. A higher review that lacks the displaced proceeding’s fact weighing, credibility, contested-fact and sentence-review functions does not replace those lost opportunities.

**Authority by component:**

- A timely direct appointment challenger receives lawful adjudication: [A timely direct appointment challenger receives lawful adjudication](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Ryder_v_United_States_merits_1995-06-12.md#public-projection), 1995-06-12. Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Breyer; nine Justices adopt this bounded rule and remedy.
- The higher court did not supply the missing Article 66 review: [The higher court did not supply the missing Article 66 review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Ryder_v_United_States_merits_1995-06-12.md#public-projection), 1995-06-12. Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Breyer; all nine adopt this case-specific rejection of cure and preserved-argument limit.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-06-12; the component authorities identified above.

**Open question:** General harmless-error availability, cure in another appellate structure, defaulted or collateral judgments and ministerial acts remain undecided. Fresh appellate adjudication does not automatically dismiss or reverse the trial conviction.

**Holdings navigation:** [A timely direct appointment challenger receives lawful adjudication](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#a-timely-direct-appointment-challenger-receives-lawful-adjudication); [The higher court did not supply the missing Article 66 review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-higher-court-did-not-supply-the-missing-article-66-review).

## Copyright

### Integrated fair use of commercial parody

**Governing question:** How §107 applies to claimed parody and its borrowing and market effects.

**Current rule:** Consider together purpose and character, the copyrighted work’s nature, the amount and substantiality taken, and effect on the potential market or value. Commercial parody may qualify without a dispositive presumption of unfairness. Parody’s particular borrowing justification requires reasonably perceptible criticism directed at the original; satire directed elsewhere lacks that justification merely as satire but may establish fair use under the factors. Transformation informs purpose without absorbing the other factors. Requesting or being denied permission does not defeat an otherwise fair use. Creative character remains relevant, though it contributes little to separating fair from unfair parody, which ordinarily uses recognizable expression. Quantity and qualitative importance must reasonably relate to the critical purpose; recognizable or central material is not excessive solely because it is central. Repetition and potential substitution remain relevant, with no fixed allowance. For transformative parody, commerciality alone creates no market-harm presumption. Examine substitution for the original and legitimate potential derivatives, including widespread comparable conduct; lost demand caused by criticism or loss of licensing the critical attack itself is not cognizable substitution. Supported nonparody derivative markets remain relevant.

**Authority by component:**

- Integrated factors, parody/satire distinction and protected creative nature: [Campbell v. Acuff-Rose Music, Inc — Commercial parody and the integrated statutory inquiry; The original song remains protected creative expression](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Campbell_v_Acuff_Rose_Music_Inc_merits_1994-03-07.md#public-projection), 1994-03-07. Souter's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, and Ginsburg; all nine adopt this proposition and its stated limits. Kennedy's separate caution does not withdraw his full join. Direct unanimous authority; no Marks inquiry.
- Purpose-related taking and cognizable market substitution: [Campbell v. Acuff-Rose Music, Inc — The lyrical reference is justified; the extent of musical borrowing remains open; Market substitution differs from harm caused by criticism](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Campbell_v_Acuff_Rose_Music_Inc_merits_1994-03-07.md#public-projection), 1994-03-07. Souter's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, and Ginsburg; all nine adopt this proposition and its stated limits. Kennedy's separate caution does not withdraw his full join. Direct unanimous authority; no Marks inquiry.
- Unchanged defense and summary-judgment burdens: [Campbell v. Acuff-Rose Music, Inc — The defense burden and limited remand survive correction of the presumptions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Campbell_v_Acuff_Rose_Music_Inc_merits_1994-03-07.md#public-projection), 1994-03-07. Souter's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, and Ginsburg; all nine adopt this proposition and its stated limits. Kennedy's separate caution does not withdraw his full join. Direct unanimous authority; no Marks inquiry.

**Present operation effective:** Campbell v. Acuff-Rose Music, Inc, 1994-03-07.

**Burdens and proof:** Fair use remains an affirmative defense. Defendants seeking summary judgment must establish it on a record permitting judgment as a matter of law; rejecting presumptions does not shift that burden. No general unpublished-work rule or conclusive parody entitlement follows.

**Holdings navigation:** [Commercial parody and the integrated statutory inquiry](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#commercial-parody-and-the-integrated-statutory-inquiry); [The original song remains protected creative expression](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-original-song-remains-protected-creative-expression); [The lyrical reference is justified; the extent of musical borrowing remains open](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-lyrical-reference-is-justified-the-extent-of-musical-borrowing-remains-open); [Market substitution differs from harm caused by criticism](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#market-substitution-differs-from-harm-caused-by-criticism); [The defense burden and limited remand survive correction of the presumptions](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-defense-burden-and-limited-remand-survive-correction-of-the-presumptions).

## Public benefits and health law

### Causal veterans benefits without provider fault

**Governing question:** Eligibility under the §1151 text governing the reviewed VA claim.

**Current rule:** Section 1151 requires injury or aggravation resulting from covered VA hospitalization, medical or surgical treatment, chapter 31 vocational rehabilitation or examination, producing additional disability or death and not resulting from the veteran’s own willful misconduct. It does not require provider negligence, other fault or an unforeseen untoward accident; the added prerequisite in §3.358(c)(3) is invalid. Actual causation remains required and the natural-progress exclusion is undisturbed.

**Authority by component:**

- Section 1151 requires causation, not provider fault or accident: [Section 1151 requires causation, not provider fault or accident](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1994/records/Brown_v_Gardner_merits_1994-12-12.md#public-projection), 1994-12-12.
